# hermes-agent :: hermes-gateway

**Files in shard:** 328 (read: 327, skipped with reason: 1)

**What this shard covers:** the entire `gateway/` subtree of the Hermes agent - a
messaging gateway that fronts one agent loop to many chat platforms (Telegram, Slack, Discord,
WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, IRC, Teams, Google Chat, email, a webhook, an OpenAI-compatible
API server, and a cross-gateway Relay). It owns the *runtime* of a turn - inbound
admission, authorization, routing identity, session persistence, cron/scheduled work,
streaming delivery, notifications, approval prompts, multi-agent hosted rooms, the
lifecycle/shutdown forensics, and the durable ledgers that make all of that survive a
crash. It is the transport and orchestration layer, not the agent loop itself.

## 1. File-by-file

### Reading method (for transparency)

326 of the 328 files in this shard are `source` (plus 2 `docs`, 1 `config`, 1 `assets`), so
there is no Tier-2 bulk table to give: **every** file is a Tier-1 entry. Files under 40 KB
were read in full; the 23 files of 40 KB and over (`run.py` 275 KB, `platforms/base.py` 278 KB,
`platforms/api_server.py` 258 KB, `run_turn.py` 141 KB, `run_shutdown.py` 125 KB,
`relay/adapter.py` 118 KB, `run_adapters.py` 116 KB, `run_notifications.py` 112 KB,
`run_startup.py` 112 KB, `run_inbound.py` 107 KB, `status.py` 105 KB and others) were read as
head (first 500 lines) + a middle window (40% offset, 500 lines) + tail (300 lines), so their
structure, seams and cited line references are real, but not every line was read. The one
binary asset was not read at all and is in Tier 3.

### Tier 1 - load-bearing source: full entry

### `gateway/AGENTS.md`
(The repo has no `docs/` directory; this file lives at `gateway/AGENTS.md`, 18232 bytes — the assigned size exactly. Line refs below are `gateway/AGENTS.md:N`.)

- **Purpose:** The gateway subsystem's agent-facing contract sheet — the invariants a coding agent must not break when touching `gateway/`.
- **Key abstractions:** One prose contract per section: "Shape" (gateway/AGENTS.md:6) maps `run.py` as facade / `run_*.py` phases / `session*.py` / `slash_commands_*.py` mixins / `authz_mixin.py` / `platforms/<name>.py` over `platforms/base.py`; "TWO message guards" (:20); "Streaming delivery contract" (:30); "Background process notifications" (:59); "`/login`" (:72); "Gateway lifecycle vs. the Desktop app" (:80); "Profile scope" (:91); "Tests" (:218).
- **Data flow:** No code flow — it names the seams. Config reaches the gateway **raw** from `run.py` + `config.py`, deliberately bypassing `DEFAULT_CONFIG`; "a key the CLI sees but the gateway doesn't means you're on the wrong loader" (:12-14). Slash commands are looked up by name through `_command_handler_table`, never an `if canonical == ...` chain (:16-17).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - Both message guards must be bypassed for approval/control commands, and those commands must be dispatched **inline** — never via `_process_message_background()`, which races session lifecycle (:20-28).
  - The streaming contract's four invariants — prefix-stable draft frames (:37), "the consumer declares the final; the adapter never guesses" (:41), interim sends carrying `metadata["_interim_send"] = True` (:45), "reconcile by edit, never by plain send" (:49) — each exist because of a *live duplicate-final incident*, not theory.
  - Intake adapter vs delivery adapter are two distinct resolvers; "never read `self.adapters[platform]` for a source; never add a third resolver" (:115-124), with a 5-row topology matrix (:124-130).
  - Multiplex profile-scoped env reads must fail closed — never borrow from `os.environ` (:148) — because a leaked allowlist skips the allow-all check and silently rejects every secondary-profile sender (#86905).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Contract tests live at `tests/gateway/test_stream_final_contract.py` and are **mutation-checked** (:54) — a test that cannot fail is treated as a defect. Slack's `startStream`/`stopStream` are Tier-2 rate-limited (~20/min) (:55-56). The known breach — the Windows shim-unlock `taskkill /T /F` teardown (#85265) — is explicitly *not* to be "fixed" by re-parenting the gateway under the backend or widening the tree-kill (:85-89). `admit_internal_event` must return a receipt: a handler returning `None` is not acceptance, and refused admission refunds every claimed batch sibling without spending an attempt (:63-65).
- **Surprising:** `builtin_hooks/` is documented as the always-registered hook extension point with **none shipped** (:11-12) — an empty package kept as a seam. `draft_stream_is_message` must be tested with `is True`, because MagicMock adapters in older tests auto-create truthy attributes (:56-57). `/login` binds the *whole install's* `providers.nous`, so shared gateways must set `allow_admin_from` (:75-76).

### `gateway/__init__.py` — Lazy PEP-562 `__getattr__` re-export shim: `_EXPORTS` (gateway/__init__.py:8-18) maps `GatewayConfig`/`PlatformConfig`/`HomeChannel`/`load_gateway_config` → `.config`, `SessionContext`/`SessionStore`/`build_session_context_prompt` → `.session`, `DeliveryRouter`/`DeliveryTarget` → `.delivery`, so importing `gateway` for a control/status client never initializes config, plugins or user data (gateway/__init__.py:6-7); unknown names raise `AttributeError` rather than importing anything (gateway/__init__.py:23-27).

### `gateway/agent_cache_pressure.py`
- **Purpose:** Supplies the memory-pressure signal for the gateway's per-session `AIAgent` cache — the LRU entry cap and the idle TTL count entries and time, never bytes, so neither sees real memory use.
- **Key abstractions:** `AgentCacheBounds` frozen dataclass (gateway/agent_cache_pressure.py:33) with `max_size`/`idle_ttl_secs`/`memory_high_mb`/`max_evictions_per_pass`/`protect_recent`; `resolve_memory_high_mb` (:103); `resolve_agent_cache_bounds` (:121); `read_anon_rss_mb` (:172); `transcript_persistence_caught_up` (:198); `plan_pressure_evictions` (:210).
- **Data flow:** raw config dict → `resolve_agent_cache_bounds` → `AgentCacheBounds`; cgroup `memory.high`/`memory.max` (own cgroup first, then root; v2 before v1) → byte limit → × `_AUTO_BUDGET_FRACTION` 0.65 → MB budget, floored at 512 MB (:67-88, :118-119). Pressure signal: own cgroup `memory.stat` `anon` → `hermes_cli.mem_trim.collect_memory_snapshot` (`rss_anon_kib` then `rss_kib`) → psutil RSS (:142-196). `plan_pressure_evictions` takes LRU→MRU ordered entries and returns a bounded shed list (:210-226).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - `memory.stat` **anon** rather than `memory.current`: reclaimable page cache is noise, and the kernel charges every process in the unit against the same cgroup (execute_code kernels, terminal children), so a self-only reading under-counts by exactly the children's share (#110549) (:142-152).
  - `anon` is read only when the cgroup is *capped*; an uncapped login-session cgroup is the whole user slice and its budget is total RAM, so self RSS stays the right scope there (:147-149, :90-101).
  - `resolve_agent_cache_bounds` reads the **raw** config, not `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, because the gateway loader does not deep-merge `DEFAULT_CONFIG` — so the caller can tell "operator chose 128" from "unset" (:121-126).
  - `protect_recent: 0` is a real setting ("shed anything"), distinct from unset — but `protect_recent: false` (False == 0) stays on the default 8 rather than silently disabling MRU protection (:129-134).
  - `plan_pressure_evictions` clamps `protect_recent` to half the cache (:216-217): a few huge transcripts can exhaust the budget alone, so a fixed guard would leave nothing to shed.
  - The auto budget is 65 % of the limit, not ~100 % (:21-22): once cgroup `memory.high` throttling starts (swap full) a SIGTERM flush cannot finish inside systemd's stop timeout.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Unknown agent shapes are treated as *not caught up* for eviction safety — "a skipped eviction costs memory, a wrong one costs the conversation" (:198-208). The v1 near-2^63 sentinel and `max` are treated as unlimited (:57-65). All `sysconf`/`psutil` reads are wrapped so a missing dependency degrades to `None` (pressure off) rather than raising (:90-101, :172-196).
- **Surprising:** The module is described as pure/read-only yet it *decides* eviction — the shedding itself lives in `GatewayRunner` (gateway/run.py), which rebuilds the transcript from the persisted session on the next turn. `max_size`/`idle_ttl_secs` stay `None` by default so `gateway/run.py` keeps its own defaults (:33-41).

### `gateway/assets/status_phrases.yaml` — Two-bucket canned-phrase list for "still working" status pings: `status:` (gateway/assets/status_phrases.yaml:1-31, 31 slow-work phrases like "still on it", "not stuck, just still running") and `generic:` (:32-52, 21 short ones like "on it", "one sec"); every entry is deliberately non-committal about liveness so a ping can never imply a false ETA.

### `gateway/authz_mixin.py`
- **Purpose:** The `GatewayRunner` mixin that decides whether a user/chat may talk to the agent — per-adapter DM policy, allowlists, pairing, trusted-upstream delegation, unauthorized-DM behavior, and the bot-loop guard.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayAuthorizationMixin` (gateway/authz_mixin.py:193) — `_intake_adapter_for` (:259) vs `_delivery_adapter_for` (:295) (the two adapter resolvers), `_adapters_for_profile` (:213), `_is_shared_bot_satellite` (:237), `_authorization_home_for_source` (:349), `_restored_source` (:390), `_own_policy_authorizes` (:470), `_adapter_extra_allowlist_authorizes` (:492), `_chat_scoped_grant` (:539), `_is_user_authorized` (:631), `_principal_authorized` (:646), `_get_unauthorized_dm_behavior` (:716); module-level pure-stdlib bech32 Nostr decoder `_npub_to_hex` (:137) / `_normalize_nostr_allow_entries` (:154).
- **Data flow:** a `SessionSource` (platform, chat_id, user_id, chat_type, is_bot) → `_principal_authorized` reads allowlists through the scoped reader `_auth_env` (`gateway.platforms._shared.platform_gate_env`) → identity/transport resolution decides *whose* allowlist applies → boolean verdict; `_is_user_authorized` then applies the bot-loop-guard veto for bot-authored messages (:641-644).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **Intake adapter ≠ delivery adapter.** Intake is the bot that *received* the event and gates intake policy; delivery is the bot that *answers*. With no live provenance `_intake_adapter_for` fails closed to `None` (:271-293) and `_delivery_adapter_for` falls back to the unique owner of `(platform, runtime_profile)` (:320-323).
  - `enforces_own_access_policy` (WeCom/Weixin/Yuanbao/QQBot/WhatsApp gate at intake) is explicitly **not** "already authorized" — those adapters default to `open`, so the verdict is trusted only under an actual `allowlist` policy (:403-411, :470-490).
  - SimpleX's allowlist matches only the stable numeric `contactId`; the display name is attacker-controlled, so matching it would bypass the allowlist (#44729) (:178-179). Buzz's allowlist accepts npub *or* hex while inbound pubkeys are always hex, so entries are bech32-decoded in pure stdlib (#78428) (:94-98, :181-189).
  - Telegram legacy path: `TELEGRAM_GROUP_ALLOWED_USERS` was once (mis)used as a chat-ID allowlist, so "-" -prefixed values are honored for backward compatibility with a one-time warning (:585-601).
  - `_own_policy_authorizes` re-checks DMs through the live adapter's `_is_dm_allowed` when present, because pairing revoke can clear `WHATSAPP_ALLOWED_USERS` while a construction-time snapshot would keep authorizing until restart (:481-489).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `user_id is None` (anonymous admins, sender_chat posts, channel broadcasts, Slack bot posts) is handled by chat-scoped grants *before* the no-user-id guard (:552-563); bot-authored messages bypass the human allowlist only via the platform's `allow_bots_env` in `{mentions, all}` mode, and no entry (plugin not loaded) means no bypass (:569-583); every boolean is compared with `is True` so a MagicMock cannot auto-authorize (:546, :672, :688).
- **Surprising:** `GATEWAY_ALLOWED_USERS` is unioned with the platform list and `*` in any list authorizes everyone (:707-714). The unauthorized-DM default becomes `"ignore"` whenever *any* allowlist is configured — "spamming unknown contacts with pairing codes is noisy and a potential info-leak" — while Email always defaults to `"ignore"` (:716-758). `_bot_loop_guard_conversation` keys the budget per *conversation*, not per sender pair, because a per-pair key would hand N bots N budgets (:612-615).

### `gateway/bot_loop_guard.py` — Per-conversation sliding-window budget that stops two Hermes profiles replying to each other: `BotLoopGuard.admit` (gateway/bot_loop_guard.py:110) counts admitted bot messages, trips a `cooldown_seconds` pause once `max_events` land inside `window_seconds`, and returns one of `disabled`/`ok`/`tripped`/`cooldown`; settings are re-read from config.yaml on *every* call (gateway/bot_loop_guard.py:72-78, :88) so an edit takes effect without a restart, and `_sweep` (gateway/bot_loop_guard.py:132) drops idle conversations at most once per window so the dict stays bounded.

### `gateway/browser_control_artifacts.py`
- **Purpose:** The one-shot, TTL-bounded artifact transport for browser control — bytes live under a controlled root while only a server-minted id travels on the wire.
- **Key abstractions:** `ArtifactStore` (gateway/browser_control_artifacts.py:114) with `store`/`validate`/`load`/`prune_expired`; `ArtifactReceipt` (:67); the error ladder `ArtifactError` → `ArtifactNotFound`/`ArtifactExpired`/`ArtifactTooLarge`/`ArtifactMimeRejected`/`ArtifactScopeMismatch`/`ArtifactChecksumMismatch`/`ArtifactTraversal` (:34-63); `artifact_scope_key` (:89); `ArtifactRateLimiter` (:298).
- **Data flow:** bytes + filename + content_type + scope → size/MIME checks → mint a 32-hex id → temp-file write + `os.fsync` + `os.replace` → index entry; a later `load` re-verifies SHA-256 and consumes atomically, then deletes the file (:161-222).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The scope key **deliberately excludes `session_id`** (:89-107): HTTP artifact routes authenticate by API key and cannot resolve a session while broker dispatch always carries one, so hashing it would make upload and dispatch never compose. Only principal (mandatory) + transport family participate; capabilities/optional ids are excluded so a reconnect keeps its artifacts.
  - `load` is one-shot and the **index entry is dropped before the unlink** (:210-222), so a concurrent second load fails closed — but a checksum mismatch does *not* consume (:216-218).
  - `_entry_for` checks the target's own expiry **before** sweeping (:250-254) so an expired artifact surfaces as `ArtifactExpired`, not `ArtifactNotFound`.
  - `_artifact_path` re-validates that the resolved path's parent equals the resolved root (:273-285) — belt-and-braces on top of the `^[0-9a-f]{32}$` id regex (:30).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Size and MIME rejections fire **before any disk write** (:164-170); the MIME list is an exact allowlist with no wildcards (:27-29); a failed write pops the index entry, unlinks the temp file and re-raises (:188-196); the post-load unlink is best-effort because "TTL sweep will retry" (:220-222); `prune_expired` also sweeps `*.tmp` files older than the TTL (:225-234).
- **Surprising:** Receipts live only in memory, so files left by a previous process are unreachable orphans "past their TTL by definition" and are swept at construction time — `_sweep_orphan_files` only touches `*.tmp` and minted-id-shaped names, never anything else in the root (:130-146). The client-supplied filename is display-only metadata, never a path: `_bounded_filename` strips separators and control characters and truncates to 160 chars (:292-295).

### `gateway/browser_control_broker.py`
- **Purpose:** Transport-neutral broker core binding an identity-scoped *controller* (the party driving a browser) to *callers* arriving on any transport.
- **Key abstractions:** `BrowserControlBroker` (gateway/browser_control_broker.py:169) with `mint_ticket`/`consume_ticket`/`attach`/`select`/`dispatch`/`complete`/`cancel`/`detach`/`disconnect`/`reset`; `ControllerScope` (:109); `Ticket` (:129); error ladder `ControllerTicketInvalid`/`ControllerUnavailable`/`ControllerCancelled`/`ControllerTimeout`/`ControllerRejected` (:88-106); `filter_browser_control_capabilities` (:74); `get_browser_control_broker()` returning the process-global `_GLOBAL_BROKER` (:507-511).
- **Data flow:** caller → `select(scope, capability)` → `dispatch` writes a `browser.controller.command` frame with a fresh `command_id` → the controller replies `complete(command_id, ok=…, result=…)` → the pending `threading.Event` is set → `dispatch` returns the result or raises (:335-390).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The `send` callback runs **outside** the broker lock (:16-17, :352-362) so a controller may call `complete` from inside its own send.
  - `capabilities` are excluded from identity equality (`_IDENTITY_FIELDS`, :121) — a same-identity reconnect *refreshes* the negotiated capability set instead of cancelling pending work (:240-260).
  - Developer Mode is read live from config on every selection when not pinned (:180-193), so flipping it off **revokes** raw CDP/eval from already-attached controllers without a restart; `browser_cdp`/`browser_evaluate` are the only privileged capabilities (:36).
  - `attach` hard-replaces a *different* controller in the same authenticated session lane — "not a recoverable reconnect" — and terminalizes the lane siblings first so session lookup stays unique (:261-268).
  - `detach` fails pending work closed (`ControllerCancelled`) while `disconnect` only marks the transport offline (:302-333).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** The timeout path re-checks `pending.done` under the lock, because `Event.wait()` can return False at the exact boundary where a completion already won and removed the pending command (:366-372); a frame that never left the building pops its pending id and re-raises (:359-362); `detach` holds the old generation's `send_lock` through cancellation so a command frame can never overtake its terminal cancel frame (:328-331); cancel frames for an offline controller are deferred (capped at 512) and flushed on reconnect (:269-283, :440-443).
- **Surprising:** Artifact actions travel by **id only** — `_validate_artifact_reference` fails closed unless an attached store approves the id, and any store exception becomes `ControllerRejected` (:425-438). `scope_for_session` treats the session id as a *hint* only and requires a server-derived principal + transport family; an ambiguous lane returns `None` (fails closed) while `lane_registered` reports `True` for an ambiguous lane (:456-475). Protocol version is checked with `type(value) is int`, so `True` is rejected (:45-47).

### `gateway/builtin_hooks/__init__.py` — Empty package whose only content is a one-line docstring ("Built-in gateway hooks that are always registered", gateway/builtin_hooks/__init__.py:1): the documented extension point for always-registered gateway hooks, with **none shipped** (gateway/AGENTS.md:11-12).

### `gateway/cgroup_cleanup.py` — Linux-only `ExecStopPost=` safety net that SIGKILLs orphans left in the gateway's systemd cgroup: `reap_cgroup` (gateway/cgroup_cleanup.py:77) signals per-PID over `cgroup.procs` instead of writing `1` to `cgroup.kill` because the kernel returns EINVAL on the cgroup-wide kill while per-PID delivery still works; it refuses (returns `None`, no signals) when a live gateway runtime is still in the cgroup (gateway/cgroup_cleanup.py:59-75, :88-99), and `main` refuses outright unless the parent is systemd (gateway/cgroup_cleanup.py:43-56, :114-127) — any other parent shares a *live* process's cgroup and would be SIGKILLed with it. The PID list is re-read after the guard, because the guard itself can take seconds and PIDs may exit or be reused meanwhile (gateway/cgroup_cleanup.py:101-104).

### `gateway/channel_directory.py`
- **Purpose:** The cached map of reachable channels/contacts per platform, rebuilt every 5 minutes and persisted to `<HERMES_HOME>/channel_directory.json`, used by `send_message` for `action="list"` and for resolving friendly names to ids.
- **Key abstractions:** `build_channel_directory(adapters)` (gateway/channel_directory.py:132); `load_directory` (:389); `resolve_channel_name` (:409); `lookup_channel_type` (:403); `format_directory_for_display` (:438); the module globals `DIRECTORY_PATH` / `CHANNEL_ALIASES_PATH` (:24-29) and `_apply_channel_aliases` (:65).
- **Data flow:** connected adapters → per-platform enumeration (`list_channels()` hook, else Discord guild walk, else Slack `users.conversations` pagination) → session-history discovery from `state.db` (with a `sessions.json` legacy fallback) → alias overlay → `atomic_json_write` to the directory path → later reads re-apply the aliases (:132-172, :389-400).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - Paths resolve **lazily** via `_directory_path()` / `_aliases_path()` (:40-46): a multiplexed gateway serves several profile homes from one process, so an import-time constant would pin every profile to whichever home imported first.
  - The user-maintained friendly-name overlay lives in a *separate* file (`channel_aliases.json`) and is re-applied on every build **and** every load (:22-29, :65-87) — hand edits to the regenerated `channel_directory.json` do not survive. An alias for an undiscovered chat creates a placeholder entry, so a fresh group is addressable by name before its first message (:78-86).
  - Session-based discovery only runs for platforms **connected in this process** (:157-165): origins of disabled or decommissioned platforms must not resurface as stale send targets. `local`, `api_server` and `webhook` are excluded outright (:36).
  - `resolve_channel_name` tries an exact **id** match first, case-sensitively, before any normalization (:415-419) so raw Slack ids (`C0B0QV5434G`) work even when the target-ref format guard did not recognize them; partial prefix matches only win when unambiguous (:431-434).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Slack pagination is capped at 20 pages (:240); a missing `channels:read` scope is logged at DEBUG (expected) while other failures warn once per (team, detail) per hour (:115-129); Slack dm/channel entries that stay raw-id (`C0`/`D0`/`G0` prefixes) are resolved later via `conversations.info` + `users.info`, concurrently per base conversation (:263-294); every JSON read tolerates a Windows BOM (`utf-8-sig`, :48-51) and a missing/non-dict file degrades to `{}` (:54-62).
- **Surprising:** Discord forum channels are typed `"forum"` because posting to a forum auto-spawns a thread post (:186-188); Discord entries carry a `guild` field, which is what lets `format_directory_for_display` group by guild and put DMs last (:452-462). `_slack_base_id` strips the `:thread` suffix because thread-qualified ids are internal routing keys, not Slack API ids (:227-229).

### `gateway/code_skew.py` — Detects that the running gateway's frozen `sys.modules` no longer matches the checkout after a hot `git pull`: `record_boot_fingerprint` (gateway/code_skew.py:30) snapshots a `git:<ref>:<sha>` fingerprint at startup, `detect_code_skew` (gateway/code_skew.py:52) compares it against disk and returns `(boot_rev, disk_rev)` short labels so risky callers (e.g. `/model` switching) can refuse with a clear "restart the gateway" message — and when the revision cannot be read (non-git install, IO error) the boot snapshot stays `None` and detection no-ops, so it never produces a false positive (gateway/code_skew.py:8-13, :19-28).

### `gateway/config.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway configuration schema — `GatewayConfig`, `PlatformConfig`, `StreamingConfig`, the `Platform` enum (with dynamic plugin members) — plus the load/validate pipeline entry point.
- **Key abstractions:** `Platform` enum + `_missing_` (gateway/config.py:217-298); `HomeChannel` (:339), `ChannelOverride` (:378), `PlatformConfig` (:408), `StreamingConfig` (:486), `GatewayConfig` (:591); `load_gateway_config()` (:825); `_validate_gateway_config` (:854); `platform_binds_port` (:320); `discord_channel_id_from_link` (:331); `_getenv` (:186).
- **Data flow:** legacy `gateway.json` → `config_loader.load_yaml_layer` (config.yaml, raw) → `GatewayConfig.from_dict` → `_apply_env_overrides` (delegates to `gateway.config_env`) → `_validate_gateway_config` → the returned `GatewayConfig` (:825-851).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - `Platform` accepts unknown names only for bundled or runtime-registered plugin adapters, via `_missing_` + `_add_pseudo_member` (:244-276) — no enum pollution. A bundled plugin whose `plugin.yaml` `name:` differs from its directory resolves through an alias map to the directory-name member (:257-263, :278-298).
  - `_getenv` reads through the **active profile secret scope** when one is present (:186-192): multiplexed per-profile secrets must win over the ambient environment.
  - `_normalize_transport_token` maps YAML 1.1 booleans to `"auto"`/`"off"` (:93-101) — bare `mode: off` parses as `False`, and `str(False).lower()` is `"false"`, which would have *enabled* streaming.
  - `multiplex_profiles` defaults to `None`, not `False` (:619): `None` means "a request the gateway settles at boot" (`resolve_multiplex_mode`), and every reader tests truthiness, so an unresolved value never multiplexes by accident (:612-618).
  - `PlatformConfig.from_dict` promotes every non-typed top-level key into `extra`, with an explicit `extra:` value winning on a clash (:444-476) — users write `platforms.webhook.port`, adapters read `config.extra["port"]`.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_coerce_num` swallows `OverflowError` so `int(float("inf"))` degrades instead of aborting the load (:104-109); watchdog knobs are range-checked with `math.isfinite` and fall back to the shipped defaults (:738-746, :792-793); `coerce_systemd_watchdog_seconds` bounds the value so `Type=notify` can never be enabled without heartbeats (:137-156); a placeholder bot token (copied from `.env.example`) is caught by `has_usable_secret(min_length=4)` and the platform is **disabled** with an error (:854-882); a malformed `config.yaml` is caught and the loader falls back to env + `gateway.json` (:831-846).
- **Surprising:** `get_connected_platforms` sorts by platform value so the rendered "Connected Platforms" prompt block is byte-stable — a reorder would bust the prompt cache (:666-670). `get_unauthorized_dm_behavior` makes Email default to `"ignore"` while a *global* `unauthorized_dm_behavior` does not override it (:811-817). `PLATFORM_TOKEN_ENV_NAMES` deliberately omits platforms that authenticate another way (#64674) — an absent platform must never be skipped for a missing token (:393-405).

### `gateway/config_env.py`
- **Purpose:** The environment-variable override pass, run **after** `GatewayConfig.from_dict`, so env always wins over config.yaml / gateway.json.
- **Key abstractions:** `_apply_env_overrides(config)` (gateway/config_env.py:352) — the ordered pipeline; `_enable_from_env` (:84); `_api_server` / `_webhook` / `_msgraph_webhook` (:112 / :123 / :135); `_enable_plugin_platform` (:183); `_apply_plugin_env_overrides` (:274); `_relay` (:300); `_scrub_explicit_markers` (:347); `_warn_explicit_disable_beats_env` (:56).
- **Data flow:** `GatewayConfig` mutated in place → plugin `env_overrides` rows (registry + bundled fallback) → core-only env rows → registry enable pass → relay exclusivity → marker scrub. The `_enabled_explicit` marker is set by the YAML loader, consulted here, and finally deleted (:347-350).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - `check_fn` is a **passive** probe and `ensure_deps_fn` the active installer; the historical wiring of the active installer as `check_fn` pip-installed Discord/Telegram/Slack/Feishu/Dingtalk SDKs on *every* `load_gateway_config()` call — including the desktop readiness probe `GET /api/status` — blocking startup and boot-looping the app at 94 % (#79812) (:203-218).
  - `is_connected` MUST gate enablement: `check_fn` alone would enable platforms the user never opted into, and those would retry-connect forever (:167-181, :286-298).
  - Relay is **exclusive**: the `GATEWAY_RELAY_URL` env stamp disables every other messaging platform, even explicitly enabled ones, because a directly-connected adapter would be a second unmanaged ingress (duplicate deliveries, split sessions, a socket disarming scale-to-zero). Opt out with `GATEWAY_RELAY_ALLOW_DIRECT_PLATFORMS=true` (:300-345).
  - The api_server/webhook secondary-profile carve-out: a secondary's `API_SERVER_KEY`/`WEBHOOK_ENABLED` must not become listener intent, because the default profile's listener already mirrors those two at `/p/<profile>/` (#100397) (:96-106).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Every plugin step is wrapped so a broken env row cannot break config load (:274-283, :286-298); `_warn_explicit_disable_beats_env` fires **once per platform per process** (:56-74) because config reloads every turn; unusable values keep their default (:30-54); the `_enabled_explicit` marker is *read* (not popped) by the plugin/relay passes but popped by api_server/webhook, and the final scrub removes it from every platform (:84-88, :347-350).
- **Surprising:** `_platform_rank` sorts registry entries by their position in the static `Platform` enum so `config.platforms` insertion order stays deterministic and independent of registry load order (:264-272) — and within one plugin entry only the order of its own `env_overrides` steps matters (e.g. Telegram's reply mode must exist before its home channel attaches to it) (:274-283).

### `gateway/config_loader.py`
- **Purpose:** The `load_gateway_config` phases — `config.yaml` / legacy `gateway.json` → the flat `gw_data` dict that `GatewayConfig.from_dict` consumes.
- **Key abstractions:** `load_yaml_layer(home, gw_data)` (gateway/config_loader.py:520); `read_yaml_layers(home)` (:487); `bridge_toplevel_keys` (:129) plus the `_TOPLEVEL_BRIDGE` table (:88-105) and its five fallback *modes* — `presence`/`gwdata`/`none`/`dict`/`nested` (:41-49); `merge_platform_sections` (:139); `bridge_platform_shared_keys` (:340); `apply_plugin_yaml_hooks` (:384); `bridge_core_env_settings` (:424); `_authored_wins` (:304); `snapshot_authored_extra` (:262); `_apply_managed_extra` (:274); `platform_section` (:184).
- **Data flow:** `config.yaml` (+ managed overlay, + `${VAR}` expansion) → flat `gw_data` → `GatewayConfig.from_dict`; platform blocks merge in the order `gateway.platforms.*` → top-level `platforms.*` → `gateway.<platform>` subsections (:139-174).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - Top-level key **presence** wins over the nested `gateway.<key>` form — not truthiness. A present-but-empty top-level value is never silently replaced by the nested one (:1-6, :107-128).
  - The bridge table is not one rule but five: `presence` (top-level present → else nested), `gwdata` (nested only when *nothing* set it yet), `none` (top-level value is None → nested), `dict` (top-level value is not a mapping → nested, accepted only if a mapping), `nested` (nested form only) (:41-49, :88-105).
  - "Authored" means config.yaml **only** (:262-272): a scratch merge would leave the legacy `gateway.json` extra in `platforms_data`, which must stay the base layer every config.yaml key overrides (:526-529).
  - `_authored_wins` warns **once** per conflicting (platform, key) across both copy sites, and only when the two values actually differ (:304-338).
  - The managed (administrator) layer wins over the user's `platforms.<plat>.extra` — pinned keys are *removed* from the authored dict and then re-merged, because key membership alone cannot carry a value across a cross-shape merge (:274-302).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** An ABSENT user `config.yaml` is an empty layer, not a reason to skip the administrator's values — a fleet host with no config.yaml must still honor them (:487-518); a MALFORMED user file raises, and the loader then falls back to env + gateway.json *without* the managed layer (:489-495, :520-522); the pure-Python `fast_safe_load` parser is used because the installer seeds a ~120 KB comment-heavy example file and nothing caches this loader (:497-503); `allow_all_users: true` is bridged into `GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` with a warning naming the grant source, and the bridge only overwrites a value **it** wrote — an operator's env var still wins (:424-486).
- **Surprising:** The bridge writes to `os.environ` at all, but does so **only** while no multiplexed secondary profile's scope is active (`profile_scoped()`), because a first-writer-wins write there would make the secondary's policy the default profile's (#80099 class) (:446-452, :462-466). `GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` is also dropped from a **child** process env when the bridge itself wrote it, so a restarted gateway re-derives the grant from its config.yaml instead of inheriting a stale open posture (:57-66).

### `gateway/control_socket.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway-owned local control surface — a POSIX `AF_UNIX` socket (or a Windows named pipe) answering versioned JSON verbs (`identify`, `status`, `rescan-profiles`, `pause-for-update`, …) so lifecycle callers can *prove* who owns the host instead of guessing from PID state.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayControlServer` (control_socket.py:196) — owns the bind path, the pointer file, and the verb-handler table (`identify`/`status` + injected `verb_handlers`).
  - `handle_request_line()` (control_socket.py:301) — one JSON line in, one JSON line out; "never raises".
  - `_session_ticket()` (control_socket.py:347) — authenticated bootstrap-ticket minting for the runtime protocol.
  - `query_gateway_control()` (control_socket.py:432) — the client wrapper; returns `None` on *any* failure so callers fall back to the state-file/scan layer.
  - `build_identify_payload()` (control_socket.py:171), `build_status_payload()` (control_socket.py:189).
  - `resolve_server_socket_path()` (control_socket.py:111) / `resolve_client_socket_path()` (control_socket.py:117) — the pointer-file fallback pair.
  - `windows_pipe_name()` (control_socket.py:44), `_squat_proof_fallback()` (control_socket.py:82).
- **Data flow:** client JSON → socket/pipe → `handle_request_line` → verb handler → JSON response → client. On POSIX the handler runs in a thread executor (control_socket.py:399-402) so handlers that read disk never stall the asyncio loop that drives every platform adapter.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. *A connectable socket with a well-formed `identify` answer IS liveness* — no PID-reuse heuristics anywhere (control_socket.py:1-9). The socket is the auth boundary; it is never a TCP port, because filesystem/pipe ACLs are what authorize the peer.
  2. The temp-dir fallback socket is only placed in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` if that directory is *provably* private (owned by this uid, mode `& 0o077 == 0`, control_socket.py:53-63); otherwise `_squat_proof_fallback` (control_socket.py:82) re-homes it under a fresh `mkdtemp` root, because a predictable shared-temp name can be pre-created by another local user.
  3. `_publish_pointer()` (control_socket.py:94) writes the pointer *after* the listener is accepting and replaces it atomically via mkstemp+`os.replace`. A client polling mid-write therefore reads an invalid pointer — a refusal, never "still starting". The same call heals an older gateway's leftover 0644 pointer.
  4. `handle_request_line` inspects the handler's signature with `inspect.signature` to decide whether to pass `params` (control_socket.py:338-341) — argument-less verbs keep a bare signature instead of every verb taking a dict.
  5. Private verbs demand `peer_subject`, `protocol == 1`, and an *exact* envelope key set (control_socket.py:394-399). A forwarded `subject` is accepted only when it is a structured `auth:v1:[provider, issuer, user]` login subject (`_structured_login_subject`, control_socket.py:148) — a bare label like `admin` could collide with another stored owner representation, so it fails closed.
  6. An oversized response gets a distinct `response_too_large` code and limit (control_socket.py:329-337) so a caller cannot misread an oversize answer as a drain or a refusal.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `start()` never raises — a bind failure logs a warning and returns `False` (control_socket.py:220-227), because the gateway must still serve messaging without a control socket. `stop()` revokes the ticket store and removes socket+pointer only when the `(st_dev, st_ino)` identity still matches (control_socket.py:287-299), so a replaced file is not unlinked. `_query_unix_socket` swallows `OSError` and returns `None`; the Windows pipe exchange runs under `run_bounded_sync` with an abandoned-at-deadline worker (control_socket.py:477-485) so a peer that never answers costs `timeout`, never forever.
- **Surprising:** the Windows path does **not** use asyncio's pipe support — the real server is the native overlapped `NativeControlServer` (control_socket.py:268-271). The asyncio `_PipeControlProtocol` class (control_socket.py:411) exists but is a secondary path.

### `gateway/cron_store_notices.py`
Installs the cron-store degraded/recovered home-channel notices: the ticker thread's transition is marshalled onto the gateway loop with `loop.call_soon_threadsafe` (cron_store_notices.py:72-78), and a "recovered" notice is deferred by `NOTICE_REPEAT_SECONDS = 3600` (cron_store_notices.py:22, :61-70) so a store flapping across the disk-full line cannot post a notice pair every tick; delivery re-checks the store's real state before sending (cron_store_notices.py:36-50).

### `gateway/cwd_placeholder.py`
Resolves a placeholder `terminal.cwd` (`.` / `auto` / `cwd`, cwd_placeholder.py:12) to the `TERMINAL_CWD` to set, returning `None` to leave it unset: local → `MESSAGING_CWD` or the home fallback, docker + workspace-mount → the host `MESSAGING_CWD` (so `terminal_tool` can map `/host/project` → `/workspace`), any other backend → `None` (cwd_placeholder.py:15-38) — pinning that the gateway must not blindly map host `Path.home()` into container backends.

### `gateway/dead_targets.py`
Persistent per-profile registry of delivery targets proven *whole-chat* dead (`forbidden`, chat-level `not_found`, dead_targets.py:23) so cron ticks stop burning flood-control budget; thread/topic-level `not_found` is deliberately excluded (dead_targets.py:31-45) because adapters self-heal those by retrying without `reply_to`, and only a later successful send clears the flag (dead_targets.py:94-107).

### `gateway/delivery.py`
- **Purpose:** Routes cron output and agent responses to explicit targets (`telegram:123`), platform home channels, the origin chat, or local files.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `DeliveryRouter` (delivery.py:164) with `deliver()` (delivery.py:174), `_deliver_local()` (delivery.py:210), `_deliver_to_platform()` (delivery.py:260).
  - `DeliveryTarget.parse()` (delivery.py:124) — parses `origin` | `local` | `<platform>` | `<platform>:<chat_id>[:<thread_id>]`.
  - `DeliveryTransport` (delivery.py:49) + `resolve_delivery_transport()` (delivery.py:66) — logical platform → live adapter.
  - `PartialDeliveryError` (delivery.py:39); `MAX_PLATFORM_OUTPUT = 4000` (delivery.py:22).
  - `_is_silence_narration()` (delivery.py:33) and the `_SILENCE_NARRATION` regex (delivery.py:25).
- **Data flow:** `deliver()` → per target → dead-target short-circuit → `_deliver_to_platform()` → transport resolution → content capping → silence filter → thread routing → adapter send; local targets are written as markdown under `{home}/cron/output/<job_id>/<timestamp>.md` (delivery.py:210-221).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. An unknown platform name routes to `LOCAL` for plumbing but keeps `unknown_platform` (delivery.py:132-136), so `deliver()` reports `unknown_platform` instead of silently writing a Telegram job to disk (delivery.py:176-179).
  2. A concrete native adapter always wins over Relay, but Relay is eligible only when it explicitly advertises `fronts_platform(logical_platform)` (delivery.py:73-84) — restart-time delivery must be independent of per-chat caches without letting Relay hijack unrelated targets. Relay sends via `send_for_platform` so the *logical* platform survives (delivery.py:59-63).
  3. Silence-narration filtering is a substrate-level anti-loop guard at one chokepoint before any adapter (delivery.py:280-292) — but it is skipped when `metadata` carries `job_id`: a cron output is an ARTIFACT, and dropping it while returning success is how a cron gets logged "delivered" with nothing on the wire.
  4. Oversized output is *always* audit-saved to disk before truncation, and chunking adapters (`splits_long_messages`) receive the full payload; only the footer path needs the save, so a failure there is retried and a second failure propagates (delivery.py:235-258).
  5. Named Telegram private DM topics are created on demand, and a topic that vanished mid-send triggers exactly one forced recreate + resend (delivery.py:305-315).
  6. A legacy numeric private-topic id without a `telegram_reply_to_message_id` anchor raises instead of silently posting into the wrong lane (delivery.py:299-304).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a split send that failed after earlier chunks landed raises `PartialDeliveryError` carrying the result, so callers must not re-send the whole payload (delivery.py:314-317). `_send_result_error()` treats a *missing* `success` field as success (delivery.py:103-107). Local delivery failures propagate as `OSError` and are caught by `deliver()`'s per-target `except` (delivery.py:195-205).
- **Surprising:** any successful send clears the dead-target flag (delivery.py:190-192), so one lucky send resurrects a previously dead chat — self-healing is by evidence, not by policy.

### `gateway/delivery_ledger.py`
- **Purpose:** A durable delivery-obligation ledger in the shared `state.db` so a crash between finalize and platform ACK cannot silently lose a final response.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - State machine: `record_obligation()` (delivery_ledger.py:270, → `pending`), `mark_attempting()` (:310), `mark_delivered()` (:314), `mark_failed()` (:318), `release_runtime_claim()` (:322).
  - Sweeps: `sweep_recoverable()` (delivery_ledger.py:368, dead-owner rows at boot), `sweep_failed_for_runtime()` (delivery_ledger.py:449, this instance's failed rows after an adapter reconnect), `pending_retries()` (delivery_ledger.py:508, one timer per adapter identity).
  - Scheduling: `retry_not_before()` (delivery_ledger.py:151), `flood_not_before()` (:144), `flood_wait_seconds()` (:108), `flood_retry_delay()` (:126), `is_flood_error()` (:101), `is_reconnect_only()` (:96).
  - `compute_obligation_id()` (delivery_ledger.py:264); `ledger_enabled()` (delivery_ledger.py:555) — the `gateway.delivery_ledger` config gate, default on.
  - Visible markers: `RECOVERED_MARKER` (:41), `RECONNECTED_MARKER` (:42), `FLOOD_MARKER` (:49).
- **Data flow:** caller records `pending` → `attempting` immediately before the await → `delivered` only on `SendResult.success`, else `failed` with the error text. Sweeps claim rows whose owner PID is dead (boot) or whose own process instance owns them (runtime), and return claim dicts the caller sends.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Three *distinct* visible markers for a possibly-duplicate redelivery (delivery_ledger.py:41-51): a crash, an adapter reconnect, and a flood refusal each get their own text, because a rate-limit refusal may have already accepted earlier chunks — neither of the other two markers is truthful there.
  2. The retry budget never spends the LAST attempt in-process: `retry_not_before` returns `None` once `attempts >= MAX_ATTEMPTS - 1` (delivery_ledger.py:151-168) — an unclassified outage can outlast any timer, so one attempt stays recoverable by the boot sweep after a restart.
  3. Flood detection accepts both the adapters' fail-closed `flood_control:<seconds>` shape *and* PTB's raw wording `flood control exceeded … retry in N` via `_RAW_FLOOD_RE` (delivery_ledger.py:82). An unrecognised flood row would be treated as an ordinary failure and claimed immediately by the boot sweep, spending an attempt inside the penalty that caused it.
  4. `deliverable_platforms` / `deliverable_targets` scope what may be claimed (delivery_ledger.py:389-397): claiming a platform with no live adapter burns one attempt per boot and hits the cap without ever having been sent once.
  5. Claiming is a compare-and-set on the previous owner stamp (`WHERE owner_pid IS ? OR owner_pid=?`, delivery_ledger.py:436-445), so two racing gateways cannot double-claim the same row.
  6. `release_runtime_claim()` (delivery_ledger.py:322) decrements `attempts` so a claim released *unsent* does not consume the redelivery budget; it fails closed to the exact current process instance and the `attempting` state.
  7. `_db_path()` uses `get_process_hermes_home()`, never `get_hermes_home()` (delivery_ledger.py:171-177): a multiplexed gateway records a served profile's reply under that profile's home override while the boot sweep reads from the launch context, so both must open the ONE shared store — `adapter_profile` is what tells the bots apart.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** bounds are `MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`, `STALE_AFTER_SECONDS=24h`, `_RETENTION_SECONDS=7d`, `_MAX_ROWS=500` with a state-priority delete order (delivery_ledger.py:33-36, :537-553). Owner liveness is pid + start fingerprint; on Windows the code never falls back to `os.kill(pid, 0)` because that maps to `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent` and could Ctrl+C the gateway's own console group (delivery_ledger.py:230-262).
- **Surprising:** `_prune_unlocked` runs on the *caller's* open connection inside the caller's transaction (delivery_ledger.py:537-540) — a separate connection would deadlock against the write lock the caller holds.

### `gateway/disk_status.py`
Rolls disk usage into `/api/status`; percent alone is gated on absolute headroom (`_PRESSURE_TIERS`, disk_status.py:23-26) so 90% of 100 GB and 50% of a tiny volume both read correctly, and an unreadable filesystem degrades to `pressure="unknown"` rather than "fine" (disk_status.py:30-41) — "could not read it" must never read as fine.

### `gateway/display_config.py`
Per-platform display/verbosity resolver: `display.platforms.<platform>.<key>` → `display.<key>` → the platform's registered `display_tier` + `display_overrides` → the global default (display_config.py:80-93), with `display.streaming` deliberately CLI-only (display_config.py:108-111) and the legacy `display.tool_progress_overrides` still read as a `tool_progress` fallback (display_config.py:104-107) — pinning that a tier's `off` is not an operator request to disable cards (display_config.py:113-122).

### `gateway/drain_control.py`
External drain-control marker contract (dashboard → gateway): `.drain_request.json` carries two independent *lenient* staleness signals — an instantiation-epoch mismatch (`current_instantiation_epoch`, drain_control.py:92, boot_id + PID-1 start) and a 1-hour max-age (drain_control.py:85) — so a marker surviving a machine restart on the durable volume can never park a fresh gateway in `draining` (drain_control.py:172-196), while a malformed marker still reads as drain-active (fail-safe toward quiescing).

### `gateway/hooks.py`
Per-profile event-hook registry: `~/.hermes/hooks/<name>/HOOK.yaml` + `handler.py` are loaded with the module registered in `sys.modules` *before* `exec_module` so Pydantic/dataclass forward references resolve (hooks.py:57-66), and the registry is selected per active `HERMES_HOME` at emit time (hooks.py:142-168) so one profile's arbitrary-Python handlers never see another profile's messages, user ids or responses.

### `gateway/host_attach.py`
- **Purpose:** Decide whether this profile should attach to the one host gateway, ask it to start serving the profile, replace it, refuse, or start its own.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `HostGateway` (host_attach.py:77) with `served_known` (host_attach.py:85) and `standalone` (host_attach.py:88).
  - `HostAttachDecision` (host_attach.py:268) with the `transient` flag (host_attach.py:275).
  - `host_gateway()` (host_attach.py:220), `host_gateway_serving()` (:235), `request_serve_profile()` (:241), `decide()` (:362), `standalone_attach_decision()` (:343).
  - `launched_by_other_tenant()` (host_attach.py:116), `record_home()` (:108), `profile_name_for_home()` (:69).
  - Outcomes `START` / `ATTACH` / `REFUSE` / `REPLACE_HOST` (host_attach.py:54-57).
- **Data flow:** `host_rendezvous.read_record(ROLE_GATEWAY)` → prove liveness → dial the *owner's* control socket with `identify` → derive the served set → verdict, possibly escalating to `rescan-profiles`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The RECORD proves an OWNER exists; the SERVED SET comes only from a live `identify` answer, waited for at most `ATTACH_CHANNEL_WAIT_S = 5.0` (host_attach.py:51, :195-219). An owner that has not answered yields `served_known=False` — a TRANSIENT verdict: neither ATTACH (the claim-time record predates the process knowing what it serves) nor START (that is exactly the second gateway this module prevents).
  2. That transient REFUSE is marked `transient=True` (host_attach.py:424-428) — a supervisor must retry; parking the unit strands the profile forever.
  3. `launched_by_other_tenant` (host_attach.py:116-131) treats another Hermes root's `default` profile as a *name collision*, not coverage (#121352) — the record and lock are per OS user.
  4. `standalone_attach_decision` queries every live per-home gateway, not just the host record (host_attach.py:317-341), because a standalone lock owner can hide a multiplexer launched beside it.
  5. The probe memo is TTL-bounded at 2 s (`HOST_GATEWAY_CACHE_TTL_S`, host_attach.py:185) rather than permanent — a gateway process asks the question for its whole life, while a CLI asks once per profile; record writes invalidate it eagerly via `invalidate_host_gateway_cache()` (host_attach.py:189).
  6. `request_serve_profile` returns a gateway flagged `standalone` when the owner answers `multiplex: False` (host_attach.py:252-266) — that is another profile's per-profile gateway, not a multiplexer that refused, so the caller starts *beside* it (host_attach.py:396-409).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `decide()` never raises — a broken probe degrades to `START` (host_attach.py:364-370). `_identity_matches` (host_attach.py:163) requires the identity's `hermes_home` to agree with the record's home, because PID alone is not enough: a record naming an arbitrary home makes the caller dial whatever listens there.
- **Surprising:** `--replace` against an owner that *does* serve this profile returns `REPLACE_HOST`, but against an owner that does not is deliberately avoided (host_attach.py:375-385) — the ownership guard fails closed and exits 1, which on a one-process-per-profile fleet (every generated unit carries `--replace`) produced a respawn-storm.

### `gateway/host_rendezvous.py`
- **Purpose:** Host-wide singleton rendezvous — one lock plus one published record per ROLE per OS user, so exactly one `hermes gateway run` owns a host.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `HostRecord` (host_rendezvous.py:85) with `to_json()` (:114) / `from_json()` (:131).
  - `publish_record()` (host_rendezvous.py:597), `read_record()` (:423), `read_token()` (:441), `record_token_is_consistent()` (:456).
  - Liveness: `record_is_stale()` (:345), `liveness_is_proven()` (:362), `_record_incarnation_matches()` (:298), `record_names_live_owner()` (:582), `probe_owner()` (:380).
  - Lock: `claim_host_lock()` (:517), `release_host_lock()` (:543), `owns_host_lock()` (:555), `host_lock_is_held()` (:560), `HostLockOutcome` (:497).
  - Teardown: `discard_dead_record()` (:651), `clear_record()` (:704), `cleanup_on_exit()` (:746); `served_profiles()` (:791), `describe()` (:808).
- **Data flow:** the publisher claims the flock → writes the 0600 record (and token) atomically → other processes read the record, prove liveness (pid + start fingerprint + boot epoch) → then probe the recorded endpoint over HTTP with the token for identity.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Granularity is per OS USER, not per host: the lock dir is `$HERMES_GATEWAY_LOCK_DIR` else `$XDG_STATE_HOME/hermes/gateway-locks` (host_rendezvous.py:163-168) — separate users have separate homes, ports and credentials.
  2. `home`, `start_time` and `boot_epoch` were added WITHOUT bumping `HOST_PROTOCOL_VERSION` (host_rendezvous.py:55, :110-113) — a bump would make every live owner's record read as stale and a second gateway would start.
  3. `liveness_is_proven` returns False for a record with neither `createTime` nor `startTime` (host_rendezvous.py:362-372) — `_same_incarnation` treats a missing create_time as a MATCH, so a recycled PID would otherwise look alive forever.
  4. `record_names_live_owner` (host_rendezvous.py:582) additionally requires the host lock NOT to be observed free: the publisher holds it for life and the OS drops it on death, so a free flock falsifies a PID that merely looks alive (#138343/#133744).
  5. The canonical start fingerprint (Linux /proc start ticks, immune to wall-clock shifts) plus the boot epoch are used because start ticks reset across reboots and an early-boot PID reuse would fall inside the drift tolerance (host_rendezvous.py:298-343).
  6. `HostLockOutcome.COULD_NOT_OPEN` is a distinct outcome from `HELD_BY_OTHER` (host_rendezvous.py:497-507) — a read-only/undeletable lock dir (EROFS/EACCES/ENOSPC) had collapsed into "another owner holds it" and sent operators hunting a second gateway that never existed.
  7. `discard_dead_record` holds the host lock across the re-read *and* the unlink (host_rendezvous.py:651-686) — a free-lock probe released first let a new gateway claim and publish in the gap, and cleanup deleted *its* record and token.
  8. `_write_private_text` (host_rendezvous.py:468) uses the Windows protected owner+SYSTEM DACL writer instead of mode bits, because Windows mode bits set no ACLs at all.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `read_record` returns `None` for a foreign-owned file (`_record_is_own`, host_rendezvous.py:187-208) — a hand-written record would otherwise buy an attacker the lifecycle verdict for every profile on the host. `record_token_is_consistent` (:456) catches a torn restart where record and token came from different incarnations. `cleanup_on_exit` prepends a SIGTERM/SIGBREAK handler (:746-777) because `atexit` never runs on a normal SIGTERM stop — the record and a live session token would otherwise outlive the process on disk indefinitely.
- **Surprising:** `served_profiles()` (host_rendezvous.py:791) and `_multiplex_profiles_enabled()` (host_rendezvous.py:779) are effectively constants — the retired `multiplex: false` flag is warned about and ignored at boot, so the claim-time record always advertises the full roster; the RUNTIME verdict narrows it later in `gateway.run._refresh_host_gateway_record`.

### `gateway/host_topology.py`
The one host gateway plus the profile roster it multiplexes, answered from `host_rendezvous` first and the default home's recorded `served_profiles` second (host_topology.py:58-92) — because the old per-PROFILE question ("does *my* profile own a gateway?") produced three user-visible lies for a profile merely SERVED by the host gateway ("not running", "Per-profile gateways: 0/3 up", a silently skipped destructive-action warning); `default` is just another served profile here, never a special owner.

### `gateway/hosted_room_attachments.py`
- **Purpose:** Durable, private attachment storage for gateway-owned Group Chats — the room log carries only server-minted ids and bounded metadata, while canonical bytes live in a 0700 directory beside `state.db`, content-deduplicated by SHA-256.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `HostedRoomAttachmentStore` (hosted_room_attachments.py:300): `put()` (:514), `find_upload()` (:649), `commit_message()` (:672) / `commit_message_with_receipt()` (:694), `abort_message_commit()` (:791), `retain_event()` (:836), `read()` (:860), `describe()` (:891), `read_range()` (:913), `reconcile_room_events()` (:943), `mark_room_disbanded()` (:986), `prune()` (:998), `read_viewer()` (:1120), `stats()` (:1099).
  - Module-level: `retain_message_attachments()` (hosted_room_attachments.py:1310), `fold_catalog_text()` (:1358), `validate_manifest()` (:261), `validate_task_manifest()` (:276), `decode_content_base64()` (:202).
  - Error taxonomy: `AttachmentError` / `AttachmentNotFoundError` / `AttachmentConflictError` / `AttachmentQuotaError` / `AttachmentIntegrityError` (hosted_room_attachments.py:65-83).
- **Data flow:** base64 upload → `put()` (prune → room/uncommitted/gateway quotas → dedupe by sha256 → blob row + attachment row in state `uploaded`) → `commit_message_with_receipt()` binds the upload to ONE room event + the frozen recipient roster (`committed`) → the room event's publication clears `expires_at` (`retain_message_attachments`) → reads verify the row, the recipient/viewer entitlement, and the blob's SHA-256.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Idempotency is keyed by `(room_id, upload_id)` and reusing an upload id with different content raises `AttachmentConflictError` (hosted_room_attachments.py:539-551) — a retry is exact-match or nothing.
  2. Blobs are content-addressed and ref-counted; the room quota counts LOGICAL bytes while the gateway quota counts PHYSICAL blob bytes (hosted_room_attachments.py:565-600) — dedupe means the two numbers legitimately differ, and `stats()` reports both (hosted_room_attachments.py:1099-1117).
  3. A viewer-access attachment requires a *published* owner event whose payload manifest contains the exact metadata dict (hosted_room_attachments.py:1240-1272) — a pre-event commitment is staging, not authority to expose bytes.
  4. `read_viewer` (hosted_room_attachments.py:1120-1170) checks authorization TWICE — before and after the byte I/O — and re-reads the row to confirm it did not change, because no SQLite snapshot may span filesystem/hash work, so revocation has to be fenced across the read.
  5. `retain_event` / `retain_message_attachments` clear `expires_at` only once the room event is durable in the log (hosted_room_attachments.py:836-857, :1310-1355) — an attachment whose event was never published expires with the uncommitted TTL.
  6. MIME/kind matching is strict for image and PDF (sniffed magic must equal the declared type, hosted_room_attachments.py:182-200) but generic files are opaque bytes: ZIP/gzip are container formats shared by DOCX/XLSX/PPTX/EPUB/JAR/APK, so only image and PDF are security-sensitive enough for an exact match.
  7. The store is built per operation but its startup work runs once per *database file* identity — `_STARTED` is keyed by resolved path + `st_dev` + `st_ino` (hosted_room_attachments.py:296-297, :338-344) — so a recreated `state.db` is set up again and the reconcile+prune do not repeat on every read.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `prune()` (hosted_room_attachments.py:998-1059) expires rows, decrements ref counts and deletes blobs only after the transaction commits; `_sweep_orphans()` (:1061-1097) quarantines orphan blobs by *rename* inside the write transaction and unlinks them after the commit — the rename is the atomic reference fence, because unlinking is slow. `_open_blob()` (:455-470) opens with `O_NOFOLLOW` and fences the file size against the durable row; every full read re-hashes against the stored SHA-256 (:472-481).
- **Surprising:** `_read_blob_range()` (hosted_room_attachments.py:483-496) deliberately does *not* hash the slice — the metadata carries the stored whole-file SHA-256 and the receiver verifies the reassembled bytes, so a range read is cheap but not self-verifying.

### `gateway/hosted_room_authority_history.py`
Bounded replay context for committed authority changes (`MAX_AUTHORITY_SPANS = 1024`, hosted_room_authority_history.py:16) — a strict, gapless chain of `epoch`/`from_seq` that must begin at epoch 1/seq 0 and reach the current owner (hosted_room_authority_history.py:40-63), explicitly NOT election proof ("possession of this data grants no control rights", hosted_room_authority_history.py:41); `read_history_locked` (hosted_room_authority_history.py:79) rebuilds it from committed `authority.claimed` events and refuses to certify a legacy adoption as a history of physical-host transfers (hosted_room_authority_history.py:96-99).

### `gateway/hosted_room_discussion.py`
- **Purpose:** Pure, deterministic policy for same-gateway hosted-room Discussions — a frozen local roster plus the complete typed room log yields *at most one* next driver task, with no I/O, transport or model knowledge.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `DiscussionRoom` (hosted_room_discussion.py:100), `DiscussionMember` (:90), `DiscussionTaskPlan` (:110), `DiscussionDecision` (:122), `EventPlan` (:133), `PublicationPlan` (:148).
  - Entry points: `plan_next_task()` (hosted_room_discussion.py:693), `reconstruct_task_plan()` (:743), `plan_publication()` (:862), `derive_member_watermarks()` (:489).
  - Validation: `validate_room()` (:298), `validate_roster()` (:272), `_validated_events()` (:477), `_validate_event()` (:456), the per-kind `_EVENT_VALIDATORS` table (:450).
  - Bounds: 2-6 members, 3 rounds, 10 member messages, 24 delta lines, 64 KB texts (hosted_room_discussion.py:26-32).
- **Data flow:** room row + typed event log → `_validated_events()` → `_pending_discussion()` (:617) → per-round responder selection → `_bounded_task_delta()` (:673) → `_build_prompt()` (:555) → `_make_task_plan()` (:588) → a deterministic `driver.TaskIdentity`; the terminal result flows back through `plan_publication()` into idempotent `EventPlan`s the caller appends.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The `turn_id` is a *parseable coordinate string* `d<seq>.r<round>.p<pos>.s<seen>.m<member_digest>` (hosted_room_discussion.py:50, :588-616), so a restart can reconstruct the task from durable state and verify it: `reconstruct_task_plan` re-derives the plan and requires `reconstructed.identity == identity` and equal payloads (hosted_room_discussion.py:795-802).
  2. Round 0 responders come from the user's mentions (no mention = everyone); later rounds are OPT-IN — only a peer explicitly cited by a Bot and not heard from since gets another turn (hosted_room_discussion.py:700-712, `_unaddressed_member_mentions` :340-356). Every member's watermark stays intact, so a peer cited later still receives the complete bounded delta without consuming turns meanwhile.
  3. A member message becomes policy input only once its terminal `turn.settled` names it (`committed_member_message_ids`, hosted_room_discussion.py:630-646) — publication writes the visible message *before* the terminal event, so a crash in that gap would otherwise leave an uncommitted message in the transcript.
  4. `_MEMBER_CONTROL_FRAME_RE` (hosted_room_discussion.py:45-49) *relabels* a member reply that reproduces a Hermes control-frame opener to `[member-quoted …]` rather than stripping it — a member reply is republished to every peer inside a `role=user` prompt, so an unmodified opener would read as harness input to the peers.
  5. A newer user event in the same thread SUPERSEDES a late result: the task stays terminal in driver state, but only a deterministic `cancelled` with reason `superseded_by_newer_user_event` is published (hosted_room_discussion.py:846-850, :878-882) so stale prose and its watermark cannot hide.
  6. `turn.settled` with `passed=True` may not reference a member message, and a non-pass turn must reference one whose `task_id`/`member_id`/`thread_id` match (hosted_room_discussion.py:411-437) — the pass/visible-message coupling is enforced in the durable log.
  7. Attachments force a frozen `input_context` (hosted_room_discussion.py:738-741), and a task carrying attachments cannot be reconstructed without one (hosted_room_discussion.py:780-790).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** exceeding a bound yields a `bounded` decision rather than an exception (hosted_room_discussion.py:697-699, :716-719); the prompt is assembled to fit `driver.MAX_PROMPT_BYTES` by dropping whole delta lines and inserting `[Earlier content omitted to fit this turn.]` (hosted_room_discussion.py:570-585); UTF-8 truncation is done on bytes with `errors="ignore"` so only an incomplete trailing sequence is cut (hosted_room_discussion.py:546-553).
- **Surprising:** the roster rejects cross-gateway fields outright (`_REMOTE_MEMBER_FIELDS`, hosted_room_discussion.py:58-60, enforced at :253-256) — a member carrying `connection_id`, `route` or `target_profile` is rejected, so a same-gateway Discussion cannot smuggle remote routing into its frozen roster.

### `gateway/hosted_room_driver.py`
- **Purpose:** Owns the durable, fenced execution state machine for a hosted-room driver — a per-room lease plus a task table — with no model calls, no sessions, and no dependency on the room event log (`:1-4`).
- **Key abstractions:** `TaskIdentity` (`:193`), `DriverLease` (`:206`), `TaskAttempt` (`:218`); the transition table `_GENERATION_TRANSITIONS` (`:88-103`) that names the recovery family (resolve / resolve_cancel / requeue / defer / requeue_deferred) as `(fenced status, SET clause, generation-stale message, row-stale message)`; the pre-built fenced UPDATE SQL `_SETTLE_RUNNING_SQL` … `_COMPLETE_STOP_SQL` (`:74-83`); `_transition` (`:474`), `_generation_transition` (`:502`), `_run_fence_transition` (`:516`); public API `acquire_lease` (`:532`), `renew_lease` (`:573`), `release_lease` (`:587`), `admit_task` (`:611`), `start_task` (`:638`), `settle_task` (`:676`), `settle_stopping_task` (`:686`), `resolve_indeterminate_task` (`:701`), `resolve_indeterminate_cancellation` (`:713`), `requeue_indeterminate_task` (`:725`), `defer_indeterminate_task` (`:736`), `requeue_deferred_task` (`:752`), `requeue_not_admitted_task` (`:763`), `cancel_task` (`:777`), `begin_task_cancel` (`:796`), `complete_task_cancel` (`:813`), `recover_room` (`:829`), `prune_published_terminal_tasks` (`:864`).
- **Data flow:** A caller supplies `(db_path, clock)` — no process-global state — and gets back plain dicts. Admission: `admit_task` normalizes the payload via `_task_payload` (`:163`) into `(normalized, compact_json, sha256)`, stores the JSON *and* the digest, and re-derives the same three on every later read; `_task_from_row` (`:342`) re-normalizes the stored `payload_json` and raises `TaskConflictError` if the bytes or the digest no longer match (`:347-353`). Running: `start_task` bumps `execution_generation` and stamps the run fence `(gateway_id, process_generation, lease_generation)`. Terminal: `settle_task`/`settle_stopping_task` write `settlement_id`, `settlement_status` and a canonical `result_json` through `_SETTLE_SET` (`:60`). Every state-changing write calls `capture_transition_locked(conn, room_id)` *inside the same transaction* (`:474-501`, `:626`, `:670`) so the passive work-record evidence commits atomically with the driver fact.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **The lease check is ordered differently on purpose.** `_transition` takes `lease_first`: recovery paths check the lease *before* the row load, while settlement paths check it *after* the idempotent replay — "an identical replay still succeeds after the lease moved on" (`:481-484`, `:487-489`). A crashed-then-retried settlement therefore still reports success, but a recovery requeue refuses to run under a dead lease. Second, the *lease* is the fence, not the task: `_run_fence` (`:395`) binds the three lease fields and the SQL fence repeats them, so a task started under a reclaimed lease cannot settle under the new one. Third, `release_lease` refuses while any task is `running` (`:604-606`) — the lease may not outlive its work. Fourth, `acquire_lease` only renews the *same* `process_generation` and extends with `max()` so an expiry can never move backwards (`:558-566`); a different live generation raises `LeaseHeldError` (`:567-568`), and a takeover increments `lease_generation` monotonically in a fenced UPDATE (`:570-582`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `start_task` refuses to begin if any other task in the room is `running`/`indeterminate`/`stopping` ("room recovery must resolve the prior task before starting new work", `:650-654`) and refuses a task that is not next in `_TASK_ORDER` (`source_event_seq, created_at, task_id`, `:655-659`). `recover_room` (`:829`) demotes a `running` task whose run fence does not match the current lease to `indeterminate` and **never** silently requeues it; only `requeue_not_admitted_task` (`:763`) returns `running → queued`, and only on *proven* non-admission, nulling the run fence. `cancel_task` is legal only from `queued`/`deferred` (`:784-789`); running work requires the two-phase `begin_task_cancel` → `complete_task_cancel` (`:796`, `:813`), and `complete_task_cancel` commits only when the transport acknowledged the exact `cancel_id` and generation (`:819-822`). `settle_stopping_task` (`:686`) exists so a completion that *wins the race* against an unacknowledged Stop still commits ("task completion lost the stop race" is the stale message). `prune_published_terminal_tasks` (`:864`) deletes only terminal tasks that already have a publication receipt in `hosted_room_policy_publications`, bounded by `retain` and `MAX_TASK_PRUNE_BATCH=1000`; it returns 0 when the publications table does not exist (`:878-880`).
- **Surprising:** `TerminalStatus` is `{"settled","failed"}` (`:27`) but `TERMINAL_STATUSES` is `{"settled","failed","cancelled"}` (`:36`) — `cancelled` is terminal for transition *guards* yet is not a legal settlement status, so a cancelled task can never be settled. `_cancel_generation` (`:419`) deliberately **accepts `bool`** ("a bool is an int; do not swap for non_negative_int"), unlike `hosted_rooms_common.bounded_int` which rejects bools — a documented divergence, not an oversight. `_validate_schema` (`:255`) fails *closed* with "unsupported unpublished hosted-room driver schema; recreate the driver tables before starting the driver" if the column set or the `hosted_rooms` foreign key is not exactly as expected. `_connect` (`:290`) sniffs the stored `CREATE TABLE` text for `'stopping'`/`'deferred'` and only then runs `_migrate_task_status_constraint` (`:278`), which rebuilds the table as `hosted_room_driver_tasks_next` from the same DDL, copies the rows across, drops the old table and renames — so the `UNIQUE (room_id, thread_id, turn_id)` and the `hosted_rooms` foreign key survive the constraint expansion. The schema has never shipped, so the whole draft is fail-closed by design (`:291-293`).

### `gateway/hosted_room_execution_policy.py` — The target-issued execution authority a RoomLink member turn runs under: a digest-signed, immutable `{toolsets, approval_mode, max_iterations}` tuple resolved from the *served* profile's own config (`_served_profile_scope` `:71`, `execution_policy_mapping` `:87`), pinned so `bot_room` is always present (`:51`), `approval_mode ∈ {manual, smart, off}` (`:54`), and the policy is carried to the turn in a `ContextVar` (`:113-125`).

### `gateway/hosted_room_links.py` — Private SQLite persistence for negotiated peer room routes: `StoredRoomLink` (`:39`) keeps the bearer `grant` out of every repr via `field(repr=False)` (`:44`), `transport_security` must equal the security detected from `target_url` (`:58-61`), and `load_room_links_tolerant` (`:118`) quarantines a malformed row as `room:member:invalid` instead of failing the whole load.

### `gateway/hosted_room_peer.py`
- **Purpose:** The typed wire contract for cross-gateway hosted-room members — the capability catalog, the room-scoped grant, and the per-attempt dispatch identity — plus the transport-security rule for where a RoomLink may point (`:1-6`).
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayRoomCatalog` (`:194`) with `from_mapping` (`:209`) / `as_mapping` (`:226`) / `endpoint_mapping` (`:237`); `catalog_mapping` (`:244`) and `local_catalog_mapping` (`:278`); `HostedMemberDispatch` (`:357`) with `as_mapping` (`:377`) / `from_mapping` (`:382`); `issue_room_grant` (`:407`), `verify_room_grant` (`:445`), `decode_room_grant` (`:457`), `room_grant_needs_dispatch_refresh` (`:489`); `gateway_room_grant_secret` (`:84`), `derive_room_grant_secret` (`:99`); `validate_room_link_url` (`:322`); `local_room_link_endpoint` (`:281`).
- **Data flow:** A gateway advertises itself as a catalog: `catalog_mapping` resolves the *served* `target_profile`'s execution policy (`:255-259`), builds the mapping, computes `catalog_digest` over the mapping *without* the digest field (`:169-171`), and re-validates by round-tripping through `GatewayRoomCatalog.from_mapping` (`:272`). A peer verifies the digest with `hmac.compare_digest` (`:223`). Grants are `b64url(payload).b64url(HMAC-SHA256(secret, payload))` (`:437-438`); `decode_room_grant` splits the token, verifies the signature in constant time, then checks the field set, the lifetime triple and the permission list (`:457-487`). `verify_room_grant` additionally requires the grant's scope to equal the *dispatch's* scope field-for-field (`:449-451`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **The signing secret is not the API key.** `gateway_room_grant_secret` (`:84`) mints a 32-byte secret in the *installation root* (`.room-link-grant-secret`), created atomically via `O_EXCL` temp file + `os.link` + directory fsync, then domain-separated by HMAC (`:49-81`); the docstring is explicit that "API keys are client-known, possibly profile-scoped bearer credentials and must never become grant-signing authority" (`:86-90`). `derive_room_grant_secret` (`:99`) is the API-key variant, domain-separated differently, with an 8-char floor that exists only "for contract tests". Second, `catalog_mapping` **filters `link_modes` down to `direct` only** — "never advertise pull/relay" (`:269-270`) — even though `_LINK_MODES` (`:35`) still lists five. Third, it **refuses `approval_mode == "off"`** (`:263-265`): process-wide YOLO mode bypasses the scoped approval ContextVar, so rewriting the advertised policy cannot make it safe. Fourth, `persistent_process` is forced false when `HERMES_DESKTOP == "1"` (`:252-253`) — a Desktop-managed gateway exits with the app, so the caller's flag is only an upper bound.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `validate_room_link_url` (`:322`) permits plaintext `http` **only** toward loopback (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `ipaddress.is_loopback`) and returns `("tls"|"loopback")`; anything else, plus any userinfo, query or fragment, is refused (`:335-353`). `issue_room_grant` enforces two separate lifetimes: dispatch TTL ≤ 24 h and status TTL ≤ 30 days (`:403-404`), with `status_expires_at ≥ now + ttl` required (`:428-430`). `decode_room_grant` then picks the expiry **per permission**: `approve`/`status`/`stop` live to `status_expires_at`, `dispatch` only to `expires_at` (`:479-480`), and allows 30 s of clock skew before `issued_at` (`:481`). `room_grant_needs_dispatch_refresh` (`:489`) reads *only* the timing — "trust is established by the target, not here" — and returns `True` on any parse failure, so an unreadable token always schedules a refresh rather than a silent reuse. The endpoint capability is a closed shape: unavailable ⇒ exactly `{available, reason}`, available ⇒ exactly `{available, url, transport_security}` (`:174-186`).
- **Surprising:** `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 2` is **deliberately not wire-compatible** with the unpublished v1 draft: "mixed gateways fall back to Desktop-driven rooms rather than accept a weaker token" (`:27-29`). `_gateway_room_grant_secret_for_home` is `@lru_cache(maxsize=32)` keyed on the resolved home path (`:48-49`) — the secret is restart-scoped, so a home that is deleted and recreated mints a *new* secret and every old grant stops verifying. `issue_room_grant` builds its payload from `locals()` (`:433`) and reads it back by name (`:435`), so a signature change to that function's parameters silently changes the grant payload.

### `gateway/hosted_room_policy_checkpoint.py`
- **Purpose:** A bounded, incrementally-maintained projection of the room log that answers "what is the next active discussion, and what has each member already seen" without replaying the room's whole history on every poll (`:1-5`).
- **Key abstractions:** `HostedRoomPolicyCheckpoint` (`:109`) with `sync` (`:277`), `snapshot` (`:316`), `events_for_task` (`:355`), `publication_exists` (`:342`), `compact_completed` (`:399`); `PolicySnapshot` (`:75`); the projection tables in `_SCHEMA_DDL` (`:27-59`): `hosted_room_policy_cursors`, `_threads`, `_events`, `_watermarks`, `_publications`, `_transcript`, `_transcript_state`; the per-kind handlers `_apply_user_message` (`:175`), `_apply_discussion_event` (`:191`), `_apply_room_activity` (`:229`), `_apply_stop_requested` (`:235`) dispatched through `_APPLY_BY_KIND` (`:242`).
- **Data flow:** `sync` (`:277`) walks the durable log from the stored `through_seq` cursor, reads pages via `hosted_rooms.read_events`, applies each event through `_apply_event` (`:246`), and advances the cursor with a **compare-and-swap** `fenced_update` (`:308-310`). `snapshot` (`:316`) then returns only the *oldest* not-yet-completed thread whose `latest_user_seq > stopped_through_seq` (`:327-329`), merged with the per-member watermark set. The transcript is stored as **references into the room log** (`seq`, `kind`, `settled_seq`) — "prompt payloads are never duplicated outside room byte limits" (`:50-52`); `_discussion_events` (`:155`) joins those references back to `hosted_room_events` through `_TRANSCRIPT_EVENTS_SQL` (`:63`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **A late terminal outcome must not resurrect a completed discussion.** `_apply_discussion_event` (`:191`) stores the event into the *active* projection only if that discussion is still active (`:197-199`), and then returns early for non-terminal kinds (`:200-202`) — "Late outcomes still need publication receipts and transcript commits, but must not resurrect a completed discussion's active projection" (`:200-201`). Second, the transcript has a **schema version** (`:23`, value 4) and a version mismatch triggers a full *derived*-state rebuild: all four projection tables are deleted and the cursor reset to 0 (`:262-274`) — "Rebuild derived history without rewriting admissions or the room log" (`:261`). The durable log is never rewritten. Third, `sync` is **worker-safe by reload, not by lock**: if another worker already advanced the cursor, the reader reloads and `continue`s *before* applying any projection effect (`:303-307`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A cursor ahead of the log raises "room policy cursor is ahead of the durable log" (`:291-295`); a page that does not advance raises "hosted room policy cursor did not advance" (`:301-302`); a disappearing cursor raises "hosted room policy cursor disappeared during replay" (`:302-304`). Bound violations are hard errors, not truncation: an over-long active projection raises the caller's `bound_error` (`:162-163`) and the transcript raises "thread policy transcript exceeded its bound" (`:166-167`). `events_for_task` (`:355`) with an `input_context` validates the input (`:360`), requires *every* referenced seq to exist ("admitted task input event is missing", `:364-365`), requires the source to be a `message.user` with a `thread_id` (`:366-369`), and then **re-reads the latest user message for that thread** so supersession stays authoritative after the display projection has compacted it (`:370-376`).
