The source event itself may have aged out of *both* bounded projections while a deferred task is still retryable — "its frozen prompt lives in the task" (`:390-391`).
- **Surprising:** `publication_exists` (`:342`) keys `deferred` on `(task_id, kind, execution_generation)` but keys `settled`/`failed`/`cancelled` on `task_id` **alone** (`:344-352`) — a deferred publication is per-generation, a terminal one is per-task. Also, `_published_task_events` (`:409`) recovers a task's receipts by *string surgery* on the task id: `task_id.removeprefix("dtask:")` is used to find the events `dmessage:<digest>` and `dterminal:<digest>` (`:415-422`), and it raises "published task receipt identity changed" if either event's payload `task_id` no longer matches (`:426-427`) — the ids are derived from the digest, not stored as a column.

### `gateway/hosted_room_replicas.py`
- **Purpose:** Storage-only replica and takeover primitives for a non-authority gateway's durable copy of a room log, and the fenced primitives that let it continue the room if the authority host dies (`:1-7`).
- **Key abstractions:** `ReplicaError` / `ReplicaGapError` / `ReplicaEpochRegressionError` (`:32-34`); `ingest_page` (`:144`), `replica_state` (`:184`), `promote_replica` (`:198`), `demote_room` (`:247`); the two replica tables in `_initialize_replica_schema` (`:37`); `_validate_page` (`:87`); `_replica_row_state` (`:116`); `_store_replica` (`:128`).
- **Data flow:** `ingest_page` (`:144`) validates a verbatim `read_events()` page (`events` + `authority`), opens an IMMEDIATE transaction, refuses an epoch regression (`:158-159`), keeps only events with `seq > last_seq` (`:160`), refuses the first new seq unless it is exactly `last_seq + 1` (`:161-162`), inserts them into `hosted_room_replica_events`, and accumulates the byte cost against `MAX_REPLICA_EVENT_BYTES` (`:166-170`). `promote_replica` (`:198`) copies the replica log into the *authoritative* `hosted_room_events` table, appends a lineage-proving `authority.claimed` control event at `epoch + 1` (`:222-226`), and deletes the replica rows (`:239-240`). `demote_room` (`:247`) records `authority.lost` and adopts the observed newer lineage so no local send can commit at the stale epoch (`:278-283`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **The DDL is executed twice on purpose.** `_replica_transaction` (`:55`) first initializes the schema on its own autocommit connection, then runs the *same* initializer again inside the IMMEDIATE transaction — "that double init is the established statement order" (`:57-58`). Second, the takeover is *provable* rather than merely allowed: `promote_replica` writes `previous_gateway_id`, `authority_epoch`, `promoted_from_replica` and the `reason` into the claim payload (`:223-227`) so that wherever the claim replicates, the old epoch is provably stale. Third, the module explicitly declines to decide *when* takeover is safe: "Storage primitives only: the caller decides *when* takeover is safe" (`:6-7`), repeated at `:203-205`.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `promote_replica` refuses if this gateway already holds the authority (`:213-214`), if the `room_id` already exists in the local authoritative store (`:215-216`), or if it belongs to a disbanded room (`:217-218`). `demote_room` is idempotent per lineage: an exact `(gateway_id, epoch)` match returns `{"idempotent": True}` without writing (`:265-269`); a non-superseding observed epoch raises `ReplicaEpochRegressionError` (`:270-271`); a room not locally authoritative raises "room is not locally authoritative; nothing to demote" (`:272-273`). A new room is admitted only under `MAX_REPLICA_ROOMS = 256` (`:116-125`) and the replica byte store is capped at 256 MB (`:24`). `_validate_page` requires `page.authority` to be present ("page.authority is required for replication", `:92-93`) and rejects non-contiguous sequences within the page (`:101-103`).
- **Surprising:** `ingest_page` is *not* transactional with the caller's replication transport in a strict sense — it silently drops events it already has (`seq > last_seq`, `:160`), so a replayed page is a no-op rather than a conflict, but a page that *skips* a sequence is a hard `ReplicaGapError`. And `_control_event` (`:68`) builds the control event id as `system:authority-<kind>:<epoch>` (`:69`), which means the *same* authority claim at the same epoch always produces the same event id — the event id is derived from the epoch, not from a unique nonce.

### `gateway/hosted_room_task_input.py` — Validates the bounded, immutable input reference a compacting policy caller carries: exactly `{watermark, event_seqs}` (`:10-11`), a non-negative int watermark (`:13-15`), 1..128 sequence numbers (`:16-17`) that must be *strictly increasing after the watermark* and ≤ 2^63-1 (`:18-23`) — the ordering rule is what makes the frozen input replayable against the durable log.

### `gateway/hosted_room_work_lineage.py` — Validates *passive* work evidence against committed authority spans without ever electing an owner: `source_prefix_locked` (`:5`) replays the room log from seq 1 and requires every event's seq, epoch, gateway actor and `authority.claimed` boundary to match the span (`:17-21`), `validate_provenance` (`:27`) requires every receipt to sit inside a matching authority span (`:30-35`), and `task_origins` (`:38`) maps each task to the `(gateway_id, epoch)` that authorised it.

### `gateway/hosted_room_work_records.py`
- **Purpose:** Captures a bounded, digest-sealed **evidence** snapshot of what a room's driver actually did — never executable state, never an authority decision (`:1`).
- **Key abstractions:** `validate` (`:143`); `capture` (`:267`) / `capture_locked` (`:277`) / `capture_transition_locked` (`:242`); `_capture_tasks` (`:207`); `prepare_delivery_locked` (`:342`); `delivery_status_locked` (`:378`); `acknowledge_locked` (`:412`); `acknowledgement` (`:403`); `history_anchor` (`:196`); `roster_digest` (`:398`); `_validate_roster` (`:328`); error classes `WorkRecordError` / `InvalidStoredWorkRecord` / `WorkRecordCapacityError` / `WorkRecordPrefixError` (`:38-50`).
- **Data flow:** `capture_locked` (`:277`) reads the room row, asserts local authority (`:279-281`), takes `seq = next_seq - 1` as the history tip (`:282`), assembles `content` = version / room / home_install_id / authority / `roster_sha256` / `history{seq, event_sha256}` / availability / tasks / receipts / `limitations` / `stop` (`:296-306`), computes `history_anchor` as a digest over the exact stored row (`:196-205`), and for epoch > 1 also computes `lineage_sha256` from the authority spans (`:307-310`). The record is then validated (`:314`), cross-checked against the live roster (`:315`, `:328`), and provenanced against the spans (`:316-317`) before `storage.save_locked` writes it. A record whose content digest equals the previous one is **not** rewritten — the previous revision is returned (`:319-320`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **The evidence is never an execution gate.** `capture_transition_locked` (`:242`) wraps the capture in a `SAVEPOINT work_transition_capture`; a rejected evidence write rolls back only the savepoint and logs, while the caller's driver transition still commits (`:256-262`). "The caller's transaction owns both facts and evidence" (`:243-245`). Second, **the record is a passive snapshot with a pinned self-description**: `LIMITATIONS` (`:25-26`) — "process_local_approvals_not_captured", "field_journals_not_captured", "external_effects_not_captured", "absent_record_is_not_non_admission", "not_execution_checkpoint" — must match *exactly* (`:169-170`), so a reader is told in-band what the record cannot prove. Third, `version` is derived from the authority epoch (1 for epoch 1, 2 otherwise, `:297`) and v2 additionally requires `lineage_sha256` plus `incompleteness == ["prior_authority_work_unknown"]` (`:154-157`) — the record admits it cannot know the previous authority's work.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_capture_tasks` (`:207`) returns a *reason* rather than throwing when the task store is missing (`"task_store_missing"`), a payload cannot be re-derived (`"unsupported_task"`) or the slice exceeds its bound (`"bounds_exceeded"`, `:207-240`); the record is then marked `availability="unavailable"` and `reason` set, and an unavailable record **must not carry tasks or receipts** (`:137-138`). If the record still exceeds `MAX_BYTES` after validation, tasks and receipts are dropped and availability flips to `unavailable`/`"bounds_exceeded"` (`:311-313`). `capture` (`:267`) commits the *invalid disposition* of a bad stored record and then re-raises `InvalidStoredWorkRecord` (`:271-276`) — it never replaces malformed bytes with a fabricated fresh snapshot. `prepare_delivery_locked` (`:342`) refuses a pending record whose source changed (room, authority, roster) (`:363-369`), returns `None` when the same `route_generation` already reached a `BLOCKED_DELIVERY_STATUSES` state (`:358-360`), and **freezes the anchor even while history lags**: "Recapturing the moving source tip on each attempt can otherwise starve a busy group indefinitely" (`:373-375`) — it returns the record only when `history.seq <= through_seq`.
- **Surprising:** **The `stop.closing` and `stop.revocation_complete` flags are structurally always `False` in this runtime.** `capture_locked` sets `fence = None  # This runtime has no durable peer revocation completion proof.` (`:284`) and derives both booleans from `fence is not None` (`:299-300`), so the record can never claim a closing room. Separately, the acknowledgement shape (`:403-410`) is explicitly "never an admission or execution receipt": it carries `{"room_id", "revision", "digest", "passive": True}` and, for v2, the authority and lineage digest — and `acknowledge_locked` (`:412`) compares the *canonical encoding* of the ack to `encode(acknowledgement(record))` (`:413-414`), so an ack that differs in a single byte is rejected rather than partially accepted.

### `gateway/hosted_room_work_storage.py`
- **Purpose:** The physical store behind the work-record evidence — table shape, byte budget, immutability triggers, and the older-writer guards that freeze evidence when authority moves (`:1`).
- **Key abstractions:** `initialize` (`:11`) / `_initialize_locked` (`:30`); `_guards` (`:106`); `scope_disposition` (`:93`); `retained_fields` (`:159`); `row_size_sql` (`:168`); `usage_sql` (`:174`); `validate_stored` (`:179`) / `validate_stored_locked` (`:192`); `scope` (`:220`); `save_locked` (`:224`); `_invalid_guards` (`:241`); `INVALID_TABLE` (`:8`).
- **Data flow:** `initialize` (`:11`) branches on `conn.in_transaction`: standalone callers get `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (so the writer is reserved *before* any schema read — "A deferred SAVEPOINT can lose its write upgrade to another publisher without honoring busy_timeout", `:12-15`), while nested callers get a `SAVEPOINT work_record_initialize` that is rolled back and released on any failure (`:19-25`). `_initialize_locked` (`:30`) creates the invalid table and the two evidence tables, then `_guards` installs the budget, immutability, delete and replace triggers. `save_locked` (`:224`) computes the row's scope from the record, runs `work._budget` (`:231`) and does an upsert on `(room_id, producer_gateway_id, producer_epoch[, target_install_id])` (`:232-235`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **Four different trigger families protect the same rows.** (1) A *budget* trigger (`trg_{table}_budget_v4_*`, `:126-135`) aborts the write when the store would exceed `MAX_STORE_BYTES`/`MAX_STORE_ROWS` — enforced in SQLite, not in Python, because a Python check is racy across the 4 MiB / 512-row limits. (2) An *immutability* trigger (`:136-141`) forbids changing the identity columns and forbids changing `record_json`/`revision`/`digest`/`disposition` once the row is no longer `current`. (3) A *delete* trigger (`:142-145`) refuses to delete a non-current row while its room is still live. (4) A *replace* trigger (`:147-152`) refuses `INSERT OR REPLACE` when an immutable snapshot would be destroyed — "INSERT OR REPLACE performs a delete internally. Refuse it before the uniqueness conflict can remove an immutable snapshot" (`:146-147`). A fifth trigger, `trg_work_authority_transition_v2` (`:151-152`, `:153-157`), fires `AFTER UPDATE OF authority_gateway_id, authority_epoch ON hosted_rooms` and freezes the old authority's evidence — "A raw authority update by an older owner must also freeze old evidence" (`:150`). Second, **orphaned evidence is preserved, not deleted**: a legacy row whose parent room no longer exists is moved to `INVALID_TABLE` with its original bytes and *no invented producer* (`:56-63`, `:31-35`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `scope_disposition` (`:93`) is a read-only projection used both for live rows and for migrated ones: a row whose producer no longer matches the room's authority becomes `superseded_authority` (pending, unacked) or `historical` (acked or source rows) (`:103-104`). `validate_stored_locked` (`:192`) invalidates **only the unchanged current version**: it matches every column with `"k" IS ?` rather than `=`, because `IS` preserves NULL/BLOB distinctions in older stores (`:204-209`). Historical rows are never rewritten (`:198-199`). `_invalid_guards` (`:241`) makes `INVALID_TABLE` absolutely immutable — every INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE aborts, because "Opaque evidence cannot acquire a first-writer owner or be reclaimed by an ordinary writer" (`:242-243`). `row_size_sql` (`:168`) charges every original variable column *plus a 20-byte reservation* for the longest bounded disposition, so freezing/cleanup can still run at capacity (`:169-170`).
- **Surprising:** The legacy-migration path in `_initialize_locked` **drops and recreates the evidence table inside the transaction** to preserve the surrounding cleanup triggers, rather than renaming the table — "Preserve surrounding cleanup triggers by recreating the same table name inside this transaction, rather than renaming its referents" (`:41-42`) — and it re-creates the dropped triggers verbatim afterwards (`:84-85`). A legacy row that *validates* is admitted as `producer = record["home_install_id"]`, `epoch = 1`, `disposition = "current"`; a legacy row that does not validate gets `producer = ""`, `epoch = 0`, `disposition = "invalid"` (`:64-83`) — an invalid row is never given a plausible producer.

### `gateway/hosted_rooms.py`
- **Purpose:** The durable, append-only source of truth for gateway-hosted Group Chats: room identity, the event log, authority epochs, and every side table the hosted-room stack depends on (`:1-6`).
- **Key abstractions:** the error family `HostedRoomError` → `RoomNotFoundError` / `RoomHistoryExpiredError` / `RoomConflictError` / `RoomProbeUnavailableError` / `EventConflictError` / `EventCursorConflictError` / `EventAttachmentConflictError` / `AuthorityConflictError` / `AuthoritySupersededError` (`:181-206`); the schema `_SCHEMA_DDL` (`:87-149`) and its self-describing probe `_REQUIRED_COLUMNS` (`:150-151`); `default_db_path` (`:404`); `local_authority_gateway_id` (`:422`); `_connect` (`:446`) / `_read_connection` (`:454`); `create_room` (`:865`), `rename_room` (`:927`), `append_event` (`:956`), `read_events` (`:1163`), `room_state` (`:1037`), `claim_authority` (`:1062`), `disband_room` (`:1131`), `request_room_stop` (`:1052`); the peer-route family `list_room_link_records` (`:615`), `upsert_room_link_record` (`:626`), `revoke_room_grant_scope` (`:681`), `reserve_peer_room` (`:725`), `upsert_remote_run_receipt` (`:797`); the retention machinery `_prepare_event` (`:542`) / `_prune_disbanded_rooms_locked` (`:576`).
- **Data flow:** A room row carries `authority_gateway_id` + `authority_epoch` + `next_seq` + `event_bytes` + `revision`; events are keyed `(room_id, seq)` with a `UNIQUE (room_id, event_id)` (`:106-113`). `append_event` (`:956`) validates the actor against `_EVENT_KINDS_BY_ACTOR` (`:50-57` — a `user` may only append `message.user`, a `gateway` only the gateway kinds), requires `authority_gateway_id`/`authority_epoch` to match the room, allocates `seq = next_seq`, charges the event's bytes, inserts, and advances `next_seq` under a fence (`:986-993`). `read_events` (`:1163`) returns a monotonic delta page with `cursor`, `latest_seq`, `has_more` and the current `authority` (`:1193-1196`). Authority transfer is a compare-and-swap on `(authority_gateway_id, authority_epoch)` plus an `authority.claimed` event (`:1084-1100`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **Profile gateways write to the shared ROOT `shared-state.db`, never the master `state.db`.** `default_db_path` (`:404`) resolves `~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/` up two levels, because "pointing profile gateways at the master session store makes every profile process a long-lived writer on state.db — the recurring multi-writer corruption vector observed across a 6-gateway fleet (state.db WAL/FTS collisions during bot-gateway restart storms, 2026-09-03)" (`:410-419`). Second, **control events get a reserved capacity headroom** that ordinary events do not: `_prepare_event` (`:542`) adds `CONTROL_EVENT_COUNT_RESERVE = 64` events and `CONTROL_EVENT_BYTE_RESERVE = 1 MiB` when `allow_control=True` (`:548`), so a room already at its byte limit can still be claimed, disclaimed or disbanded. Third, `MAX_GATEWAY_EVENT_BYTES = 16 MiB` is deliberately far below the per-room 256 MB (`:40-43`): "event accounting excludes SQLite indexes and repeated room ids, so the logical budget must stay well below the physical-file limit" (`:41-42`). Fourth, `user_event_id` (`:250`) maps a *client* retry key into the server-owned `user:<sha256>` namespace, so a client cannot collide with a system or member event id. Fifth, `_legacy_members_match` (`:266`) tolerates an older room's member list gaining routing metadata during a legacy adoption (`:262-263`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Replaying an `event_id` with identical content returns the original (`idempotent=True`); different content raises `EventConflictError` (`:972-976`). `expected_latest_seq` gives an optimistic-concurrency check and raises `EventCursorConflictError` "room changed before event publication" (`:983-984`). `claim_authority` (`:1062`) on replay raises `AuthoritySupersededError` when the claim itself succeeded but a later claim has since moved the epoch on (`:1074-1076`) — a distinct error from `AuthorityConflictError` so the caller can tell "I lost" from "I won then was overtaken". `disband_room` (`:1131`) is idempotent through `_disband_replay` (`:1113`) and writes a *permanent* identity tombstone into `hosted_room_retired_ids` (`:1155`) so a retired coordinate can never name a different room. `_prune_disbanded_rooms_locked` (`:576`) deletes the dependent tables in a fixed order (`_DEPENDENT_TABLES`, `:565-571`) and keeps only the tombstone. `_probe` (`:1006`) opens the store with a **0.05 s** timeout and never creates or migrates it, because `probe_hosted_room` (`:1019`) "runs on the synchronous prompt-admission path … so it fails fast under contention instead of blocking the WebSocket reader for SQLite's ten-second timeout" (`:1020-1022`); a SQLite error there becomes `RoomProbeUnavailableError` rather than a false `False` (`:1014-1015`). `create_room` runs the caller's `admit(conn)` hook **first**, inside the write transaction, so an owner claim commits together with the room (`:870-874`).
- **Surprising:** `_room_from_row` (`:500`) carries the note "sqlite3.Row: `x in row` scans values, so `.keys()` is load-bearing" (`:501-502`) — `"disbanded_at" in row` tests the *values*, not the column names, so the code must use `row.keys()`. `rename_room` (`:927`) updates the room row *before* inserting its event, and says so: "order is load-bearing" (`:940-941`). `read_events` (`:1163`) does not simply `LIMIT` — it computes a cumulative byte window in SQL and then **binary-searches the largest prefix whose serialized page fits `MAX_LOG_PAGE_BYTES` (2 MiB)**, raising "hosted room event exceeds replay page limit" if even one event does not fit (`:1198-1207`). `upsert_remote_run_receipt` (`:797`) treats a remote run receipt as immutable — a conflicting `run_id`/`session_id` for the same logical task raises rather than updating (`:805-811`).

### `gateway/hosted_rooms_common.py` — The leaf validation layer every hosted-room module shares: `identifier` (`:23`, with `pattern=None` to skip the shape check), `bounded_int` (`:35`, which rejects `bool`), `exact_fields` (`:42`), `text` (`:59`, bounded by UTF-8 bytes), `compact_json`/`canonical_json` (`:72`, `:77`), `connect` (`:105`, which runs schema initialization in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction so a crash rolls back the whole DDL/data migration), and `fenced_update` (`:130`, a compare-and-swap UPDATE that raises unless exactly one row changed).

### `gateway/hosted_rooms_legacy_import.py` — One-shot copy of the pre-isolation `hosted_room*` rows from `state.db` into `shared-state.db` (fixing #109775, where an upgraded install found every room "not found"): the marker row `hosted_room_legacy_imports` keeps it one-shot (`:21-22`), `_SKIP_TABLES` never copies the driver *leases* because "a lease is a ~15s heartbeat plus a process generation, so a copied lease names a process that is gone" (`:23-25`), and a failed source is remembered in the process-local `_failed_sources` set so a broken legacy file cannot re-warn on every poll (`:26-29`, `:44`).

### `gateway/kanban_watchers.py`
- **Purpose:** The `GatewayRunner` mixin that owns the three kanban background loops — the notifier poller, the embedded dispatcher, and the artifact uploader — plus the singleton-lock ownership the dispatcher holds for the process lifetime (`:1-8`).
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayKanbanWatchersMixin` (`:40`) with `_kanban_notifier_watcher` (`:60`), `_kanban_dispatcher_watcher` (`:257`), `_kanban_dispatcher_boot` (`:209`), `_deliver_kanban_artifacts` (`:152`), `_kanban_sub_op` (`:123`), `_kanban_advance` / `_kanban_unsub` / `_kanban_rewind` (`:142`, `:145`, `:148`), `_sleep_between_ticks` (`:52`), `_release_kanban_dispatcher_lock` (`:46`).
- **Data flow:** The notifier polls `kanban_notify_subs`; per subscription it claims `task_events` newer than the stored cursor, sends one message per event, then advances the cursor via `_kanban_advance` (`:60-110`). Cursor writes go through `_kanban_sub_op` (`:123`), which pins first-board resolution so the sub's board slug resolves to the same DB the notifier reads (`:126-130`) — otherwise "cursors silently reset" on a dispatcher-pinned box. The dispatcher boot (`:209`) resolves config, `kanban_db` and the lock; each tick reaps worker zombies, checks the emergency stop, optionally auto-decomposes, dispatches once per board, and probes the ready queue for a stuck-detector (`:257-330`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **A subscription is removed only when the task is `archived`, never on `done`.** The docstring is explicit: "`done` is reversible, so the cursor — not unsubscribing — is the dedup mechanism (unsub-on-terminal dropped users when the dispatcher respawned a crashed task)" (`:68-71`). Second, `_sleep_between_ticks` (`:52`) sleeps in 1-second slices rather than one long sleep, "so `stop()` never waits a full interval" (`:53-55`) — the shutdown latency is bounded by 1 s, not by the dispatch interval. Third, the artifact uploader de-duplicates by **basename** against the staged copies, because "a staged copy and the scratch original it was copied from are the same deliverable; on a review handoff the original still exists, so prose mentions of it must not upload the file a second time" (`:176-181`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** One tick's failure never stops the next — every tick body is wrapped in `try/except Exception` that logs and continues (`:105-108`, `:303-306`). A config read failure leaves the notifier **enabled** ("cannot load config (%s); continuing enabled", `:66-67`, `:89-91`), while the *dispatcher* boot fails **closed** ("cannot load config (%s); disabled", `:218-220`) — opposite failure postures in adjacent code, deliberate because a notification is cheap and a runaway dispatcher is not. The singleton lock lives at the machine-global kanban root so it serialises *all* gateways (`:232-235`); a contended lock means this gateway does not dispatch at all (`:236-240`), while an *unavailable* lock only warns and proceeds on config control alone (`:241-245`). `_deliver_kanban_artifacts` (`:152`) skips missing files, logs upload errors and never raises (`:190-207`); images ride one `send_multiple_images` batch (`:194-199`) and videos go through `send_video` (`:203-205`).
- **Surprising:** The dispatcher is *not* the only thing that can dispatch — `dispatch_in_gateway` is "the primary control; this [lock] is the backstop" (`kanban_watchers_common.py:113-122`), and the boot path reads the config **once** at boot (restart to apply) while the auto-decompose toggle is re-read **every tick** (`kanban_watchers_common.py:66-88`, #49638). The "stuck" detector fires only after 6 consecutive ticks with a non-empty ready queue and zero spawns, and then no more than once per 300 s (`:310-322`).

### `gateway/kanban_watchers_common.py` — The plumbing the kanban notifier and dispatcher share: a **fresh empty `Context`** for every offloaded call so a lingering `delegate_task` child marker cannot make the DB guard false-trip for these process-owned writers (`:21-26`), the live-re-read auto-decompose settings that fail *safe* to `(False, 3)` (`:66-88`), the `hermes pause` check that fails *open* if `agent.estop` is unimportable (`:100-110`), and the three-state singleton lock — `held` / `contended` / `unavailable` — where only `contended` forbids dispatching (`:113-139`).

### `gateway/kanban_watchers_dispatcher.py`
- **Purpose:** The embedded dispatcher's settings resolution and per-tick per-board work: enumerate boards, dispatch once per board, detect a stuck queue, and auto-decompose triage cards (`:1-6`).
- **Key abstractions:** `_DispatcherSettings` (`:34`) and `_resolve_dispatcher_settings` (`:47`); `_KanbanDispatcher` (`:121`) with `board_db_fingerprint` (`:140`), `is_corrupt_board_db_error` (`:154`), `_quarantine_lifted` (`:159`), `tick_once_for_board` (`:176`), `tick_once` (`:216`), `ready_nonempty` (`:220`), `auto_decompose_tick` (`:247`), `_decompose_one` (`:288`); `_default_profile_secret_scope` (`:307`); `_log_spawn_results` (`:329`).
- **Data flow:** `tick_once` (`:216`) enumerates boards *every tick* so a board created mid-run is picked up without a restart (`:122-123`). `tick_once_for_board` (`:176`) takes a fingerprint of the board DB, checks the quarantine, then opens a **per-board connection** under `pin_first_board_resolution()` and calls `dispatch_once(conn, board=slug, **kwargs)` — the per-board connection means "boards never share a connection or claim across each other" (`:180-182`). `ready_nonempty` (`:220`) probes each board for a spawnable ready task (and a review task only when review dispatch is enabled) to drive the stuck detector. `auto_decompose_tick` (`:247`) sets `HERMES_KANBAN_BOARD` per board around each decomposer call and restores the previous value in a `finally` (`:265-270`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** **A corrupt-looking board DB is quarantined by fingerprint, not by name.** `board_db_fingerprint` (`:140`) is `(resolved path, mtime_ns, size)`; `_quarantine_lifted` (`:159`) keeps the board out for `CORRUPT_BOARD_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS = 300` only while the fingerprint is *unchanged* — if the file changes, the quarantine lifts immediately ("transient WAL/open races can look like 'malformed' for one tick", `:122-127`, `:166-171`). Second, `ready_nonempty` (`:220`) deliberately does **not** treat a full control-plane lane as stuck: "Control-plane lanes (e.g. `orion-cc`) are pulled by terminals via `claim_task` and never spawnable — a queue full of those is 'correctly idle', not 'stuck'" (`:221-224`). The review column is probed only under the same gate the dispatcher uses (`:224-225`). Third, `_default_profile_secret_scope` (`:307`) reinstalls the *launch* profile's secret scope for the tick, because the tick runs in a fresh context where `get_secret` would otherwise fail closed, and the decomposer's aux LLM reads `auxiliary.*` from `get_hermes_home()` (`:309-316`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Every board tick is individually try/except'd so one bad board cannot stop the others (`:196-210`); a corrupt DB is quarantined and logged with a remediation hint ("Move or restore the file, then run `hermes kanban init`", `:198-206`) while any other exception is logged with a traceback (`:208-210`). Settings parsing is total: a bad `dispatch_interval_seconds` falls back to 60 (`:48-52`), a bad `failure_limit` to `kb.DEFAULT_FAILURE_LIMIT` (`:72-78`), a bad `dispatch_stale_timeout_seconds` disables stale detection (`:81-87`), and the interval is floored at 1.0 s because "tighter than this is a footgun" (`:53`). `default_assignee` is `None` for an empty string, which means "no fallback, keep skipping" — the backward-compatible default (`:89-93`). `max_in_progress` falls back to a **memory-derived** default when unset, "or None where total memory can't be read" (`:59-68`).
- **Surprising:** `auto_decompose_tick` mutates `os.environ["HERMES_KANBAN_BOARD"]` rather than passing a board argument, because "the decomposer connects with no board kwarg (same pattern as the dashboard specify endpoint)" (`:262-263`) — a deliberate process-global mutation, scoped and restored per board. `_log_spawn_results` (`:329`) returns whether *any* board spawned and is "Quiet by default: an idle gateway stays silent" (`:335-336`); it also probes `crashed`/`timed_out`/`auto_blocked` with `hasattr(..., "__len__")` (`:340-342`) because those attributes are not present on every result shape.

### `gateway/kanban_watchers_notifier.py`
- **Purpose:** Claims terminal kanban task events per subscription and delivers them to a chat platform as a text ping and/or a synthetic agent wake, with a durable cursor as the only dedup mechanism.
- **Key abstractions:** `TERMINAL_KINDS` (11 kinds, `gateway/kanban_watchers_notifier.py:47`) vs `_WAKE_KINDS` (8 kinds that must wake the creator, :50) — the two sets differ on purpose; `diagnostic_event(ev)` (:53) separates "infrastructure needs attention" from "an owner decision exists"; `_Collector` (:226) holds one tick's claim state (`notifier_profiles`, `active_platforms`, `include_unowned` = `runner._owns_kanban_dispatcher_lock()`, :236); `_EVENT_FORMATTERS` (:472) maps kind → `(msg, wake_handoff, wake_review_detail)`; `_KanbanNotification` (:491) delivers one subscription's claimed batch; `_adapter_for_subscription` (:162) resolves the durable route fail-closed; `MAX_SEND_FAILURES = 12` (:75).
- **Data flow:** tick → `_notifier_collect` (:356) → `_Collector.collect` (:252) resolves each board's *resolved* DB path, dedupes pins (:262-273) → read-only sub probe `_board_has_subs` (:277) → `connect()` → `list_notify_subs` (:340) → `_claim_for_sub` (:302) → `claim_unseen_events_for_sub` (:314) returns `(old_cursor, cursor, events)` → delivery dicts → `_KanbanNotification.deliver` (:748) → `_send_pings` (:711) sends one adapter message per event and checkpoints `last_ping_event_id` per event (:730), then the wake leg (:794-812) → finally `advance()` the cursor (:818) and unsubscribe only on `task.status == "archived"` (:820).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The subscription is **never** removed on a terminal event, only when the task reaches the irreversible `archived` status (:60-74, :820) — deleting it on `crashed` meant a worker that crashed, got reclaimed, ran again and crashed a second time notified only once, because the sub was gone after the first event; the cursor (advanced atomically by `claim_unseen_events_for_sub`) is the dedup mechanism instead, and `done` is reversible. `archived`/`unblocked` are in `TERMINAL_KINDS` but have **no formatter** and are excluded from `_WAKE_KINDS` (:469-471) — they are claimed so the cursor advances past them, but they are silent bookkeeping. The whole tick resolves *pin-first* (:34, :253): on a box where `HERMES_KANBAN_DB` pins the board, every enumerated slug must map to the pinned file, not to that slug's own empty DB. Route matching reuses `route.matches(...)` (:194, :207) rather than a second hand-maintained equality check, so canonical route order and equal-specificity ties survive.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** One bad subscription cannot block the tick (per-sub try/except in `collect_board`, :344-350). `present_notification` returning False skips the event rather than counting as a failure (:687); a `SendResult(success=False)` *without* an exception is promoted to a failure so the event is not lost (:689-693). `WakeNotAccepted` (startup / full queue) rewinds and keeps the sub alive regardless of how long admission takes (:802-806) — a full queue is not a dead destination. 12 consecutive send failures drop the sub; fewer rewind the claim so the next tick retries (:544-554). An unknown platform enum advances the cursor and returns (:749-752). The adapter route is **re-checked after claiming** (:756-763) because config/adapters can change between ticks; a disconnect rewinds. Non-push adapters (api_server) always report `success=False`, so the doomed text ping is skipped when a wake is requested — the self-post *is* the delivery (:717-724).
- **Surprising:** `_NL = "\n{}"` (:384) is dead — defined, never referenced. `format_event` (:562) mutates `wake_handoff`/`wake_review_detail` as a side effect, and `deliver` re-formats the same events once per wake group (:780), so formatting runs twice per batch. When `warning_notifications_enabled(platform)` is False the events are split into diagnostic and non-diagnostic groups (:775-780) producing **up to two separate wake payloads**, not one. For push adapters the wake targets `task.session_id` but for non-push it targets `sub["chat_id"]` (:585-592) — `task.session_id` may be a *worker* session for a child task and would misroute the wake.

### `gateway/lifecycle_ledger.py`
- **Purpose:** Durable termination-reason evidence for the gateway: a sentinel at `<HERMES_HOME>/state/gateway.lifecycle.json` closes the gap left by an unclean death (SIGKILL / kernel OOM / VM death) that runs no exit handler.
- **Key abstractions:** `record_startup(home, defer_integrity_check=True)` (:311) — boot entry point, returns the evidence dict; `detect_unclean_exit(home)` (:156) — read-only verdict; `mark_exited(exit_code, reason, home)` (:409) — clean-exit marker; `check_state_db_integrity(home, deadline_s)` (:232) → `"ok"` / `"absent"` / first `quick_check` complaint; `run_deferred_integrity_check(evidence, ...)` (:350) — the post-connect scan; `sample_memory()` (:64) — a cheap `/proc` RSS+MemAvailable+swap snapshot; `_pid_is_sentinel_owner(pid, start_time, create_time)` (:111); `read_prior_exit_label(profile_home)` (:432).
- **Data flow:** boot → `record_startup` → `detect_unclean_exit` reads the sentinel, requires `phase == "running"` (:159) and that the recorded PID is **not** a live owner (:161) → evidence enriched from the shutdown-watchdog heartbeat (`last_heartbeat_at`, `last_heartbeat_mem`, `suspected_oom` via `_suspected_oom`, :174-180) → appended to `logs/gateway-exit-diag.log` as `gateway.previous_unclean_exit` (:301) and logged at WARNING (:302-308) → a new sentinel is claimed with `phase="running"`, `pid`, `start_time` (ledger claim), `create_time` (psutil process birth) and the carried-forward `prior_unclean_exit` / `prior_suspected_oom` flags (:329-344).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `start_time` (the ledger claim) and `create_time` (the OS process birth) are **different quantities** and are kept separate: the old code compared the sentinel's `start_time` against `gateway.status.get_process_start_time` (proc ticks on Linux, centiseconds elsewhere), which never matched, so *every* `--replace` handover read as an unclean death (:113-121). Ownership is now decided on `create_time` within ±2s (:136-137), with a pre-stamp sentinel falling back to "the owner was born before it claimed, so a later birth is a PID reuser" (:138-139). `os.kill(pid, 0)` is deliberately not used for the liveness probe because on Windows it sends `CTRL_C_EVENT` to the target's console group (:124-127).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Nothing in the module raises — every write/append is wrapped and downgraded to `logger.debug` (:94-95, :107-108, :326-327, :345-346, :428-429). `mark_exited` only rewrites a sentinel provably owned by this PID (:418-419) so a `--replace` replacement is not clobbered by the outgoing process's teardown; a `pid=None`/malformed sentinel has unknown ownership and is left alone (:410-414). `check_state_db_integrity` never raises; a deadline converts the SQLite `OperationalError: interrupt` into `"check-incomplete: exceeded Ns"` (:262-266). The SQLite progress handler always returns 0 (never abort the PRAGMA) unless an explicit `deadline_at` is supplied (:219-229).
- **Surprising:** `record_startup` defaults to **deferring** the integrity scan: it is forensic and must not gate platform startup — a multi-GB store's `quick_check` blocked the `:8644` fleet peer-DM transport for minutes (:311-319). The deferred scan is also skipped entirely for a rollback-journal store, because its SHARED lock would block every live commit for the whole run; the on-disk mode decides, not the config, and an undeterminable mode is skipped too (:367-383). `read_prior_exit_label` treats `phase == "running"` as **unclean**, because at container boot the old PID namespace is simply gone (:432-437).

### `gateway/local_desktop_ui.py`
- **Purpose:** The desktop window bridges for a local-viewer's turn running on the session authority: preview/terminal/window reads, preview actions, theme/tour/setup-beat cards, the connections card, and the background-process sinks.
- **Key abstractions:** `CALLBACK_ATTRS` (:37) — the 8 agent attributes `wire` installs per turn; `wire(turn, agent)` (:250) returns the `(key, publish)` token; `release(token)` (:280); `_emit` (:82) the `desktop_ui` event sink; `_install_emitter` (:94), `_install_process_sinks` (:117), `_install_connection_hook` (:132) — the three process-wide installs; `_ask` (:210) and `_ask_result` (:157) — the two server-request shapes; `_theme` (:168), `_tour` (:189), `_preview_action` (:227).
- **Data flow:** `wire` installs the process-wide sinks once, then binds the 8 GUI callbacks on the cached agent to `_ask`/`_ask_result` against the session's `event_stream`; the renderer answers `preview.read` / `preview.act` / `terminal.read` / `window.read` / `theme.apply` / `theme.pick` / `tour` / `setup_beat` server requests, whose result is JSON-decoded back into the tool's return value.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Two *different* routing keys are used. The emitter routes an event by the turn's bound `HERMES_SESSION_ID` through `_turns` (:70-71, :85-87), but the process sinks route by the process's `session_key` through `_process_routes` (:76-77, :103-114) because a background process outlives the turn that started it. `_install_connection_hook` **re-wraps** whatever `ConnectionOperation.on_change` currently is and tags itself `_local_desktop_ui` so it never wraps itself; because it re-wraps whenever another hook replaced it, install order between the sidecar and this module does not matter (:132-154).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_emit` raises `RuntimeError("no Hermes desktop window is attached to this turn")` when the event is raised outside a local-viewer turn (:88-90) — that replaced the old "only available in the Hermes desktop app" lie with a real diagnosis. `_preview_action` uses a 10s **probe** first and only escalates to the full 45s deadline once the renderer has answered once, and a 30s cooldown after an unanswered probe, so waits cannot stack (:227-247); `_tour` has the same probe/answered ladder (:189-202). `theme.pick` gets 300s against `theme.apply`'s 15s (:57-58). `release` only drops the routing entry if the stored publisher is still the one it installed, so a newer turn of the same session is not unregistered (:280-287). Sink installation failure is caught and warned — the turn must still run (:259-260).
- **Surprising:** `_PREVIEW_ACTION_BRIDGE_UNAVAILABLE` and `_TOUR_BRIDGE_UNAVAILABLE` are pre-baked JSON error strings (:48-54, :62-67) that diagnose **desktop-app version skew** ("the desktop app's renderer, which updates separately from this backend") — a frontend/backend release mismatch diagnosed from the backend. `connection_callback` is fire-and-forget: `publish(...) and None` (:276) — the card answers later by `op_id`, so the tool thread never blocks on the renderer.

### `gateway/local_viewer_events.py`
- **Purpose:** Projects the classic CLI's in-process agent callbacks (tool line, kawaii thinking text, reasoning, MoA references, hearts, every `_print_fn` line) onto the canonical gateway execution event stream — and only for a local viewer's turn.
- **Key abstractions:** `_TURN_ATTRS` (:23) — the agent attributes `wire` overwrites; `_DESKTOP_UI_TOKEN` (:26) — the private key carrying the desktop-UI registration; `_MOA_KEYS` (:28) — the MoA facade event → payload map; `wire(turn, agent)` (:34) → the previous-values dict; `restore(agent, previous)` (:164); `publish_progress(turn, event_type, name, preview, kwargs)` (:129); `show_reasoning(turn)` (:64) and `launch_shows_reasoning(policy, authority)` (:81); `_is_active_session_subagent` (:90) / `_publish_subagent` (:109); `note_tool_start` (:187) / `complete_fields` (:206) / `todo_state` (:230); `prompt_timeout_s(turn, kind)` (:243); `publish_control_if_changed(turn)` (:174).
- **Data flow:** `wire` snapshots the agent's current callbacks, replaces them with closures over `turn._publish_execution(event_type, payload)` (generation-fenced), and returns the snapshot; `restore` puts the old values back and releases the desktop-UI token (:164-171). Tool timings and edit diffs are stashed on the turn object itself (`turn.__dict__["_local_tool_starts"]`, `_local_edit_snapshots`) and popped by `complete_fields` (:192, :203, :210, :214).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A `reasoning_callback` is installed only when `show_reasoning(turn)` or the launch source is `tui`/`gui` (:50) — installing a reasoning sink makes the *agent* ask the provider for reasoning, so installing it unconditionally would change the outbound request, not just the rendering. Each delta re-checks the session's display flag so `/reasoning show|hide` applies mid-turn (:51-54). `show_reasoning` is a three-level resolution: the session's `show_reasoning` flag → the launch source (`_CONFIG_DEFAULT_SOURCES = {"tui","gui"}`, :61) → `display.show_reasoning` from the profile config (:81-87).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_publish_subagent` takes the stream's `lock` and compares `executing[session_id] == generation` to choose in-turn vs out-of-turn publication, so the in-turn/out-of-turn decision cannot race the end of the turn (:115-123). A background `delegate_task` outliving its spawning turn is published with `execution={}` instead of borrowing a later turn's execution identity (:124-126). `moa.progress` without both `refs_done` and `refs_total` is dropped, and `moa.phase` without a phase is dropped (:157-160). `note_tool_start` captures the pre-write file snapshot *because the agent fires the start callback before the tool writes* (:188-203); `complete_fields` renders the diff and returns `inline_diff=None` when there is none (:206-227). `prompt_timeout_s` returns `None` on a messaging turn so the approval frame is unchanged (:243-258).
- **Surprising:** `_is_active_session_subagent` reaches into `tools.delegate_tool_registry` through `sys.modules.get(...)` and uses its private `_active_subagents`, `_active_subagents_lock` and `_is_descendant_of` (:94-106) — deliberate coupling to a private API. A queued grandchild is announced before it registers, so `subagent.spawn_requested` falls back to the spawning parent's record (:103-105). A classic CLI turn receives MoA references *regardless* of its reasoning display, while other viewers get them only with reasoning shown (:145).

### `gateway/media_fetch.py` — Pulls `MEDIA:` files out of a remote terminal sandbox (ssh/daytona/vercel) into the host document cache; pins that the remote path is screened against the *host* denylist twice — before the fetch and again after `readlink -f`, failing closed when it cannot resolve — so a remote fetch can never become a bypass of the local denylist (`gateway/media_fetch.py:97-104`), and strict mode fetches nothing at all because a fetched copy would land in an allowlisted root and skip the recency gate strict mode exists for (:81-83).

### `gateway/media_policy.py` — The single config→env bridge for media-delivery policy (`gateway.strict`, `media_delivery_allow_dirs`, `trust_recent_files`); pins that an explicitly-set env var WINS over config.yaml so shell overrides survive (`gateway/media_policy.py:86-90`), and that under a HERMES_HOME override nothing is bridged at all — the routed profile's own config is read instead, because `os.environ` is process-shared and a routed cron fire would otherwise hand its allowlist and strictness to the launch profile (:27-39, :106-108).

### `gateway/media_repair.py` — Repairs model-mangled `computer_use` screenshot paths inside an explicit `MEDIA:` directive; pins the repair's narrowness: it only rewrites a path whose `computer_use_<uuid>` basename exactly matches a canonical capture path returned *this* turn (`gateway/media_repair.py:111-125, :139-143`), and it never auto-attaches — validation still runs afterwards (:6-8) — while JSON tool content is parsed rather than regex-scanned, because JSON escaping doubles backslashes and a raw-text hit would yield a path that exists nowhere (:64-69).

### `gateway/memory_monitor.py` — A daemon thread emitting one grep-friendly `[MEMORY] rss=… gc=… threads=… uptime=…` line every N seconds; pins that the reported "rss" is `ru_maxrss`, a **high-water mark** on Linux rather than current RSS, and that it is divided by 1024 on Linux but by 1MiB on macOS where the unit is bytes (`gateway/memory_monitor.py:31-37`), with `psutil` current RSS only as the Windows fallback.

### `gateway/memory_status.py` — The read-only `memory` block for the unauthenticated `/api/status`; pins that a stale heartbeat sample (>150s old) still reports its numbers but keeps `pressure="unknown"`, so a dead gateway's final gasp cannot render a live "critical" banner forever (`gateway/memory_status.py:30, :100-104`), and that "could not read it" must never read as "fine" (:50-52).

### `gateway/message_timestamps.py` — Parses, strips and renders the `[Tue 2026-04-28 13:40:53 CEST]` prefix exactly once so replay does not accumulate prefixes; pins that with several stacked prefixes the one **closest to the text** wins, preserving the platform-send time of legacy rows like `[processing time] [platform time] [sender] message` (`gateway/message_timestamps.py:116-128`), and that a pre-existing embedded prefix beats the supplied `ts_value` (:131-136).

### `gateway/mirror.py` — Appends a "delivery-mirror" record to a target session's transcript so the receiving-side agent knows what was sent; pins the role contract: `mirror` metadata is dropped at the SQLite boundary, so an assistant-role mirror replays as a real turn and yields assistant→assistant pairs that break strict-alternation providers — a cron brief must pass `role="user"`, which collapses safely via the consecutive-user merge (`gateway/mirror.py:44-49`), and `_append_to_sqlite` deliberately raises so a failed write cannot look mirrored (:129-141).

### `gateway/pairing.py`
- **Purpose:** Code-based approval of unknown users on messaging platforms (8-char codes, salted-hash storage, 1h TTL, rate limit, lockout), with the pairing store kept in two-way sync with the operator's allowlist env var.
- **Key abstractions:** `ALPHABET`/`CODE_LENGTH`/`CODE_TTL_SECONDS`/`RATE_LIMIT_SECONDS`/`LOCKOUT_SECONDS`/`MAX_PENDING_PER_PLATFORM`/`MAX_FAILED_ATTEMPTS`/`DECLINE_DEDUPE_SECONDS` (:29-37); `PairingStore` (:325) with `generate_code` (:474), `approve_code` (:499), `approve_request` (:537), `revoke` (:442), `list_pending` (:556), `clear_pending` (:576), `is_approved` (:418), `has_recent_decline` (:610) / `record_decline` (:619); `_default_pairing_dir` (:58); `_user_id_aliases` (:96) / `_user_ids_match` (:108) / `_matching_ids` (:114); `_configured_allowlist` (:135) / `_sync_allowlist_add` (:153) / `_sync_allowlist_remove` (:224) / `_sync_live_adapter_allowlist_remove` (:197).
- **Data flow:** unknown DM → `generate_code` (lockout + rate-limit + max-pending gates, :480-497) → a pending entry keyed by `secrets.token_hex(8)` holding only `{hash, salt, user_id, user_name, created_at}` → the code is DM'd → the owner approves via `approve_code` (the code) or `approve_request` (the server-side id) → pending entry deleted, `{user_id: {user_name, approved_at}}` written to `<platform>-approved.json` (mode 0600, :278-279), the allowlist env mirror updated, and the live in-process adapter's `_allow_from`/`_allowed_user_ids` snapshots purged on revoke (:197-221).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `PAIRING_DIR = None` on purpose (:43) — an eagerly computed path would freeze the HERMES_HOME/profile context at gateway boot, so the gateway and the `hermes pairing` CLI wrote to *different* directories and gateway-issued codes became unrecoverable (#93449); `_default_pairing_dir()` therefore resolves fresh on every call (:58-59). The `_locked()` context manager takes the **file lock first, then `self._lock`** (:376-388) because `is_approved` runs on the gateway event loop under `self._lock`; holding the file lock while waiting (up to 10s) would stall every chat on that loop, and `is_approved` never takes the file lock, so the order is safe. `_load_approved` opens the file, `fstat`s it and keys its cache on `(st_dev, file_signature(st))` (:398-416) — "opening first preserves fail-closed authorization if permissions change", and fstat identifies the actually-opened file even during atomic replacement.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `approve_request` is neither counted toward nor gated by the brute-force lockout (:537-554) — a request id is only obtainable by an authenticated admin, so a stale id is "the row expired", not an attack, and counting it would let a few GUI clicks lock the operator out. A successful approval **resets** the persisted failure streak (:463-472), because isolated typos must not accumulate into a spurious lockout. `approve_code` checks the lockout *before* the lookup, so an already-issued valid code cannot bypass it (:513-515). Codes are whitespace-stripped and upper-cased because chat UIs insert visual spacing between characters (#89937, :512). Legacy plaintext entries are skipped and pruned at TTL (:519-521, :364-374). `PermissionError` on the data file is logged **loudly** with a Docker-specific remedy (root-owned 0600 file written by `docker exec` as root, then read by the gosu-dropped gateway) because the generic `OSError` branch would silently leave the user unauthorized (:254-273). The allowlist mirror write is best-effort and swallows every exception (:146-150) — the pairing store stays the authoritative grant.
- **Surprising:** The lockout is announced with `print(...)` to stdout rather than through the logger (:644-645) — every other event in the module goes through `logger`. Revoke matches on WhatsApp *aliases*, not exact ids (:224-239), because approve usually records a phone number while revoke is given a JID/device form. `_sync_live_adapter_allowlist_remove` mutates the adapter's allowlist set **in place** (`intersection_update`, :213) because Discord's approval views and VoiceReceiver hold that same set object.

### `gateway/parked_route_refusal.py` — Refuses inbound traffic routed to a parked profile, never silently dropping it; pins the two refusal shapes — `ParkedRouteRefused` (503 + `Retry-After`, so the provider redelivers) vs `ParkedProfileRefusal` (acked in-channel, because a direct bot has already confirmed the update to its platform and will never redeliver it) (`gateway/parked_route_refusal.py:31-46`), and that the in-channel notice is worded as a generic outage so it does not mark the never-admitted message answered and Discord's backfill can pick it up later (:70-73).

### `gateway/platform_activation.py` — Decides which deferred platform plugins a config/env pass must import; pins that the activation rule **over-approximates on purpose**: a false positive costs one import, a false negative would leave a configured platform down (`gateway/platform_activation.py:18-19`), and evidence is gathered from four independent places — YAML config blocks, env names (process, profile `.env`, secret scope, managed env), the `auth.json` credential pool, and a legacy `gateway.json` row (:10-17, :124-131).

### `gateway/platform_env.py` — The declarative env→config bridge a platform plugin attaches to its `PlatformEntry`; pins that `bundled_sibling()` executes a bundled plugin's `gateway_env.py` **from its file** under a private name without importing the plugin package at all, so a parked profile's config read or `HERMES_SAFE_MODE` still gets the credential rows and the duplicate-credential guard (`gateway/platform_env.py:187-214`), and that `home_step`/`reply_mode_step` use `partial` rather than closures so `hermes_cli.profile_channels` can read the env names a step owns from `step.keywords` (:122-129).

### `gateway/platform_registry.py`
- **Purpose:** The registry platform adapters (built-in and plugin) self-register into, with lazy/deferred loading so a cold start never imports the ~20 SDK-heavy bundled adapters.
- **Key abstractions:** `PlatformEntry` (:42) — a ~130-field dataclass carrying the whole adapter contract (factory, probes, authz env names, dashboard metadata, env bridge); `PlatformRegistry` (:190) with `register` (:406), `register_deferred` (:252), `get` (:447), `all_entries` (:459), `plugin_entries` (:465), `configured_entries(wanted)` (:390), `loaded_entries` (:401), `unregister` (:425), `required_env_names` (:476), `registered_names` (:469), `is_registered` (:484), `pending_names` (:386), `create_adapter` (:492); the CAS pair `snapshot_registration` (:271) / `restore_registration` (:279); `note_activation_env` / `activation_env` (:261/:267); `core_ships_platform(name)` (:180); the module singleton `platform_registry` (:530).
- **Data flow:** plugin manifest → `register_deferred(name, loader, scope=…)` → a lookup calls `get()` (:447) which detects a pending loader and calls `_resolve` (:301) → the loader runs **outside** the registry lock while an in-flight `threading.Event` lets concurrent readers wait on the same materialization (:323-334) → the loader imports the plugin module, which calls `register(entry)` → the entry lands in the scope's map and shadows the process-global map for lookups in that scope (:399, :404, :463).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `check_fn` (PASSIVE dependency probe, must be side-effect free) and `ensure_deps_fn` (ACTIVE installer) are two **separate** fields, split because of #79812 (:49-73): when the installer was registered as `check_fn`, every status display pip-installed SDKs as a side effect (desktop boot-loop at 94%); when the passive probe was registered instead, `create_adapter` returned `None` before `connect()` could lazy-install, so the deps never installed at all (Teams deadlock). `create_adapter` is the one place the installer runs (:505-509). `restore_registration` is a compare-and-swap on the full `(entry, loader)` state, so a later registration is never removed and a deferred loader displaced by a concrete one can be put back (:279-299). `_caller_plugin_scope` reaches `sys._getframe(2)` to infer the owning plugin from the caller's module name when a plugin registers outside a `PluginContext` (:34-39).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_resolve` refuses to block on a sibling load when running on a plugin deadline worker (the parent may hold the lock) and returns unloaded (:312-315); a recursive request while a load is in flight logs a warning and returns (:330-332). A failed loader's identity is preserved in `_consumed_loaders` until ownership teardown can CAS-restore the displaced predecessor (:215, :349-350), and a cancelled in-flight load re-resolves its predecessor rather than reporting a one-shot false negative (:335-338, :355-356). `is_registered` counts a *deferred* platform as registered so cheap membership checks (toolset resolution, webhook deliver-target checks) never trigger a heavy import (:484-490). `configured_entries(wanted)` materializes only the platforms the profile actually configures (:390-399). `create_adapter` swallows every probe/factory exception and returns `None` with a WARNING rather than propagating (:498-526).
- **Surprising:** `core_ships_platform` (:180) exists so a *user* plugin may replace a bundled adapter but may never mark it `trusted_inbound` — that flag waives user allowlists and pairing entirely, and a chat platform's senders are people; the field is documented as "never set it for a chat platform" (:111-114). The scope key is a `hermes_home_key()` string (:222) and scoped entries **overlay** the process-global ones (:399, :404, :463) — one multiplexed gateway serves several profiles, each with its own plugin set.

### `gateway/platforms/ADDING_A_PLATFORM.md`
- **Purpose:** The step-by-step checklist for adding a messaging platform to the gateway - 16 numbered integration points for the built-in path plus the zero-core-change plugin path.
- **Key abstractions:** the plugin seam (`plugin.yaml` + `adapter.py` + `ctx.register_platform()`) with its optional hooks `env_enablement_fn` / `apply_yaml_config_fn` / `is_connected` / `cron_deliver_env_var` / `standalone_sender_fn` (`:17-50`); the shared `WhatsAppBehaviorMixin` pattern for two transports of one platform, with the ruling that the mixin must come FIRST in the bases list so its `format_message` overrides the base default (`:63-77`); the built-in checklist sections 1-16 (`:102-430`).
- **Data flow:** the doc IS the flow: adapter -> `Platform` enum -> `_instantiate_adapter()` -> `pairing.py`/`authz_mixin.py` allowlist names -> `SessionSource` -> `platform_hint` -> toolset -> cron `platform_map` -> `send_message_tool` `platform_map` -> channel directory -> status display -> setup wizard -> redaction -> docs -> tests.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** every credential read goes through `gateway/config.py::_getenv` (the active profile's secret scope) and adapter-side reads through `platforms/_shared.get_scoped_secret` - "Never `os.getenv` here and never write `os.environ`: under `gateway.multiplex_profiles` the process env is the DEFAULT profile's" (`:196-200`). The plugin's `gateway_env.py` is read **directly from the file** without importing the plugin package, so a parked profile's config read still gets the credential rows (`:182-185`). A plugin must own its YAML schema through `apply_yaml_config_fn` instead of growing core `gateway/config.py` boilerplate (`:27-36`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the plugin path degrades gracefully - interactive UX (clarify buttons, exec-approval buttons, slash confirm, model picker, choice picker) all fall back to plain text when not overridden (`:128-138`); native plugin handlers must call `self._wire_plugin_handlers(native_client)` once in `connect()` so a plugin loaded mid-run is rewired by `run_plugin_rewire.py` (`:86-91`); redelivered inbound IDs are dropped by a `MessageDeduplicator` held as an adapter attribute, and "the runner's reconnect copies its live IDs into the rebuilt adapter, a hand-rolled cache starts empty" (`:161-162`).
- **Surprising:** the checklist ends with a grep for the OTHER platforms' names across `gateway/ tools/ agent/ cron/ hermes_cli/ toolsets.py` - "Check each file in the output - if it mentions other platforms but not yours, you missed it" (`:433-444`). A 16-step manual checklist plus one grep is the whole verification strategy.

### `gateway/platforms/__init__.py` — Package facade for platform adapters: re-exports `BasePlatformAdapter`, `MessageEvent`, `SendResult` (`:3-4`) so callers import from `gateway.platforms` rather than a concrete adapter module, and pins the public surface in `__all__` (`:6`).

### `gateway/platforms/_http_client_limits.py` — The single httpx connection-limit factory for every long-lived platform client: `platform_httpx_limits()` (`:37`) returns 10 keepalive connections / 2.0 s expiry (`:21-22`) instead of httpx's 5 s default, because peer-initiated FINs left in `CLOSE_WAIT` behind macOS/Cloudflare Warp proxies otherwise walk into the 256-fd limit; env overrides (`HERMES_GATEWAY_HTTPX_MAX_KEEPALIVE` / `..._KEEPALIVE_EXPIRY`) apply only when parseable and > 0 (`_positive_env`, `:25`), and the factory returns `None` when httpx is absent (`:40`).

### `gateway/platforms/_shared.py` — Cross-adapter credential/config plumbing that must never read another profile's secrets: `get_scoped_secret` (`:22`) treats an installed secret scope as authoritative and only falls back to `os.environ` when `UnscopedSecretError` proves this IS the default profile (`:36-39`), with a one-shot externally-managed-credential rung for startup gates that run before any scope exists (`:31-41`, process-cached at `:53`); `platform_gate_env` (`:69`) drops the `os.environ` fallback entirely under multiplexing (`:83`) so the first-writer YAML→env bridge cannot leak one profile's allowlist into another (#72348); `extra_or_secret` (`:106`) is the ONE env→YAML→default reader whose `blank_is_unset=False` lets `allowed_channels: ""` mean "clear the whitelist" while an explicit `False` in YAML stays a real value (`:120-128`); `yaml_env_setter` (`:151`) refuses to write `os.environ` under a secondary profile's scope (#80099), and `apply_yaml_bridge` (`:189`) / `seed_extra_from_env` (`:208`) table-drive that rule plus the env→extra direction; `env_is_connected` / `config_connected_extra` (`:233`, `:245`) resolve `hermes_cli.gateway.get_env_value` at CALL time so patched setup-status tests observe the same value.

### `gateway/platforms/access_policy_mixin.py` — Shared DM/group intake gate for adapters that self-gate (Weixin, WeCom, QQBot, WhatsApp, Yuanbao): `OwnAccessPolicyMixin.__init_subclass__` (`:27`) REFUSES a subclass with an empty `ALLOW_ALL_ENV_PREFIX`, because a host that forgot its prefix would read the generic `GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` and silently deny every open-DM deployment whose setup wrote `<PLATFORM>_ALLOW_ALL_USERS`; `_is_dm_allowed` (`:51`) requires an explicit opt-in env even under the `open` policy and pairing never implies access, `_is_dm_intake_allowed` (`:57`) admits everyone under `pairing` so the handshake can run but never admits a blank principal, `_is_group_allowed` (`:65`) never forwards `disabled`/`pairing`/unknown policies, and every env read goes through `get_scoped_secret` so a secondary bot cannot inherit the default profile's opt-in (#93522).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server.py`
- **Purpose:** the aiohttp OpenAI-compatible HTTP front door of the gateway (`:1`) — `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/responses`, `/v1/models`, `/v1/capabilities`, `/api/sessions`, `/v1/runs`, `/api/jobs`, `/health*`, browser-control and artifact routes — 4611 lines read via head/middle/tail windows plus targeted structure maps, not every line.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `APIServerAdapter(OpenAICompatRoutesMixin, BasePlatformAdapter)` (`:1100`); the whole route set is returned as data by `_http_route_table()` (`:1646`) so multiplex tests can assert the `/p/<profile>/` mirrors without binding a listener.
  - `_STATIC_FEATURE_FLAGS` (`:67`) / `_CAPABILITY_ENDPOINTS` (`:79`) — an endpoint naming a feature is advertised only while that flag is truthy on this listener (`session_continuity_header: "X-Hermes-Session-Id"`, `session_key_header: "X-Hermes-Session-Key"`).
  - `_IdempotencyCache` (`:886`) — process-global TTL/LRU + in-flight dedupe for the pre-authority path; `_make_request_fingerprint` (`:929`) hashes `repr()` of a selected key subset, so body key ORDER changes the fingerprint and an otherwise-identical reordered retry is a conflict, not a replay.
  - `_ProviderAuthResolutionError` (`:1001`) — the typed error that keeps provider-auth failure out of the generic 500 path.
  - `ThreadSafeAsyncQueue` (`:246`) + `_sse_frame` (`:260`) — the SSE plumbing; `put_threadsafe` (`:250`) is what lets the agent's worker thread push deltas into a loop-bound queue.
  - `_select_agent_runtime` (`:2210`) — the model/provider precedence chain; `_create_agent` (`:2279`) builds the `AIAgent`; `_run_agent` (`:4153`) runs it off-loop.
  - `connect` (`:4438`) / `disconnect` (`:4562`) — bind and teardown.
- **Data flow:** aiohttp request → middlewares (profile prefix `:1621`, CORS `:711`, body limit `:730`, security headers `:748`) → `_check_auth` (`:1487`) → handler → `_select_request_route` → `_create_agent` → `AIAgent` in an off-loop worker (`_api_runs._submit_api_worker`) → deltas into the `ThreadSafeAsyncQueue` → SSE frames or a JSON envelope → `ResponseStore.put` for `/v1/responses`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. `connect()` registers every route TWICE — native and `/p/{profile}` mirror (`:4474-4475`) — and only THEN a catch-all `*` `/p/{profile}/{tail:.*}` (`:4478`), so a secondary profile's inbound-port platform (Twilio, LINE, Teams) is served on the same listener without shadowing the native handlers.
  2. A rejected `API_SERVER_KEY` is a NON-RETRYABLE fatal error (`:4448`), not `return False`: the reconnect watcher would otherwise re-instantiate the adapter every backoff step and leak the ResponseStore's sqlite fds until EMFILE (#38803). Same treatment for `EADDRINUSE` (`:4530`, #52132). The key must also be ≥16 usable chars (`:4407-4437`) — a guessable key on a terminal-capable endpoint is remote code execution — and the strength check FAILS CLOSED when it cannot be verified (`:4418-4430`).
  3. The bind loop REBUILDS the `web.AppRunner` per attempt (`:4513-4523`) rather than reusing it, because aiohttp registers a site with its runner before binding and a failed start leaves the site registered.
  4. `disconnect()` closes the ResponseStore connections (`:4562-4590`): 2 fds per adapter × the reconnect backoff ≈ 12 fds/hour exhausted the 2560-fd limit after ~12 h of failed reconnects (#37011).
  5. `_run_agent` keeps `self._inflight_agent_runs` incremented in the HANDLER task (`:4326`/`:4335`) with the worker-scoped count riding along, so the shutdown close gate still sees the thread after the handler task is cancelled (#116535).
  6. `_concurrency_limited_response` (`:4047`) bounds total in-flight agent runs (configurable, #7483) and is checked at the TOP of every OpenAI handler, before any body parsing.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** 10 MB body cap enforced twice (constant `:235`, `client_max_size` `:4469`) because chunked bodies carry no `Content-Length` (`:743`); `_check_auth` compares `token.encode()` bytes because `hmac.compare_digest` raises TypeError on a non-ASCII str and the token is raw client input — a stray byte must 401, not 500 (`:1502-1505`); a named profile with no profile-scoped key fails CLOSED (`:1493-1499`) rather than inheriting the owner's key; drain returns 503 (`:1241`); `X-Hermes-Session-Id` is capped at 256 chars (`:1699`) and path-safety-checked before it reaches on-disk filenames; errors reach the client only through `_redact_api_error_text` (`:800`).
- **Surprising:** `send()` always fails with "API server uses HTTP request/response, not send()" (`:4597`) — the adapter satisfies the platform interface but is purely inbound; `disconnect()` uses `getattr` for every attribute because it tolerates bare `__new__` fixtures (`:4571`).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_authority_runs.py` — Durable-claim run controls for `/v1/runs`: `store_error_response` (`:22`) maps a `RuntimeStoreError.reason` to HTTP — `invalid_params`→400, `runtime_draining`/`storage_unavailable`→503 with `Retry-After: 1`, everything else→409 — except `admission_queued`, which gets 503 WITHOUT `Retry-After` plus `x-should-retry: false` (`:28-32`) because the work IS accepted and queued, so an SDK must not re-send it; `run_projection` (`:68`) treats `unknown` as NON-terminal (a later `resolve-unknown` can still settle it) and reuses the run SSE's own `terminal_run_status` mapper so a `partial` turn — outcome `completed` but failed in the run stream — is not reported as `completed` (`:78-85`); `respond_run` (`:113`) accepts a body only if its key set is exactly `{request_id, execution_generation, choice|answer}` and the generation matches exactly (`stale_generation`), and `stop_run` (`:138`) survives the drain claiming the head between its read and the cancel commit by re-reading the admission and stopping the live run instead of refusing it.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_bot_chat.py`
- **Purpose:** route peer turns (`hermes peer dm` → `/api/sessions/{id}/chat[/stream]`, `hermes peer run` → `/v1/runs`) into the profile's canonical Bot Chat under session authority.
- **Key abstractions:** `create_peer_bot_chat` (`:34`), `admit_peer_turn` (`:128`), `await_peer_receipt` (`:173`), `await_live_delivery` (`:163`), `bot_chat_tip` (`:116`), `_refusal` (`:69`), `reset_local_title_owner` (`:92`/`:103`).
- **Data flow:** `POST /api/sessions` titled "Bot Chat" → `create_peer_bot_chat` → `session_bot._create_bot_chat` under a `Principal('api-peer', …)` → the session's FIFO owns it; a peer turn → `admit_peer_turn` → `session_bot.deliver` → receipt → `await_peer_receipt` polls `peer_wait_admission` until the delivery settles for good.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Binding the Bot Chat as an API conversation (`run_api_turn`) is REFUSED BY DESIGN: the chat's only writer must be the authority FIFO, so a Desktop showing it is a viewer, never a lease-holding executor (module docstring `:1-11`).
  2. `create_peer_bot_chat` fires only for the exact title `BOT_CHAT_TITLE` and refuses any extra field BY NAME (`:52-59`, `_PEER_CREATE_FIELDS` `:26`) — a create carrying a model lock, route, options or system prompt is refused before anything is written, because the Bot Chat is created with the profile's own model and prompt.
  3. `await_peer_receipt` registers its waiter BEFORE re-reading the receipt (`:189-192`): a settle between the two operations either shows up in the read or resolves the future, whereas a settle before both would leave a future nobody pops.
  4. `_owner_published` (`:212`) waits for the owner's reply watcher before answering, because the receipt write runs off-loop and a re-read of the delivery could still say `queued` after the sender already holds the reply.
  5. The refusal taxonomy separates `bot_chat_unbound` / `bot_chat_not_local` (`:69-89`): a chat that is not local may be DISPLACED (moved aside as 'Bot Chat (<source> <id>)' on the next delivery), but local history owned by another login or profile is never moved.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_reset_local_title_owner` (`:103`) returns `[]` for archived rows, hidden rows without `include_hidden`, a non-matching `source`, or a lineage of length ≤ 1; a `RuntimeStoreError` from `local_receipt` degrades to `[]`; an interim `failed` receipt (retry still eligible) reads as PENDING, never as the answer (`:197-199`); the retry tick is 50 ms while an interim retry is being admitted, else 500 ms or the remaining budget (`:206-210`).
- **Surprising:** `_PENDING = ('queued', 'claimed')` (`:22`) — "claimed" (an admitted retry) counts as still-pending, so the waiter keeps polling instead of reporting the interim failure as the answer; `bot_chat_tip` (`:116`) matches by COMPRESSION TIP equality, not by id, so any segment of a reset lineage still finds the canonical chat.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_fire_startup.py` — The startup gate for the Chronos fire webhook: `refuse_until_started` (`:30`) polls the runner's `_running` flag until `FIRE_STARTUP_WAIT_SECONDS` = 7.0 (`:21`) and then answers a retryable 503 with the dashboard's own 60 s `Retry-After`; 7 s is deliberately UNDER the dashboard forwarder's 10 s timeout, because past it NAS sees a 503 for a handler that is still running the job and the retry would run it twice (`:23-25`); the drain check is re-run after EVERY poll (`:44-47`) since a gateway that starts draining mid-boot must not claim the fire, and `live_adapters_once_started` (`:55`) returns the LIVE `runner.adapters` so relay-fronted/E2EE platforms — which hold no native credential — can still be delivered to (the adapter itself is not published into `runner.adapters` until its `connect()` returns).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_memory_sessions.py` — A session-keyed `MemoryManager` registry that exists because the api_server adapter builds a FRESH `AIAgent` per request while external memory providers deliver recall as the PREVIOUS turn's background prefetch HELD ON THE PROVIDER INSTANCE (docstring `:1-13`): `checkout` (`:66`) pops the session's manager and SHUTS DOWN one checked in before `RETRY_HORIZON_S` (the housekeeping fence on that id aged out, so the id may now name a different session and must not inherit its recall), `checkin` (`:84`) parks the manager under `agent.session_id` (which carries a mid-turn compression rotation) and evicts by idle TTL / LRU overflow, and eviction runs on a background thread (`:119-122`) so the reply never waits on a provider drain — under the OWNING profile's scope (`:125-141`), because a provider reads its home and credentials at call time and eviction happens inside whichever request happened to trigger it.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_openai_routes.py`
- **Purpose:** the OpenAI-shaped routes and their SSE writers, as a mixin mixed into `APIServerAdapter` — 1573 lines read via head/middle/tail windows plus a structure map, not every line.
- **Key abstractions:** `OpenAICompatRoutesMixin` (`:728`) with `_handle_chat_completions` (`:813`), `_handle_responses` (`:1309`), `_handle_get_response` (`:1448`), `_handle_delete_response` (`:1460`), `_run_idempotent` (`:1051`), `_responses_durable_replay` (`:1271`); `_ResponsesStream` (`:248`) — the per-request SSE emitter; `_iter_stream_items` (`:29`) — the keepalive-aware queue drain; `_cap_history_tool_outputs` (`:151`), `_trim_tool_items` (`:221`).
- **Data flow:** JSON body → `_parse_chat_messages` (`:660`) / `_parse_responses_input` (`:684`) → history + user message → `prepare_context` (the durable admission) → `_run_agent`/`run_agent` → callback deltas pushed into the `ThreadSafeAsyncQueue` → `_iter_stream_items` → `_ResponsesStream.dispatch`/`write_event` (`:293`) → SSE frames → `ResponseStore.put` of the terminal envelope.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Every api_server-internal import is LAZY inside each method (module docstring `:3-7`) because `api_server.py` imports this module at top level (a cycle) and lazy lookup keeps `patch("...api_server.X")` effective.
  2. `write_event` (`:293`) validates every event's `output_index` against the globally allocated prefix; a late/backpressured observer that missed the prefix flips `_suppress_incremental` and completes from the canonical terminal snapshot instead of emitting a reordered stream (`:294-317`).
  3. `_post_stream_transform` (`:127`) handles `transform_llm_output` rewriting the FINAL after the deltas were already on the wire: a pure append emits only the suffix; a non-append rewrite emits the whole rewritten text behind a localized notice.
  4. `_turn_reasoning_text` (`:203`) reads the reasoning from the assistant messages the agent ALREADY PERSISTED rather than re-accumulating callback deltas, so the non-streaming `reasoning_content` is exactly what the stream carried and cannot double-count the post-response fallback.
  5. `_build_response_conversation_history` (`:1472`) stores a compressed transcript AS-IS instead of prepending the uncompressed history: the compressed transcript shares no input prefix, so prepending would both bloat the stored context and re-trigger compression every request.
  6. A streaming completion is registered as a RUN keyed by the COMPLETION id, never the shared session key (`:745`), so concurrent turns cannot cross-resolve; `POST /v1/runs/{completion_id}/approval` answers for it and 409s after the turn ends.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - A non-streaming hard failure (no usable text AND failed/partial) returns HTTP 502 with an OpenAI error envelope and `X-Hermes-Completed: false` (`:1000-1007`) so SDK clients raise instead of rendering the failure string as `message.content`; a SOFT partial returns 200 with `finish_reason="length"` plus a `hermes` extras block.
  - The agent can fail AFTER the queue drains (`:1146-1152`), so the finish chunk carries a non-`stop` reason and an `error` field; a recovery path that returns text without firing any delta emits it once at the end (`:1163-1169`), mirroring `_ResponsesStream.collect_result`.
  - Client disconnect → `_abandon_agent_task` interrupts the agent; `CancelledError` persists an `incomplete` snapshot and re-raises; an unhandled agent error emits `response.failed` instead of a bare `TransferEncodingError` (`:1230-1250`).
  - `_iter_stream_items` (`:29`) writes `: keepalive\n\n` every 10 s of idle because remote API clients commonly have a 20 s idle deadline.
  - `_cap_history_tool_outputs` (`:151`) caps tool outputs / tool-call argument blobs in the STORED COPY only (opt-in), because a single stored snapshot embeds the full cumulative history and one 677 KB write bloated `response_store.db` (#82513).
  - `_trim_tool_items` (`:221`) trims the `response.completed` payload in place so it stays under ~100 KB — clients already received the full details incrementally.
- **Surprising:** `_handle_responses` (`:1309`) refuses `conversation` + `previous_response_id` together (400), and `_responses_durable_replay` (`:1271`) replays a SETTLED idempotent record BEFORE the conversation name is expanded — the first success already advanced the conversation, so re-expanding would build a different admission payload and refuse the retry as a conflict; an UNSETTLED record answers 409 `unknown_execution` rather than re-running a request whose earlier attempt may have executed.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_request_options.py` — The per-request `model_options` decoder, lifted out of the frozen `api_server.py` facade (docstring `:1-7`): `_request_reasoning_config` (`:13`) accepts the structured `reasoning` object or the legacy `reasoning_effort`, treats `enabled: False` / `"none"` as an explicit off, and IGNORES unknown effort values instead of raising; `_request_service_tier` (`:37`) returns the sentinel `_REQUEST_OPTION_MISSING` when neither `service_tier` nor `fast` is present, so an ABSENT option stays distinguishable from an explicit `None` (which `fast: false` maps to).
