### `gateway/platforms/api_server_response_admissions.py` — The Responses API's durable admission layer: `find_admission` (`:27`) resolves an idempotent key to its canonical admission through the compact pointer and REFUSES a key whose admission tombstone has aged out (`admission_conflict`, `:41-45`) or whose caller reused the key for a changed request (`:53-58`) — an expired or deleted key is NEVER a new request, it is a spent identity; `prepare_context` (`:142`) validates first-time requests and replays accepted ones, expanding `conversation`/`previous_response_id` into history only AFTER the admission lookup (`:150-160`); `_admit_steps` (`:80`) makes the final lookup and the admission ONE API admission drive so simultaneous observers converge on a single admission, and a definite pre-admission refusal forgets the reserved identity while a storage-unprovable one leaves the binding intact for later recovery (`:92-101`).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_response_identity.py` — Deterministic public identity for a durable Responses admission: `durable_response_id` (`:12`) is `resp_` + sha256(request_key)[:28], re-derivable by the store when a replay record was never written, and `next_item_id` (`:18`) derives each output item's id from (response_id, kind, call_id, commentary flag, occurrence) — so the final message is distinct from a live commentary item and ids survive body eviction without retaining any transcript or tool output.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_response_store.py`
- **Purpose:** the SQLite store behind `previous_response_id` chaining, named conversations and Idempotency-Key identity for `/v1/responses`.
- **Key abstractions:** `ResponseStore` (`:21`) with `put` (`:116`), `get` (`:98`), `bind_request_key` (`:214`), `bind_request_admission` (`:262`), `output_index` (`:233`), `order_output` (`:247`), `_settle_terminal_admissions` (`:134`), `_forget_identities` (`:182`), `_evict_and_commit` (`:193`), `delete` (`:274`).
- **Data flow:** request → `bind_request_key` (INSERT OR IGNORE + fingerprint compare) → `bind_request_admission` → `put(response_id, envelope)` → `_evict_and_commit` (body LRU by `accessed_at`, then the identity bound) → later `get` / `get_conversation` for chaining.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. FOUR tables, not one: `responses` (body LRU, 100 rows) vs `response_keys` / `response_admissions` / `response_output_order`. The compact identity tables deliberately OUTLIVE body eviction so an exact retry still replays, conflicts or reconstructs its terminal result (docstring `:23-32`).
  2. The identity lifecycle is a COUNT bound over SETTLED identities only (`IDENTITY_RETENTION_FACTOR * max_size` = 10×, `:16`/`:179`); an unsettled identity (admission pending, running, or outcome unknown) neither counts nor is ever dropped (`:31-33`).
  3. `_settle_terminal_admissions` (`:134`) settles an identity whose canonical admission already finished but left no replay record — it reads the profile's `state.db` READ-ONLY, including retirement tombstones — and runs at open and before every bound/delete decision, so settlement never depends on an HTTP observer existing.
  4. `_mark_settled` (`:126`) guards the JSON with `json_extract(CASE WHEN json_valid(data) THEN data END, '$.response')` (`:18`) so a CORRUPT body reads as absent instead of raising SQL.
  5. `put` uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` for `idem:` rows and `INSERT OR REPLACE` for everything else (`:118-120`) — a second observer must not overwrite the already-committed idempotent record.
  6. `output_index` (`:233`) allocates a per-item index BEFORE live publication with `INSERT OR IGNORE … COALESCE(MAX(output_index)+1,0)`, so retries and parallel observers share one order.
  7. The DB degrades to `:memory:` if the connect fails (`:56-58`), gets a WAL fallback for NFS/SMB/FUSE homes (`:60-61`), and the file plus its `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars are chmod 0600 (`:87`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a corrupt JSON body is evicted and reported as absent (`:112-115`); `bind_request_admission` raises `RuntimeStoreError('admission_conflict')` when the row already names a different admission (`:262-267`); `forget_unadmitted_request` (`:269`) deletes a key only if it has NO admission row, so a poisoned identity cannot be silently reused; `_forget_identities` (`:182`) deletes from the three child tables FIRST and `response_keys` LAST so the selector sees the same rows in each; the idem replay record stores `events` alongside the envelope so a streaming retry can re-emit the exact frames.
- **Surprising:** the store holds TWO id spaces — response bodies keyed by `resp_…` and idempotent replay records keyed `idem:<scope>:<key>` — and `delete()` drops a settled identity by its `response_id`, while the identity's own durable id is re-derived by `durable_response_id(request_key)` for a record that was never written.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_room_dispatch.py` — RoomLink dispatch validation and hidden member-session ownership: `_normalize_room_dispatch` (`:75`) accepts ONLY `input` and `hosted_room_dispatch` (400 otherwise), verifies the room grant with `permission="dispatch"`, requires the target profile AND install id to be the LOCAL ones (a cross-profile dispatch is refused), and compares the execution-policy and capability digests with `hmac.compare_digest` (`:104-106`); the `Idempotency-Key` must be exactly `room:{task_id}:{execution_generation}` (`:108-110`) so a replay cannot be re-run against a new generation; `_ensure_hosted_member_session` (`:17`) reuses the `Group: <room_id>` namespace on purpose so a Desktop-assisted group and a hosted group share one transcript, while a conflicting title under another session id fails closed instead of merging (`:42-47`), and policy/catalog drift is mapped to a 403 with distinct codes (`:63-73`).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_room_grants.py`
RoomLink room-member grant handlers (invite/capabilities/refresh/revoke) that pin the two-TTL grant model: a dispatch `ttl_seconds` (60..86400) may be renewed only by a live `"dispatch"` bearer and only up to the hard `status_expires_at` (`L183`, `L191`, `L200`); a changed `policy_digest` aborts renewal with 403 (`L22-25`, `L194`); revoke deliberately skips the revocation lookup so a lost-response retry still authenticates with the grant it just denylisted (`L220-222`); `_room_grant_error_response` splits 401 (invalid/expired) from 403 (`RoomGrantReauthorizationRequired`, revoked/superseded/policy-changed) (`L28-35`); `_local_target` binds a grant to one (profile, installation_id) pair (`L50-57`).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_run_idempotency.py`
`RunIdempotencyStore` (`L53`) — SQLite-backed `(scope, key)` reservations for `POST /v1/runs` that admit a request inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` under a `threading.Lock` (`L122-131`), classify replays with a constant-time `hmac.compare_digest` fingerprint (`L50`), prune a record only once its stored run is terminal so a long room turn may outlive the 24h window (`L169-187`), and downgrade to `:memory:` on a failed connect — which flips the honest `durable` flag to False (`L61-64`, `L73-84`); only fingerprints and public status are stored, never bodies or credentials (`L54-56`).

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_runs.py`
- **Purpose:** The entire `/v1/runs` surface — admission, pollable status, SSE event stream, and the control endpoints (approval, clarify, steer, stop, resolve-unknown). *Read as head/middle/tail windows (lines 1-500, 614-1113, 1234-1533) plus the two gaps 500-613 and 1113-1240, not every line.*
- **Key abstractions:** `_RunStream` (`L121`) — per-run event fan-out with a 1000-event backlog and a 256-slot subscriber queue; `_RunLaunch` (`L519`, `@dataclass(slots=True)`) — the state a background task needs after the request's middleware scope is gone; `terminal_run_status` (`L186`) — the single place a `run_conversation` result becomes `completed`/`failed`/`cancelled`; `_submit_api_worker` (`L49`) + `api_worker_live_count` (`L43`) — the executor-thread lifetime counter the shutdown gate reads; `_ROOM_CONTROL_PERMISSIONS` (`L364`) — URL suffix → room-grant permission; `_RunLaunch.approval_session_key` (`L545-548`).
- **Data flow:** `POST /v1/runs` → idempotency `lookup` (`L770`) → concurrency cap (`L776`) → session selection + live-tip resolution (`L784-793`, `_resolve_live_session_id` `L578`) → `_RunStream` + `_set_run_status` (`L805-809`) → atomic `reserve` (`L811`) → `_RunLaunch` (`L821`) → admission to a live Bot Chat owner OR the session-authority FIFO (`_admit_run_to_authority` `L683`) → background task `_execute_run` (`L1023`) or `_execute_run_via_live_owner` (`L972`) → terminal event + status → SSE replay from `_RunStream.backlog`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The terminal vocabulary is collapsed so `completed: true` never rides next to `partial: true` (`L186-196`): an interrupted turn is `cancelled`; anything that ended without finishing (failed, partial, or `completed is False` from the iteration budget) is `failed`.
  - Two independent worker-lifetime counters exist on purpose (`L33-46`): the handler-side count drops in the handler's `finally` when the request is cancelled, but the executor thread behind `run_in_executor` keeps running — so the shutdown close gate reads this module-level count instead.
  - The approval session key IS the run id (`L545-548`) — "session ids are conversation scopes, not authorization namespaces"; a room grant can therefore resolve only one exact request and never widen to session/always (`L1338`).
  - `_request_owns_run` (`L1168`) fails CLOSED when a run has no in-memory owner stamp: ownerless is "an unanswered authorization question", not a run anyone may control (`L1173-1177`, #93689).
  - The SSE handler flushes `": open\n\n"` before the first event (`L1263`) because aiohttp withholds headers until the first body write — without it a subscriber connecting before `approval.request` sees no bytes and `fetch()`/`EventSource` never resolve.
  - `_run_idempotency_scope` (`L375`) hashes the room-identity tuple into an opaque scope and never persists the bearer credential; the fingerprint is a SHA-256 over the request body + session key (`L660-667`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a slow subscriber that overflows its queue is detached and gets an overflow sentinel → the stream closes rather than silently dropping events (`L146-150`, `L1288-1290`). A replay older than the retained backlog emits `replay.truncated` with `oldest_retained_seq` (`L1264-1272`). SSE writes are bounded by `_RUN_STREAM_WRITE_TIMEOUT = 5.0` (`L118`) and a timeout force-closes the response (`L1243-1248`). The events endpoint waits up to 1s (20 × 0.05s) for the run to register before 404ing (`L1226-1231`). `_sweep_orphaned_runs_once` (`L1515`) expires transport buffers by transport age but keeps live control state until the task returns. A shutdown landing mid-run rewrites the terminal status to `interrupted` (`L987-990`, `L1048-1051`). An exception whose `reason == "admission_queued"` is NOT a failure — the status returns to `queued` so a poller does not resubmit (`L1133-1139`). A provider-auth failure is caught separately and reported with the controlled user text (`L1128-1131`). A refused admission drops every reservation AND forgets the idempotency key (`L709-716`) so an exact retry is refused again rather than replaying a run nobody executes.
- **Surprising:** `_handle_runs` parses the body with `await request.json()` (`L728`) while every sibling handler uses `self._read_json_body(request)` — two different body/error paths in one module. `_tool_completed_preview` (`L106`) redacts BEFORE truncating so a cut can never leave a secret's prefix on the wire. `_run_usage` (`L867`) maps the agent's session counters to the wire's `usage` keys via `_USAGE_FIELDS` (`L88`), where cache-read tokens ride along so a cost poller does not book them as full-price input.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_store.py`
`selected_session_db` (`L5`) — returns the SessionDB only for a home this runtime actually *reserved* an authority for (`authority_for_home`), never because a multiplex route merely names it, and refuses the store once the adapter's DB cache is closed (`L11-13`); borrowed stores are never adapter-owned.

### `gateway/platforms/api_server_turn_boundary.py`
`response_turn_start_index` (`L31`) — locates where the current turn begins inside the agent's FULL transcript by anchoring on this turn's user row via `reanchor_current_turn_user_idx` (`L47-56`), because compaction and host-history repair legitimately break any prefix match; the prefix comparison is only the fallback for mocked/legacy paths, and it compares `role/content/tool_calls/tool_call_id` rather than whole dicts, because the agent stamps its copies with `timestamp`/`_db_persisted`/`reasoning`/`finish_reason` (`L9`, `L12-28`, #95137/#101644/#82513).

### `gateway/platforms/base.py`
- **Purpose:** The base class and shared toolbox every gateway platform adapter inherits (`BasePlatformAdapter`, `L1856`) — connect/auth lifecycle, outbound send, inbound session scheduling, media caching, proxy and SSRF policy. *Read as head (1-500), class head (1856-1960), middle (1961-2460) and tail (4603-4902) windows plus a def-index scan, not every line of the 4902.*
- **Key abstractions:** `BasePlatformAdapter` (`L1856`) with class-level capability flags (`supports_code_blocks` `L1861`, `supports_status_text` `L1863`, `supports_async_delivery` `L1878`, `splits_long_messages` `L1880`, `interactive_resume` `L1895`, `serves_profile_prefix` `L1900`); `SendResult` (`L1642`), `ExecApprovalPrompt` (`L1621`), `CachedMedia` (`L1491`), `TextDebounceState` (`L1570`), `EphemeralReply` (`L1724`); policy helpers `resolve_proxy_url` (`L328`), `is_network_accessible` (`L254`), `ssrf_redirect_guard` (`L466`), `validate_media_delivery_path` (`L1107`), `get_inbound_media_max_bytes` (`L494`), `truncate_message` (`L4842`).
- **Data flow:** inbound event → `build_source` (`L4782`) stamps the profile → `_canonicalize` (`L2405`) pins a `RoutingIdentity` → session key → per-session task → handler → outbound `send` / `truncate_message`; media bytes → `cache_image_from_bytes` (`L620`) / `cache_audio_from_bytes` (`L690`) / `cache_video_from_bytes` (`L720`) / `cache_document_from_bytes` (`L1468`) → a path validated by `validate_media_delivery_path` (`L1107`) before any local file is handed to a platform.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - UTF-16 accounting: Telegram's 4096 counts code units, so `utf16_len` (`L226`) and `_custom_unit_to_cp` (`L235`) exist; `_prefix_within_utf16_limit` (`L249`) refuses to split a surrogate pair, and `truncate_message` takes an injectable `len_fn` (`L4843`) so one splitter serves every platform.
  - `truncate_message` (`L4842`) reopens a code fence in the next chunk with the same language tag, closes an orphaned one at the chunk end, and refuses to split inside an inline code span (an unpaired backtick breaks MarkdownV2) (`L4880-4891`); multi-chunk output gets `(i/n)` indicators (`L4900-4901`).
  - The macOS system proxy is memoised for 60s (`L276-312`) because `scutil --proxy` is a fork+exec on the SEND path (per chunk, per attachment), and the FAILURE is cached too so a broken `scutil` does not re-fork on every chunk (`L297-300`).
  - `resolve_proxy_url` (`L328`) reads the per-platform proxy var SCOPE-AWARE (the secondary profile's own `.env`, not the shared process env) while the generic `HTTPS_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` fallback stays a raw process-env read — the split is deliberate (#108440, `L337-343`).
  - `_notify_fatal_error` (`L2163`) shields the handler task and keeps a strong ref (asyncio holds only weak refs) because the handler is usually awaited from an adapter task that the gateway's own `disconnect()` cancels — unshielded it died mid-flight and left a zombie gateway with no platforms (#81335).
  - `_cleanup_finished_session_task` (`L4724`) deletes the done-task entry ONLY if the guard was already released; deleting it unconditionally made `_session_task_is_stale` report "not stale", the orphaned lock was never healed, and the session deadlocked permanently (#48300).
  - `_requeue_backoff_delay` (`L4661`) backs off only when the SAME event bounced back (same `message_id`, or same `timestamp` for an id-less event) — a re-dispatch of the just-dispatched message hot-loops for the whole busy window (#123229); the first bounce stays immediate because restart auto-resume relies on one self-bounce (`L4682-4683`).
  - `enforces_own_access_policy` (`L1992`) is trusted ONLY when the effective policy is a real `"allowlist"`, never the default `"open"`, because open access on a network-exposed adapter is a fail-open (SECURITY.md §2.6).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the inbound media cap (`DEFAULT_INBOUND_MEDIA_MAX_BYTES` = 128 MiB, `L491`; `0` disables, `L497`) is enforced in the shared `cache_*_from_bytes` funnel so it holds regardless of which adapter produced the bytes (`L480-490`). `is_network_accessible` fails CLOSED on DNS failure (`L266-267`). `_write_runtime_status_safe` (`L2147`) downgrades repeated status-write failures to DEBUG after logging the first per (platform, context) at WARNING. `cancel_background_tasks` (`L4743`) re-drains up to 5 rounds, bounds the gather at 5s, leaves stragglers to unwind, and flushes pending messages to disk before clearing (`L4764-4766`). `_acquire_platform_lock` (`L2190`) treats an unusable lock dir as RETRYABLE, because a non-retryable park on a gateway running only token-locked adapters exits 78 and takes cron and the Desktop attach endpoint down with it (`L2200-2213`).
- **Surprising:** `sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]))` runs at import time, in the middle of the file (`L425`) — a runtime import-order hack, not a packaging concern. `set_status_text` is in-memory only (`L1866-1873`), and `_streaming_tts_completed_turns` / `_requeue_counts` are likewise volatile, so a gateway restart loses thread-participation and streaming-suppression state. The file mixes three concerns in one module — adapter base class, media cache, and proxy/SSRF policy — and the policy half is duplicated into `base_log_safety.py`, `base_exec_approval.py` and `base_streaming_tts.py` specifically so those can be imported without pulling in the heavyweight module.

### `gateway/platforms/base_exec_approval.py`
The single source of the exec-approval prompt wording shared by the button card and the plain-text `/approve` fallback — it pins the three facts every surface must state (what Hermes wants to run, why it was flagged, that silence means the command does NOT run) (`L21-27`), keeps the English `EA_*` constants as the source of truth while every render goes through the localized accessors `ea_header_text()` / `ea_action_labels()` / `approval_timed_out_notice()` (`L30-62`), and routes the configured timeout through the single reader `tools.approval_context._get_approval_timeout` (`L65-68`) so `approvals.timeout` has exactly one implementation.

### `gateway/platforms/base_log_safety.py`
`safe_url_for_log` (`L5`) — the one URL renderer safe to log: strips userinfo, query and fragment, keeps only `scheme://netloc/.../basename` (`L15-20`), and truncates to `max_len` (`L21-23`); a URL that does not parse falls back to a raw truncation (`L11-13`), and `max_len <= 0` yields an empty string rather than the raw secret (`L7-8`).

### `gateway/platforms/base_streaming_tts.py`
The streaming-TTS contract split out of `base.py`: `AudioFormat` (`L13`) declares the PCM shape every `write_streaming_tts` chunk must match, `StreamingTTSHandle.audible` (`L29`) is the flag that stops a later failure from replaying already-audible output, and `streaming_tts_turn_key` (`L33`) makes the whole-file auto-TTS suppression *turn*-scoped rather than chat-scoped so two overlapping turns in one chat cannot suppress each other's fallback path (`L34-41`, `L44-48`).

### `gateway/platforms/event.py`
`MessageEvent` (`L45`) — the normalized inbound shape every adapter produces: it splits three identities that are easy to conflate — routing (`message_id`), the delivery ledger (`ledger_message_id`, `L63`) and the reply anchor (`reply_anchor_override`, `L68`) — keeps two private non-init fields off the wire (`_gateway_accepted`, `_notification_reply_muted`, `L115-118`), and strips Desktop's `@image:`/`@file:`/`@url:` attachment refs before command parsing (`L21`, `L125-137`) so a media prefix cannot hide a slash command; `get_command` rejects any name containing `/` so a file path is never mistaken for a command (`L144-145`).

### `gateway/platforms/helpers.py`
- **Purpose:** Shared adapter utilities: message dedup, markdown stripping, thread participation, GFM table→bullets, mention-pattern compilation, fence-aware chunking, and the SSRF-safe HTTP client factory.
- **Key abstractions:** `MessageDeduplicator` (`L20`) with `carry_inbound_dedup` (`L69`) and `hand_over_held_inbound` (`L83`); `ThreadParticipationTracker` (`L164`); `send_chunks` (`L252`); `strip_markdown` (`L152`); `convert_table_to_bullets` (`L322`); `compile_mention_patterns` (`L347`); `split_text_fence_aware` (`L573`), `split_markdown_atoms` (`L462`), `balance_fences_across_chunks` (`L523`), `merge_streaming_fences` (`L508`), `greedy_pack_blocks` (`L548`); `platform_http_client` (`L690`); `is_discord_channel_obfuscated` (`L672`).
- **Data flow:** adapter text → `strip_markdown` / `convert_table_to_bullets` (platform-specific) → `split_text_fence_aware` → `send_chunks` → `send_one`; inbound id → `MessageDeduplicator.is_duplicate` → drop; outbound HTTP → `platform_http_client` → `create_ssrf_safe_async_client` with an `ssrf_redirect_guard` response hook.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - `ThreadParticipationTracker` uses TWO locks in a strict order (`L173-186`): `_lock` guards only the in-memory set (microseconds, on the loop) and `_io_lock` serializes snapshot+write on a worker thread; `_save` must NOT hold `_lock` across `os.replace` because the adapters' `thread_id in tracker` gate runs on the loop and would stall for the whole rename (`L181-183`). `mark_async` (`L230`) moves the `atomic_json_write` off the loop entirely — an unbounded rename would stall every adapter's polling and every in-flight turn.
  - `MessageDeduplicator.is_duplicate` (`L28`) both CHECKS and INSERTS; `contains` (`L46`) is the non-inserting variant, and the two exist because the mention gate needs a pure read.
  - `send_chunks` (`L252`) stamps `partial_overflow=True` plus `delivered_chunks`/`total_chunks` into `SendResult.raw_response` when a failure happens after earlier chunks landed, so `BasePlatformAdapter._is_partial_delivery` can stop any caller (retry, plain-text fallback, cron fallback) from re-sending the head the recipient already has (`L255-258`).
  - `strip_markdown(..., keep_link_targets=True)` rewrites `[label](https://url)` as `label\nurl` (`L138-149`) because iMessage auto-links bare URLs — dropping the target makes the link unreachable, not merely unformatted.
  - `platform_http_client` (`L690`) PREPENDS `ssrf_redirect_guard` to the caller's own response hooks (`L699-700`) so the guard runs before any caller hook, and every redirect hop is re-validated (`L691-693`).
  - `is_discord_channel_obfuscated` (`L672`) checks the flag first and falls back to the `___hidden___` name, deliberately over-hiding a channel that is literally named that (`L679`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `MessageDeduplicator` evicts by TTL, then by insertion age, keeping the newest `max_size` when all entries are still fresh (`L38-43`). `ThreadParticipationTracker._load` swallows every read error and returns `[]` (`L192-197`) — a corrupt state file degrades to "no threads known"; only the last 500 threads are kept (`L203-205`). `cancel_task` (`L100`) refuses to await the current task (self-cancel would deadlock) and swallows the dying task's exception at teardown. `bounded_put` (`L112`) re-inserts a key to move it to the newest position so live entries outlast stale ones. `compile_mention_patterns` (`L347`) has TWO config dialects — config-style (list or string) and wakeword-style (JSON list, comma/newline-separated, or scalar) — and drops an invalid regex with a warning instead of failing the adapter (`L361-368`).
- **Surprising:** `strip_markdown` is a fixed ordered rule list (`L124-132`) applied before link handling, and the order is load-bearing and documented (`L121-123`): `**bold**` first, then `*italic*`, then the word-boundary-guarded underscore forms (so `snake_case` survives), then fences, inline code, headings — there is no rule for `~~strikethrough~~`. `split_at_paragraph_boundary` (`L442`) prefers a blank line, then a sentence-ending punctuation followed by a newline (CJK `。！？` included, `L430`), then any newline, then a forced cut — and guarantees `head + tail == text`. `split_markdown_atoms` (`L462`) never lets a table line and a non-table line share an atom (`L490-491`).

### `gateway/platforms/media_cache.py`
The one mime↔extension table pair (`L21-36`) that lets each adapter keep its own historical behaviour via `overrides` / `use_defaults` / `use_mimetypes` (`L44-59`) — the forward map deliberately prefers the real-world extension over the RFC-correct one (`audio/ogg` → `.ogg`, not `.oga`) because downstream STT/vision pipelines whitelist real extensions (`L17-18`) — and `cache_media_bytes` (`L69`) picks the image/audio/document cache primitive by mime class, importing the heavyweight `base` module lazily because some adapters import this one very early (`L78-80`).

### `gateway/platforms/msgraph_webhook.py`
- **Purpose:** The Microsoft Graph change-notification ingress adapter: validates Graph's subscription handshake, admits notifications, and surfaces each change as an internal `MessageEvent`.
- **Key abstractions:** `MSGraphWebhookAdapter` (`L100`) with `serves_profile_prefix = True` (`L103`); `_parse_allowed_source_cidrs` (`L58`); `_render_template` (`L83`); `_ingest_notification` (`L187`) returning `accepted|duplicate|auth|other`; `_read_notifications` (`L232`); `_build_message_event` (`L293`); `_render_prompt` (`L302`).
- **Data flow:** `GET /msgraph/webhook?validationToken=…` → echo as `text/plain` (`L183-185`); `POST` → source-IP check (`L208`) → body read + size cap (`L232-251`) → per notification: resource filter (`L192`) → `clientState` compare (`L194`) → receipt dedup (`L198-200`) → schedule (`L201`) → `MessageEvent(internal=True)` (`L300`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - `_verify_client_state` compares as BYTES (`L285`) because `hmac.compare_digest` raises `TypeError` on a non-ASCII `str` and `clientState` is request-controlled — comparing the raw str would crash the handler on a hostile payload.
  - The HTTP status is chosen by the COUNTS, not by the first failure (`L216-230`): anything ingested or deduped → 202; every item failed auth → 403 so Graph stops retrying a forged batch; a legitimate Graph retry carrying a valid `clientState` still gets 202.
  - The adapter REFUSES TO START on a network-accessible bind without `extra.allowed_source_cidrs` (`L129-142`) — a missing allowlist is a fail-closed startup, not a runtime warning; loopback binds may omit it.
  - `DEFAULT_HOST = None` (`L35`) makes aiohttp bind one socket per address family; the old `"0.0.0.0"` default was unreachable over IPv6-only private networks.
  - `_build_message_event` derives the message id from the notification's own `id` when present, else a SHA-1 of the sorted JSON (`L294`), and stamps `internal=True` (`L300`) so the event bypasses user authorization.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** body size is checked twice — from `Content-Length` before reading and from the actual bytes after (`L238`, `L244`) — and over-limit is 413. A read failure is 400, a JSON/charset failure is 400, a non-list `value` is 400 (`L246-251`). A scheduler that raises aborts the whole batch with 503 (`L220-222`). The receipt cache is a bounded FIFO of 5000 ids (`L287-291`). `_admit_notification` (`L325`) writes through `tracked_write(..., ordered=True)` off the owner loop so a held SQLite writer cannot freeze every session while Graph waits (`L349-358`).
- **Surprising:** `send` (`L163`) is a no-op that only LOGS the first 200 characters of the content and returns `success=True` — the adapter is ingress-only yet reports a successful send. `_render_prompt` (`L302`) reads `self.config.extra.get("prompt", "")` with no `or {}` guard, unlike `__init__` which normalises `extra = config.extra or {}` (`L107`) — a config whose `extra` is `None` raises `AttributeError` on the first notification. The envelope timestamp written by `_admit_notification` is a fixed epoch, `datetime.fromtimestamp(0, UTC)` (`L346`), not wall time.

### `gateway/platforms/send_targets.py`
The public contract a platform plugin declares for `send_message`: `NOT_EXPLICIT` (`L15`) is the sentinel that stops target parsing and skips core's generic id rules, the three parser factories (`L18-32`) return `(chat_id, thread_id)` only on a FULL regex match (a partial match falls through to core), and `StandaloneSendPolicy` (`L36`) pins the independent switches that decide whether a send bypasses the live adapter for `standalone_sender_fn` — `media` vs `all_sends`, `captionable`, `empty_list_between_chunks` (`media_files=[]` vs `None` for non-final chunks), `mentions` (re-pinged per message, so only the first payload carries them) and `text_direct`.

### `gateway/platforms/shared_ingress.py`
The multiplex `/p/<profile>/` forwarding layer: `bind_listener` (`L57`) either binds a port OR publishes a frozen `web.Application` for the default profile's listener to serve; `dispatch_profile_ingress` (`L120`) resolves the profile's OWN adapter app and never falls back to the default's (`L135-148`, the un-prefixed path keeps serving the default untouched), re-clones the request with the chosen adapter's own `client_max_size` (`L150`) and runs it inside `_profile_runtime_scope` (`L155`); a parked profile answers 503 with `ParkedRouteRefused.headers` instead of 404 (`L130-134`).

### `gateway/platforms/tcp_site.py`
`start_tcp_site` (`L32`) — the macOS-exclusive bind with a TIME_WAIT escape hatch: on EADDRINUSE it probes the port with `has_live_listener` (`L20-29` — a refused connect proves the address is free, while any other OSError counts as "live" so the caller stays exclusive), stops the dead site aiohttp already registered before binding (`L59`) and rebinds once with `reuse_address=True` — but only for an explicit host, because a wildcard host's foreign listener cannot be probed and must keep winning (`L55`).

### `gateway/platforms/webhook.py`
- **Purpose:** Generic aiohttp webhook receiver: authenticate a provider-signed POST, render it into an agent prompt, admit the run durably, and route the agent's reply back out.
- **Key abstractions:** `_WebhookDeliveryIdentity` (frozen dataclass, `gateway/platforms/webhook.py:84`) = (profile, route, delivery_id) → `session_chat_id` (`:96`) as `webhook:v2:<base64url(json)>`; `route_from_chat_id` (`:106`) decodes that, falling back to the legacy `webhook:{route}:{delivery}` spelling that pre-upgrade admissions still carry. `WebhookAdapter` (`:204`): `connect` (`:278`), `send` (`:317`), `_handle_webhook` (`:663`), `_validate_signature` (`:858`), `_render_prompt` (`:912`), `_deliver_github_comment` (`:951`), `_mirror_delivery` (`:1046`). Sentinels `_PROFILE_REJECTED` (`:52`) and `_UNPARSEABLE` (`:53`).
- **Data flow:** POST → `_resolve_route` (`:624`) → `_read_authenticated_body` (`:530`, auth-before-body) → `parked_profile_refusal` → rate limit (`:370`) → `_parse_body` (`:557`) → MCP control envelope → event-type allow-list → `route_filters_match` → route script (worker thread) → `_render_prompt` → `_apply_skills` (`:645`) → `_admit_agent_run` (`:790`) → `admit_producer` → 202 `accepted`; the agent's later reply arrives at `send` (`:317`) → `retained_destination` → `_deliver_to` (`:335`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Presence of `X-Webhook-Signature-V2` COMMITS to V2 and never falls back to legacy V1 (`:886-895`) — otherwise an attacker strips the timestamp from a captured mixed V1+V2 request and replays it against the still-present body-only V1 signature. `deliver_only` / `cron_job` / `coalesce` routes are marked `non_durable` (`:715-717`) so they use the in-memory `_record_delivery_id` guard (`:382`) instead of the admission ledger: a recorded id would answer the provider's retry as a duplicate once the profile is served again. `INSECURE_NO_AUTH` is refused at startup unless the bind host is loopback (`:265-270`), and again per dynamic route (`:417-436`). `toolsets_for_source` keys the route on `user_id` (`webhook:{route}`) and NOT on `chat_id` (`:396-411`) because chat_id embeds a caller-supplied delivery id and `:` — the comment cites GHSA-2fmg-cjqm-hhrj. The `token` property (`:247`) hashes only the STATIC routes' secrets, so a hot-reload of dynamic subscriptions cannot re-key already-admitted deliveries.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the body cap is enforced three times — Content-Length, `web.HTTPRequestEntityTooLarge` (chunked/lying length), and a post-read length check (`:531-548`) — with `client_max_size` as the server-level backstop. A blank/whitespace-only secret is rejected by `_is_usable_secret` (`:132-140`) because whitespace is truthy to `if not secret`. `gh pr comment` validates `repo` against `_REPO_RE` and `pr_number` as a positive int BEFORE shelling out (`:958-968`, CLI-arg-injection guard) and runs via `asyncio.to_thread` with a 30s timeout (`:975-980`) because inline it froze every adapter and timer on the gateway loop. `_github_env` (`:990-1003`) DROPS `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` from `os.environ` and re-adds only the routed profile's, because under multiplex `os.environ` carries the default profile's token. `_prune_delivery_info` (`:347-359`) bounds `_delivery_info` by TTL even for runs that never answer.
- **Surprising:** `send()` reads the delivery entry with `.get()` and NEVER pops it (`:317-320`) — both the interim status messages and the final response need it, so the map is TTL-pruned rather than consumed. `_mirror_delivery` mirrors the delivered text into the target chat's session with `role="user"` (`:1046-1071`): the text is not the target session's agent speaking, and a labelled user turn merges safely on strict-alternation providers. `_handle_cron_trigger` (`:592-622`) answers 202 immediately and runs the job on a worker thread — the rendered prompt is transient per-run context, the job's own prompt/skills/model apply. `disconnect()` flushes the coalescer first (`:309-310`) so buffered events are dispatched, not dropped, on shutdown/reconnect. (Read head/middle/tail windows of 1071 lines, not every line.)

### `gateway/platforms/webhook_coalesce.py` — Per-route debounce/supersede buffer: `WebhookCoalescer` (`gateway/platforms/webhook_coalesce.py:66`) + `PendingEvent` (`:49`) + `validate_coalesce_config` (`:27`) pin that only the LATEST event of a group is dispatched after a quiet window (default 30s) capped at `max_wait_seconds` (default 300s) past the group's FIRST event, so a steady stream cannot starve dispatch; a key that does not resolve returns `None` and the caller dispatches immediately rather than collapsing unrelated entities (`:80-99`).

### `gateway/platforms/webhook_delivery.py` — Where a webhook's answer goes: `validate_destination` (`gateway/platforms/webhook_delivery.py:22`) persists ONLY the fields the selected sender actually consumes (credentials/whole rendered payloads in `deliver_extra` are dropped, `:56-60`); `retained_destination` (`:71`) re-reads the destination from the signed ingress admission and raises `admission_conflict` when the route digest changed — retained data is not a grant; `route_digest` (`:9`) is a SHA-256 over the route config.

### `gateway/platforms/webhook_filters.py` — Declarative route filters + script transforms: `WebhookRouteProcessor` (`gateway/platforms/webhook_filters.py:107`) pins that a filter spec is `all`/`any`/`not` combinators over field operators (first present key wins, `:96-104`) and that an UNRESOLVED field is the `_MISSING` sentinel, never `None`, so `not_equals` on a missing field passes and `exists` can distinguish absent from null; a route script must live under `HERMES_HOME/scripts` (`:40-57`) and a non-zero exit, empty stdout, `[SILENT]`, or a `__hermes_ignore__` flag DROPS the webhook (`:169-183`).

### `gateway/platforms/webhook_ingress.py`
- **Purpose:** The receipt-only admission path for webhook/producer deliveries — turns an HTTP event into a durable, idempotent, ordered commit in the routed profile's session FIFO.
- **Key abstractions:** `producer_scope` (`gateway/platforms/webhook_ingress.py:9`) — async CM resolving the owning profile/authority and pinning identity through the runner's single canonicalization seam. `admit_producer` (`:122`) — the one admission entry point, returning `None` when a plugin skipped or consumed the delivery. `_admit_once` (`:158`), `_webhook_retry` (`:234`), `finalize_webhook` (`:263`), `recover_webhook_finalizations` (`:283`), `parked_profile_refusal` (`:57`). `_deliveries` (`:189`) + `_delivery_turn` (`:193`) — the in-process per-delivery serialization table.
- **Data flow:** `producer_scope` → `_prepared` (`:211`) prepares the payload (which runs the SAME authorization as admission, so a refused sender raises there) → `_delivery_turn` serializes by (profile, principal, provider id) → `_committed` (`:229`) reads the admission rows → `_admit_once` runs the retry check, the `pre_gateway_dispatch` rewrite hook and the `post_gateway_admission` consumer hook → `authority.admit_native`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A hook rewrite is committed with the fingerprint of the delivery AS RECEIVED (`admitted._ingress_sha256`, `:150-155`) so a provider retry that resends the original body resolves to the same row even though a plugin rewrote the text. A cancelled admission write still commits (it is shielded): `native_write_started` (`:137`) distinguishes "the write began" from "the write did not", so a cancelled delivery does NOT release media it already wrote (`:146-151`). Parked is retryable, refused is not — `_coalesced_route_refusal` (`:98-119`) asks admission's own source builder so the edge and the admission give one answer, and an explicit `/p/<profile>/` prefix wins over `profile_routes`. `_webhook_retry` strips today's `webhook_delivery`/`webhook_route` from the candidate payload when the retained row has neither (`:243-250`): "a pre-destination receipt is still a receipt, not permission to infer again".
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `len(rows) != 1 or not same` → `RuntimeStoreError('admission_conflict')` (`:252-253`) — two admissions for one identity is a conflict, not a duplicate. `_release_preparation` (`:202-207`) releases media copied into the retained store for a delivery that was skipped/consumed/cancelled; a path another admission or in-flight capture holds is kept. `finalize_webhook` (`:263-280`) is one guarded UPDATE requiring the epoch fence, a terminal admission with matching generation/owner_epoch, and NO non-terminal admission for that session — so a one-shot webhook session ends only when it is the only open generation. `recover_webhook_finalizations` (`:283-315`) re-checks current route authorization before closing a settled webhook; `KeyError`/`RuntimeStoreError` → `'unavailable'`. Hooks fire once per admission and never for a replay of an already-committed delivery (`:53-58`).
- **Surprising:** A plugin that skips (hook returns `None`) or consumes the delivery means NOTHING is admitted at all and the prepared media is released (`:130`, `:159-183`). The `post_gateway_admission` consumer's reply rides on `event._consumer_reply` and is delivered by the CALLER (`gateway/platforms/webhook.py:826-836`), not by this module.

### `gateway/platforms/webhook_mcp_events.py` — MCP-Events control envelopes on webhook routes: `handle_mcp_event_control` (`gateway/platforms/webhook_mcp_events.py:106`) pins that a body with a top-level `type` is a control envelope — `verification` echoes the challenge, `terminated` removes the route and notifies, `gap` records a fresh cursor, and an UNKNOWN type is acknowledged `{"status":"ignored"}` so the server stops retrying it and it never starts a run; an occurrence (no `type`) continues through the normal route pipeline, deduped by `eventId` rather than the per-attempt `webhook-id` (`:48-55`).

### `gateway/platforms/whatsapp_common.py`
- **Purpose:** Transport-agnostic WhatsApp behavior shared by the Baileys bridge and Cloud API adapters: allow-list/DM/group gating, mention detection, quoted-reply-to-bot detection, broadcast filtering, markdown conversion, chunk budgeting.
- **Key abstractions:** `WhatsAppBehaviorMixin(OwnAccessPolicyMixin)` (`gateway/platforms/whatsapp_common.py:53`); the module docstring (`:4-12`) states the host-adapter attribute contract (`config`, `name`, `_dm_policy`/`_group_policy`, `_allow_from`/`_group_allow_from`, `_mention_patterns`, `_reply_prefix`) because the mixin owns no state. `_stash` (`:33`), `_header_to_bold` (`:44`), `format_message` (`:268`), `_should_process_message` (`:247`), `_live_dm_allow_from` (`:131`), `resolve_whatsapp_bridge_dir` (`:289`).
- **Data flow:** raw dict from either transport → `_should_process_message` (`:247`) gates DM/group/free-response/mention → outbound markdown → `format_message` (`:268`) → WhatsApp syntax; `_outgoing_chunk_limit` (`:86`) budgets the reply prefix.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `format_message` stashes fenced and inline code as `\x00<tag><n>\x00` placeholders BEFORE the italic regex runs (`:271-272`) — otherwise a `*` inside a code block becomes WhatsApp italics. Italic is converted BEFORE bold (`:275-276`) with lookarounds `(?<!\*)\*(?!\s|\*)` so `**bold**` is not first mangled into `_bold_`. `_effective_reply_prefix` returns `""` unless the mode is `self-chat` (`:75-77`) and `_outgoing_chunk_limit` reserves room for the prefix with a 1024 floor (`:86-88`). `_live_dm_allow_from` (`:131-143`) re-reads the env key when the allowlist was env-seeded so a pairing revoke takes effect without a restart; a REMOVED key means empty, not the construction snapshot — while config-seeded adapters keep the in-memory set, because a stale env value must not broaden access.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_normalize_whatsapp_id` strips the device suffix (`<user>:<device>@lid` → `<user>@lid`) (`:146-149`). Broadcast pseudo-chats (`status@broadcast`, `*@broadcast`, `*@newsletter`) are filtered even in self-chat/fromMe mode (`:153-157`, `:249-251`). A mention pattern that fails `re.compile` is logged and skipped, never fatal (`:203-207`). `_clean_bot_mention_text` returns the ORIGINAL text when stripping would empty it (`:237-245`). Allowlist matching resolves BOTH sides through `gateway.whatsapp_identity.expand_whatsapp_aliases` (`:161-178`) because inbound senders arrive as `<id>@lid` while allowlists hold phone numbers.
- **Surprising:** `MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH = 4096` is a deliberate UX cap, not the ~65K protocol max (`:56-57`). `_select_allowlist` (`:113-123`) decides by key PRESENCE: an explicitly empty config list wins and stays authoritative, while env carriers must be truthy. `resolve_whatsapp_bridge_dir` (`:289-306`) MIRRORS a read-only install tree (e.g. `/opt/hermes`) into HERMES_HOME so `npm install` works there.

### `gateway/plugin_keyless_inject.py` — Target selection for a key-less `PluginContext.inject_message(text)`: `resolve` (`gateway/plugin_keyless_inject.py:68`) pins a three-step ladder (the bound local session → the ONE classic-CLI-launched local session with a viewer → refusal BY NAME) and never guesses however many sessions are open; refusals are rate-limited to one warning per (plugin, kind) per 300s so a timer-driven plugin cannot flood the log (`:32-50`).

### `gateway/plugin_messages.py` — Where `PluginContext.post_message` lands: `resolve` (`gateway/plugin_messages.py:86`) + `PostTarget` (`:29`) pin that the route is NEVER chosen by the plugin — it is the session bound in the calling context; a local session has no transport of its own, so the text goes to its viewers as a keyed `notification.show` (`:117-122`) and an edit re-publishes the same key, while a messaging chat sends through the adapter that answers it and an adapter without edit support gets a fresh send with the handle following the new message (`:147-176`).

### `gateway/profile_routing.py` — Routes a sender/location to a different profile: `ProfileRoute.specificity` (`gateway/profile_routing.py:77`) pins the additive weight user_id 16 > thread_id 8 > chat_id 4 > guild_id 2, matched CONJUNCTIVELY and sorted most-specific-first, so a route applies only to the bot of its `bot_profile` (`:81-109`); a WhatsApp `chat_id` matches across number/JID/LID via `expand_whatsapp_aliases` while group/broadcast/newsletter JIDs stay exact-only (`:32-49`).

### `gateway/readiness.py` — Bounded, non-destructive readiness probes: `collect_runtime_readiness` (`gateway/readiness.py:97`) pins that a probe never mutates and never exposes config values, credentials, paths or payloads (status and counts only), that the state.db check is a read-only URI connection with `PRAGMA query_only=ON` and a 1s timeout so it cannot compete with writers (`:23-50`), and that a corrupt store is reported `unavailable` regardless of what the in-memory cache claims (`:84-95`).

### `gateway/relay/__init__.py`
- **Purpose:** Gateway-side config/env resolution, connector HTTP provisioning, and platform registration for the experimental "Gateway Gateway" relay lane.
- **Key abstractions:** `relay_url` (`gateway/relay/__init__.py:122`), `relay_platform_identities` (`:129`), `relay_fronted_platforms` (`:147`), `relay_connection_auth` (`:175`), `relay_endpoint` (`:184`), `relay_route_keys` (`:192`), `relay_instance_id` (`:206`), `relay_wake_url` (`:214`), `relay_display_name` (`:222`), `relay_relevance_policy` (`:282`), `self_provision_relay` (`:510`), `send_relay_policy` (`:641`), `register_relay_adapter` (`:699`), `_resolve_relay_identity_token` (`:419`).
- **Data flow:** env var → `_env_or_cfg` (`:82`, env first then `gateway.<key>` in config.yaml) → `relay_url()` → `_post_provision` (`:344`, POST `/relay/provision`) → the response secret is written into `os.environ` (`:586-589`) → `register_relay_adapter` (`:699`) builds a `WebSocketRelayTransport` and registers the `relay` platform in `platform_registry` (`:752-761`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_post_provision` treats "no secret in the response" as a VALUE, not an error, and fails only on a malformed discriminator: the ONLY credential-less shape the connector emits is literal JSON `false` (`secretIssued: false`) — `true` with no secret, or a non-boolean, raises (`:409-416`). `self_provision_relay` writes credentials only when `result.get("secret")` is PRESENT and the env var is unset (`:586`); an empty write would look "unset" on the next pass while `deliveryKey` had already been clobbered. The secret is never logged (`:576-577`) and creds live ONLY in process memory, never `~/.hermes/.env` (`:516-517`), so a hosted container re-provisions every boot. `relay_display_name` drops the stock brand `"Hermes Agent"` (`:247-248`) because forwarding it would prefix every reply `**Hermes Agent:**` and shadow the connector's linked-owner fallback, which actually disambiguates. `relay_relevance_policy` returns `None` when `require_mention` is UNSET (not falsy) (`:293-295`, `:334-335`): an explicit `require_mention: false` is a non-default choice that MUST be declared or the connector would mention-gate an agent configured to free-respond. `_launch_profile_scope` (`:63-79`) deliberately uses `_profile_runtime_scope`, not `launch_profile_scope_if_multiplexed`, whose first entry would freeze the launch env snapshot before startup wrote `GATEWAY_RELAY_*` into `os.environ`.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `relay_explicitly_disabled` (`:93-119`) honours only an explicit YAML `enabled` key (`_enabled_explicit`); a legacy `gateway.json` block stays advisory, and an absent key keeps URL-only activation. The ambient-token endpoint (mode 1b) body must match `_AMBIENT_TOKEN_SHAPE` (`:32-35`) — a JWT or ≥32-char opaque token — so plain-text error bodies like `unauthorized` fail closed (`:472-480`); a JSON envelope is exempt from the shape gate (`:466-471`). Exactly one of client_id/client_secret raises loudly naming the missing half and NEVER GETs the IdP (`:483-492`). `self_provision_relay` never raises: a failed identity resolution or a failed platform POST logs and returns False so the gateway still boots (`:534-538`, `:568-573`) and is PARTIAL-FAILURE-TOLERANT across platforms. `send_relay_policy` (`:641-696`) never raises and never blocks boot — relevance is an optimization layered on the authorization gate.
- **Surprising:** `self_provision_relay`'s trigger is deliberately NOT `is_managed()` (`:519-522`) — a NAS-hosted Fly agent is not managed, yet "pointed at a connector without a pinned secret" is the real signal, and it self-guards (an enrolled gateway has a PINNED secret → skipped). `relay_fronted_platforms` (`:147-154`) reads the same env source as the live adapter, so a standalone scheduler can use it with no live adapter. `register_relay_adapter(force=True)` builds a TRANSPORT-LESS adapter that keeps the placeholder descriptor (`:717-733`) — that is the test path. The registered platform's `check_fn` is `lambda: True` (`:757`): the relay platform always reports available; real health is the transport's.

### `gateway/relay/adapter.py`
- **Purpose:** The single generic `BasePlatformAdapter` that fronts one or many platforms over a connector WebSocket, with capabilities negotiated by a `CapabilityDescriptor`.
- **Key abstractions:** `RelayAdapter(RelayDiscordInteractionMixin, BasePlatformAdapter)` (`gateway/relay/adapter.py:109`). Capability surface: `_descriptor_for_chat` (`:252`), `max_message_length_for_chat` (`:260`), `supports_draft_streaming` (`:266`), `stream_is_message_for_chat` (`:310`), `supports_native_task_cards` (`:326`). Stream/draft: `_draft_key` (`:336`), `_card_key` (`:360`), `_match_open_draft` (`:374`), `send_draft` (`:490`), `_seal_open_draft` (`:565`), `_absorb_into_open_draft` (`:639`), `abandon_open_draft` (`:782`). Egress: `_outbound` (`:399`), `_gated_op` (`:417`), `send` (`:1333`), `send_for_platform` (`:1236`). Routing caches: `_capture_scope` (`:1072`), `_with_scope` (`:1108`), `fronts_platform` (`:1125`). Prompts: `_mint_prompt` (`:1826`), `_minted_here` (`:1842`), `_consume_prompt_response` (`:2005`), `_PROMPT_RESOLVERS` (`:2301`). Lifecycle: `connect` (`:798`), `_watch_for_revocation` (`:853`), `disconnect` (`:1146`), `go_dormant` (`:1195`).
- **Data flow:** connector WS → `connect` (`:798`) → `transport.handshake()` → `_apply_descriptor` (`:872`) → inbound handler → `_on_inbound` (`:881`) → dedupe → `_capture_scope` → `_remember_discord_labels` → `_stamp_slack_session_thread` → `_consume_prompt_response` → `_localize_inbound_media` → `handle_message` (or `admit_relay_delivery` in native mode, `:912-919`); the agent's turn egresses back over the SAME WS through `send`/`send_draft`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `supports_draft_streaming` requires BOTH the descriptor flag AND `"draft" in supported_ops` (`:266-283`) — `supported_ops` is fail-open for legacy connectors, but "draft" did not exist pre-contract so it must NOT fail open. An authorization DECLINE is not lane unavailability: `_gated_op` returns `None` for a decline by default, but callers whose fallback would leak pass `surface_declines=True` (`:417-468`); `_absorb_into_open_draft` refuses to fall back to a plain send after a declined seal, because that re-delivers the refused content to the same chat (`:659-669`). `draft_stream_is_message` and `stream_is_message_for_chat` are SLACK-ONLY and derived from the platform NAME, not a descriptor bit (`:196-199`, `:310-324`) — the base `send_draft` contract is Telegram-shaped, and setting this for any other connector intercepted the turn-final into `draft(final=true)` so no history message was ever posted. A prompt id is prefixed with a per-process nonce `_prompt_owner_nonce` (`:199-200`) because the connector fans a button press to EVERY live gateway session of the tenant while `_pending_prompts` is process-local. The inbound dedupe key includes the platform (`:925-940`) because one socket can front several platforms whose numeric ids may collide; a message with no platform message id NEVER dedupes (fail-open: dropping a real message beats rerunning one). `disconnect()` (`:1146-1193`) measures its budget from the top and threads the REMAINDER into `transport.disconnect(budget_s=...)` — otherwise the runner's `wait_for` cancels teardown mid-drain and the transport's fail-pending loop is skipped.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a post-seal straggler draft frame is swallowed and reported as SUCCESS (`:502-518`) because the content is already in the sealed message — but the FIRST swallow per key logs a warning, since a burst means something sealed a live stream mid-flight. An AMBIGUOUS seal (lost ack) retries the SAME idempotent frame once (`:606-609`); two consecutive losses return `success=False` with `raw_response={"ambiguous": True}` rather than being read as a refusal (`:615-623`). `send_draft` keeps interception ARMED on an ambiguous transport outcome (`:526-546`) and DISARMS only on a definite connector rejection (`:547-551`). `_on_inbound` pops the dedupe key on `CancelledError` (`:906-911`) — teardown cancels the reader mid-frame, so the connector replays it and the replay must be admitted, not dropped as seen. `_ack_emoji` (`:2164-2185`) treats both `""` and `"relay"` as unresolved because `_event_from_wire` maps an absent/unknown platform to `Platform.RELAY` (`:2146-2151`).
- **Surprising:** `Platform` is a plain Enum, so `str(member)` yields `"Platform.TELEGRAM"` — `_ack_emoji` reads `.value` first or every lookup misses (`:2170-2184`). Telegram's curated reaction vocabulary excludes ✅/❌, so the Telegram lane uses 👀/👍/👎 while Slack/Discord keep ✅/❌ (`:2130-2145`). The adapter still presents to the runner as `Platform.RELAY` even while fronting Discord/Telegram/Slack (`:118-120`), and `_capture_scope` deliberately skips the generic `"relay"` platform value (`:1082-1087`). `connect(is_reconnect=...)` accepts the kwarg and IGNORES it (`:798-815`) — relay's queue lives in the connector's durable buffer. `_profile_from_session_key` (`:98-107`) returns None for the legacy `agent:main` namespace so single-profile wire frames stay byte-identical. (Read head/middle/tail windows of 2305 lines plus two gap windows, not every line.)

### `gateway/relay/adapter_discord_interaction.py`
- **Purpose:** Normalizes connector-forwarded Discord interactions (slash commands, button presses, modal submits) into `MessageEvent`s on the same agent path as ordinary chat.
- **Key abstractions:** `RelayDiscordInteractionMixin` (`gateway/relay/adapter_discord_interaction.py:25`); `_on_passthrough` (`:84`), `_discord_interaction_to_event` (`:114`), `_decode_prompt_token` (`:206`), `_render_modal_submit` (`:216`), `_render_interaction_options` (`:236`), `_remember_discord_labels` (`:30`), `_discord_chat_labels_for` (`:60`).
- **Data flow:** connector passthrough frame → `_on_passthrough` (`:84`) → `_discord_interaction_to_event` (`:114`) builds a `SessionSource` with platform DISCORD → `_capture_scope` → the prompt-token gate → chat labels from the text lane → `relay_passthrough_event` context → `handle_message` (`:103-105`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the `SessionSource` uses the LOGICAL platform (DISCORD), not RELAY (`:161-165`) — session keys must match the connector's capability binding (`platform="discord"`), `/sethome` must file under the logical platform, and `_capture_scope` skips the generic `"relay"`. `chat_type` is `"group"` for a guild channel, not `"channel"` (`:167-169`) because both the connector's capability binding and the native Discord adapter key guild channels as `"group"`. A thread is detected from the PARTIAL channel object's `type` in (10, 11, 12) with the parent from `parent_id` (`:157-171`) — without it an interaction sent inside a thread landed in a per-user `"group"` session beside the thread's. `user_name` is DERIVED from the interaction (nick → global_name → username), never remembered (`:147-153`) — a remembered name would outlive a nickname change. A prompt-token component press becomes a STRUCTURED prompt answer; a foreign `custom_id` keeps the best-effort TEXT shape (`:188-203`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_on_passthrough` NEVER raises (`:91-112`) — a malformed forward must not kill the read loop. A body that is not JSON, or not a dict, yields `None` (`:119-124`). The chat-labels store read is moved off the loop with `asyncio.to_thread` because it takes the DB's writer lock (`:69-71`); a store fault is logged at debug and the interaction is NOT dropped (`:72-76`) — labels only keep the prompt cache warm. A label record that fails to write is popped so the next message retries (`:52-56`).
- **Surprising:** `chat_type`/`thread_id`/`parent_chat_id` are reconstructed from the interaction body, and `delivered_via_upstream_relay=True` is set LOCALLY and never read off the wire (`:177-179`) — it engages `/sethome`'s via-relay guard. The prompt-token alphabet is the connector's `promptCodec.decodePromptCallback` alphabet `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,32}` (`:21-22`).

### `gateway/relay/auth.py` — Gateway-side relay WS-upgrade auth: `make_upgrade_token` (`gateway/relay/auth.py:36`) pins the exact wire bytes `base64url(f"{gateway_id}:{exp}:{sig}")` with `sig = HMAC_SHA256(f"{payload}:{exp}", secret).hexdigest()` and UNPADDED base64url, because the connector's `relayAuthToken.ts` `makeToken` must match byte-for-byte and verifies against a multi-secret rotation list (`:1-8`, `:25-33`).

### `gateway/relay/command_manifest.py` — The gateway-declared Discord slash-command manifest for the relay lane: `build_relay_command_manifest` (`gateway/relay/command_manifest.py:46`) pins that the manifest MIRRORS the native tree (same names, same descriptions) and that a new entry needs NO new handler, because interactions return through the passthrough plane as ordinary `"/name args"` COMMAND events (`:1-11`).

### `gateway/relay/descriptor.py` — the frozen handshake capability profile: `CapabilityDescriptor` (descriptor.py:21) with `CONTRACT_VERSION = 1` (descriptor.py:17) and `LEGACY_OPS = ("send","edit","typing","follow_up")`; pins the rule that an empty `supported_ops` means LEGACY (not "nothing supported") so a new op is True only when explicitly advertised, and that `from_json` (descriptor.py:85) normalizes a garbage `max_message_length` to 4096 at the trust boundary so the adapter can always chunk.

### `gateway/relay/egress.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway half of relay egress authorization: refuse outbound relay frames whose destination the gateway has no provenance for, and recognise the connector's *definite* authorization decline without degrading it into a transport error.
- **Key abstractions:** `is_egress_decline` (egress.py:49) — a decline requires failure **and** NOT `ambiguous` **and** (code `egress_declined` or the uniform text marker). `declined_send` (egress.py:72) — the single unwrapper for `SendResult` objects, checking `raw_response` first, else the error text. `RelayRouteUnknown` (egress.py:143) — "routing unknown", deliberately distinct from "no relay configured". `authorize_relay_target` (egress.py:485) returns an error string (never raises) — `None` means the send may proceed. `_live_relay_fronted` (egress.py:170) / `_relay_fronted` (egress.py:235) / `relay_routed_platform` (egress.py:332) decide whether the guard applies at all. `attested_relay_targets` (egress.py:401) builds the allowed destination set.
- **Data flow:** `send_message(target)` → `authorize_relay_target(platform, chat_id, thread_id)` → `relay_routed_platform` → (`_relay_fronted` → `_live_relay_fronted` preferred over the env/config snapshot) → if relay-routed, the target must be in `attested_relay_targets` (home channel ∪ channel directory ∪ session origins) → else a refusal string. Outbound results come back and are classified by `declined_send`/`is_egress_decline`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The file is written as a post-mortem of five review rounds that found the *same* fail-open defect at five boundaries; `_live_relay_fronted` (egress.py:170) is deliberately **inverted** to fail *closed* by default — every `return None` is guarded by an explicit narrow check, and the outer handler converts anything else into `RelayRouteUnknown`. Previously the function asked "did something go wrong?" and answered `None`, which *means* "not relay-routed, skip authorization" (egress.py:184-207). Second: the thread id is part of the destination, not a formatting detail — on Discord the thread is the literal REST target, so an attested parent chat cannot vouch for a caller-supplied thread; the check is the *bound* form `f"{target}:{thread}" in attested` (egress.py:526-540), and the bare `thread in attested` arm was removed because it proved nothing about parentage.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_is_missing_gateway_relay` (egress.py:153) distinguishes "the relay package is absent" (`ModuleNotFoundError` with `name` in `gateway`/`gateway.relay`/`gateway.relay.egress`) from "a nested dependency failed to load" — the latter is a fault and raises `RelayRouteUnknown` rather than being flattened into an empty fronted set (egress.py:249-263). `_has_live_native_adapter` (egress.py:283) re-raises `RelayRouteUnknown` instead of returning False, and has an explicit "no `except: return True` here" note (egress.py:305) because that shape recreated the bypass. `_session_ids` (egress.py:373) recovers a parent chat from the structured `thread_id` field, never by splitting on `":"` — splitting invented attestations for Matrix room ids like `!room:server.org` (egress.py:385-397). `_is_unresolved_handle` (egress.py:453) exempts only `@`-prefixed Telegram targets, and only when no native Telegram credential exists (a native token sends outside the connector entirely, so the connector never authorizes it).
- **Surprising:** The Telegram `@username` carve-out (egress.py:544-566) is a *deliberate hole with a second layer* — the gateway cannot resolve `@channel` to a numeric id, so refusing would be "fail always", and the connector's own egress floor remains the authoritative check; the module records that resolving the handle before authorizing ("option 2") was deliberately NOT done here because it needs a new wire surface. `is_egress_decline` treats the decline sentence as *uniformly uninformative* on purpose — it must not leak whether a destination belongs to another tenant or to nobody (egress.py:5-24).

### `gateway/relay/inbound_admission.py` — native admission of a connector-delivered relay message: `admit_relay_delivery` (inbound_admission.py:10) admits the frame on the socket reader, but with a `pre_gateway_dispatch`/`post_gateway_admission` plugin hook it runs in a per-chat task chain (`_admit_after`, inbound_admission.py:33) so one chat's deliveries still commit in arrival order; pins the ACK rule — a permanent refusal returns and is ACKed, any other failure withholds the ACK so the connector redelivers, and a parked target is refused-to-sender and ACKed so one dead profile cannot stall the connector's ordered ack-gated buffer drain for every other profile.

### `gateway/relay/media.py` — the relay media plane client: `RelayMediaClient` (media.py:51) uploads local bytes to `/relay/media` and returns a connector re-host reference, or downloads a re-host reference to a local temp file (media.py:71, media.py:111); pins `MEDIA_MAX_BYTES = 25 MiB` (media.py:31) mirrored from the connector so an oversized file fails fast instead of round-tripping to a 413, and the bearer is presented *only* for `/relay/media/` URLs (public CDN pass-throughs are fetched without it).

### `gateway/relay/transport.py` — the `RelayTransport` Protocol (transport.py:30) that the gateway↔connector wire contract must satisfy: `connect`/`disconnect`/`handshake`/`send_outbound`/`get_chat_info`/`send_interrupt`/`go_idle`/`send_follow_up` plus the two callback seams `InboundHandler` (transport.py:21) and `PassthroughHandler` (transport.py:28); pins that the gateway dials OUT (production = `ws_transport.py`, tests = in-memory stub) and that `go_idle` is a *connector-authoritative* flip awaited by ack (transport.py:78).

### `gateway/relay/ws_transport.py`
- **Purpose:** The production WebSocket `RelayTransport`: the gateway dials the connector's `/relay` endpoint and speaks newline-delimited JSON frames, with outbound calls blocked on a per-`requestId` future until the matching `outbound_result`.
- **Key abstractions:** `WebSocketRelayTransport` (ws_transport.py:308). `_dial_and_start` (ws_transport.py:415) dials, installs the reader, and sends one `hello` **per fronted identity**. `_request_response` (ws_transport.py:642) is the single outbound path. `_read_loop` (ws_transport.py:693) parses frames and dispatches via the `_FRAME_HANDLERS` table (ws_transport.py:996). `_event_from_wire` (ws_transport.py:174) rebuilds a `MessageEvent`; `_media_types_from_wire` (ws_transport.py:147) builds a MIME list parallel to `media_urls`; `PassthroughForward` (ws_transport.py:251) / `_passthrough_from_wire` (ws_transport.py:273). Auth state: `_auth_revoked` (ws_transport.py:536), `_dial_generation`, `_auth_retry_generation`, `_auth_retry_pending` (ws_transport.py:388-409).
- **Data flow:** outbound → `send_outbound` → frame `{"type":"outbound","requestId":uuid4,"action":…}` (+`platform`/`botId` when known) → connector replies `outbound_result` → `_on_outbound_result` (ws_transport.py:975) sets the future. Inbound → connector `inbound` frame → `_on_inbound` (ws_transport.py:936) → `MessageEvent` → the registered `InboundHandler` → ACK (`inbound_ack` with `bufferId`) only after the handler takes it. `going_idle` → `going_idle_ack` → `go_idle` returns True.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A post-handshake `4401` is **not** immediately treated as revocation: an expired upgrade token produces the identical close code, and a scale-to-zero suspend landing mid-dial made that routine, so the first `4401` earns exactly one immediate re-dial with a freshly minted token; only a `4401` **on that retry generation** latches `_auth_revoked` (ws_transport.py:717-757, `_latch_if_fresh_token_refused` ws_transport.py:843). Generation-based, not flag-based, so a descriptor-then-4401 on the retry connection still counts as a refusal of the fresh token. Second: `go_dormant` (ws_transport.py:607) closes the socket *without* setting `_closing`, so the reader still arms the reconnect supervisor — but on the dormant cadence — because `disconnect()` cancels the supervisor and an unexpected close re-dials immediately (which would defeat the platform's suspend window); on a missed `go_idle` ack it returns **without** closing, since nothing will suspend it.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Exactly one dialer may be live — a reader that dies while a dialer is mid-dial must not start a second (ws_transport.py:764-775). `_request_response` distinguishes a *write* failure (frame never sent → no `ambiguous` flag) from a *future* failure (frame sent, outcome unknown → `ambiguous: True`), and a timeout is explicitly `ambiguous` (ws_transport.py:665-686) so an egress decline is never confused with a transport drop. The reader's `finally` fails every pending future with a *dict* result, never an exception (ws_transport.py:770-778). `disconnect` uses `asyncio.wait` (not `wait_for`+`gather`) so a timeout does not cancel the futures the `finally` owns, and the drain grace is clamped to the caller's remaining budget minus three teardown awaits (ws_transport.py:55-61). `_close_code_of`/`_close_reason_of` (ws_transport.py:787, ws_transport.py:799) read `.rcvd`/`.sent` because `websockets` 13+ deprecated `.code`.
- **Surprising:** `_media_types_from_wire` (ws_transport.py:147) resolves MIME types **by URL lookup** into `media[]`, never by position, because the two wire fields are independent and may disagree in order — yet the returned list must always be the same length as `media_urls` (padded with `""`), because consumers index the two lists pairwise. Also: the `hello` frame for Discord carries a `command_manifest` (ws_transport.py:449-458) built by the *gateway* so the connector (which holds the bot token) can reconcile Discord's app-command registration — enrichment that never blocks the handshake.

### `gateway/response_filters.py`
- **Purpose:** Decide whether a completed agent turn should be **delivered** to the chat (not what is persisted in history).
- **Key abstractions:** `LIVE_GATEWAY_SILENT_MARKERS` (response_filters.py:19) — a closed set including the Chinese forms `[静默]`, `静默`, `[沉默]`, `沉默` because a non-English lane translates the sentinel rather than dropping it, and the translated control token then reaches the user as content. `_BRACKETED_SILENCE_MARKERS` (response_filters.py:27) is *derived* from that set so a new marker cannot be added to one rule and forgotten in the other. `is_intentional_silence_response` (response_filters.py:76) — exact match only. `is_autonomous_silence_response` (response_filters.py:85) — loose match for cron/webhook lanes. `is_partial_silence_marker` (response_filters.py:167) — holds back a streamed prefix of a marker. `display_kind_for_event` (response_filters.py:111), `is_machinery_display_kind` (response_filters.py:146), `silence_allowed` (response_filters.py:156), `reply_expected_metadata` (response_filters.py:161).
- **Data flow:** agent response text → `_canonical_silence_candidates` (response_filters.py:66) → exact/loose match → the *current turn's persisted display kind* decides whether silence is allowed at all.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Silence is a *deliberate* marker, not an empty output: prose that merely mentions `NO_REPLY` must be delivered, and a blank response is the empty-response failure path, not silence (response_filters.py:76-84). The gate is the **turn's persisted display kind**, passed in by the caller rather than inferred from the transcript, because the inbound user row is not persisted yet and a previous internal row must never authorize silence on a human turn (response_filters.py:146-154). `MACHINERY_DISPLAY_KINDS` (response_filters.py:37) and `PRODUCER_CARD_KINDS` (response_filters.py:41) are deliberately **kept apart** so a kind added to the silence set never becomes nameable.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_strip_edge_silence_punctuation` (response_filters.py:56) strips stray edge punctuation but keeps `[]` structural, so a malformed `[SILENT` cannot become `SILENT`. `_MARKER_LENGTH_CAP = 64` (response_filters.py:44) bounds the candidate search — a long response is never a marker. `_is_edge_silence_punctuation` uses `unicodedata.category(ch).startswith("P")`.
- **Surprising:** `is_partial_silence_marker` (response_filters.py:167) exists because a streamed buffer whose canonical form is a non-empty *prefix* of a marker ("NO" on the way to "NO_REPLY") must be held back — otherwise a raw marker is shown to the user and then retracted. A scheduled heartbeat prompt is self-injected but deliberately stays **non-internal** so authorization and the emergency stop still apply to it (response_filters.py:118-124).

### `gateway/restart.py`
- **Purpose:** The single source of truth for gateway restart/drain budget arithmetic across four supervisors (systemd, launchd, s6, external).
- **Key abstractions:** `GATEWAY_SERVICE_RESTART_EXIT_CODE = 75` (EX_TEMPFAIL) and `GATEWAY_FATAL_CONFIG_EXIT_CODE = 78` (EX_CONFIG) (restart.py:13, restart.py:17). `map_fatal_config_exit_for_launchd` (restart.py:20) maps 78→0. `read_launchd_exit_timeout_s` (restart.py:123) / `parse_launchd_exit_timeout` (restart.py:84) read the live `launchctl print` value. `resolve_launchd_capped_drain` (restart.py:163), `effective_stop_drain_timeout` (restart.py:188), `effective_stop_watchdog_delay` (restart.py:204). `resolve_cron_drain_budget` (restart.py:324), `resolve_systemd_timeout_stop_sec` (restart.py:342), `resolve_restart_exit_wait_budget` (restart.py:359). `is_gateway_supervisor_process` (restart.py:242), `is_supervised_gateway_launch` (restart.py:259), `is_global_startup_conflict` (restart.py:226), `is_container_restart_context` (restart.py:274). The `parse_*_timeout` family (restart.py:288-321).
- **Data flow:** config values + supervisor facts → `parse_*` (normalization) → `resolve_*` (budget arithmetic) → the stop path's drain/watchdog numbers → systemd `TimeoutStopSec` / launchd `ExitTimeOut` sizing.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `0` is a **deliberate disable** in `_parse_timeout_keeping_zero` (restart.py:288) and must not fall through to the default — hence a separate parser per setting rather than one `or default` idiom. The launchd GUI domain **clamps** `ExitTimeOut` (measured: plist 215 → live 60), so the gateway can only *read* the live value and fit its SIGTERM stop inside it (restart.py:66-73) — draining past it is not "a longer drain" but a mid-SQLite-write SIGKILL. `resolve_cron_drain_budget` **only extends** the chat drain, never shortens it, and is capped at the watchdog leash minus a cleanup reserve (restart.py:324-340).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `read_launchd_exit_timeout_s` returns `None` for "no launchd budget applies" and callers must treat `None` as **fail-open** (the configured drain stands). `effective_stop_drain_timeout` / `effective_stop_watchdog_delay` are duck-typed and `getattr`-guarded on purpose, because shutdown-path tests drive `_stop_impl` from bare doubles that are not `GatewayRunner` instances (restart.py:188-201). `resolve_restart_exit_wait_budget` returns `math.inf` for a non-finite drain ("wait indefinitely", not a crash in the integer envelope) (restart.py:359-366).
- **Surprising:** `is_gateway_supervisor_process` (restart.py:242) deliberately does **not** read the generalized `HERMES_SUPERVISED_CHILD` launcher marker, because the Windows Scheduled-Task launcher sets it without a restart policy and routing its `/restart` through exit 75 would leave the gateway dead — but `is_supervised_gateway_launch` (restart.py:259) *does* read it, because that is the identity the self-targeting guards key on (a kill issued from inside such a gateway takes down the process hosting the caller). `launchd_job_label` (restart.py:92) reads two env vars (`XPC_SERVICE_NAME` and the wrapper-forwarded `HERMES_LAUNCHD_LABEL`) through one `ai.hermes` predicate, because app-coalition labels (`application.<bundle>…`) report `exit timeout = 1` and would cap the drain to 0.

### `gateway/restart_loop_guard.py` — the auto-resume restart-loop breaker: `check_and_record` (restart_loop_guard.py:101) records a restart-interrupted boot in `<HERMES_HOME>/gateway/restart_loop.json` and returns True once a **chained** boot count reaches `max_restarts` (3), at which point auto-resume is skipped; the chain is built on the inter-boot gap (`DEFAULT_MAX_GAP_SECONDS = 300`) rather than a fixed window so a slow loop cannot drop its history every boot, and every I/O failure fails **open** (`_load_boots` returns `[]` on any error) so a broken state file never wedges the gateway.

### `gateway/rich_sent_store.py` — a local `(chat_id, message_id) → {text, media}` index at `<HERMES_HOME>/state/rich_sent_index.json`, because Telegram does not echo a rich message's content back in `reply_to_message` and WhatsApp quotes carry only a quoted id or a thumbnail stub, so a reply to something we sent arrives with no quotable text; pins the `_LOCK` (rich_sent_store.py:29) because `atomic_json_write` makes each *write* atomic, not the load/merge/save triple — two concurrent callers would each load the same pre-state and the later `os.replace` would drop the other key.

### `gateway/run.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway process facade: config→env bridging, the `GatewayRunner` controller (run.py:3341), the platform adapter registry glue, housekeeping threads, and the CLI `main()` (run.py:5111).
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayRunner` (run.py:3341) is a **20-mixin MRO composition** (`GatewayAuthorizationMixin, GatewayKanbanWatchersMixin, … GatewayAgentCacheMixin, GatewayRuntimeInitMixin, GatewayProfileReconcileMixin, GatewayPluginRewireMixin`) — the file is the facade, the behaviour lives in the `gateway/run_*.py` mixins. Module-level helpers: `_resolve_runtime_agent_kwargs` (run.py:2301), `_resolve_gateway_model_context` (run.py:2364) returning the frozen `_GatewayModelContext` (run.py:2354), `_load_bridge_config` (run.py:2118), `_bridge_config_to_env` (run.py:2073), `_resolve_gateway_model` (run.py:2900), `_dispose_unused_adapter` (run.py:3210), `_reconnect_backoff` (run.py:3239), `_own_policy_open_startup_violation` (run.py:2239), `_best_effort` (run.py:2224). `MultiplexConfigError` (run.py:1641) and `HygieneTurnHoldExceeded` (run.py:1646). Session-state plumbing: `_session_state` (run.py:3412), `_peek_session_state` (run.py:3419), `legacy_dict_property` views over `self._sessions` (run.py:3390-3412). Hygiene: `_hygiene_cooldown_for_failure` (run.py:122), `run_codex_hygiene_compaction` (run.py:223), `hygiene_compaction_recovered` (run.py:178). Error classification regexes: `_TELEGRAM_NOISY_STATUS_RE` (run.py:89), `_GATEWAY_AUTH_ERROR_RE` (run.py:410), `_GATEWAY_RATE_LIMIT_RE` (run.py:416), `_GATEWAY_CONNECTION_ERROR_RE` (run.py:437), `_GATEWAY_ENDPOINT_UNREACHABLE_RE` (run.py:454).
- **Data flow:** `main()` → `_guard_corrupt_user_config` (run.py:5098) → identity/watchdog/stdio steps → `start_gateway` (run.py:5091) → `gateway.run_bootstrap.start_gateway` → `GatewayRunner(config)` → adapters → `runner.start()` → housekeeping + cron threads → `wait_gateway_runtime` → `runner.stop()` → `_start_gateway_shutdown_tail` → `_exit_after_graceful_shutdown` (run.py:5198). Config → env bridging happens at **import** time (run.py:2126-2160) so every child process inherits the same credentials; the runner's per-turn model route is resolved by `_resolve_gateway_model_context` (run.py:2364) from config + runtime + custom providers.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The whole file is written as a *registry of past defects*, each with an issue number, so the next reader sees why a branch exists. Examples: the hygiene failure streak lives in SQLite keyed by the rotation-stable `session_key` because the in-agent equivalent (`ContextCompressor.record_timeout_failure`) is **structurally always 0** from the gateway — hygiene builds a fresh `AIAgent` per run and re-binds state (run.py:122-158). `hygiene_compaction_recovered` (run.py:178) requires `rotated or in_place` *and* `compression_made_progress`, because the degenerate path reuses the pre-compression counts and would read a no-op as success. The codex app-server hygiene path compacts the **live** agent via `thread/compact/start` and never evicts it — evicting destroys the only real context, leaving the next turn on an empty thread while Hermes still mirrors a full history (run.py:223-286). `_CONVERSATION_SCOPED_STATE` (run.py:2284) replaced four hand-copied per-boundary pop-lists that drifted (run.py:2270-2284).
