- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_exit_after_graceful_shutdown` (run.py:5198) uses `os._exit` (not `sys.exit`) because `Py_FinalizeEx` joins every non-daemon thread — a wedged worker would strand the gateway half-shut-down — and it therefore releases the PID file and runtime lock **explicitly** (bypassing `atexit`) and marks the lifecycle ledger `clean` so the next boot's unclean-death detector fires only for a genuine SIGKILL/OOM (run.py:5210-5240). The `GatewayRunner._session_db` property (run.py:3530) resolves the `AsyncSessionDB` **per access** through a per-path cache and *borrows* the `SessionStore`'s handle, because two writer connections to one `state.db` doubled the fd budget for nothing and doubled again per profile on a multiplexed gateway (run.py:3454-3505). `_ensure_windows_gateway_venv_imports` (run.py:422) skips a venv built for a different Python version — its compiled modules crash this one.
- **Surprising:** The runner is registered into a module-global **weakref** (`_gateway_runner_ref = _weakref.ref(self)`, run.py:3470) which `gateway/relay/egress.py` reaches into to ask the live adapter which platforms it fronts — a deliberate cross-module coupling through a weakref rather than an import. `_ADAPTER_DISCONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECS_DEFAULT` and friends are imported *back* out of `gateway.run` by `gateway/run_adapters.py` and `gateway/relay/ws_transport.py` to keep the env-var override in one place.

### `gateway/run_active_work_verb.py` — the `active-work` control verb (run_active_work_verb.py:52) answers "what would a stop/restart of this gateway interrupt?" from the **running process** rather than from `gateway_state.json`, which names each unit only while draining and omits async delegations; pins that the read happens on the gateway event loop via `loop.call_soon_threadsafe` (run_active_work_verb.py:66) because a read on the socket executor thread can race a worker being added and an error there reads as "no work".

### `gateway/run_adapters.py`
- **Purpose:** The `GatewayAdapterLifecycleMixin` — adapter connect/teardown, fatal-error recovery, the reconnect watcher, and multiplexed per-profile adapters.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayAdapterLifecycleMixin` (run_adapters.py:70). `_wait_or_detach` (run_adapters.py:74) — waits on a task and *detaches* it on deadline, explicitly not `asyncio.wait_for` (which waits for the cancelled child, so a `connect()` that swallows `CancelledError` blocks recovery forever). `_connect_adapter_bounded` (run_adapters.py:201) / `_connect_adapter_with_timeout` (run_adapters.py:180). `_platform_connect_timeout_secs` (run_adapters.py:161) — Telegram's 180 s is *not* spent at cold start (45 s initial cap) because the initial connect gates `running` for every platform. `_handle_adapter_fatal_error_detached` (run_adapters.py:296) / `_handle_adapter_fatal_error_impl` (run_adapters.py:365). `_queue_retryable_fatal_platform` (run_adapters.py:252) — must not await; it runs *before* any disconnect. `_spawn_supervised` (run_adapters.py:456) — supervised long-lived task with capped respawn. `_reconnect_failed_platform` (run_adapters.py:794). `_install_reconnected_adapter` (run_adapters.py:908). Multiplex: `_start_secondary_profile_adapters` (run_adapters.py:968), `_start_one_profile_adapters` (run_adapters.py:1252), `_primary_resource_claims` (run_adapters.py:1030), `_refuse_duplicate_claim` (run_adapters.py:1165), `_adapter_credential_fingerprint` (run_adapters.py:1881), `_adapter_listener_claim` (run_adapters.py:1868). Scoping: `_canonicalize` (run_adapters.py:1689), `_make_profile_message_handler` (run_adapters.py:1710), `_make_default_profile_message_handler` (run_adapters.py:1745), `_make_adapter_auth_check` (run_adapters.py:1928). `UNRESOLVED_PROFILE_HOME` (run_adapters.py:62) — a sentinel that is deliberately *not* `None`.
- **Data flow:** platform config → `_instantiate_adapter` (run_adapters.py:1908) → `_create_adapter` (run_adapters.py:1900) → `_connect_adapter_with_timeout` → `self.adapters[platform]`; a runtime fatal → `_handle_adapter_fatal_error` → queue entry in `_failed_platforms` (with `credential_claim`/`listener_claim`/`predecessor`) → the reconnect watcher ticks → `_reconnect_failed_platform` → `_install_reconnected_adapter` (which hands over the predecessor's held inbound and dedup caches).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A retryable platform is queued **before** the disconnect await, because a wedged `close()` (or one that swallows `CancelledError`) previously left platforms permanently deaf inside a live process even after the network recovered (run_adapters.py:390-402). `_queue_retryable_fatal_platform` also calls `_ensure_reconnect_watcher_running()` when the platform is **already** queued (run_adapters.py:265-281) — that is precisely the state in which the watcher has had time to die from budget exhaustion, and without the backstop the result is a silent permanent outage that the stranded check treats as safe. `on_all_adapters_down` (run_adapters.py:288) adds a `stay_alive` policy because the desktop app's direct `hermes serve` child has no supervisor, so a failure exit only severs the UI's websockets (run_adapters.py:325-350). `UNRESOLVED_PROFILE_HOME` exists because overloading `None` for both "this is the launch profile's own work" and "this named profile's home does not resolve" let an inbound message on a secondary's bot run with the **launch profile's** `.env` (run_adapters.py:56-64).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A failed reconnect that never installs the adapter is explicitly disposed (`_dispose_unused_adapter`) because its `__init__`-opened resources (e.g. `ResponseStore`, 2 fds each) leak and hit the 2560-fd limit after ~12 h of failed reconnects at the 300 s backoff cap (run_adapters.py:845-878). A platform with no bot credential is dropped from the queue rather than retried forever (run_adapters.py:808-812). `_connect_adapter_with_timeout` forwards `is_reconnect` to `adapter.connect()` so a cold boot drops the stale server-side queue while a watcher reconnect preserves it, so messages sent during the outage are delivered rather than silently dropped (run_adapters.py:180-199). `_make_adapter_auth_check` (run_adapters.py:1928) re-enters the owning profile's scope per call because a secondary-owned bot's callback fires off the adapter's event loop, outside the runtime scope; the gate's scoped env read would otherwise fall back to `os.environ` (the default profile's env) and deny the secondary's own allowlisted callers.
- **Surprising:** `_install_reconnected_adapter` (run_adapters.py:908) does **not** stamp "connected" unconditionally: Telegram's degraded reconnect returns `True` from `connect()` so the gateway stays up while its own ladder retries, and stamping "connected" would undo the adapter's accurate status. The duplicate-credential refusal at run_adapters.py:1165 does **not** disconnect the refused adapter — it never connected, and for a same-credential Photon adapter `disconnect()` would shut down the primary profile's live sidecar.

### `gateway/run_agent_cache.py`
- **Purpose:** The `GatewayAgentCacheMixin` — the per-session `AIAgent` cache, session model overrides, turn leases, run generations, prompt pinning, and the three eviction strategies (LRU cap, idle TTL, RSS pressure).
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayAgentCacheMixin` (run_agent_cache.py:48). `_agent_config_signature` (run_agent_cache.py:101) — the 16-hex-char cache key. `_extract_cache_busting_config` (run_agent_cache.py:50) — flat `section.key` map of values that must rebuild the cached agent, including the **live tool registry generation** (MCP reloads mutate the registry without touching `config.yaml`). `_rehydrate_session_model_override` (run_agent_cache.py:148), `_apply_session_model_override` (run_agent_cache.py:198), `_claim_one_turn_restore` (run_agent_cache.py:229), `_restore_session_model_override` (run_agent_cache.py:239), `_fallback_baseline_model` (run_agent_cache.py:260). Turn lifecycle: `_release_running_agent_state` (run_agent_cache.py:296), `_drop_turn_slot` (run_agent_cache.py:328), `_held_turn_lease` (run_agent_cache.py:356), `_release_turn_lease` (run_agent_cache.py:366), `_rebind_turn_lease` (run_agent_cache.py:381), `_begin_session_run_generation` (run_agent_cache.py:457), `_invalidate_session_run_generation` (run_agent_cache.py:467). Prompt pins: `_pinned_session_context_prompt` (run_agent_cache.py:719), `_pinned_channel_inputs` (run_agent_cache.py:749), `_ephemeral_change_key` (run_agent_cache.py:777). Eviction: `_evict_cached_agent` (run_agent_cache.py:822), `_sweep_agent_cache_under_pressure` (run_agent_cache.py:997), `_release_pressure_batch` (run_agent_cache.py:1072), `_enforce_agent_cache_cap` (run_agent_cache.py:1088), `_sweep_idle_cached_agents` (run_agent_cache.py:1123).
- **Data flow:** config + runtime credentials + enabled toolsets + ephemeral prompt + user identity → `_agent_config_signature` → the `_agent_cache` OrderedMap keyed by session key; each turn reuses the cached `AIAgent` (frozen prompt + schemas → prompt-cache hits) or rebuilds it when the signature changes; eviction paths pop the entry, then release resources on a **daemon thread** under the owning profile's scope.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `user_id`/`user_id_alt` participate in the cache key (run_agent_cache.py:101-140) because memory providers (Honcho) freeze the peer identity at first-message init; without them a shared-thread session key reused one cached agent across distinct users and cross-attributed messages (#27371) — trading prompt-cache warmth for correct attribution. The pin mechanism (`_pinned_session_context_prompt`, run_agent_cache.py:719) exists because the rendered session-context bytes are *nondeterministic*; the key is a hash of exactly the rendered inputs and the invariant is that **any** input whose change alters the bytes MUST appear in it (run_agent_cache.py:777-780) — an omission means a stale pinned prompt, an extra only re-renders. An **internal** event (kanban wake, delegation completion) reuses an existing pin verbatim, because re-rendering from a rebuilt source re-keyed the pin and the next human turn re-keyed it back, rewriting already-sent system bytes each time (A→B→A).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_evict_cached_agent` refuses to tear down an agent that is mid-turn (its client/sandbox/child subagents are in use) and defers it to `state.turn.retired_agents` when `release_after_turn` is set (run_agent_cache.py:845-855). `_drop_turn_slot` does **not** clear `_interrupt_requested` — only the turn finalizer does, so the flag cannot silently kill the session's *next* message — and it sweeps lease tokens from generations older than the current one, because a hung evicted turn's finalizer may never run and would otherwise pin its `_SessionLease` forever (run_agent_cache.py:328-355). `_run_release_in_profile_scope` (run_agent_cache.py:893) re-enters the **owning** profile's scope in the release thread, because threads start with an empty context and `on_session_end` reads credentials/home at call time. `_refresh_agent_cache_message_count` (run_agent_cache.py:606) re-baselines the stored `message_count` after each turn, or the coherence guard would rebuild every turn and destroy prompt caching. `_sweep_agent_cache_under_pressure` (run_agent_cache.py:997) never evicts mid-turn agents or transcripts not yet on disk, and logs a distinct warning when the pressure valve cannot act because transcripts are not reaching the session DB.
- **Surprising:** `_release_evicted_agent_soft` (run_agent_cache.py:947) deliberately **preserves** terminal sandbox / browser / background processes (the session may resume) and only frees the LLM client pool and `_session_messages`; a plain eviction is *not* a session boundary — `_evict_cached_agent_at_boundary` (run_agent_cache.py:872) is, and it commits memory first (`on_session_end`). `_agent_cache_bounds` (run_agent_cache.py:969) resolves from an **empty** config rather than the dataclass default on a config-read failure, because the default has `memory_high_mb=None` (pressure pass off) but an absent section means "auto" — a transient read failure must not permanently disable the OOM valve.

### `gateway/run_api.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway's local HTTP/WS listener: the native session WebSocket plus the lazily-mounted dashboard API.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayAPIHandle` (run_api.py:30), `GatewayDashboardMount` (run_api.py:38) — an ASGI shim that imports and mounts `hermes_cli.web_server.app` on first use, `start_gateway_api` (run_api.py:138), `warm_gateway_dashboard` (run_api.py:207), `stop_gateway_api` (run_api.py:226), `GatewayRuntimeAPI` (run_api.py:246) — the ticket-redeeming wrapper on the `/api/ws` subprotocol boundary.
- **Data flow:** `start_gateway_api` → `build_listener_server(mount, host, port, lifespan="off")` → the dashboard is imported **off the loop** on first request (or warmed after READY) → `GatewayRuntimeAPI` intercepts `/api/ws` upgrades → `_gateway_ws_ticket_from_subprotocol` → `session_ticket_store.redeem(ticket, purpose="interactive")` (or `"worker-adoption"`) → `authority_for_profile_id` → `handle_ws(ws, auth_identity={…,"native_bootstrap":True}, subprotocol=CANONICAL_GATEWAY_PROTOCOL, operator=operator)`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The dashboard is **not** imported before the listener binds: `hermes_cli.web_server` is ~690 routes / ~0.6 s, and importing it before binding stalled every cold start and the control socket's identify (run_api.py:3-12). `withhold_session_token = True` is set on mount (run_api.py:70-74) because the SPA session token is a bearer credential any loopback peer could read from `GET /`, so the gateway-hosted listener never publishes it. A gated listener (non-loopback bind or `dashboard.public_url`) *does* mount eagerly, because it needs the dashboard's auth providers and refuses to start without one (run_api.py:186-188). The module owns **only** HTTP resources — it does not bootstrap an agent, scheduler, hosted room, or a second gateway (run_api.py:6-9).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A second listener in the same process is refused: `start_gateway_api` raises if `_ACTIVE_RUNNER` is set or the already-imported dashboard app already has a `gateway_runner` (run_api.py:150-153). The mount is idempotent under an `asyncio.Lock` and re-checks `self._closed` after the off-loop import and after the lifespan `__aenter__` (run_api.py:98-125) so a listener that stopped mid-import does not leave a half-mounted app. If the dashboard fails to load, the mount answers `500 {"error": "dashboard_unavailable"}` for HTTP and closes a WS with code 1011 (run_api.py:128-136). `GatewayRuntimeAPI.__call__` logs a refused upgrade with the cause **before** uvicorn's bare 500, because the dialling cron/managed worker would otherwise see an opaque `InvalidStatus` (#139922) — but only if nothing was sent yet (run_api.py:265-281).
- **Surprising:** The WS gate has three separate refusals: a not-ready/draining gateway closes with **1013** (run_api.py:292-300); a socket admitted *into* the restart wait (to answer a parked approval/clarify) outlives the wait, while one opened before it is cut, because a held socket (a kanban worker polling its card) would otherwise pin the drain to its cap (run_api.py:302-315); and an unauthorized ticket closes with **4403** (run_api.py:333-338). `stop_gateway_api` (run_api.py:226) uses `asyncio.shield(asyncio.gather(...))` so cleanup still reaches adapter/worker settlement even when the listener task raised.

### `gateway/run_bootstrap.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway's process startup phases, split out of the `gateway.run` facade: PID/host claim, control socket, cron+housekeeping threads, and the shutdown tail.
- **Key abstractions:** `start_gateway` (run_bootstrap.py:728) — the real phase sequence. `_start_gateway_replace_existing_instance` (run_bootstrap.py:30), `_start_gateway_configure_logging` (run_bootstrap.py:137), `_start_gateway_make_shutdown_signal_handler` (run_bootstrap.py:174), `_start_gateway_claim_pid_file` (run_bootstrap.py:242), `_start_gateway_start_control_socket` (run_bootstrap.py:271), `_start_gateway_start_cron_and_housekeeping` (run_bootstrap.py:380), `_start_gateway_shutdown_tail` (run_bootstrap.py:459), `_launch_home_may_multiplex` (run_bootstrap.py:511), `_reserve_waiting_out_browse_reconcile` (run_bootstrap.py:594), `_start_gateway_reserve_profiles` (run_bootstrap.py:622), `_start_gateway_install_signal_handlers` (run_bootstrap.py:652), `_start_gateway_run_runner` (run_bootstrap.py:668), `_start_deferred_state_db_integrity_check` (run_bootstrap.py:13).
- **Data flow:** `start_gateway` → resolve terminal cwd → `apply_nofile_soft_limit` → `record_boot_fingerprint` → config verdicts → `_host_attach_or_none` → duplicate-PID check → `_start_gateway_reserve_profiles` → `_start_gateway_claim_pid_file` → logging → `GatewayRunner(resolved_config)` → `initialize_gateway_runtime` → signal handlers → planned-stop watcher thread → control socket (fatal if absent) → `start_gateway_runtime_api` → `runner.start()` → cron+housekeeping → `publish_gateway_runtime_ready` → `wait_gateway_runtime` → `runner.stop()` → `_start_gateway_shutdown_tail`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The **config verdict precedes the duplicate-instance guard** — `--replace` must not stop a healthy gateway for a launch that cannot start (run_bootstrap.py:756-762). The PID file is claimed *before* adapters, so of two concurrent `run --replace` only the O_EXCL winner opens sockets; and `--force` (not `--replace`) is the only thing that skips the host-lock refusal (run_bootstrap.py:776-782). The control socket is **fatal**: local clients attach only with tickets minted on that listener, so startup aborts without it (run_bootstrap.py:861-864). A planned-stop **marker-polling thread** runs on every platform because Windows has no `add_signal_handler` and `hermes gateway stop`'s SIGTERM (mapped to `TerminateProcess`) would never invoke the handler — the drain loop would never run, `mark_resume_pending` would never fire, and sessions would be silently lost across restarts (#33778) (run_bootstrap.py:840-856). The state-db integrity scan is deferred to a daemon thread because running it on the loop held every local listener open and unanswered for the whole `quick_check` (minutes on a multi-GB `state.db`), so local clients hung instead of being refused (run_bootstrap.py:13-28).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_start_gateway_replace_existing_instance` refuses to signal a process it cannot prove belongs to this profile (a poisoned PID record would create a cross-profile restart loop) and stamps `startup_failed` **on disk** with a bounded 5 s wait, because every CLI exit funnels through `os._exit()` and a fire-and-forget stamp can die in the queued writer, leaving the loop this refusal exists to break running (run_bootstrap.py:52-84). It escalates SIGTERM → SIGKILL → confirm-dead before clearing PID/locks, or two gateways would share a token (run_bootstrap.py:104-127). `_reserve_waiting_out_browse_reconcile` (run_bootstrap.py:594) waits out a *browse-reconcile* holder (a short schema catch-up) but refuses at once against a positively-identified real owner, and the flock exclusion is never weakened. `_start_gateway_reserve_profiles` (run_bootstrap.py:622) raises `SystemExit(78)` (EX_CONFIG) when the profile is already served by a live multiplexer — a configuration verdict, not a transient fault — so systemd's `RestartPreventExitStatus=78` parks the unit instead of restart-looping. The shutdown tail (run_bootstrap.py:459) never `join()`s the cron ticker: an in-flight cron delivery is a coroutine on *this* loop, and a synchronous join would block the loop so the delivery could never run (#58818).
- **Surprising:** The cron ticker is **not** gated on `gateway.multiplex_profiles` — one gateway process per host owns every profile's cron store, and gating the tick set on that flag left every non-launch profile's jobs in a store no ticker visited, so they silently never fired (run_bootstrap.py:384-391). `_launch_home_may_multiplex` (run_bootstrap.py:511) distinguishes an *explicit* `multiplex_profiles: true` on a named launch home (→ EX_CONFIG exit 78) from the *implicit* default, which is downgraded in place: the process comes up standalone, says why, and the config is flipped so the boot reserves and serves only the launch profile.

### `gateway/run_busy.py`
*(manifest path `source/gateway/run_busy.py`; actual repo path `gateway/run_busy.py` — the `source/` prefix does not exist in this checkout. Read head 1–500, mid 655–1154, tail 1339–1638, plus gap windows 500–660 and 1150–1345.)*

- **Purpose:** Owns what happens when a message arrives while a session's agent is already running — queue/steer/interrupt/redirect resolution, the busy ack, the mid-run slash-command dispatch tables, and the destructive-slash confirmation gate.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayBusySessionMixin` (gateway/run_busy.py:104) — bound onto `GatewayRunner` via the MRO; every `gateway.run` import is lazy inside method bodies so `patch("gateway.run.X")` still intercepts (gateway/run_busy.py:1-6).
  - `approval_input_words(input_key)` (gateway/run_busy.py:33) — English approval synonyms ∪ the active language's `approval.inputs.*` list, deduped, English first.
  - `BUSY_ACCEPTED` contextvar (gateway/run_busy.py:54) + `_busy_accepted()` (gateway/run_busy.py:57) — the local session's `pre_command` hook is awaited only once a mid-run command has *accepted* it, so a refused command fires nothing.
  - `GatewayBusySessionMixin._resolve_busy_steer_or_redirect` (gateway/run_busy.py:663) returns `GatewayRunner._BusySteerOutcome` — a forward reference to the class the mixin will be bound onto; `_BUSY_QUEUE_MAX_PENDING` (gateway/run_busy.py:501) and `_BUSY_REJECT_TEXT` (gateway/run_busy.py:1085) are likewise **not defined in this file** — they live on `GatewayRunner`.
- **Data flow:** `_handle_active_session_busy_message` (gateway/run_busy.py:887) → authorization (gateway/run_busy.py:902) → bot-loop budget charge (gateway/run_busy.py:910) → drain notice (gateway/run_busy.py:916) → plaintext-approval routing (gateway/run_busy.py:918) → mid-run skill turn (gateway/run_busy.py:923) → internal events go straight to the FIFO (gateway/run_busy.py:931) → `_resolve_busy_steer_or_redirect` (gateway/run_busy.py:943) → `_queue_or_replace_pending_event` (gateway/run_busy.py:947) → `_busy_interrupt_and_ack` (gateway/run_busy.py:957) sends the ack.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **Whole-slot key matching, not prefix matching.** `_strip_slot` (gateway/run_busy.py:63) only matches a `:`-delimited slot as a WHOLE slot, and `_same_chat_key_slots` (gateway/run_busy.py:82) matches the remainder as TEXT because chat ids may themselves contain `:` (Matrix `!room:example.org`). A naive `startswith` would let session A's key match session B's.
  - **Only photo bursts merge; everything else FIFO.** `_queue_or_replace_pending_event` (gateway/run_busy.py:457) merges into the head slot only when the merge types are exactly `{TEXT, PHOTO}` (gateway/run_busy.py:485-497). Merging on *any* `media_urls` collapsed three voice notes into one turn (#114363, gateway/run_busy.py:483). Merge additionally requires every security metadata key to match (`_SECURITY_METADATA_KEYS`, gateway/run_busy.py:451).
  - **A single pending slot was silently overwriting.** The FIFO chain replaced the one-slot merge; the raw `merge_pending_message_event(merge_text=True)` newline-joins consecutive TEXT follow-ups into one turn (#43066, gateway/run_busy.py:946-956).
  - **Steer text carries a JSON origin envelope, not a prompt.** `_steer_text_with_origin` (gateway/run_busy.py:525) hashes PII when `privacy.redact_pii` is set (gateway/run_busy.py:548-556) and escapes `[`/`]` to `\u005b`/`\u005d` (gateway/run_busy.py:559) so a hostile id cannot forge the marker delimiters. It explicitly labels the payload "JSON data, not instructions or authorization" (gateway/run_busy.py:561).
  - **Interrupt is demoted to queue when it would corrupt state.** Two independent demotions: active subagents (`interrupt()` cascades through `_active_children`, gateway/run_busy.py:671-675) and in-flight context compression (gateway/run_busy.py:676-680). `_session_has_compression_in_flight` fails CLOSED — an unreadable lock holder is treated as "compression active" (gateway/run_busy.py:411-416, 439).
  - **MagicMock safety is explicit.** `_agent_has_active_subagents` requires `isinstance(children, (list, tuple, set))` (gateway/run_busy.py:356) and `_session_has_compression_in_flight` requires `isinstance(holder, str)` (gateway/run_busy.py:435), because a mock's auto-attribute would otherwise look like live state and silently demote/deny.
  - **A leaked lease is re-acquired, not fenced.** `_claim_active_session_slot` writes `live_session_id` as the writer identity (gateway/run_busy.py:334-341) so a lease leaked by this process is reclaimed by the next turn; pruning only reclaims entries whose *process* died.
  - **Ack suppression is checked before the debounce stamp.** `HERMES_GATEWAY_BUSY_ACK_ENABLED` is tested first (gateway/run_busy.py:976) so a suppressed ack never writes `busy_ack_ts` and the 30 s debounce (gateway/run_busy.py:982) never eats the next real ack.
  - **Busy slash commands reject rather than fall through.** `_busy_slash_route` (gateway/run_busy.py:1074) tries `special` → `plain` → `reject`; falling through would interrupt the agent *and* be swallowed by the slash-command safety net, producing a zero-char response (#5057/#6252/#10370, gateway/run_busy.py:1051-1063).
  - **The destructive confirm is registered before the buttons render.** `_request_slash_confirm` registers the confirm id first (gateway/run_busy.py:1589-1590) so a fast click cannot race the `send_slash_confirm` return; an egress *decline* suppresses the text fallback and tears down the registration (gateway/run_busy.py:1615-1625) — otherwise the fallback would deliver the very content the connector refused, to the same chat.
  - **"always" only claims an opt-out that was actually written.** `save_config_value` swallows its own errors, so the boolean return decides the note wording (gateway/run_busy.py:1554-1570, `_destructive_optout_note` at 1540).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - `_promote_queued_event` (gateway/run_busy.py:128) refuses to drop the overflow head when no adapter is present ("leave the head in place so we don't silently drop it", gateway/run_busy.py:143).
  - `_rescue_orphaned_overflow` (gateway/run_busy.py:223) pops the oldest orphan but *keeps the slot occupied* with the next one, preserving the arrival-order invariant; it returns the event already removed from both stores so the post-turn dequeue cannot run it twice (gateway/run_busy.py:230-231).
  - `_interrupt_running_agent_for_busy_event` swallows every exception (gateway/run_busy.py:784-785) so a failed interrupt never blocks the ack.
  - `_is_stale_restart_redelivery` (gateway/run_busy.py:1424): a missing dedup marker + `_booted_from_restart` + <60 s since start ⇒ one-shot suppress (gateway/run_busy.py:1446-1455); otherwise an old marker older than 300 s is ignored so a fresh `/restart` is never swallowed (gateway/run_busy.py:1475-1477).
  - `/stop` scoping is three-tiered: `_same_chat_runs` (gateway/run_busy.py:1358) → `_sibling_thread_run_keys` (gateway/run_busy.py:1386) → `_chat_scoped_run_keys` (gateway/run_busy.py:1402); a stop from inside a thread reaches only runs whose own thread slot matches, or runs with no thread slot (the rolling-DM shape).
- **Surprising:**
  - `_queue_during_drain_enabled` (gateway/run_busy.py:107) only queues across a *restart* when the mode is `queue`/`steer` — `interrupt` drops the message, so a restart in interrupt mode loses the user's follow-up.
  - `_busy_reply_to` (gateway/run_busy.py:568) anchors a Telegram DM topic reply to the thread but returns `None` for other Telegram threads; for an internal wake it returns `None` at all, because an internal event's `message_id` is its *producer identity*, not a platform message id (#139611, gateway/run_busy.py:571-573).
  - `_steer_running_agent` returns `bool(self._steer_active_subagents(...)) or accepted` (gateway/run_busy.py:397) — the subagent fan-out result is ORed in *first*, so a parent that refused the steer still reports success if a child accepted it.

### `gateway/run_common.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_common.py`.) — Leaf constants shared by `gateway/run.py` and its `run_*` mixins; holds only `_UNSET` (gateway/run_common.py:8), a sentinel that distinguishes "caller passed no metadata" from "caller passed None" for default-argument values, and imports nothing from `gateway.run` so the value resolves at `def` time without an import cycle (gateway/run_common.py:1-5).

### `gateway/run_config_loaders.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_config_loaders.py`.)*

- **Purpose:** `GatewayConfigLoadersMixin` — every config.yaml/env read the gateway runner performs (busy modes, reasoning effort, service tier, timeouts, human delay, fallback chain, provider routing).
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayConfigLoadersMixin` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:45) — bound onto `GatewayRunner` via the MRO; `gateway.run` internals are imported lazily *inside method bodies* precisely so `patch("gateway.run.X")` keeps intercepting them at call time (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:1-6).
  - `_cfg_str` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:49) / `_env_or_cfg_str` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:55) — the canonical "env first, then config" pair.
  - `_load_prefill_messages` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:60), `_resolve_model_for_channel` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:111), `_get_system_prompt_for_channel` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:129).
  - `_resolve_session_reasoning_config` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:177) / `_set_session_reasoning_override` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:197); `_resolve_session_service_tier` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:207) / `_set_session_service_tier_override` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:223).
  - `_load_busy_input_mode` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:247), `_load_busy_text_mode` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:253), `_busy_modes_from_config` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:266), `_busy_text_timing_from_config` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:280), `_human_delay_from_config` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:303), `_snapshot_profile_busy_modes` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:333), `_effective_busy_mode` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:358).
  - `_load_launchd_exit_timeout` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:393), `_load_env_or_agent_cfg_timeout` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:426), `_load_background_notifications_mode` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:480), `_load_fallback_model` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:510), `_refresh_fallback_model` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:519), `_apply_fallback_chain_to_agent` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:559).
- **Data flow:** config.yaml / env → per-profile snapshot (`_snapshot_profile_busy_modes`, gateway/run_config_loaders.py:333) → per-source resolution (`_effective_busy_mode`, gateway/run_config_loaders.py:358) → the busy handler in `run_busy.py`; fallback chain → disk re-read per agent create/reuse → `agent._fallback_chain` / `_fallback_model` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:576-577).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **`fail_closed=True` on the fallback refresh.** `_refresh_fallback_model` calls `load_user_config_effective(cfg_path, fail_closed=True)` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:550) so a torn mid-edit write *raises* instead of returning an empty fail-open result that would WIPE the last-known-good chain (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:548-553). The fallback loader itself is fail-open (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:512-517) — the two reads are deliberately asymmetric.
  - **One last-known-good chain per HOME, not one per runner.** `self._fallback_model_by_home` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:538-541): a single runner-wide slot would hand every secondary profile the default profile's chain (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:532-533).
  - **Service tier uses a sentinel, not `None`.** `service_tier_override is not _SERVICE_TIER_UNSET` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:215) — presence, not truthiness, decides, because "priority" and `None` (explicit normal) are both real overrides (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:209-211).
  - **Per-session field writes, never a lazy dict init.** `_set_session_reasoning_override` writes a field on the session state; the old lazy `self._session_reasoning_overrides = {}` replaced the WHOLE dict and raced concurrent sessions (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:201-205).
  - **The launchd budget outranks the configured drain.** `_load_launchd_exit_timeout` reads the live `ExitTimeOut` and caps the drain, because the per-user launchd domain clamps it (measured 60 s on macOS 26) and a stop that drains past it is SIGKILLed mid-teardown — the unclean-exit half of the state.db corruption class (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:396-401). A misconfigured drain is warned about **at boot**, not at the next SIGKILL (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:412-417).
  - **The env override reads through the secret scope.** `_load_background_notifications_mode` uses `platform_gate_env` rather than `os.environ` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:486-487) so a served secondary profile sees *its own* `.env` value, not the launch profile's.
  - **Legacy `busy_text_mode` is honored only when explicitly set.** Otherwise it is derived from `busy_input_mode` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:253-263) so existing queue setups keep working.
  - **A cooldown owns the fallback lifecycle.** `_apply_fallback_chain_to_agent` skips the rewrite while the agent is held on an activated fallback provider under a rate-limit cooldown (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:572-574) — `restore_primary_runtime` owns that lifecycle.
  - **The unavailability memo is cleared only on a real content change** (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:584-587), preserving the per-message no-op refresh's rate-limiting benefit.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - `_busy_text_timing_from_config` rejects negatives *and* NaN (`value != value`, gateway/run_config_loaders.py:294) and warns naming the key rather than silently coercing (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:297).
  - `_human_delay_from_config` rejects non-integer, negative, or inverted bounds and falls back to the natural 800–2500 ms range (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:317-330); `custom` bounds are read per profile without consulting process env (#116895).
  - `_load_prefill_messages` returns `[]` on a missing file, a non-list JSON payload, or any parse error, logging a warning each time (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:78-90).
  - `_load_env_or_agent_cfg_timeout` treats `0` as a valid value and warns only when a *supplied* value fails to parse (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:429-439).
  - `_load_launchd_exit_timeout` returns `None` (fail-open, drain unchanged) when the process is not launchd-owned or `launchctl print` is unavailable (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:403-409).
  - `_load_provider_routing` and `_load_fallback_model` both fail open to `{}`/`None` (gateway/run_config_loaders.py:504-517).

### `gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py`.)* — `DeliveryQueueWatch` (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:23) stamps each served profile's `cron/deliveries.db` queue file **and its `-wal` sidecar** by `(mtime_ns, size)` (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:42-54) and reports `changed()` when any stamp moves (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:56-61); `wait_for_next_tick` (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:64) sleeps in ≤1.0 s slices (`DELIVERY_QUEUE_WATCH_SECONDS`, gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:19) and drains the moment a stamp moves, because a worker that finished in 8 s used to reach the user a minute later — the housekeeping tick's own drain remains only as the fallback for what the stat cannot see (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:1-9). The watch deliberately does **not** reset itself after draining: the drain's own status writes move the stamp once more and cost one empty pass, after which the stamp is stable (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:25-28); `refresh_homes()` re-resolves the served profile set once per tick, not per poll, because a directory read under multiplex is expensive (gateway/run_delivery_queue_watch.py:37-40).

### `gateway/run_goals.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_goals.py`.)*

- **Purpose:** `GatewayGoalsMixin` — the `/goal` continuation loop, the heartbeat poller, and the `/loop` idle-wakeup watcher.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayGoalsMixin` (gateway/run_goals.py:28).
  - `_goal_max_turns_from_config` (gateway/run_goals.py:32), `_warm_goals_session_db` (gateway/run_goals.py:49), `_manager_for_event` (gateway/run_goals.py:74), `_synthetic_prompt_event` (gateway/run_goals.py:103).
  - `_register_heartbeat_watch` (gateway/run_goals.py:118), `_heartbeat_poll_once` (gateway/run_goals.py:129), `_heartbeat_poll_watch` (gateway/run_goals.py:138), `_start_heartbeat_poller` (gateway/run_goals.py:192).
  - `_send_goal_status_notice` (gateway/run_goals.py:227), `_defer_goal_status_notice_after_delivery` (gateway/run_goals.py:251), `_post_turn_goal_continuation` (gateway/run_goals.py:295), `_run_post_turn_hooks` (gateway/run_goals.py:351), `_post_turn_loop_completion` (gateway/run_goals.py:402), `_loop_wakeup_fire_one` (gateway/run_goals.py:424), `_loop_wakeup_watcher` (gateway/run_goals.py:512).
- **Data flow:** turn ends → `_run_post_turn_hooks` (gateway/run_goals.py:351) → goal judge (sync aux-LLM call) → deferred status notice → optional continuation enqueued into the adapter FIFO (gateway/run_goals.py:347) or, on LOCAL, committed via `admit_route_wake` (gateway/run_goals.py:338); separately, the heartbeat poller wakes idle watched sessions and the loop watcher scans persisted `loop:*` rows and injects synthetic prompts.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **The goals SessionDB is warmed off-loop before every read or write.** A cold cache runs the `state.db` init on the loop thread and freezes it; a cold cache at the turn boundary *drops the read/write* (gateway/run_goals.py:50-52, 283). `_warm_goals_session_db` is called before the session lookup (gateway/run_goals.py:64), before every manager factory (gateway/run_goals.py:292) and before each loop scan (gateway/run_goals.py:540).
  - **Every aux-LLM call goes through `_run_in_executor_with_context`.** `judge_goal()` is a synchronous HTTP call of 10–40 s that would block Discord heartbeats (gateway/run_goals.py:317-325); the same hop is required because a *bare* executor hop drops the profile HERMES_HOME override and secret scope, so a served secondary's tick would be written into the DEFAULT profile's state.db (gateway/run_goals.py:416-419).
  - **A synthetic prompt drops the stored source's `message_id`.** `_synthetic_prompt_event` calls `replace_source(source, message_id=None)` (gateway/run_goals.py:111-112) because the stored message_id is the message that *registered* the watch; keeping it would make every progress bubble and final reply quote a stale message.
  - **The heartbeat poller is tagged as a supervised watcher.** `task._hermes_supervised_watcher = True` (gateway/run_goals.py:213) so `_scale_to_zero_has_live_background_work()` does not treat the gateway as busy forever — the task is an infinite loop and would never end.
  - **The loop watcher scans every served profile's store.** `list_active_loops` alone reads only the launch home's store, so a `/loop` set from a secondary profile's chat would never fire (gateway/run_goals.py:518-521, 551-558).
  - **The adapter map is chosen by the loop's own profile, fail closed.** `self._adapters_for_profile(profile)` (gateway/run_goals.py:442) rather than `self.adapters`, because the latter is the default profile's map and a secondary session's wakeup would inject via the default bot on a bare chat_id — a Telegram DM landing in the user's chat with the *other* bot (gateway/run_goals.py:439-441).
  - **Loop wakeups bypass the inbound estop gate, so they check pause explicitly.** They are injected as internal events (gateway/run_goals.py:500), which the inbound estop gate does not see; `check_paused("loop")` is called explicitly and does *not* claim the tick, so it stays due (gateway/run_goals.py:475-478).
  - **The goal status line is deferred until after delivery.** `_defer_goal_status_notice_after_delivery` registers a post-delivery callback when the adapter has one and delivers directly otherwise — never drops (gateway/run_goals.py:251-278); otherwise "✓ Goal achieved" would precede the visible final response (gateway/run_goals.py:327).
  - **Empty turns do not drive `/goal`, but must still release a `/loop` tick.** The hook list is built conditionally: the goal continuation hook is inserted only when `final_text.strip()` (gateway/run_goals.py:369-371).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - `_manager_for_event` returns `(None, None)` when the manager module is unavailable or the session lookup fails (gateway/run_goals.py:77-84); a session entry without a `session_id` is treated as absent (gateway/run_goals.py:72).
  - `_heartbeat_poll_watch` drops a watch whose session no longer exists in the store (gateway/run_goals.py:142-145) and one whose heartbeat was removed (gateway/run_goals.py:167-170).
  - A busy session, an active-session claim, or a queued message means the tick is *not* claimed — it stays due until user work drains (gateway/run_goals.py:155-160).
  - `check_paused("heartbeat")` leaves the tick unclaimed so it fires after `hermes resume` (gateway/run_goals.py:163-164).
  - `_heartbeat_poll_once` catches per-watch exceptions so one bad watch cannot stop the loop (gateway/run_goals.py:135-136); the loop watcher catches per-scan errors (gateway/run_goals.py:559-560).
  - On LOCAL, the status line is published to the viewer's event stream (`status.update`), not sent — the local adapter's send is a no-op (gateway/run_goals.py:229-238).
  - The `_loop_wakeup_fire_one` failure path calls `mgr.abandon_tick()` so a failed injection does not leave the tick claimed (gateway/run_goals.py:505-508).
- **Surprising:** `_post_turn_loop_completion` is a no-op unless `state.awaiting_response` is set (gateway/run_goals.py:414) — i.e. a loop tick that was never injected is not "completed" by an unrelated turn.

### `gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py`.)* — Accounting for a heartbeat admission attempt. `resolve_heartbeat_owner` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:12) admits only the ORIGINAL lineage: when the resolved session id differs from `event._heartbeat_session_id`, it compares against the compression tip and rejects unless they match (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:18-24), then stores a *value* (`_heartbeat_resolved_session_id`, gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:27) rather than the mutable routing entry, because preparation and hooks can yield to `/new` or `/stop` before the agent runner starts (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:25-26). `heartbeat_owner_is_current` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:31) requires the current owner to exist, be non-suspended, and carry the expected session id. `settle_heartbeat_attempt` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:39) refunds an unexecuted fire via `manager.abandon_fire()`. `admit_heartbeat` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:47) coalesces overdue ticks behind the canonical FIFO rather than an adapter-local slot: admissions live on the route's LOGICAL owner (the compression root), not its transcript tip, and a LOCAL route's heartbeat rides `local_automation_v1` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:58-60); an existing pending heartbeat on that owner short-circuits the whole admission (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:61-63). On `BaseException`, readiness is not a delivery attempt and cancellation before commit is not an ACK, so the fire is only refunded when `_gateway_accepted` is not `True` (gateway/run_heartbeat_acceptance.py:75-79).

### `gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py`.)* — `restore_heartbeat_watches` (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:22) is a retryable startup/poll scan that recovers heartbeat watches from the gateway's canonical persisted routing index (the SessionStore's origin + exact key), deliberately not from a second heartbeat routing snapshot, which also covers pre-upgrade state (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:25-27). All storage work runs off-loop so a cold profile DB cannot block adapters (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:28). The cheap gate `profile_has_active_heartbeat` over the watched homes skips the per-origin scope re-parse entirely when nothing is persisted (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:41-44); `_watched_homes` (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:10) includes `default` under multiplex because a `-p work` multiplexer's own home is not `~/.hermes` and dropping it would hide its heartbeats forever (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:11-13). Sessions are grouped by profile scope and each scope is entered ONCE per scan, not once per session, because a scope entry hydrates the secret scope and terminal policy (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:46-49, 59-61). Failed reads never prune existing watches (each is caught and logged at DEBUG, gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:57-58, 66-69), and after the executor hop the session id is re-checked with `peek_session_id` in case a reset/compression published a new owner meanwhile (gateway/run_heartbeat_restore.py:75-77).

### `gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py`.)* — `housekeeping_media_caches` (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:20) prunes nine caches (Image, Document, Audio, Video, Screenshot, Spillover, Terminal temp, Bot DM, Bot relay) with `MEDIA_CACHE_MAX_AGE_HOURS`; the spillover cache is deliberately given `max_age_hours=None` so it keeps the session retention window (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:39). `maybe_post_auto_prune_prompt` (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:45) asks each served profile's home channel how to resolve a low disk, cheaply (two syscalls + a YAML read per profile) with the database estimate running only for a profile that can actually receive it (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:47-48); the snooze is recorded **only on a delivered post** — a failed send must not swallow the question (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:66-68). `publish_local_auto_prune_notice` (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:74) posts one sticky `notification.show` per profile per snooze window to Desktop/Ink TUI viewers, guarded by an in-flight claim in `_LOCAL_NOTIFIED` so only one estimate runs per home at a time; if nobody saw it, the claim is popped so the next window may ask again (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:98-105). `clear_local_auto_prune_notice` (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:108) publishes `notification.clear` once `/autoprune` is answered on that local session. `_post` (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:121) is fully best-effort: it enters the profile's async runtime scope, uses each transport's `typed_command_prefix` so the suggested `/autoprune` command is spelled in that platform's prefix (gateway/run_housekeeping_disk.py:133), and returns whether at least one send was delivered.

### `gateway/run_idle_exit.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_idle_exit.py`.)* — Idle exit for an auto-started (unmanaged) gateway: a client that finds no gateway starts a detached `gateway run --idle-exit`, and nothing else ever stops it, so every throwaway home used to leave a ~270 MB daemon behind (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:3-5). `busy_reason` (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:152) enumerates every reason to stay up — attached clients, an outstanding session ticket, recent client activity, already-stopping, in-flight agent work, background work, messaging adapters, ledger rows (admissions/managed workers/hosted-room tasks), cron jobs, kanban boards, and per-profile heartbeat/loop/handoff gates (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:155-172) — and **fails CLOSED**: any exception is "idle probe failed", i.e. the gateway stays up (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:173-175). `unmanaged_idle_exit_watcher` (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:187) polls on `min(30, max(0.5, window/4))` seconds, requires **two consecutive idle readings** so one racing write cannot slip between them, and re-checks `busy_reason` synchronously right before acting (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:209-212). `_begin_idle_exit` (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:178) withdraws admission *synchronously* — no await between the last idle check and the stop — and publishes `draining` with `drain_reason: idle_exit` before anything else so a racing client waits for the process to go and starts a fresh one (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:180-183). `arm_unmanaged_idle_exit` (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:216) arms only when `_detect_supervisor() == "manual"`; a gateway with systemd/launchd/Desktop supervision never arms it (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:220-222). `_messaging_busy` excludes `Platform.LOCAL` because the local viewer never holds the gateway up (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:84), and `_kanban_busy` opens each board read-only with `file:{path}?mode=ro` and a 2 s timeout (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:118). The self-ended exit is recorded with the `auto-started gateway idle` prefix so a reconnecting client's `--recover` can tell it apart from an operator stop (gateway/run_idle_exit.py:36, 182).

### `gateway/run_idle_gates.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_idle_gates.py`.)* — Idle gates for the gateway's per-profile pollers. Entering `_profile_runtime_scope` costs a config.yaml load, a `.env` parse, secret hydration and a terminal-policy build, and on a multiplex gateway the pollers paid that per profile per tick with nothing to do — so each gate answers "does this profile's store hold work?" from the goals-cached SessionDB with ONLY the `HERMES_HOME` contextvar installed (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:3-6). `_profile_session_db_probe` (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:18) sets and then resets the home override around `_get_session_db()`. **Every gate fails OPEN**: an unavailable store, a failing read or a corrupt row is "cannot prove emptiness", never "idle" (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:7, 33-41). The exported gates are `profile_has_active_heartbeat` (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:44), `profile_has_active_loop` (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:50) and `profile_has_pending_handoff` (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:56); `off_loop_gate` (gateway/run_idle_gates.py:60) runs a sync gate through the runner's executor hop and keeps the historical always-enter behaviour for runners that have no executor (bare test stand-ins for the handoff watcher).

### `gateway/run_inbound.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound.py`. Read head 1–500, gap 500–740, mid 737–1236, gap 1236–1545, tail 1543–1842.)*

- **Purpose:** The inbound message pipeline for `GatewayRunner` — admission gates, authorization, command resolution, the busy fast path, media/text preparation, durable-turn markers, and the authority handoff.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayInboundMixin(GatewayInboundHooksMixin, GatewayInboundTranscriptionMixin, GatewayPluginInjectionMixin)` (gateway/run_inbound.py:71).
  - `discord_triggering_note` (gateway/run_inbound.py:50) / `strip_discord_triggering_note` (gateway/run_inbound.py:58).
  - `_hm_admit_event` (gateway/run_inbound.py:143) — the ingress gates; `_hm_estop_gate` (gateway/run_inbound.py:273) / `_hm_estop_turn_allowed` (gateway/run_inbound.py:246).
  - `_hm_update_prompt_reply` (gateway/run_inbound.py:313), `_hm_clarify_reply` (gateway/run_inbound.py:358), `_hm_slash_confirm_reply` (gateway/run_inbound.py:454).
  - `_hm_evict_idle_stale_agent` (gateway/run_inbound.py:484), `_hm_evict_reaped_agent` (gateway/run_inbound.py:537), `_hm_evict_running_agent` (gateway/run_inbound.py:563).
  - `_hm_handle_running_session_message` (gateway/run_inbound.py:741), `_hm_resolve_command` (gateway/run_inbound.py:802), `_hm_dispatch_idle_commands` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1146), `_hm_authority_admission` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1203), `_handle_message` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1242).
  - `_prepare_inbound_message_text` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1652), `_classify_inbound_media` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1408), `_decide_image_input_mode` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1754), `_mark_durable_active_turn` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1711), `_clear_durable_active_turn` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1726).
- **Data flow:** `_handle_message` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1242) → `_hm_admit_event` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1246) → estop gate (gateway/run_inbound.py:1262) → pending-reply intercepts (gateway/run_inbound.py:1267) → authority admission (gateway/run_inbound.py:1271) → stale-agent eviction (gateway/run_inbound.py:1276) → busy fast path (gateway/run_inbound.py:1280) → idle command dispatch (gateway/run_inbound.py:1282) → Telegram-lobby / external-drain refusals (gateway/run_inbound.py:1289-1299) → `_claim_active_session_slot` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1304) → FIFO orphan rescue (gateway/run_inbound.py:1309) → sentinel claim (gateway/run_inbound.py:1314) → `_handle_message_with_agent` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1329) → post-turn hooks (gateway/run_inbound.py:1341) → `finally`: restore one-turn model override (gateway/run_inbound.py:1350), clear the durable marker (gateway/run_inbound.py:1356), release the running-agent slot by generation (gateway/run_inbound.py:1361), release the turn lease (gateway/run_inbound.py:1364).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **The session sentinel is claimed before any await.** `_AGENT_PENDING_SENTINEL` is installed at gateway/run_inbound.py:1314, before `_handle_message_with_agent`; without it a second message arriving during the many awaits in between passes the "already running" guard and spins up a *duplicate* agent for the same session (gateway/run_inbound.py:1301-1303).
  - **Session ContextVars are reset at handler entry.** The per-message task was `create_task()`'d with a copy of the spawning context, which may carry ANOTHER message's `HERMES_SESSION_*` vars; until `_set_session_env` binds ours a subprocess would read the foreign identity (gateway/run_inbound.py:153-160).
  - **The Slack ignored-channel guard runs first of all.** It precedes startup-restore queueing, plugin hooks, authorization and session setup, so an ignored channel can never reach pairing/auth/session state (gateway/run_inbound.py:178-193).
  - **Admission returns `(event, source, is_internal)`, not a bool**, because the `pre_gateway_dispatch` hook may have *rewritten* the event (gateway/run_inbound.py:143-147, 215-218).
  - **An unauthorized DM has three behaviors and one of them is silence.** `pair` mints a code, `decline` sends one short refusal per sender per dedupe window, `ignore` sends nothing at all (an allowlist exists, so any reply would leak that the bot exists) — and `ignore` mints *no pairing request*, because strangers must not create server state when the owner restricted access (gateway/run_inbound.py:229-240; gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:4-11).
  - **The decline stamp is written BEFORE the send**, so a delivery hiccup cannot become a decline storm; without a store there is no dedupe state and the answer is silence (gateway/run_inbound.py:103-117).
  - **A synthetic heartbeat blocked by the estop gets `""`**, not the pause notice: nobody typed it, and `settle_heartbeat_attempt` leaves its tick due for after `hermes resume` (gateway/run_inbound.py:289-291).
  - **Clarify replies distinguish three outcomes.** `TEXT_RESOLVED` returns `""` so the adapter does not double-post (gateway/run_inbound.py:388-412); `TEXT_REJECTED_SELECTION` *retains* the clarify for retry (gateway/run_inbound.py:413-416); `TEXT_REJECTED_PROSE` *cancels* it and continues through normal routing — because `redirect()` degrades to `steer()` while tools execute, and that steer cannot drain until the clarify tool returns (gateway/run_inbound.py:417-421).
  - **A pending dangerous-command approval outranks a pending slash-confirm.** `_hm_slash_confirm_reply` returns `None` when `has_blocking_approval` is set, so `/approve` there unblocks the waiting tool thread rather than the card (gateway/run_inbound.py:462-465).
  - **A recognized slash command during a pending `/update` prompt writes a BLANK response**, so the detached update process takes the prompt's default and exits instead of blocking on stdin until the watcher timeout (gateway/run_inbound.py:342-355).
  - **The activity clock is authoritative for staleness.** `_hm_evict_idle_stale_agent` evicts only when the agent has been *idle* past the threshold; the emergency wall TTL (`max(timeout*10, 7200)`) is a fallback used only when no usable activity summary exists — a total age alone never makes an actively progressing turn stale (gateway/run_inbound.py:521-528). Host sleep is not idleness: the turn poll loop's `awake_idle` meter is applied (gateway/run_inbound.py:512-515).
  - **The reaped-session check demands real answers.** `isinstance(sid, str)` and `_is_ended(sid) is True` keep a stubbed store or a `MagicMock` inert (gateway/run_inbound.py:547-553); the pending sentinel is never evicted (gateway/run_inbound.py:487-488).
  - **`/stop` and `/new` fence the route and cancel only `messaging:` rows.** A FIFO paused on a lost (`unknown`) turn is preserved — its pause notice promised the queued followers stay saved — so the fence is skipped and only the claimed generation is interrupted (gateway/run_inbound.py:613-651).
  - **A Telegram TEXT follow-up inside the post-start grace window is queued, not interrupted.** `HERMES_TELEGRAM_FOLLOWUP_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 3.0) is measured from the turn's `started_ts` (gateway/run_inbound.py:653-675).
  - **Media are re-homed BEFORE any consumer reads `media_urls`.** `rehome_inbound_media(event)` is the first statement of `_prepare_inbound_message_text` (gateway/run_inbound.py:1660), because vision, STT and the document notes all read that list.
  - **The reply-to pointer is injected even when the quoted text is already in history** — it is disambiguation (*which* prior message), not deduplication, and a preview here would silently lose later list items and code (gateway/run_inbound.py:1558-1564).
  - **The Discord triggering-message note is OUTERMOST**, so `strip_discord_triggering_note` can peel exactly it off the persisted transcript row without touching the reply pointer — persisted verbatim it pollutes FTS and memory (#71304/#114719, gateway/run_inbound.py:58-68, 1566-1577).
  - **Per-attachment MIME beats the message-level type.** A document sent alongside an image is not routed as an image; `MessageType.AUDIO`/mixed-DOCUMENT audio is a *file* attachment and never STT (gateway/run_inbound.py:1412-1427).
  - **A failed authority admit stays the authority's answer** (a refusal notice or a turn error), never a pending legacy turn — that would run unadmitted, outside the FIFO's order (gateway/run_inbound.py:1232-1234).
  - **The durable-turn token never enters public state.** `_mark_durable_active_turn` keeps the ownership token off the event's public metadata, transcripts and platform payloads (gateway/run_inbound.py:1720-1723).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - `_clear_durable_active_turn` retries 3× and never blocks agent/lease release; a stale marker is bounded by the agent timeout and the clean-start discard (gateway/run_inbound.py:1726-1752).
  - `_restore_pending_one_turn_model_override` is generation-guarded, so a displaced turn's finalizer is a no-op (gateway/run_inbound.py:1366-1385).
  - `TurnLeaseTimeoutError` returns before the `/goal` judge, so the judge cannot consume the resend notice and enqueue a synthetic continuation loop (gateway/run_inbound.py:1330-1339).
  - The skill-slash rewrite runs in the executor because it is disk-bound (cold skill scan, skill file loads, an unavailable-skill `rglob` over every skills dir) and on a large install it held the loop past the liveness watchdog (#111091, gateway/run_inbound.py:1159-1165).
  - The image-input decision does blocking network I/O (models.dev fetch, Ollama `/api/show`) and is therefore offloaded to a thread (gateway/run_inbound.py:1434-1438); any failure falls back to `"text"` mode (gateway/run_inbound.py:1795-1797).
  - The Slack ignore check reads the *routed* adapter's list rather than the default config's (gateway/run_inbound.py:181-185).
  - `_hm_pending_reply_intercepts` refuses to fire for a non-control event, a `_fifo_only` command turn, or an executing admission (gateway/run_inbound.py:1136-1138).
- **Surprising:**
  - The comment block at gateway/run_inbound.py:1253-1260 describes a "TERMINAL-DECLINE LATCH TEARDOWN" that is **no longer in the code** — the teardown was deleted but the explanatory comment stayed, making the file describe a mechanism that does not exist. It is the only place in the file where a comment documents absent behavior.
  - `_hm_pre_gateway_dispatch_hook` is skipped while an admitted row executes (gateway/run_inbound.py:77-79 of `run_inbound_hooks.py`), and a *local* CLI/TUI/Desktop turn never fired it on main — so a `skip` must not drop committed input. The messaging path runs it BEFORE auth so plugins can handle unauthorized senders, while the producer path runs it AFTER the delivery's authorization (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:70-72).
  - `_hm_update_prompt_reply` accepts `/approve` and `/deny` as yes/no shorthand for the pending updater (gateway/run_inbound.py:323-326) — the only place a slash command is reinterpreted as a raw response.

### `gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py`.)* — `run_post_admission_hook` (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:34) fires the `post_gateway_admission` plugin hook once per non-internal message that survived authorization, bot admission, pause/drain, pending-reply intercepts, the running-session lane and idle slash-command dispatch, inside the claimed session slot (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:3-5). It runs under the message's **ROUTED profile scope** (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:39) — the receiving bot's handler binds the transport home (auth reads its `.env`), which differs from the runtime home when the bot serves a chat for another profile (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:5-7); the scope is a no-op outside multiplex (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:27-31). It is **fail-open**: a callback that raises, times out, or returns anything other than `{"action": "handled"}` is logged and ignored so the message proceeds to the agent and one buggy plugin can never blackhole a profile's traffic (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:9-12, 50-54); `asyncio.CancelledError` is re-raised rather than swallowed (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:48-49). The payload is a plain snapshot of scalars — never live runner/session-store handles (gateway/run_inbound_consumer.py:11-12, 43-47).

### `gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py`.)*

- **Purpose:** `GatewayInboundHooksMixin` — the plugin and user extension points on the inbound pipeline: `pre_gateway_dispatch`, `post_gateway_admission`, `pre_command` / `command:<name>`, user `quick_commands`, and plugin-registered slash commands.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `GatewayInboundHooksMixin` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:48); `_EXEC_QUICK_COMMAND_TIMEOUT = 30` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:29); `_stop_exec_tree` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:32).
  - `_hm_post_admission_consume` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:51), `_hm_pre_gateway_dispatch_hook` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:65).
  - `_hm_quick_commands` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:114), `_hm_expand_alias_quick_command` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:120).
  - `_hm_command_hooks` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:131), `_hm_run_exec_quick_command` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:178).
  - `_hm_dispatch_quick_and_plugin_commands` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:209), `_hm_dispatch_plugin_command` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:245), `_hm_run_plugin_handler` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:262).
- **Data flow:** event → `pre_gateway_dispatch` (may skip or rewrite) → authorization → admission → `post_gateway_admission` (may consume) → command resolution (`_hm_resolve_command` in `run_inbound.py`) → `pre_command` (observer) + `command:<canonical>` (interceptor) → quick commands (exec/alias) → plugin command handler → agent.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **`executing_admission.get()` suppresses the pre-dispatch hook.** A messaging turn already passed the hook before its commit, and a local CLI/TUI/Desktop turn never fired it on main, so a `skip` must not drop committed input (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:74-79). The same guard blocks the post-admission consume (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:56-61).
  - **The hook's position differs by lane.** On the messaging path `pre_gateway_dispatch` runs BEFORE auth so plugins can handle unauthorized senders; on the producer path (webhook/relay, `admit_producer`) it runs AFTER the delivery's authorization, so it never sees a refused sender (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:70-72).
  - **Hook results are a first-decisive-result protocol.** `{"action": "skip"}` drops the event, `{"action": "rewrite", "text"}` replaces `event.text` via `dataclasses.replace` and carries the callback over to the rewritten event (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:106-107), `allow`/None dispatches normally; the loop breaks on the first decisive result (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:109, 111).
  - **`pre_command` is an observer; `command:<name>` is an interceptor.** The former fires and ignores its results (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:141-147); the latter may return `{"decision": "deny"|"handled"|"rewrite"}` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:149-175).
  - **The running-agent path deliberately does NOT fire these hooks** — a slow or hostile plugin must not interfere with the operator's escape hatches for a live agent (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:136-137).
  - **A `type: exec` quick command runs a shell in the gateway's own process**, which holds every API key in `os.environ` — hence the sanitized `build_subprocess_env()` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:180-188). Output is redacted with `redact_sensitive_text` before it is returned (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:195-197).
  - **The whole process tree is killed, not just the shell.** `sleep 120 & wait` leaves the sleep running if only the shell is killed; `kill_process_tree` reaches descendants (`taskkill /T` on Windows) and the POSIX branch kills the shell's process group, made possible by `start_new_session=True` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:32-45, 191).
  - **Timeout and cancellation share one cleanup path.** The tree kill is `asyncio.shield`ed so a cancelled await still cannot leave the command running, and `CancelledError` is re-raised while a timeout returns the localized "timed out" string (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:199-205).
  - **Quick commands re-run the access gate.** They are never in the command registry, so the early gate never fires for them; `_check_slash_access` is applied again at this dispatch sink so a non-admin cannot reach an exec quick command (#44727, gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:220-227).
  - **Plugin command names normalize underscores to hyphens**, so Telegram's underscored autocomplete form matches a plugin registered with hyphens (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:250-255).
  - **Sync plugin handlers run on the gateway pool, never the loop thread.** Blocking I/O there starves the liveness watchdog and the process exits 75 mid-handler (#105279, gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:272-275, 293-298).
  - **The handler's session env is bound here.** This dispatch sits *before* the agent-turn path binds `HERMES_SESSION_*`, so a handler reading `get_session_env()` would see an empty or a foreign (cron agent's) session (#108698, gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:269-271); the session entry is looked up — never created — so `/new` can report the right `old_session_id` (#123245, gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:276-280).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:**
  - Every hook dispatch is wrapped: `pre_gateway_dispatch` failures are logged as a WARNING and dispatch continues (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:87-89); `pre_command` and `command:<name>` failures are DEBUG-logged and non-fatal (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:146-147, 157-159).
  - A plugin handler that raises propagates to the caller — the exact registered name is resolved by `gateway/session_commands.py` (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:265-267).
  - An alias quick command with no `target` returns None (no rewrite) and the dispatcher answers with the usage string (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:122-125, 236-239); an `exec` quick command with an empty `command` is refused (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:231-232); any type other than `exec`/`alias` is refused (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:234-235).
  - A `rewrite` decision with an empty `command_name` is ignored (falls through to the next result) (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:172-175).
  - The drain gate rejects any command while the gateway is draining (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:214-215).
- **Surprising:** `ctx.post_message` from a plugin handler reaches the chat *even before its session is stored* (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:291) — the handler is invoked with a `build_session_context` whose `session_key` is derived from the source when no entry exists (gateway/run_inbound_hooks.py:281-283).

### `gateway/run_inbound_media.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound_media.py`.)* — `rehome_inbound_media` (gateway/run_inbound_media.py:15) moves adapter-cached attachments from the **launch** home's `cache/` into the **active (routed) profile's** `cache/` and repoints `event.media_urls`. Adapters download and cache an attachment *before* the gateway routes the event to a profile, so on a multiplexed gateway the file landed under the launch home while the routed turn's sandbox mounts and vision's `_media_cache_roots` resolve the routed profile's `cache/` — the agent was handed a mounted, empty directory (#101134, gateway/run_inbound_media.py:16-23). It is a no-op when the active home equals the launch home (gateway/run_inbound_media.py:29-30), it is idempotent (a moved entry is no longer under the launch home), and it rewrites the path inside `event.text` for adapters that already baked the cached path into the message text (gateway/run_inbound_media.py:49-50) using the agent-visible cache path translation. Per-attachment failures are logged as WARNING and skipped, leaving that URL in place (gateway/run_inbound_media.py:45-47).

### `gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py`.)* — `GatewayInboundTranscriptionMixin` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:16): `_enrich_message_with_transcription` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:19) transcribes voice clips and returns `(enriched_text, successful_transcripts)`; when STT is disabled it still probes each clip's duration and emits a neutral `[The user sent a voice message: <abs path> (duration: …)]` note, returning an empty transcript list (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:28-35). A missing transcription module yields `[voice message could not be transcribed]` rather than an exception (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:41-43). `_transcribe_pending_audio_event_once` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:77) caches the result on the event (`_gateway_pending_stt_text`, `_gateway_pending_stt_transcripts`) so the interrupt monitor and the pending-drain path share one STT call and one echo, and it re-runs in a loop if a voice note was merged in during the await, because publishing a transcript that silently omits the new note would be worse than re-transcribing (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:96-103); per-clip results are memoized separately so a re-run transcribes only the newly merged clips (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:89-94). `_echo_pending_stt_transcripts_once` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:108) tracks the echo as a **COUNT, not a set** — identical transcripts are distinct deliveries, and since a re-run returns earlier transcripts as a prefix, only the unsent tail is echoed (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:112-122). `_untranscribed_audio_note` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:156) never says "no STT provider" or mentions setup steps: persisted in history such a note makes the model keep volunteering STT-setup advice. A successful-but-empty transcript (silence, cut-off) yields an explicit sentinel telling the model not to guess, because empty quotes make the agent reply to nothing and can loop (#41603, gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:175-184); the transcript is wrapped in a *plain quoted line* because a "The user sent a voice message…" wrapper read as a meta-instruction and made the LLM comment on voice mode (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:185-187). `_prepend_media_prefix` (gateway/run_inbound_transcription.py:149) drops the Discord empty-content placeholder as redundant.

### `gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py`
*(actual repo path `gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py`.)* — Two audiences, two rules for an unauthorized inbound sender (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:3-11). The **stranger** gets a pairing code (behavior `pair`) or nothing at all (`ignore`: an allowlist is configured, so any reply would leak that the bot exists); the **owner** gets a WARNING log naming the sender's ID and the allowlist/pairing-mode fix, surfaced once per (platform, user) per gateway process. `unauthorized_owner_hint` (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:53) treats the display name as attacker-controlled: it is passed through `neutralize_untrusted_inline_text` with a 64-char cap (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:24, 62) and then stripped of `@<>[]()`*_~#` so a hostile name cannot ping the channel or smuggle a link — the ID next to it is the value the owner acts on (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:63-66). `UnauthorizedOwnerNotifier` (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:75) keeps one notice per (platform, user_id) in a bounded `OrderedDict` LRU of 2048 entries, evicting the oldest when a bot rotates IDs (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:27, 82-94). `notify` returns early when the stranger's DM *is* the home channel (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:101-104) — posting there would answer the unauthorized user, which the ignore behaviour exists to prevent. `pairing_profile_arg` (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:32) emits `-p <profile> ` only for a non-default profile, and `pairing_code_reply` (gateway/run_inbound_unauthorized.py:40) states the code's validity in hours derived from `CODE_TTL_SECONDS` with a singular/plural i18n key.

### `gateway/run_notifications.py`
- **Purpose:** Delivers process-completion, watch-pattern and async-delegation notifications to the right chat, and owns the durable claim/preflight/ack state machine around each completion delivery.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayNotificationsMixin(GatewayUpdateNotificationsMixin, GatewayNotificationOwnershipMixin)` — `gateway/run_notifications.py:172`; `_CompletionClaim` dataclass — `:180` (fields `proceed`, `early_result`, `defer`, `owner_session_id`, `proven_session_id`, `delegation_id`, `claim_id`); `INTERNAL_NOTIFICATION_FOOTER` — `:86`; `_DURABLE_CLAIM_OPS` map (claim kind → `tools.async_delegation` fn) — `:85`; `_COMPLETION_BATCH_KEY_FIELDS` — `:176`; `_ASYNC_GROUP_KEY_FIELDS` — `:177`; `_MAX_COMPLETION_INJECTION_ATTEMPTS = 8` — `:68`; `_WATCH_INJECTION_RETRY_BASE_DELAY = 4.0` — `:72`; `_completion_delivery_identity` — `:973`; `_preflight_completion_delivery` — `:1111`; `_inject_watch_notification` — `:868`; `_deliver_completion_notification` — `:1214`; `_async_delegation_watcher` — `:1578`.
- **Data flow:** `process_registry.completion_queue` → `_async_delegation_watcher:1578` (2 s tick; orphan sweep + watch drain) → group by `_ASYNC_GROUP_KEY_FIELDS` → `_deliver_async_delegation_group:1457` → `_deliver_completion_notification:1214` → `_preflight_completion_delivery:1111` (claims the durable row, proves the owner) → `_inject_watch_notification:868` → `admit_process_notice` (under a session authority) or `admit_internal_event` → adapter `handle_message`. Process watchers are a second path: `arm_process_watcher:1747` → `_run_process_watcher:1763` → `_enqueue_process_completion_notification:1405` (0.1 s fan-in window) → `_flush_process_completion_batch:1325`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. **Adapter acceptance is NOT proof of delivery.** The pre-flight exists because the inner resolver can still fail closed *inside* the pipeline *after* the adapter accepted, which would falsely ack the durable row — so the target is verified first, giving drops an honest durable disposition (`:1111-1117`, `:1240-1246`).
  2. `_mark_internal_notification:96` **appends** the footer, not prepends, so start-anchored consumers (session timeline, memory gating, desktop notification detection, the `SYSTEM` prefix) keep matching; it is idempotent so a retried delivery never stacks footers (`:103-105`).
  3. A synthetic completion is injected **without its original reply anchor** — `source = replace_source(source, message_id=None)` (`:924-926`); the stale `message_id` (the message that started the process) rides metadata as `original_trigger_message_id` only, because an event anchored there made a finished job quote an old Discord DM on every reply-anchoring platform (#52694, `:911-917`).
  4. `_completion_delivery_identity:973` returns `None` for a legacy completion with no `started_at` — delivered **undeduplicated** rather than risk suppressing a real completion; a `task_failure_notice` gets a distinct identity `f"task_failure:{task_idx}"` so the interim per-task notice cannot mark the batch's final result as already delivered (`:986-993`).
  5. `_drain_watch_notifications:710` always drains the queue (so events do not rot) but evaluates the `display.background_process_notifications` gate **per event inside that event's own profile scope** — one shared queue carries every served profile's events (`:718-726`).
  6. `_requeue_refused_watch_event:752` counts only refusals proven *permanent* (`_refusal_permanent`) toward the drop cap of 8; a transient refusal (restart wait, parked profile, full FIFO) waits it out however long it lasts, on a jittered backoff from 4 s capped at 60 s (`:75-78`).
  7. Coalesced batches are given a prompt-level instruction to send **at most one consolidated user-facing response** — 10 entries max, 800 chars each, redaction applied *before* slicing (`:1289-1317`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_inject_watch_notification:868` is tri-state — `True` = adapter accepted, `False` = retryable adapter failure, `None` = no gateway route **or** a notice pinned to a permanently-gone session (terminal drop). `_preflight_completion_delivery:1136-1152` distinguishes `terminal` (drop, durable claim settled as `drop`) from `retry` (`defer`, spends no delivery attempt). `_flush_process_completion_batch:1358-1365` re-populates undetached entries on `CancelledError` and resolves every waiter as retryable so a watcher future is never stranded (`:1380-1383`); the batch is detached *before* delivery so a completion arriving mid-flight can schedule the next flush (`:1330-1334`). `_completion_delivery_ready:1080` refuses to spend a durable attempt when the target adapter or its `state.db` is unavailable (`:1085-1092`). `_self_post_api_server:784` never self-posts an async-delegation completion on a non-push `api_server` surface — it persists a delivery row instead, because the client owns the next turn there (`:787-795`).
