- **Surprising:** the pre-flight claims the durable row only for a **non-`task_failure_notice`** `async_delegation` — the interim notice shares the same `delegation_id`, so claiming it would acknowledge the FINAL result before it exists (`:1120-1126`). `_served_api_server_wake_profile:823` **raises** `LookupError` when the event names a served profile but the session is not in that profile's own store — never a wake in the default profile's store (`:838-844`). `_restore_secondary_completion_ledgers:1540` / `_sweep_orphaned_completion_ledgers:1549` exist because `process_registry.restore_completions()` covers only the launch profile's `state.db`; a secondary's rows would otherwise never be replayed after a restart. The completion-identity dedup map is an in-process `OrderedDict` capped at 2048 — it closes queue/watcher races inside one gateway, *not* exactly-once across a crash (durable replay state stays owned by `tools.async_delegation`, `:1002-1008`).

### `gateway/run_notifications_ownership.py`
- **Purpose:** Proves which session owns a pinned async/process completion and canonicalizes a delegate child's pin to its human-facing parent before any route moves.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayNotificationOwnershipMixin` — `:32`; `_MAX_DELEGATE_PROVENANCE_HOPS = 16` — `:29`; `_lookup_completion_owner` — `:93`; `_resolve_compression_lineage_target` — `:35`; `_closed_local_target` — `:68`; `_watch_pin_gone` — `:141`; `_resolve_async_delegation_session` — `:168`; `_requeue_uncertain_completion` — `:264`.
- **Data flow:** `_classify_completion_owner` (in `run_notifications.py:1031`) → `_lookup_completion_owner:93` walks `model_config._delegate_from` up to 16 hops, returning `("deliver"|"terminal"|"retry", session_id, row)` → `_resolve_async_delegation_session:168` maps the proven owner onto the live route via `advance_compression_session` (compression lineage) or `switch_session(..., expected_session_id=prior)` (CAS, so a concurrent `/new` wins) → returns the `SessionEntry` the delivery must use.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. **Only `model_config._delegate_from` authorizes walking out of an execution transcript.** Generic parent pointers also describe compression and branches (`:97-101`); a current route is an explicit ownership boundary, not a route to repair.
  2. User closure is a boundary at **every** hop, including execution rows (`:117-120`).
  3. **Fail-closed on unresolvable provenance** — a cycle, missing parent, malformed config or a chain longer than 16 hops DROPS the injection rather than falling back to the current route entry (`:135-138`, `:222-227`); the completion stays readable in the delegation records either way.
  4. A row with `source == "subagent"` and no `_delegate_from` is `terminal` (never delivered); an ordinary session is `deliver` (`:129-131`).
  5. A pinned row ended for a **non-user** reason (idle/timeout) is retargeted to the chat's *current* session, not dropped (`:200-207`) — the row would otherwise be acked and silently lost.
  6. The hop cap exists only to stop a corrupt chain turning one completion event into an unbounded run of sequential session reads; real nesting is bounded far lower by `delegation.max_spawn_depth` (default 2) (`:26-29`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `run_generation` is snapshotted **before** the ownership awaits (`:186-188`) so a concurrent `/stop` or `/new` wins and the injection is dropped rather than racing the route. `switch_session` is called with `expected_session_id=prior_session_id` (`:239-244`) — a CAS that lets a route replaced meanwhile win over the stale completion. `_requeue_uncertain_completion:264` pops the in-memory delivered markers under `_completion_delivery_lock` and then calls `requeue_admitted_completion`; boundary verdicts stay dropped. `_watch_pin_gone:141` returns True only for a `terminal` verdict — a `/resume` reopens the pinned session, so the process's later notices are admitted again (`:151-154`).
- **Surprising:** `_closed_local_target:68` is for a **LOCAL** conversation a viewer closed: the completion is kept as a note on the conversation's closed physical tip (`session_completion_notes`, #134889 LC-7) — but only when the pinned session (or its compression tip) is the one the route still names, because a local `/new` opens a new route so the replacement never matches (`:72-76`, `:80-86`). A local tip lookup that fails returns `""` so the compression path can defer it (`:87-91`).

### `gateway/run_notifications_update.py`
- **Purpose:** Watches the `hermes update --gateway` marker files and streams the update's output, forwards its prompts, and sends the completion notice into the right chat.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayUpdateNotificationsMixin` — `:43`; `_UpdatePaths` — `:47` (six markers under `HERMES_HOME`: `.update_pending.json`, `.update_pending.claimed.json`, `.update_output.txt`, `.update_exit_code`, `.update_prompt.json`, `.update_response`); `_UpdateTarget` — `:65`; `_UPDATE_NOTIFY_MAX_ADAPTER_WAIT_SECONDS = 3600.0` — `:35`; `_watch_update_progress` — `:229`; `_send_update_notification` — `:300`; `_marker_profile` — `:102`; `_marker_age_seconds` — `:116`.
- **Data flow:** the separate `hermes update --gateway` process writes `.update_output.txt` / `.update_prompt.json` / `.update_exit_code`; the watcher polls them (2 s poll, 4 s stream interval, 1800 s deadline), strips ANSI, sends fenced ≤3500-char chunks, forwards the prompt as platform-native buttons, and on exit code 0/≠0 sends the finished or failed notice.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. `pending → claimed` is a file **rename** (`paths.pending.replace(paths.claimed)`, `:305-310`) — a filesystem-level mutex between concurrent gateway processes over the same marker set; a `FileNotFoundError` is treated as "someone else claimed it".
  2. The 3600 s cap exists because **nothing bounds the adapter wait**: a marker naming a platform that is not configured at all would keep itself on disk and re-log a deferred line on every poll, in every process, forever (`:33-35`). After the cap the notification is dropped **loudly**, the markers cleared, and `True` returned so the caller stops rescheduling the watcher (`:336-347`).
  3. The prompt marker is kept on disk until answered so a restarted watcher can re-forward it, and `SessionState.persistent.update_prompt_pending` stops the poll from re-forwarding the same prompt every 2 s (`:219-222`, `:287-291`).
  4. The requester's **own** profile bot is resolved from the marker's `profile` field, else the `agent:<profile>:` lane of its `session_key` (`:102-114`, `:146-149`) — a bare `self.adapters` lookup is the default bot under multiplexing.
  5. Output is read as **bytes** and decoded with `errors="replace"` (`:183-191`, `:344`) — update logs can contain invalid UTF-8.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the 1800 s timeout writes exit code `"124"` and sends the timed-out message (`:294-299`); the completion-only fallback writes `"124"` too when the deadline passes with no exit code (`:175-177`). `_defer:312` moves the claimed file **back** to `pending` (`paths.claimed.replace(paths.pending)`) so the next attempt re-reads it (`:318-320`). An exception anywhere in `_send_update_notification` is caught and logged as a warning, and the `finally` still unlinks the markers when `cleanup` is still True (`:369-372`) — a failed send loses the notification rather than retrying forever.
- **Surprising:** `_read_update_output_since:183` returns `(text, len(data))` — the offset advances to the **file length**, not the length of the decoded text, so a multi-byte character split across a poll boundary cannot desync the offset (`:186-191`). `send_update_prompt` is looked up on `type(adapter)` (the class), not the instance (`:206-210`), so a MagicMock instance cannot fake button support. `_marker_age_seconds:116` returns `None` for a marker with no parseable `timestamp` (older markers) and the caller then keeps the *old* retry behavior rather than guessing (`:121-124`).

### `gateway/run_plugin_injection.py`
- **Purpose:** The process-wide injector that lets a plugin start a gateway turn in its own profile, with two admission rules — an existing session key, or a messaging origin.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayPluginInjectionMixin` — `:26`; `_install_plugin_message_injector` / `_clear_plugin_message_injector` — `:27` / `:33`; `_schedule_plugin_message_injection` — `:39` (thread-safe scheduler, returns `bool`); `_plugin_session_key_admissible` — `:140`; `_plugin_messaging_session_owned` — `:154`; `_plugin_local_session` — `:172`; `_plugin_origin_source` — `:266`; `_plugin_origin_admissible` — `:304`; `_admit_plugin_injection` — `:363`.
- **Data flow:** plugin → `PluginContext.inject_message` → `publish_gateway_message_host` (process-wide) → `_schedule_plugin_message_injection:39` (synchronous profile/admission checks) → `asyncio.create_task` on the gateway loop → `_dispatch_plugin_message_injection:188` / `_dispatch_plugin_origin_injection:322` → under a session authority `authority.admit_automation(adapter, event, identity)` (durable FIFO, `:363-390`); without one, `adapter.handle_message(MessageEvent(internal=True, allow_gateway_control=False))`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The origin path **never chooses a profile**: the gateway's own routing for that chat through that profile's adapter must already land in the plugin's profile, so a human follow-up in the same chat keys into the same session (`:295-302`, module docstring `:1-7`).
  2. `_plugin_origin_admissible:304` — under a session authority a plugin turn **borrows** the connector provenance of a human message already committed in that chat, and the gateway never mints it: *a plugin can continue a chat, not open one* (`:310-320`).
  3. `source._transport_adapter_ref = weakref.ref(adapter)` is set on the origin (`:293-295`) so the identity resolves exactly as it would for a human message in that chat.
  4. Authorization is **rechecked at dispatch** with `allow_adapter_delegation=False` (`:129-131`) — adapter-time grants (Discord roles) do not count for a plugin; host-side plugin code is trusted to ask, never to widen access.
  5. `_plugin_local_session:172` resolves the live session through `authority_for_home(self, plugin_home)` — **never** through the source's profile, whose fallback is the ambient scope (a default-profile local source carries none, so under beta's scope it would read as beta's) (`:180-184`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_plugin_injection_accepting:112` refuses injections while the gateway is draining. `RuntimeStoreError` from `admit_automation` is caught and logged, returning `False` (`:388-391`). A plugin with no `plugin_home` is deliberately left to dispatch — `_plugin_messaging_session_owned` returns `True` (`:160-161`) and `_plugin_origin_admissible` returns `True` (`:307-308`). The scheduler logs dispatch failures and never raises, because the plugin already received `True` (`:99-108`). The `keyless` target is resolved in `gateway/plugin_keyless_inject.py` (`:59-65`).
- **Surprising:** a keyed injection carries `gateway_session_strict: True` in its metadata (the key is pinned) while an **origin** injection is explicitly **non-strict** — the key is pinned against topic recovery and the session may not exist yet (`:217-222`, `:350-356`). The durable admission identity is `json.dumps(["plugin", plugin_id, session_key, uuid4().hex])` — the uuid makes each admission unique so the FIFO cannot dedupe two distinct plugin turns away (`:372-375`).

### `gateway/run_plugin_rewire.py` — the late-plugin-load seam: subscribes once per plugin-manager scope to `on_plugin_loaded` and re-wires that profile's live adapters' slash-command/button/inbound-transform handlers on the loop (`run_plugin_rewire.py:29-79`), deliberately scoping to handlers only so a mid-run-loaded plugin's tools and prompt sections stay deferred to the next session for the prompt-cache invariant; `reload_plugins_verb:81` adds a control-socket force re-discovery whose adapter count is read on the loop *after* the re-wire callback (FIFO), so the reported "active now" is truthful.

### `gateway/run_profile_reconcile.py`
- **Purpose:** Keeps the multiplexed served-profile set in step with `profiles/` while the multiplexer runs — hot add, hot remove, and re-scan on credential change — instead of snapshotting it once at boot.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayProfileReconcileMixin` — `:72`; `profile_serve_signature(home)` — `:50` (stat signature of `config.yaml` + `.env`); `_PROFILE_RESCAN_INTERVAL_SECS = 30.0` — `:35`; `_OWN_GATEWAY_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SECS = 5.0` — `:40`; `reconcile_served_profiles` — `:163`; `_apply_profile_changes` — `:235`; `_multiplex_for_added_profile` — `:331`; `_serve_profile_runtime` — `:396`; `_unserve_profile` — `:455`; `_profile_lifecycle_verb` — `:537` (`serve_profile_verb:587` / `unserve_profile_verb:583`); `_for_each_served_profile` — `:619`; `profile_scoped_chore` — `:649`; `migrate_profile_identity_verb` — `:655`; `purge_profile_identity_verb` — `:697`.
- **Data flow:** `profiles_to_serve(multiplex=True)` (on disk) is diffed against `self._served_profile_homes` (the live reservation) under `_reconcile_lock()` — added → `reserve_profile_home` + `serve_profile_runtime` + `_start_one_profile_adapters`; removed → `_unserve_profile`; changed (config/.env signature) → adapter rebuild only. Triggered by the `rescan-profiles` control verb and by the supervised `_profile_reconcile_watcher:106` every 30 s.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. Only **three** things were start-time snapshots (the secondary adapter set, the `served_profiles` record, the cron ticker's profile list); everything else already reads `profiles_to_serve()` live, so reconciling those three is enough (`:1-13`).
  2. The own-gateway liveness probe runs **off the loop and bounded** at 5 s (`:195-215`): inline on the loop thread it parked the shutdown-watchdog liveness probes and the multiplexer was hard-killed with exit 75 (#132547) — a Windows named pipe stalls in the control-socket read until its peer answers.
  3. A probe **timeout is not "own gateway running"**: the profile is skipped for that cycle without being remembered as blocked (`:207-214`); a probe skip is never a deletion (`:176`).
  4. `_apply_profile_changes` re-reads `profiles_to_serve()` **after** the adapter connects (`:305-310`) — a profile deleted while an adapter was still connecting must not be recorded back (the deleter's signal timed out against this lock and `rmtree` already ran).
  5. A transient adapter failure leaves the signature **unacknowledged** so the next reconcile retries; only a `MultiplexConfigError` is a deliberate park (`:277-291`). The signature refill inside `_note_served_profiles` would otherwise park a transiently-failed profile exactly like the config-error case, so transiently-failed names are popped from `_served_profile_signatures` (`:313-323`).
  6. `_unserve_profile:455` runs the **whole** teardown inside the removed profile's own runtime scope with `hydrate_secrets=False` (`:471-473`) — teardown hooks read config and credentials at call time, and unscoped they resolved against the LAUNCH home, so a hook needing this profile's credential failed closed or borrowed the launch profile's.
  7. The gateway process is a **second holder the CLI-side delete cannot reach**, so unserve must release the profile's routed log handles (`logs/.__agent.lock`) and its scoped MCP servers' `mcp-stderr.log` handle itself (`:504-521`, #130244). Both calls BLOCK, so they run via `asyncio.to_thread` — otherwise every other served profile's events stall behind them (`:511-519`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `serve_profile_verb`'s handler returns `{"pending": True}` on a 5 s timeout (`:571-574`) — the client must not read an incomplete teardown as "stopped". `_unserve_profile` honours a False retirement verdict: the profile leaves the served set but **keeps** its reservation and store handles (`:481-488`) — raising there would abort the reconcile for every other profile. `_serve_profile_runtime:406-424` distinguishes an ownership-retained failure (a turn outlived its Stop) from an unusable store, parking the first and releasing the reservation for the second. `_resync_changed_hook_consent:136` leaves a **failed** sync unrecorded and retries next tick, and the key covers `config.yaml` too because a sync that read an empty/unreadable config applied nothing (`:143-147`). `_mcp_config_reconciler:591` reconciles on **drift**, not on a config edit — that is what revives a server whose *first* connect failed (#112445); a chronically failing server is retried on its own 30 s→600 s cooldown, not every tick.
- **Surprising:** `purge_profile_identity_verb:697` is deliberately **not** part of `_unserve_profile()` — that path also unserves names still alive elsewhere in the identity story (a rename's old name leaves the served set exactly like a delete does), and purging there would race the rekey it is supposed to leave intact (`:700-706`). `_multiplex_for_added_profile:331` starts multiplexing **in place** with no restart and no drain: it flips `self.config.multiplex_profiles`, re-wires every primary adapter's handlers and republishes the served set (`:374-386`) — the boot decision is re-made on every rescan, so flipping `gateway.auto_multiplex_migration` back to true converges without a restart (`:344-346`).

### `gateway/run_runtime.py`
- **Purpose:** Owns the session-authority lifecycle for the gateway bootstrap — one authority per reserved profile home — plus park/serve/unserve, native-row recovery, and the drain/settle sequence.
- **Key abstractions:** `reserved_profile_homes` — `:22`; `_build_profile_authority` — `:36`; `_store_open_failure` — `:61`; `park_profile` / `unpark_profile` / `profile_parked` / `refuse_parked_scope` / `parked_profile_map` — `:86` / `:95` / `:101` / `:108` / `:120`; `initialize_gateway_runtime` — `:135`; `reserve_profile_home` / `release_profile_home` / `_withdraw_profile_home` — `:194` / `:206` / `:217`; `TURN_SETTLE_SECONDS = 45.0` — `:237`; `STOP_TAIL_RESERVE_SECONDS = 10.0` — `:240`; `stop_authority_turns` — `:249`; `_stop_spawning_managed` — `:292`; `_retire_profile_authority` — `:323`; `ProfileOwnershipRetained` — `:376`; `serve_profile_runtime` — `:380`; `unserve_profile_runtime` — `:459`; `drain_gateway_runtime` — `:601`; `settle_gateway_runtime` — `:622`.
- **Data flow:** `initialize_gateway_runtime:135` → `process_ownership.owns(home)` per home → `SessionAuthorities` registry → per-home `_build_profile_authority:36` (scope-bound handle, then `initialize_session_authority`) → `descriptor['served_profiles']` + `TicketStore`. Hot path: `serve_profile_runtime:380` builds an authority and recovers bot mailbox / local sessions / webhook finalizations / published API turns under `owner_scope(authority)` (`:399-410`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The **launch** profile is always index 0 and its failure **aborts** the start; a secondary's failure parks only that profile (`:158-163`, `:505-511`) — under multiplex, taking every other profile down with it would repeat on every restart (#140018).
  2. `_build_profile_authority:36` asserts the authority's DB actually **belongs to** the reserved home (parent-dir check, `:50-52`) — the scoped handle cache could otherwise hand back the wrong store.
  3. `_store_open_failure:61` deliberately does **not** reopen the store to learn why: reopening could create or migrate it and would skip the caches' backoff; it reads the caches' recorded `last_error` instead (`:66-74`).
  4. A parked profile is refused **retryably** (`runtime_draining`, a 503 with `Retry-After`), not with the final `profile_mismatch` of a profile this gateway does not serve (`:108-117`).
  5. `release_profile_home:206` **raises** if the profile's authority has not retired (`:208-211`) — the reservation is released last, after every handle into that home closes.
  6. `_retire_profile_authority:323` runs the hosted-room stop *alongside* the worker join under the same deadline, but only **after** the turns are signalled — the room threads wait for the member turns they observe, so the turns must be stopped first or the room stop cannot finish (`:337-343`).
  7. On retirement every remaining waiter is failed with `RuntimeStoreError('runtime_draining')` and its row stays **queued** for the next owner, so nothing is resent (`:364-371`).
  8. `settle_gateway_runtime:622` never raises for a task that misses its budget — it logs and leaves the task running for recovery, because raising would skip teardown and disarm the watchdog (`:626-632`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `serve_profile_runtime:380` retries an earlier failed attempt with `timeout=0`; if it still will not retire, `ProfileOwnershipRetained` is raised and the caller parks the profile while keeping the reservation (`:390-398`). `_drop_retired_local_adapter:420` removes a LOCAL adapter still bound to the previous authority so the new one can own it (a retained writer never reaches here). `recover_served_native_rows:443` catches per-authority failures so one bad ledger neither stops the others nor stops the reconcile (`:451-455`). `drain_gateway_runtime:601` withdraws the public ingress callback (`set_message_handler(None)`) **without** disconnecting egress, so already-admitted work can still deliver (`:609-617`).
- **Surprising:** `drain_gateway_runtime:604-607` deletes `descriptor['drain_reason']` when it equals `RESTART_WAIT_REASON` — "stopping now: no client attaches to answer anymore". `publish_gateway_runtime_ready:574` refuses to publish `ready` if the session API task is already done or the runner is draining (an aborted boot must not publish readiness). `_stop_spawning_managed:292` records a stop **cause** per generation in `authority.pending_stop_causes`, but only when the user's Stop did not already latch that generation (`:296-305`) — a user's Stop latched first keeps no cause.

### `gateway/run_runtime_init.py`
- **Purpose:** Writable initialization of the runner's mutable state — config-derived settings, the session store, lifecycle flags, caches, the session DB, and the registry/clock set.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayRuntimeInitMixin` — `:8` with `_init_runtime_settings` — `:9`; `_init_session_store` — `:37`; `_init_lifecycle_state` — `:49`; `_init_runtime_caches` — `:135`; `_init_session_db` — `:163`; `_init_registries_and_clocks` — `:207`.
- **Data flow:** config YAML → `_load_*` helpers → instance attributes; `SessionStore` + its `AsyncSessionStore` facade + `DeliveryRouter` (`:37-47`); `RecoverableHandleCache` for the session DB (`:178-181`); `process_registry.has_active_for_session` is injected as the store's process-registry reset guard (`:40-42`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The file exists to separate **writable initialization from process ownership** (`:1-2`) — the process-level ownership registry lives in `gateway/run_runtime.py`, and mixing the two made the init order unreadable.
  2. `_init_lifecycle_state:49` is a wall of comments encoding load-bearing invariants: the run-generation counter (#28686) is **never** reset; the turn lease is keyed by SESSION_ID, not routing key, because `switch_session`'s many-to-one mapping is invisible to routing-key guards (`:84-86`); the last-known-good model fallback exists because a transient mtime-keyed config-cache miss would build the agent with `model=""` and every API call would fail HTTP 400 "No models provided" (`:95-104`).
  3. `_init_session_db:163` keeps `_session_db_pinned = _SESSION_DB_UNPINNED` (`:172`) and resolves handles **per path** (`:173-179`) — a handle bound at construction time is pinned to the process's root home, but `/resume`, `/title`, `/history` and session search all run inside `_profile_runtime_scope` and must see that profile's own `state.db` (#88532).
  4. The `state.db` startup maintenance is a **housekeeping chore**, not a constructor step (`:196-205`) — its `git gc` repacks the whole store for tens of seconds, and running it before the control socket/adapters made the first restart of the day look hung and fail fleet verification.
  5. That maintenance now runs **once per served profile**, each under its own scope (`:186-194`) — bound to `self._session_db` it only ever pruned the launch profile's store, and the launch profile's `retention_days` decided whether a secondary was ever pruned.
  6. `_init_runtime_caches:135` pins `_primary_profile_name` to `"default"` whenever the config multiplexes (`:146-150`) — a multiplex host's primary map is always the default profile even when a named profile launched the process (that launcher is a secondary adapter owner).
  7. The slash-confirm counter is `itertools.count(1)` because the state lives module-level in `tools.slash_confirm` and the counter must stay **compact** — Telegram caps `callback_data` at 64 bytes (`:159-161`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_init_session_db` tolerates a failed store open: it logs a **warning** (not DEBUG, so it lands in `errors.log`) and keeps running, stashing the error in `_session_db_init_error` for later surfacing (`:180-184`) — an NFS `HERMES_HOME` silently loses `/resume` otherwise. `_init_runtime_settings` snapshots the secondary-profile busy modes at multiplex startup; handlers never reread config (`:24-26`).
- **Surprising:** `_init_registries_and_clocks:207` seeds `_last_inbound_at = time.time()` (`:232`) so a fresh gateway is not "idle since epoch" for scale-to-zero, and arms a 30 s loop-liveness heartbeat so a supervisor can tell "process alive" from "loop frozen" (#66892, `:234-238`). `_fatal_handler_tasks` is a strong-ref set so the event loop cannot GC a fatal-error handler task mid-run (`:157`). The `_hermes_supervised_watcher` tag that lets the scale-to-zero check exclude permanent watchers is set on the heartbeat task in `run_startup.py:880`, not here.

### `gateway/run_shutdown.py`
- **Purpose:** Stop/drain/restart orchestration, the scale-to-zero suspend path, and the active-work accounting that decides what a shutdown must wait for.
- **Key abstractions:** `_log_suppressed` — `:121` (the "suppress(Exception) but LOG it" seam used at every best-effort boundary); `_notice_target_key` / `_delivery_target_key` — `:147` / `:152`; `GatewayShutdownMixin(GatewaySessionEndMixin)` — `:173`; `_StopContext` — `:177`; `_active_work_count` — `:193`; the `_scale_to_zero_*` family — `:321-704`; `_enter_external_drain` / `_exit_external_drain` / `_drain_control_watcher` — `:706` / `:718` / `:735`; `_drain_work_counts` / `_drain_active_agents` — `:804` / `:811`; `_awaitable_work_count` / `_describe_active_work` / `_await_active_work_before_restart` — `:1547` / `:1568` / `:1608`; `request_restart` — `:1674`; the `_stop_*` phases — `:1822-2185`; `_stop_impl` — `:2187`; `stop` — `:2227`.
- **Data flow:** `stop():2227` → `_stop_impl:2187` (under a thread watchdog) → `_stop_begin_teardown:1822` → `_stop_drain_active_work:1853` → (if timed out) `_stop_interrupt_remaining_work:1902` → `settle_gateway_runtime` → `_stop_finalize_agents_and_adapters:1944` → `_stop_release_runtime_state:1978` → `_stop_quiesce_and_close_session_dbs:2030` → `_stop_persist_exit_state:2109`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. **Fail-AWAKE vs fail-SAFE accounting.** The drain counters swallow errors to 0, which is fine for a drain, but the scale-to-zero *suspend predicate* reads an unreadable source as **work** (sentinel 1) so a transient read failure cannot make live work look idle and reopen a mid-job freeze (`:206-213`, `:458-470`).
  2. The shutdown watchdog is a plain **OS thread**, not an asyncio timeout, because asyncio timeouts cannot recover a frozen loop — it dumps faulthandler stacks and `os._exit`s past drain+grace (`:2187-2200`); it is skipped under pytest so `stop()`-driving tests never get a delayed hard exit (`:2199`).
  3. The executor is quiesced **before** the SessionDB close, and the close is **skipped entirely** if any worker is still live (`:2030-2060`) — a late write after `SessionDB.close()` checkpoints the WAL, reopens the handle and splits the WAL generation (#101093, #101064).
  4. Cron/API/deferred work lives on the loop's *default* executor, not `self._executor`, so the join cannot see it; those are counted separately and also block the close (`:2062-2074`, #102198).
  5. `request_restart:1674` creates the restart task under an **empty `Context()`** (`:1705-1706`) — a copied context would run the HOST restart as that profile (watcher `HERMES_HOME`, `stop()`'s flushes).
  6. The restart task is deliberately **not** in `_background_tasks` (`:1695-1706`) — `_stop_impl` cancels those, which would cancel `_run_restart` while it awaits `_stop_task` and skip `_shutdown_event.set()` / `_exit_code = 75` (#12875).
  7. `.clean_shutdown` is **not** written when the drain timed out (`:2119-2125`) — the next startup must recover the interrupted turns.
  8. A signal-initiated shutdown persists `gateway_state=running` so `container_boot` auto-starts the gateway on the next boot (`:2137-2143`, #42675).
  9. The scale-to-zero suspend requires an **acked** `go_dormant()` (`:566-580`) — without the ack inbound is not buffered, so suspending would freeze a live destination; the code refuses to suspend unprotected and releases the re-dial hold.
  10. `_scale_to_zero_await_freeze_gap:651` slices on the **wall clock**, not `asyncio.sleep`, because a Fly suspend stops `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` while `CLOCK_REALTIME` keeps tracking: measured monotonic advanced 0.501 s across a 252.219 s freeze, so a single sleep would resume with its remainder and delay the drain re-dial on every wake.
  11. `_flush_agent_transcript_at_shutdown:1134` persists the in-flight transcript before teardown, and on a flush failure dumps the live history to a JSON recovery snapshot rather than losing it (`:1160-1170`).
  12. `_cleanup_agent_resources:1265` drains the memory manager's queue **before** `shutdown_all()` (which gives the single serialized worker only ~5 s and cancels the rest), so a `/reset` or rotation cannot drop writes and load stale memory (#73297).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_stop_release_runtime_state:1978` skips cancelling `_stop_task` and `_restart_task` (`:1981-1984`). The Windows restart-watcher's spawn failure logs only the interpreter basename and the numeric errno — never argv, env, watcher source, or `str(exc)` (which may carry a full path) (`:1440-1452`); the spawn is retried once without the job-breakaway bit because a job without `BREAKAWAY_OK` rejects `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` (`:1432-1440`). The Windows watcher uses a Win32 handle-based existence check because on Windows `os.kill(pid, 0)` is **not** a no-op — it maps to `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent` (bpo-14484) (`:78-95`). `_notify_active_sessions_of_shutdown:1023` keeps the per-session pings even when the drain marker suppresses only the home-channel broadcast, and the check fails **toward** the louder behaviour (`:1100-1107`). The adapter map is snapshotted with `list(...)` because `adapter.send()` can hit a fatal path that pops the adapter → "dictionary changed size during iteration" (`:1110-1111`).
- **Surprising:** `_drain_active_agents:811` gives cron/API their **own** deadline (the cron floor), separate from the chat-turn deadline (`:838-847`) — on `restart_drain_timeout`'s 0 default they were killed after 0.00 s (#82161, #132989). `_awaitable_work_count:1547` excludes wedged turns and restart-safe external cron workers: an observed live 100-minute job held the gateway in `draining` for ~30 minutes. `_describe_active_work:1568` exists so `hermes update` / `hermes gateway status` can **name** the work instead of printing a bare count. `_stop_kill_tool_subprocesses:1742` is called twice (post-interrupt and final cleanup) and marks in-flight cron jobs interrupted, because `kill_all()` is a global sweep with no per-job targeting — a job mid-dispatch lost its tool subprocess and its agent thread may still emit a plausible response from truncated output (`:1765-1781`, #60432).

### `gateway/run_shutdown_session_end.py`
- **Purpose:** Bounded off-loop session finalization plus out-of-band delivery of the plugin `on_session_finalize` messages — split out of `run_shutdown.py` and bound via `GatewaySessionEndMixin` (`:15`); the decisive rule is that both the finalize and the reset run in a **housekeeping executor under the owning profile's scope** with a 10 s bound, and on timeout the worker thread is simply left to finish (`:56-81`).

### `gateway/run_startup.py`
- **Purpose:** The startup sequence (recover, connect, wire, spawn), the auto-resume / crash-recovery paths, and the CLI→chat handoff.
- **Key abstractions:** `recover_left_core_in` / `recover_left_core_at_gateway_start` — `:40` / `:50`; `_own_policy_envs` — `:64`; `GatewayStartupMixin` — `:78`; `_wait_bounded_or_release` — `:218`; `_finish_startup_restore` — `:240`; `_await_startup_boot_sends` — `:286`; `_claim_pending_obligations` / `_redeliver_claimed_obligations` — `:338` / `:447`; `_resume_pending_candidates` / `_schedule_resume_pending_sessions` — `:556` / `:600`; `_recover_unclean_sessions` / `_ledger_crash_left_replies` / `_crash_left_reply` — `:743` / `:758` / `:793`; `_start_prefilter_platforms` / `_start_connect_pending` / `_start_aggregate_connect_results` — `:1208` / `:1265` / `:1320`; `_start_handle_no_connections` — `:1424`; `_start_finish_wiring` — `:1532`; `start` / `_start_impl` — `:1635` / `:1644`; `_process_handoff` — `:1849`.
- **Data flow:** `start():1635` → `_start_impl:1644` → faulthandler → startup environment/logs → access-policy check → `_start_recover_previous_run:1156` (plugins, relay, hooks, crash recovery) → restore gate ON → prefilter + concurrent connect → aggregate results → secondary profiles → `_start_finish_wiring:1532` (boot sends, pending-spool recovery, auto-resume, drain the restore queue, native-session recovery, state.db warning) → background watchers.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The restore **gate** serializes startup auto-resume against inbound: inbound queues in `_startup_restore_queue` until every synthetic resume turn finishes, and the gate is released in a `finally` (`:266-271`) — a stuck flag would queue every non-internal inbound forever (#116514).
  2. The gate opens on a **bounded** wait and the resume turns are **not** cancelled (`:243-258`) — safe because `_schedule_resume_pending_sessions` claims each `_running_agents` slot *synchronously* first, so drained inbound queues behind.
  3. Boot-path **sends** (restart notification, planned-restart replay, obligation redelivery) run in a bounded, abandonable task, but the **ledger claim and the `resume_pending` clear run inline first** (`:296-310`) — deferring the claim let a hung notification expire the gate with zero rows claimed, so answered turns were replayed *and* redelivered (#91969).
  4. `_start_handle_no_connections:1424` deliberately does **not** exit 78 when the failures are mixed (some fatal, some transient): under a supervisor honoring the exit-78 contract that would take the gateway permanently down over a blip, and under anything else it crash-loops — either way the retryable platforms never get their retry (`:1466-1495`).
  5. A boot that could not start every configured platform stamps `degraded`, not `running` (`:96-97`, `:1694-1697`) — one WARNING next to "Gateway running with N platform(s)" let a gateway with no API server at all look healthy for half an hour.
  6. `_consume_clean_shutdown_marker:736` **raises** on failure instead of continuing — continuing with the old receipt would let a later unclean exit masquerade as clean (`:1183-1190`).
  7. `_recover_unclean_sessions:743` replaces the old 120 s recency sweep: an **unmarked** session finished its turn (the marker is held until the reply is ledgered), so nothing re-runs it — only the turns the dead process left marked are recovered.
  8. `_crash_left_reply:793` returns `""` for a machinery turn or a muted diagnostic wake (nothing would have been presented) and `_unexpected_silence_reply()` when the silence was *not* allowed (`:820-826`).
  9. `_schedule_resume_pending_sessions:600` skips a session when any authority `holds_admission` it — legacy synthetic resume must not race a restored FIFO (`:614-618`).
  10. The resume marker is stamped naive-local by the previous process, possibly across a DST change, so freshness uses `_is_fresh_gateway_interruption` rather than wall-clock subtraction (`:620-622`).
  11. `_start_connect_pending:1265` settles the in-flight connects **first** (so a completed adapter's disconnect cannot unblock a sibling) and then tears down the adapters that succeeded (`:1292-1308`).
  12. `_process_handoff:1849` uses `switch_session` to re-point the session key at the CLI session id and evicts the cached `AIAgent` so the synthetic turn rebuilds against the CLI session id like `/resume` (`:1860-1872`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_drain_startup_restore_queue:130` marks each replayed event `_hermes_startup_restore_replay` so `_handle_message` does not re-queue it while the gate is still closed, and one bad replay does not abort the drain (`:146-152`). `_await_startup_warmup:201` opens the gate anyway on timeout — availability outranks prompt completeness for a wedged init. `_start_install_faulthandler:892` registers SIGUSR2 with `chain=False` — SIGUSR2's default disposition is "terminate", so chaining to it would kill the gateway the operator was trying to inspect (`:907-911`). `_log_agent_budget:82` resolves the turn limit the same way the turn loop does (`none`/`unlimited` spellings) instead of `int()` on the raw string with an invented `500` default (#116888).
- **Surprising:** `_start_prefilter_platforms:1208` **skips** a platform on the default profile when the multiplexed primary has no bot credential — starting it would queue an infinite reconnect loop that can never heal (#64674) — and then warns loudly if no profile at all provided the token (`:1408-1420`). `_start_log_systemd_timing_alignment:992` warns when systemd's `TimeoutStopSec` no longer covers the drain window (a unit file from before an upgrade may SIGKILL mid-drain). `_handoff_resolve_destination:1736` mirrors each adapter's **own** inbound key shape (Telegram forum topics key as `group:`, Slack keys a thread reply on the parent's type, Discord keys on the thread's own id) so the first reply after a restart lands on the same key (#111896, #112918).

### `gateway/run_topics.py`
- **Purpose:** Telegram forum-topic mode and Discord auto-thread binding/rename for GatewayRunner.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTopicThreadsMixin` — `:40`; `_telegram_topic_profile_name` — `:46`; `_telegram_topic_mode_enabled` — `:63`; `_recover_telegram_lobby_thread_id` — `:174`; `_transfer_telegram_root_dm` — `:213`; `_sanitize_telegram_topic_title` / `_sanitize_discord_thread_title` — `:306` / `:311`; `_is_discord_auto_thread_lane` — `:319`; `_recover_discord_auto_thread_source` — `:327`; `_relay_auto_thread_info` / `_await_relay_auto_thread_info` — `:376` / `:404`; `_rename_discord_auto_thread_for_session_title` — `:422`; `_restore_telegram_topic_session` — `:647`.
- **Data flow:** a Telegram DM carrying a `thread_id` → topic-mode predicates (a `state.db` `is_telegram_topic_mode_enabled` read) → binding rows `(chat_id, thread_id) → session_id`; the auto-title thread then fires a rename back onto the loop.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  1. The Discord title cap is measured in **UTF-16 code units**, not Python code points, so truncation uses `utf16_len` / `_prefix_within_utf16_limit` (`:311-317`) — a code-point slice would split a surrogate pair. Telegram's cap is 1-128 chars, so the sanitizer keeps 120 (`:306-309`).
  2. `_recover_discord_auto_thread_source:327` recovers the auto-thread markers from the session's **opening** event (`entry.origin`, then the row's `origin_json`) because native Discord stamps them only on the opening event and a later title retry on a rebuilt agent arrives unmarked (`:336-364`).
  3. The relay rename path asks the **connector** to enforce the no-clobber guard from its own created-name memory (`:453-462`) — the gateway cannot reproduce the initial name byte-for-byte, and normalization drift silently declined every rename; the native path instead guards on `only_if_current_name`.
  4. `_is_relay_discord_channel_lane:366` is a **shape-only** check that deliberately does not consult the send-result cache — before delivery the feedback cannot exist; the rename lane polls it at fire time (`:366-374`).
  5. `_relay_auto_thread_info:376` prefers the connector's per-message `prospective_thread_id` stamp (`:389-393`) — exact even when several auto-threads spawn from one channel — and falls back to the per-chat send-result cache, which only ever renamed the **first** thread.
  6. The operator-topic check uses `getattr(type(adapter), "_get_dm_topic_info", None)` and then `isinstance(operator_topic, dict)` (`:571-580`) — `getattr()` on a MagicMock auto-creates attributes, so every test double would match; only dict-shaped returns count.
  7. The Telegram rename verifies the topic binding still points at **this** session before renaming (`:583-593`) and skips operator-declared topics (`extra.dm_topics`) so auto-rename does not silently mutate operator config.
  8. `_telegram_cooldown_elapsed:104` keys the debounce on `(profile, chat_id)` so profiles sharing a Telegram private `chat_id` under multiplex do not suppress each other's lobby reminders (#76423).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_recover_telegram_lobby_thread_id:174` returns `None` for a non-lobby, unbound thread id — a brand-new DM topic is also "unknown" until its first message is recorded (`:186-188`). `_recover_telegram_topic_thread_id:166` returns `None` when `dm_topic_auto_transfer` is on, so `_transfer_telegram_root_dm` can open a fresh topic (and fall back to this recovery itself). `_transfer_telegram_root_dm:213` returns `(thread_id, True)` for a created topic, `(thread_id, False)` when creation failed and it fell back to lobby recovery, and `None` when not applicable (`:213-238`). `_restore_telegram_topic_session:647` refuses a session that is not telegram-owned or not the caller's, and treats an "already linked" `ValueError` from the DB as the already-linked message rather than an error (`:678-684`).
- **Surprising:** `_send_telegram_topic_setup_image:290` sends a **bundled binary asset** (`assets/telegram-botfather-threads-settings.jpg`) — one of the few binary assets referenced by the gateway code, and it is not in this manifest. The Discord rename's `TypeError` is logged at **WARNING** (adapter API mismatch) while every other failure is DEBUG (`:463-469`). `_schedule_rename_from_title_thread:470` copies the source with `replace_source` so the title thread never shares the live dataclass with the loop, and keeps the live transport owner (a multiplex route may run under a profile that does not own the Discord token) (`:476-488`).

### `gateway/run_turn.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway's agent-turn execution spine (`GatewayTurnMixin`, gateway/run_turn.py:174) — inbound message → session resolution → agent run → transcript persistence → delivery, plus the remote-proxy, background-task and MCP-reload side paths.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTurnMixin` (gateway/run_turn.py:174) is composed of `GatewayTurnPrepareMixin`/`GatewayTurnHygieneMixin`/`GatewayTurnPersistenceMixin` (gateway/run_turn.py:169-171); module-level pure helpers `bound_model_input_without_hygiene` (gateway/run_turn.py:119), `hygiene_no_commit_reason` (gateway/run_turn.py:145), `is_context_overflow_failure_result` (gateway/run_turn.py:99), `_tool_call_logger` (gateway/run_turn.py:55); the orchestration seams `_handle_message_with_agent` (gateway/run_turn.py:215), `_run_agent` (gateway/run_turn.py:994), `_run_agent_inner` (gateway/run_turn.py:2293), `_run_agent_via_proxy` (gateway/run_turn.py:822), `_run_background_task_inner` (gateway/run_turn.py:482), `_execute_mcp_reload` (gateway/run_turn.py:642).
- **Data flow:** `event` + `source` → `_hmwa_resolve_session` → `_hmwa_prepare_turn` (returns `_PreparedTurn`) → `_run_agent` → `TurnRunner.run_sync` on an executor thread → `agent_result` dict → `_hmwa_shape_agent_response` → `_hmwa_persist_turn_transcript` → `_hmwa_deliver_turn_response`; the agent's own callback lane is the `TurnContext` one-slot holders wired at gateway/run_turn.py:1132-1173.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `bound_model_input_without_hygiene` keeps the leading `system`/`session_meta` rows and then *refuses* to start the kept tail on a `tool` row, because dropping the parent `assistant(tool_calls)` while keeping its tool result is an invalid sequence for every provider (gateway/run_turn.py:138-142); it is payload-only — the stored transcript is never touched, so the agent's durable-prefix slice stays valid (gateway/run_turn.py:123-127). A context overflow is recognised only by multi-word phrases, and a bare `400` counts only on a long session, so "rate limit exceeded" is not misread as an overflow (gateway/run_turn.py:110).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** proxy SSE without a terminal `[DONE]` keeps the partial text and warns instead of presenting truncation as an answer (gateway/run_turn.py:948-957); malformed SSE frames are skipped rather than aborting the stream (gateway/run_turn.py:910-911); an unbounded SSE buffer raises rather than growing (gateway/run_turn.py:942-943); a stale run generation discards the result and its deferred post-delivery callback (gateway/run_turn.py:931, gateway/run_turn.py:975, `_hmwa_discard_stale_result` in the persistence mixin); model/credential resolution failure returns a *failed* turn via `stamp_failure`, never a completed turn with an apology (gateway/run_turn.py:1233-1242, in the runner file).
- **Surprising:** the process-wide `hermes.tool_calls` logger with exactly one `RotatingFileHandler` exists because the former per-turn logger name leaked one `logging.Logger` per logged turn (#62950) (gateway/run_turn.py:55-76). `_run_agent_evict_on_fallback` deliberately skips eviction on a failed run, because evicting would loop bad-model → fallback → evict → recreate (gateway/run_turn.py:1654-1659). The queued-follow-up `return` sits *inside* the `try`, so the `finally` cancels the progress/log tasks and aborts streaming TTS *before* the follow-up turn runs (gateway/run_turn.py:2391-2399), and `_run_agent_mark_streamed_delivery` runs after that `finally` — outside the protected block (gateway/run_turn.py:2401).

### `gateway/run_turn_followup_ack.py` — Fires the adapter's `on_processing_start`/`on_processing_complete` for queued follow-ups drained *in-band* by the turn runner (the only other call site is `_process_message_background`, so read-receipt reactions would otherwise skip queued/interrupting/steer-demoted messages, #72502); the bracket at gateway/run_turn_followup_ack.py:17 requires both a real inbound marker (`message_id` or `raw_message`) and an adapter that *overrides* `on_processing_start` (so synthetic drains stay silent and complete-only adapters are not handed a completion for a turn delivered later), and a cancelled follow-up is classified CANCELLED only when the *current* task is in the adapter's `_expected_cancelled_tasks` — anything else is FAILURE, because Signal/Matrix leave the in-progress marker in place on CANCELLED (gateway/run_turn_followup_ack.py:33-50).

### `gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py`
- **Purpose:** Pre-turn auto-compression ("session hygiene") of pathologically large transcripts, plus the fail-closed bounding of the model payload when compression does not land.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTurnHygieneMixin` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:42), `_HygienePlan` dataclass (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:43-50), `_hmwa_hygiene_settings` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:94), `_hmwa_hygiene_plan` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:156), `_hmwa_hygiene_wait_for_summary` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:230), `_hmwa_hygiene_cancel_or_adopt` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:288), `_hmwa_hygiene_adopt_transcript` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:498), `_hmwa_hygiene_apply_result` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:585), `_hmwa_hygiene_codex_compaction` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:642), `_hmwa_hygiene_detached_attempt` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:699), `_hmwa_run_session_hygiene` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:765), `_bound_hygiene_payload` (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:816).
- **Data flow:** transcript `history` → settings (model/context length/knobs) → plan (needs_compress decision) → detached `AIAgent._compress_context` in the *default* executor → `CompressionCommitFence` → adopt (rotation or in-place) → session rebind + turn-lease rebind → transcript published to `attempt.history`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the 0.85 hygiene threshold is deliberately *higher* than the agent's own 0.50 compressor — hygiene is a safety net for sessions that grew between turns, not a second compressor (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:97-99). Token count is taken from the API's reported `last_prompt_tokens`, else a persisted anchor, else a rough estimate, and the estimate's known 30-50% overrun is **not** compensated with a threshold multiplier (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:168-187). A compression that would *grow* the transcript (observed 427K→598K) is refused and the original kept (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:515-526).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the transcript is rewritten only on *rotation* — in-place compaction already soft-archived rows, and `rewrite_transcript()` would `DELETE` them (#61145) (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:526-539); persistence of the rotated child happens *before* the live entry is repointed, so a failed write cannot leave the live entry on an empty session (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:540-553). The compression worker runs in the **default** executor, not `self._get_executor`, so a hung summary cannot occupy an agent-work slot (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:723), but inside `copy_context().run` so the profile secret scope survives (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:725-732). The failure cooldown is DB-backed so a restart does not re-trigger the same failing compression (#74136) (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:193-210). Turn-hold expiry is an *availability boundary, not a failure*: the streak must not advance, and a watermark-fenced worker **keeps** its commit admission so the summary is adopted later (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:339-395).
- **Surprising:** when hygiene does not land, the payload is bounded by `hard_msg_limit` while the *stored* transcript is left unchanged — the identity below the limit, a hard cut above it (#111988) (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:816-828, gateway/run_turn.py:119). A codex app-server route bypasses the detached agent entirely, because the real context is the server-side thread and the detached path's finally-eviction would destroy it (gateway/run_turn_hygiene.py:642-662).

### `gateway/run_turn_persistence.py`
- **Purpose:** The turn's persistence half — turning the raw agent result into the outbound text and writing the durable transcript rows.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTurnPersistenceMixin` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:54), `_hmwa_shape_agent_response` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:55), `_hmwa_prepend_reasoning` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:153), `_hmwa_runtime_footer_line` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:198), `_hmwa_post_turn_hooks` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:221), `_hmwa_failed_turn_notice` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:257), `_hmwa_classify_turn_failure` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:286), `_hmwa_compression_exhaustion_reset` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:323), `_hmwa_rotate_exhausted_session` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:368), `_hmwa_user_transcript_entry` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:405), `_hmwa_persist_turn_transcript` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:432), `_hmwa_deliver_turn_response` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:527), `_hmwa_agent_error_reply` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:580).
- **Data flow:** `agent_result` → `(response, _intentional_silence, agent_messages)` → reasoning prepend → footer → failure notice → compression-exhaustion reset (may rotate `session_entry`) → transcript rows (`session_meta` / `user` / `assistant`) → adapter delivery.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** two *opposite* persistence rules live in one classifier — a context-overflow failure must **not** persist the user message (the session would grow and reproduce the failure forever, #1630/#9893) while a transient failure (429/timeout/5xx) **must** (dropping it causes severe context loss on retry, #7100) (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:286-321). The *terminal* turn of a queued `/queue` chain owns the silence verdict, not the event that opened the chain (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:76-93). A failed turn is closed with an assistant boundary only when the durable tail is still an open `user` row, so a platform redelivery cannot stack a second assistant row (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:273-284).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `agent_persisted` defaults to "a session DB exists", which is how the agent's own SQLite flush is not duplicated (#860/#42039) (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:453); duplicate platform `message_id`s are skipped on the transient-failure path (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:475-479). Session-id rotation is *identity-guarded*: it applies only if the entry still points at the run's start session, otherwise the sync is skipped with a log (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:127-144). An API_SERVER session is deliberately **not** reset on compression exhaustion, because every API door re-validates the binding and a rotated entry would `admission_conflict` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:340-345); a LOCAL session's reset is handed to the authority drain because the store refuses while an admission is started (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:352-366).
- **Surprising:** user-facing error text never contains raw exception text; a 429 is split into "plan usage limit" vs "transient rate limit" by reading `error.type == "usage_limit_reached"` and `resets_in_seconds` (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:615-629), and the runtime footer is suppressed entirely on a finite (`-z`, `chat -q`) turn whose reply *is* the printed answer (gateway/run_turn_persistence.py:198-205).

### `gateway/run_turn_prepare.py`
- **Purpose:** The pre-agent half of a turn — resolve the route/model/runtime, resolve the session, assemble the `_PreparedTurn` the agent will run on.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTurnPrepareMixin` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:93), module-level `_resolve_policy_agent_runtime` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:42) and `_recover_or_remember_model` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:67), `_resolve_session_agent_runtime` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:94), `_resolve_turn_agent_config` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:213), `_hmwa_resolve_session` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:289), `_hmwa_heal_telegram_topic_binding` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:367), `_hmwa_open_session` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:416), `_hmwa_auto_load_skills` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:480), `_hmwa_acquire_turn_lease` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:508), `_hmwa_first_contact_notes` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:529), `_hmwa_apply_message_timestamp` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:587), `_PreparedTurn` dataclass (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:618-630), `_hmwa_prepare_turn` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:632).
- **Data flow:** `(event, source, session_entry, session_key)` → session open / auto-reset boundary → task-local env → pinned context prompt → sidecar notes → turn lease → transcript load → hygiene → inbound text (images/timestamps) → `_PreparedTurn` + env tokens.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** an *empty* resolved model is recovered from the last-known-good model (per session, else process-wide), because an empty model makes every API call 400 and the session goes silent (#35314) (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:67-90). Layering a session `/model` override over the default runtime sent the model to the *default provider's* endpoint, so an unavailable override provider now runs the whole default route and says so (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:155-165). The context prompt is **pinned** per session by a hash of the renderer inputs so the system prompt cannot drift turn over turn (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:652-665); a LOCAL session gets no messaging block at all, because rendering it would break the prompt cache on every surface hop of one durable session (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:654-658). Per-turn notes ride the *user* message via the api_content sidecar, never the ephemeral system prompt, which forced a full agent rebuild (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:667-668).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the turn lease fails **closed** on timeout and the session env tokens are restored before the re-raise so the exit does not leak identity (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:522-525). An unreadable store is *not* an empty conversation: `TranscriptReadError` yields a typed "history unavailable" failure instead of letting the agent invent continuity from `[]` (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:690-698). First-contact notes are skipped for LOCAL/WEBHOOK platforms because writing them would rewrite `config.yaml` under an ordinary launch (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:536-549).
- **Surprising:** the home-channel secret falls back to `os.environ` *only* for the unscoped single-profile path — a secondary profile must never borrow the default profile's home channel (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:559-568). The Telegram topic binding heal walks a compression *parent* forward to its tip and uses a CAS on the snapshot id so a concurrent `/new` wins instead of being clobbered by a stale binding (gateway/run_turn_prepare.py:391-414).

### `gateway/run_turn_progress.py`
- **Purpose:** Per-turn progress/status/tool-lifecycle plumbing — moving events from the agent's sync worker thread onto the gateway loop and out to the chat.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayTurnProgressMixin` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:104), module-level `_tool_lifecycle_payload` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:44), `_tool_complete_payload` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:58), `publish_worker_tool_event` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:73), `_append_progress_repeat_count` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:33), `_TaskCardState` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:352), `_ProgressEditState` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:564), `local_viewer_turn` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:1010), `_viewer_status_kind` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:1017).
- **Data flow:** agent thread → `progress_callback` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:147) / `combined_tool_start_callback` (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:847) → `ctx.progress_queue` → drain tasks (`send_progress_messages`, gateway/run_turn_progress.py:738; `_send_native_task_card_progress`, gateway/run_turn_progress.py:529) → adapter `send`/`edit_message`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the repeat counter `(×N)` is placed **after** the closing fence on its own line, because ` ``` (×N)` is not a valid closing fence and Telegram/Markdown renderers would leave the block open (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:33-41). The `clarify` tool is excluded from the progress bubble because the adapter's `send_clarify` *is* the user-facing rendering, and verbose mode would dump the raw args JSON directly under the prompt (#52374) (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:186-200). Native Slack task cards ride the ID-bearing callbacks, never the name-correlated text events, which would duplicate cards and mispair concurrent same-tool calls (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:181-184, gateway/run_turn_progress.py:821-845).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** an egress **decline** is terminal for the whole turn — `publication_suppressed` is stored separately from `native_failed` because reusing `native_failed` suppressed exactly one update and the next progress event then fell through to the text fallback and re-sent the same text to the refused chat (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:473-484, gateway/run_turn_progress.py:415-434). A transient edit failure keeps `can_edit=True`; only a permanent failure disables editing; flood control backs off but keeps editing (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:713-731, gateway/run_turn_progress.py:643-651). Adapters that do not implement `edit_message` get the queue silently drained instead of a bubble per update (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:749-752).
- **Surprising:** the text fallback for an *uncardable* destination (a flat DM with no thread anchor) exists only to keep a working card lane alive; with Slack's tier default (`tool_progress: off`) the lane goes silent for the turn (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:499-516). `_tool_complete_payload` carries both the modern `tool_id`/`name`/`context` contract and the legacy `tool_call_id`/`tool_name` the gateway's own consumers were built on (gateway/run_turn_progress.py:44-46).

### `gateway/run_turn_runner.py`
- **Purpose:** `TurnRunner` — the per-turn collaborator that owns every callback bound onto the cached agent for one turn, and runs the agent conversation on the executor thread.
- **Key abstractions:** `TurnRunner` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:171), `_approval_owner` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:177), `_setup_stream_consumer` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:210), `_wire_turn_agent_callbacks` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:417), `_clarify_callback_sync` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:539), `_ask_clarify_question` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:579), `_approval_notify_sync` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:663), `_render_approval_sync` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:674), `_load_turn_history` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:773), `_prepare_turn_message` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:844), `_run_conversation_with_approval` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:930), `_finish_stream_consumer` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:997), `_sync_session_after_run` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1047), `_append_auto_media_tags` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1126), `run_sync` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1155), `_run_sync_scoped` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1175); module helpers `_resolution_failure_reply` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:134), `_persisted_turn` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:81), `_LEDGER_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:70), `_CARD_DESTINATION_REFUSALS` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:45), `_TRANSCRIPT_LAG_ESCALATION_TURNS` (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:41).
- **Data flow:** `TurnContext` + `GatewayRunner` → resolve model/runtime → build/reuse agent → wire callbacks → `run_conversation` (worker thread) → result dict (with `history_offset`, usage, `persisted_turn`) → consumed by the persistence/delivery mixins.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_approval_owner = None` is a *legitimate* state (a turn with no owning session authority publishes no execution events), declared class-level so every publish/snapshot seam can read it unconditionally (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:174-177). An approval **decline** is *raised* as `_ExecApprovalDeclined` rather than returned: a plain return suppressed the text fallback but left the CENTRAL approval queue entry pending, so the dangerous command blocked until the approval timeout (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:104-111, gateway/run_turn_runner.py:733-745). The stream delta callback *raises* `_NoStreamConsumer` when nobody saw the text (e.g. a finite `-z` viewer), so the agent's partial-delivery accounting matches what the user actually saw (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:285-290).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `os.environ["HERMES_SESSION_KEY"]` is deliberately **not** written — it is process-global, so concurrent gateway sessions would clobber each other's value and a tool thread could read the wrong session key and misroute approvals (#24100) (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1186-1195). A persisted transcript that lags the live cached agent's history preserves the live conversation; three consecutive lagging turns escalate the log from WARNING to ERROR (#114266) (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:804-826). `history_offset` is forced to 0 whenever the message list lost the original prefix (session split *or* in-place compaction), because slicing past the pre-compaction length would drop everything (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1100-1104). `_finish_stream_consumer` adopts a final only when it is a genuinely completed final — an interrupt path returns a *diagnostic* `final_response` that would seal the partial answer over it (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1006-1028).
- **Surprising:** `MEDIA:` tags are appended from *this turn's* tool results only, sliced at `len(agent_history)`, so a stale tag from an earlier turn can never ride a later reply (#34608) (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:1126-1153). The clarify answer branch is on an explicit `answered` flag, never on the text — a real answer may start with `[` (a "[A] staging" label) and must not be mistaken for a sentinel (gateway/run_turn_runner.py:637-638).

### `gateway/run_turn_runner_approval_settle.py` — Arms a settle hook so an exec-approval card whose timer expires tells the chat the command did **not** run (only the TUI had registered such a hook, so on Telegram/Slack/WhatsApp the card kept live buttons after the timeout); the hook fires only for reason `"timeout"`, is guarded by `_run_still_current` so it never posts into a chat a newer run owns, edits the button card in place when the adapter returned a `message_id` (which also drops the buttons) and otherwise posts a *new* message — the plain-text prompt path must not be rewritten, since that would erase the `/approve` instructions (gateway/run_turn_runner_approval_settle.py:23-56, gateway/run_turn_runner_approval_settle.py:57-84).

### `gateway/run_turn_runner_clarify_delivery.py` — Clarify-prompt delivery as a three-state disposition (sent / failed / declined / ambiguous) plus a plain-text fallback and a bounded wait; only a *definitive* failure tears down the registration (an `ambiguous` ack timeout means the card may have posted, so the registration stays armed for a late button tap), a connector **decline** never re-sends as text (re-sending refused content as plain text is the exfiltration the egress guard exists to stop) and instead releases with `UNDELIVERED_DECLINED`, and `_clarify_send_then_wait` returns `(response, answered)` so callers branch on the explicit `answered` flag — a real answer may begin with `[` and must never be mistaken for a sentinel (gateway/run_turn_runner_clarify_delivery.py:19-25, gateway/run_turn_runner_clarify_delivery.py:42-70, gateway/run_turn_runner_clarify_delivery.py:79-118).

### `gateway/run_voice.py`
- **Purpose:** Voice-channel join/leave, transcription-driven turn routing, and auto-TTS reply decisions for the gateway runner.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayVoiceMixin` (gateway/run_voice.py:34), `_voice_key` (gateway/run_voice.py:35), `_voice_key_for_source` (gateway/run_voice.py:47), `_load_voice_modes` (gateway/run_voice.py:59), `_sync_voice_mode_state_to_adapter` (gateway/run_voice.py:107), `_handle_voice_channel_join` (gateway/run_voice.py:148), `_handle_voice_channel_leave` (gateway/run_voice.py:193), `_voice_ack_guild_for` (gateway/run_voice.py:211), `_is_duplicate_voice_transcript` (gateway/run_voice.py:233), `_voice_input_source` (gateway/run_voice.py:262), `_handle_voice_channel_input` (gateway/run_voice.py:305), `_should_send_voice_reply` (gateway/run_voice.py:371), `_send_voice_reply` (gateway/run_voice.py:419), `_deliver_voice_reply` (gateway/run_voice.py:455).
- **Data flow:** Discord voice event → `_voice_input_source` (a `SessionSource` for the bound text channel) → auth + dedupe → synthetic `MessageEvent` → the normal pipeline (`adapter.handle_message`) → optional TTS reply played in the VC or sent as a voice message.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the voice-mode key is namespaced by the *profile that owns the receiving bot* (`<profile>:<platform>:<chat_id>`), because under multiplexing two bots in one channel would otherwise flip each other's `/voice` (gateway/run_voice.py:35-46). A stored `/voice join` source is a *join-time copy* whose current `chat_name`/`chat_topic` are re-read from the channel, so a rename would not flip the pinned prompt on every switch (gateway/run_voice.py:296-300); and the bound source's `message_id` is cleared because it is the join message's id, not this turn's trigger (gateway/run_voice.py:283).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the role grant is *recomputed* for the speaker because the join copy never carried it (a role-only speaker was always refused) (gateway/run_voice.py:332-335); a parked route is refused in the voice text channel (gateway/run_voice.py:319-323). Repeated STT output is suppressed by normalised-text equality or a ≥0.95 `SequenceMatcher` ratio over 16+ chars within 12 s, keyed on (profile, guild, channel, user) (gateway/run_voice.py:233-252). Auto-TTS is refused for A2A (the peer would see a delivery failure instead of the text) and for LOCAL sessions (an owner-side synthesis nobody hears) (gateway/run_voice.py:371-395). The TTS temp files are deleted in a `finally` (gateway/run_voice.py:419-453).
- **Surprising:** the stored voice-mode file's legacy *unprefixed* keys are skipped with a warning rather than migrated, so a chat must re-enable voice mode to rebuild the prefixed key (gateway/run_voice.py:59-71). `_voice_input_source` attaches `source._transport_adapter_ref = weakref.ref(adapter)` because serialization drops transport provenance, yet authorization must still follow the receiving bot (gateway/run_voice.py:301-302).

### `gateway/run_watchers.py`
- **Purpose:** Background watcher loops bound onto `GatewayRunner` via the MRO — session housekeeping, the session-stall watchdog and the model-catalog refresher.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewaySessionWatchersMixin` (gateway/run_watchers.py:37), `_interruptible_sleep` (gateway/run_watchers.py:29), `_session_housekeeping_watcher` (gateway/run_watchers.py:40), `_session_housekeeping` (gateway/run_watchers.py:50), `_session_stall_timeout_seconds` (gateway/run_watchers.py:77), `_session_activity_for_stall` (gateway/run_watchers.py:82), `_stall_candidates` (gateway/run_watchers.py:97), `_check_session_stalls` (gateway/run_watchers.py:116), `_notify_session_stall` (gateway/run_watchers.py:145), `_model_catalog_refresh_watcher` (gateway/run_watchers.py:214), `_session_stall_watcher` (gateway/run_watchers.py:232).
- **Data flow:** agent activity summaries (`get_activity_summary()`) + adapter pending slots → `_stall_candidates` → idle-resolution → a latched notice per stall episode → chat notice; separately, idle/pressure agent-cache sweeps and the hourly `SessionStore` prune.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the stall watchdog is **notify-only** — it never kills the turn (contrast `gateway_timeout`/`shutdown_watchdog`), and progress comes *only* from `get_activity_summary()`; pending inbound is a notify gate, not a progress clock (gateway/run_watchers.py:232-241, gateway/run_watchers.py:82-95). The pending state and activity are re-read immediately before delivery, because the candidate snapshot ages while earlier candidates await sends; an abort clears the latch so a future genuine stall notifies again (gateway/run_watchers.py:174-192, #76354).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a missing `chat_id` latches the episode *without* sending (no log spam every tick) (gateway/run_watchers.py:160-163), a suppressed notification also latches (gateway/run_watchers.py:195-198), and a send failure never latches (gateway/run_watchers.py:202-210). The notice send is bounded by `asyncio.wait_for` so a wedged transport cannot block the whole watcher pass (gateway/run_watchers.py:185-194). Adapters that return `SendResult(success=False)` instead of raising are treated as failures (gateway/run_watchers.py:201-204).
- **Surprising:** the memory-pressure sweep exists because neither the LRU cap nor the idle TTL knows what a cached transcript costs — a busy gateway otherwise keeps every warm session's tool output resident until RSS hits the cgroup limit (#80764) (gateway/run_watchers.py:60-66). The stall-candidate map dedupes adapters by *identity* across the default map and every multiplex profile's map, and the first adapter to claim a session key wins (gateway/run_watchers.py:97-114).

### `gateway/runtime_bootstrap.py` — A thread-safe, 30-second-TTL store of single-use bootstrap tickets that authorise a local client to reach the gateway, with capability sets fixed per *purpose* (`interactive` = session create/read/submit/control/approve/respond, `native-http` = `http:owner`, `native-profile-create`/`native-profile-registry` = create/rename-delete of the ONE profile the ticket is minted for, `exposure` = `transport:delegate`, `worker-adoption` = `worker:adopt`); `scope='host'` is only legal for `interactive` (the Desktop's one shared socket may select every served profile), tickets are stored as a SHA-256 hash of the secret rather than in the clear, redemption is single-use and exact-match on profile/purpose unless `profile_id=None` (which accepts any served profile and lets the returned grant select the authority), and the store is capacity-capped at 1024 entries with expired ones purged on mint (gateway/runtime_bootstrap.py:9-22, gateway/runtime_bootstrap.py:25-83).

### `gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py`
- **Purpose:** The Windows named-pipe transport and identity checks for the native (local-only) bootstrap/control channel.
- **Key abstractions:** `NativeControlServer` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:309), `query_runtime_control` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:269), `judge_pipe_server` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:456), `classify_refused_owner` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:417), `refused_owner_remedy` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:432), `probe_refused_owner` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:532), `RefusedOwner` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:447); Win32 helpers `_pipe_security` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:86), `_pipe_client_sid` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:131), `_peer_subject` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:161), `_accept` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:223), `_read_line` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:242), `_complete` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:206), `_disconnect_pipe` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:188).
- **Data flow:** client `CreateFile` with `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT|SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION` → server accepts on one of 8 overlapped pipe instances → reads one line (≤64 KiB, 2 s) → identifies the peer (client token first, then the client process) → handler → one line back → `DisconnectNamedPipe`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the pipe's DACL is built as a protected SDDL naming exactly the current user's SID — no inherited Everyone/Users ACEs (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:340-360). The client dials with `SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION` so the server may *read* (never act as) the client's token, captured at dial time, so it can still identify a client that has already exited (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:131-136, gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:271-274). The pipe object's owner+DACL are read via `GetSecurityInfo` because that needs no access to the server *process* and therefore still answers when the server is elevated and the client is not (#140706) (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:86-115). `judge_pipe_server` is a *pure* function over probed Win32 facts (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:456-504).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a non-elevated client never hands requests to an elevated server, because the server's tools would then run with the full administrator token (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:474-484); when the server process is unreadable, the *owner* carries the trust and the ACE shape only filters out pipes that are not the gateway's private shape (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:485-496). A refusal between two processes of one user is classified as `gateway_elevated` or `gateway_other_session` instead of the generic `authorization` code, and `str(RefusedOwner)` is the *remedy text* (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:417-455). `_ImpersonationLeak` is deliberately a `RuntimeError`, not an `OSError`, so the serving loop cannot swallow it (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:126-129).
- **Surprising:** `DisconnectNamedPipe` is called through `kernel32` by hand because CPython's `_winapi` does not export it, and the server thread must outlive its first client — a dead pipe leaves the gateway stuck in `starting` for every later `identify` (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:188-197). `_accept` treats error 995 (`ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED`) as the only idle outcome and still serves a client that connected between the wait timing out and the cancel reaching the kernel (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:223-240). The reader must read *before* impersonating, since `ImpersonateNamedPipeClient` requires data already read from the pipe (gateway/runtime_bootstrap_windows.py:376-378).

### `gateway/runtime_contract.py` — The OS-independent launch decision table: `next_start_action(RuntimeObservation)` maps the seven runtime states onto the actions `attach` / `wait-owner` / `wait-update` / `reject-version` / `reject-access` / `reject-conflict` / `wait-service` / `start-service` / `spawn-unmanaged`; an observation with `update_paused=True` wins over every state (a pending update is not a runtime problem), `absent` is the only state that consults `installed_service`/`service_may_start`, and any unknown state raises `ValueError` rather than defaulting (gateway/runtime_contract.py:12-17, gateway/runtime_contract.py:19-37).

### `gateway/runtime_downgrade_exit.py` — Stops a gateway whose checkout was moved back to a release that predates the gateway runtime: the watcher polls its own tree every 30 s, requires **two consecutive** confirming reads that the state-store modules no longer define the `session_admissions` ledger, refuses to act while an update holds the tree, and then exits with the service-restart code (75) so systemd relaunches while the launchd plist (`KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit=false`) does not park the gateway down for good; the probes are bound at gateway start because a lazy import after the tree moved would load the *older* release's module into the running process, and the stop task is created detached and strongly held because `stop()` cancels every background task including the watcher that awaits it (gateway/runtime_downgrade_exit.py:29-42, gateway/runtime_downgrade_exit.py:53-62, gateway/runtime_downgrade_exit.py:64-104).

### `gateway/runtime_footer.py` — renders the optional trailing runtime-metadata line ("model · context % · cwd"); pins that `enabled` defaults to **False**, that a platform override is layered after the global one, and that unknown field names / fields with no data are skipped silently rather than printing `?%` (runtime_footer.py:48-62, :81-103).

### `gateway/runtime_ownership.py` — all-or-nothing flock reservation of canonical profile homes; pins that a held lock inode is **never unlinked** — only a foreign-uid inode provably unheld by a read-only `flock` probe is replaced, and every winner re-checks the path still names the inode it locked (runtime_ownership.py:1-6, :61-91, :131-137).

### `gateway/scale_to_zero.py`
- **Purpose:** decides when the gateway has been idle long enough to quiesce (relay `go_dormant`) and then suspend the machine.
