- **Key abstractions:** `scale_to_zero_enabled` (scale_to_zero.py:50), `parse_idle_timeout_seconds` (:55), `messaging_is_relay_only_or_absent` (:66), `should_arm` (:75), `is_idle` (:82), `dashboard_client_heartbeat_path`/`touch_dashboard_client_heartbeat`/`dashboard_client_last_seen` (:92-127), `self_suspend_available` (:129), `brokered_sleep_url` (:137), `suspend_available` (:164), `request_brokered_suspend` (:197), `suspend_self` (:235).
- **Data flow:** env stamp + config idle timeout + connected-platform names + last-inbound clock + dashboard heartbeat mtime → `is_idle` → relay quiesce → either a POST to the Fly flaps unix socket or a POST to the NAS broker's sleep URL.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** enablement is gated **solely by the NAS "Labs" env stamp** (`HERMES_SCALE_TO_ZERO`), *not* by config, while the idle timeout *is* config — so a config-only opt-in can never arm the watcher (scale_to_zero.py:8-9, :50-52). The gateway suspends **itself** rather than letting Fly Proxy do it, because Proxy only sees inbound proxied connections and would suspend mid-turn or before `go_dormant()` flipped the relay destination (a buffered-event black hole) (:1-9). `is_idle`'s `active_work_count` must be the BROAD aggregate (agent turns + cron + API runs); a caller that cannot read a work source must pass a **positive sentinel** to fail AWAKE (:84-89).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a missing dashboard-heartbeat file returns `None` (steady state, *not* fail-awake — otherwise no instance would ever sleep) while an unreadable file returns `now` (fail-awake); the mtime is clamped to `now` so an NTP step-back cannot park it in the future (:113-127). `parse_idle_timeout_seconds` never returns ≤ 0 (a non-positive value degrades to the 2-minute default) (:55-63). `brokered_sleep_url` validates `https` + netloc at parse time, before the watcher marks draining — a malformed value would otherwise quiesce for a suspend that could never happen (:137-161). `request_brokered_suspend` never raises, returns True only on 2xx, and does not retry (:197-232). `suspend_self` is stdlib-only raw HTTP/1.1 over `AF_UNIX` so nothing can freeze mid-await (:235-268).
- **Surprising:** `BROKERED_SUSPEND_TIMEOUT_S = 40.0` deliberately exceeds the broker's own 30 s ceiling — giving up while the broker still works would release the re-dial hold, clear the flip, and land a stop on a live destination (:173-176). `FLY_FREEZE_GRACE_S = 15.0` is sized from four measured suspends (2.25–4.19 s) whose gap grows with machine size, and is not paid on wake (:178-194). The dashboard heartbeat has **deliberately no staleness cutoff** — recency is decided by `is_idle` (:39-43).

### `gateway/session.py`
*(large file — read in three windows: head 1-560, middle 561-1120, tail 1121-1361; that is the file's full 1361-line span.)*
- **Purpose:** the message source/routing model (`SessionSource`, `SessionEntry`), the persisted routing index (`SessionStore`), and the dynamic "Current Session Context" system-prompt section.
- **Key abstractions:** `SessionSource` (session.py:67), `SessionContext` (:166), `build_session_context_prompt` (:378), `SessionEntry` (:483), `build_session_key` (:686), `SessionStore` (:785), `AsyncSessionStore` (:768), `_RouteChecks`/`_RouteDecision` (:737/:746), `build_channel_continuity_note` (:633), `sanitize_model_override` (:472), `build_session_context` (:1344), `_is_path_unsafe` (:47).
- **Data flow:** `SessionSource` → `build_session_key` (deterministic route) → `SessionStore.get_or_create_session` (single-flight, index in `sessions.json` mirrored to state.db) → `SessionEntry` → `build_session_context` → prompt section injected into the system prompt; token/cost counters and `last_prompt_tokens` flow back via `update_session` (:1070).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the key namespace is `agent:main` **byte-identical** for the default profile, but a profile literally named `main` becomes `agent:main~` because `~` is outside the profile-id alphabet — otherwise it would share every session with the default (:665-674). DM and group keys order their slots differently: DMs put the participant **before** the thread, groups/threads after (:723-726). Slack's `scope_id` is in the key but Discord's guild scope deliberately is **not**, for key compatibility with historical keys (:692-694, :719-720). `scope_id`/`guild_id` are dual-written and mirrored in `__post_init__` so readers of either agree (:83-85, :104-109, :141-144). `delivered_via_upstream_relay` is deliberately absent from `to_dict`/`from_dict` so a peer cannot forge the relay trust signal (:99-102).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_is_path_unsafe` is **strict** for a session id (it becomes a filename: no `..`, no separator, no drive letter) but `strict=False` for a logical session key, where an interior `/` is legitimate (Google Chat `spaces/<id>/threads/<id>`) — only a *leading* one is refused (:47-61, :604-609). `from_dict` refuses a partially-written `active_turn_token`/`active_turn_started_at` pair (the pair is written atomically; a half pair must not auto-resume) (:598-602). `yolo` is tri-state: rows written before the tri-state stored a bare `False` meaning "never toggled", so only their `True` is a real toggle (:614-616, :569-570). `get_or_create_session` is single-flight per key — overlapping calls, including concurrent `force_new`, share the owner's result so only one transition and one SQLite row exist (:895-931). All blocking I/O (SQLite SELECTs, index rewrite + fsync, recovery queries) runs **outside** `self._lock`, which guards only `_entries`/`_loaded` (:933-981); `_apply_route_checks` re-reads under the lock and discards the snapshot if another thread replaced the entry during the lock-free window (:983-1028).
- **Surprising:** `set_session_metadata` deliberately does **not** advance `updated_at` — a background write must not make an idle session look fresh to the restart-resume gate (:1097-1104), and `switch_session` likewise leaves it alone so a background compression cannot fake freshness (:1251-1253). The SQLite handle is resolved per call and never bound once: a multiplexed gateway serves every profile from one process, so a handle frozen to the root home would land every profile's rows in the root `state.db` (#88532) (:824-857). `remove_by_session_id` exists because this index is written back by the owning process, so a DB-only delete elsewhere is undone by the next whole-index save and the deleted conversation comes back (#42422) (:1225-1249).

### `gateway/session_a2a.py` — A2A peer forwarding with exact conversation identity; pins that only the forwarding handler can mint an `a2a` policy (via the `_forward_identity` ContextVar) and that a pre-authority titled row is warned about but **never adopted**, because its title discarded peer/tenant identity (session_a2a.py:18-27, :117-127).

### `gateway/session_acp_lifecycle.py` — ACP v0.9 has no per-session destroy, so the gateway ends an ACP session when its last viewer leaves; pins that a session with live subscribers or a busy FIFO is never ended, and that a viewer who re-attaches mid-close reopens the row (session_acp_lifecycle.py:1-7, :16-32, :56-64).

### `gateway/session_active_list.py` — the `session.active_list` verb projecting the authority's live turns; pins that the population equals the live `session.list` feed (hidden chats included) and that an admitted-but-unclaimed turn reads as `starting`, not `idle` (session_active_list.py:15-20, :34-43).

### `gateway/session_admission.py` — gateway-facing re-export of `hermes_state_keys.admission_fingerprint`; pins that the canonical payload digest is defined storage-side so the canonical store never has to import `gateway/` (session_admission.py:1-8).

### `gateway/session_ancillary.py`
- **Purpose:** Desktop/Ink projections of existing owner state plus the session-scoped process verbs (no legacy server, no manager construction, no execution on reads).
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (session_ancillary.py:24), `subagent_control` (:38), `show_tools` (:64), `reload_skills` (:107), `attach_file` (:136), `approvals` (:168), `read` (:193), `_owned_targets` (:220), `kill_process` (:240), `reload_mcp` (:254), `stop_processes` (:356), `control_snapshot` (:419), `subagent_records` (:476), `process_snapshot` (:559), `_require_current_route` (:384), `_owner_objects` (:395).
- **Data flow:** RPC verb → `authorize(connection, ref, capability)` → session-scoped lookup into the in-process registries (`tools.process_registry`, `tools.delegate_tool_registry`) → row projection; expensive scans run on worker threads via `asyncio.to_thread` (:104, :125, :251, :276).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** every verb calls `authorize` first so a deleted/evicted live entry surfaces as `not_found` rather than a KeyError deeper in the handler (:180-217 as used by each verb). Cold adoption belongs to `session.resume`, **never** an ancillary poll (:200-202). `_require_current_route` refuses a reused route — a route that moved on to another conversation may not lend its objects (:384-392). `_owned_locked` reads the process registry's internals directly rather than calling `list_sessions`, because that method refreshes recovered processes and **writes checkpoints** (:552-556).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `kill_process` raises `process_not_found`, never `not_found`, because Desktop reads the latter as "this chat is gone" (dokterdok N64) (:248-250). `reload_mcp` requires an explicit `confirm: true`, otherwise it returns `status: 'confirm_required'` without touching anything (:267-268), and refuses with `mcp_servers_unavailable` only when the reload itself took no effect (:286-291). A call naming the `rev` the session already holds is answered as **success** with `loaded_rev` — refusing it left clients retrying a parked server at poll rate with no way to close the handshake (#141769) (:284-291). A managed worker session is refused with `unsupported_projection` rather than answered with an empty success, because a process-local registry cannot describe another interpreter's live objects (:395-408). `attach_file` maps `ValueError`→`invalid_params` and `OSError`→`storage_unavailable` (:159-162).
- **Surprising:** `stop_processes` kills only the processes this session owns, never the registry-wide `kill_all` the legacy sidecar ran — on the shared owner that reached every chat, Desktop window and served profile (dokterdok N2) (:356-360). The subagent roster follows the **compression lineage** (:476-502): without it, a cached-agent rebuild (`/reasoning`, `/model`, a policy refresh, idle eviction) hid still-running children from the dock while the status bar kept counting them. `control_snapshot` deliberately avoids `_snapshot_control` because that legacy loader can write on a read (:419-428).

### `gateway/session_api.py`
- **Purpose:** server-only binding of API transcript identities (session id ↔ route key ↔ persisted storage row) to the existing TurnRunner.
- **Key abstractions:** `declared_api_session` (session_api.py:15), `implicit_api_session` (:28), `api_generation_id` (:43), `continued_api_session` (:51), `room_session_id` (:60), `hosted_member_session` (:67), `_pin_api_identity` (:82), `run_steps` (:93), `run_steps_off_loop` (:107), `bind_api_session`/`bind_api_steps` (:142/:149), `api_storage_source` (:268), `restore_api_session` (:277).
- **Data flow:** caller key / explicit session id → generation walk (`first_generation`) → `bind_api_steps` yields **one** SQLite write → binding receipt in `state_meta` + `sessions` row + `gateway_routing` entry → `restore_api_session` rehydrates the `LiveSession` and the store entry.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** an API session is the "shared-listener row of the transport matrix": the launch profile's `api_server` adapter receives `/p/<name>/` and answers it, while the turn **runs** under the authority's own profile — both ride the persisted source, `source.profile` placing the runtime and `transport_profile` naming the delivering bot after a restart (:177-189, :82-90). A deleted id stays retired **for good**: a NEW conversation that opens like a deleted one moves to the next generation `api-<digest(id,n)>` — exactly how a deleted Bot Chat is replaced (:28-48). `run_steps_off_loop` serializes API admission drives per authority with an `asyncio.Lock` so one API door's lookups/binding/admission stay one step relative to every other door, and shields the drive so a cancelled caller cannot separate a commit from the steps after it (:125-139).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** binding refuses a retired session with `not_found` and a chain whose sweep fences aged out with the retryable `chain_moved` (:203-208). A declared key already owned by another session is `admission_conflict`; a stored binding with a different `storage_source` is `permission_denied` (:209-224, :225-232). Legacy compatibility: earlier releases stored the caller-declared key directly on the session row (#139835) — a caller presenting that exact key adopts the row onto the canonical route, while any other stored key still conflicts (:233-249). A `bot_room` session must match its room identity, title and hidden flag, or the restore is `admission_conflict` (:297-301). `restore_api_session` keeps the current entry when it is already the physical target, so a compression continuation is not clobbered (:302-310).
- **Surprising:** `run_steps` drives a write-step generator inline and **re-throws** exceptions back into the generator rather than swallowing them — the generator's own error handling decides the outcome (:93-104). The `api_server` platform's `user_id` is the literal `'api'` and chat_type `'dm'` regardless of the real conversation (:182-183).

### `gateway/session_api_media.py` — API inline `data:` images become committed content-addressed media; pins that the **bytes** decide the committed type (SDKs routinely label JPEG as `data:image/png`) and that the whole batch is validated before one byte lands on disk, so a refused request leaves no staged bytes (session_api_media.py:30-56).

### `gateway/session_api_turn.py`
- **Purpose:** trusted API preparation and observation of the canonical durable FIFO.
- **Key abstractions:** `api_execution` ContextVar (session_api_turn.py:15), `AdmissionQueued` (:29), `api_settings` (:41), `check_api_turn` (:48), `_rebind_browser_control` (:93), `api_session_vars` (:110), `check_api_settings` (:123), `api_policy_scope` (:141), `admit_api_turn`/`admit_api_turn_async` (:156/:161), `schedule_orphan` (:169), `admit_api_steps` (:176), `_admit_api_payload` (:254), `_admit_target` (:273), `_one_target` (:282), `owns_api_run` (:298), `recover_api_turns`/`recover_authority_api_turns` (:317/:328), `run_api_turn` (:352), `observe_api_turn` (:377), `observe_api_controls` (:438), `prepare_api_execution` (:474), `wire_api_observers` (:512), `api_runtime_locked` (:584), `prepare_api_runtime` (:592).
- **Data flow:** HTTP door → `admit_api_steps` (payload built, media committed under a capture lease) → `retry_terminal_admission` (exact retry) → `bind_api_steps` → `admit_session_input` → durable row → drain executes → observers released via `authority.waiters` / `authority.api_observers`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `AdmissionQueued` is a refusal with committed input — the row stays queued and the next owner runs it **exactly once**, so a client that resends submits the work twice; its `str()` is the human message every API surface shows (:29-38). Route credentials never reach the persisted admission: `api_key` is stripped from the stored `route`, and a route that does not match the server's configured model route is `permission_denied` (:186-190). The browser-controller identity is **server-derived** (`_request_browser_control`, :83-91) and re-derived against the profile's *current* API key before execution — a rotated key leaves that turn unbound, exactly like a request the middleware stamped nothing for (:93-108).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_one_target` makes one Idempotency-Key name one admission and refuses the same key aimed at another session; a deleted session's tombstone still spends the key, and only a **client** key can name a tombstone (a server-minted id skips that scan) (:282-296). `observe_api_turn` shields its waiter so a cancelled request does not kill the canonical turn — only that request's observer leaves, and the observer entry goes once the last one is gone (:377-421, esp. :713-740). Observer callbacks that raise are logged and skipped so one closed socket cannot abort canonical execution or starve the other observers (:495-509). `admit_api_steps` holds a `capture_lease` so a concurrent media release cannot unlink bytes between capture and commit, and releases them on refusal (:219-233).
- **Surprising:** the API turn's `settings` are persisted per session id in `state_meta` **in the admitted turn's own transaction**, not by the drain on the owner loop (N50) (:273-280). `check_api_turn` treats `api_turn_v1` as a closed vocabulary — any unknown key is `invalid_params`, and `resume_unanswered` must be exactly `True` (:52-65). `observe_api_controls` subscribes and snapshots in **one** stream-lock hold so a prompt is either in the snapshot or arrives live, never both (:438-471).

### `gateway/session_authorities.py`
- **Purpose:** one `SessionAuthority` per reserved profile home, resolved by the active runtime scope.
- **Key abstractions:** `SessionAuthorities` (session_authorities.py:22), `LocalBot` (:114), `local_bots` (:125), `served_profile_name` (:154), `authority_for_home` (:163), `active_authority` (:178), `require_active_authority` (:187), `serving_authorities` (:210), `require_serving` (:221), `admission_owner` (:228), `holds_admission` (:240), `owner_scope` (:257).
- **Data flow:** `hermes_home_key(home)` → authority in a dict; `get_hermes_home_override()` distinguishes scoped (routed profile) from unscoped (startup/shutdown/watcher) code.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `active()` returns **None** for a scoped home this process does not serve — a routed profile must never fall back to the launch profile's ledger, and that stays true after a removed secondary leaves only the launch slot (a late callback of that profile) (:87-98). The `multiplexed` flag latches True as soon as a second home is added and is never unlatched, so a runtime that ever multiplexed keeps the scoped contract (:25-40, :95-98). A single-profile gateway is "a map of one": the launch authority answers every lookup, scoped or not, exactly as before (:1-12).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `require_active_authority` refuses with a **retryable** `runtime_draining` while the profile is parked, else `profile_mismatch`; and when given a `store` callable, that store must **be** the authority's db — an API adapter bound to another profile's SessionDB never admits here (:187-199). `require_serving` refuses verbs reached through a stale connection to a retired authority, because until a successor bumps the epoch its own epoch still passes the store fence (:221-225). `holds_admission` counts a parked profile's retained store that cannot answer as **holding** one, so startup auto-resume fails closed for that entry instead of stopping the boot (:240-254). `remove` refuses to drop the launch slot (:49-56); `add`/`replace` raise on duplicate/unreserved slots (:33-47).
- **Surprising:** `owner_scope` is a **no-op** for a single-profile gateway (ambient scope byte-for-byte) — entering a scope there would read the profile's config/.env for every handler, including `--safe-mode` launches that must never touch the profile YAML (:257-281). A standalone gateway that already hosts a second home binds the launch profile's own scope instead: unbound, every launch-profile `session.create` raised `UnscopedSecretError` (#141623) (:285-299). `admission_owner` resolves the compression root and then the local creation id, because `logical_owner` alone stops at a reset child and misses that conversation's admissions (:228-237).

### `gateway/session_authority.py`
*(large file — read in three windows: head 1-570, middle 571-1130, tail 1131-1409; that is the file's full 1409-line span.)*
- **Purpose:** the `SessionAuthority` — gateway-owned live sessions and the canonical durable FIFO that schedules them.
- **Key abstractions:** `LiveSession` (session_authority.py:43), `keyed_turn` (:103), `SessionAuthority` (:121), `authorize` (:180), `_require_admission_open` (:218), `_admission_gate` (:229), `logical_owner` (:238), `register`/`_reserve_route`/`_publish_route` (:250/:253/:264), `attach` (:323), `detach` (:363), `_publish_pending` (:405), `_schedule` (:424), `_pause` (:440), `admit_native`/`_route_turn` (:461/:475), `fence_route` (:510), `_admit_native` (:515), `submit`/`_submit` (:632/:652), `receipt` (:718), `cancel_queued`/`cancel_or_claimed` (:732/:744), `_settle_cancelled` (:777), `resolve_unknown` (:803), `interrupt` (:861), `steer_agent`/`adopt_agent` (:918/:934), `_claim_next` (:1002), `_stamp_claim` (:1080), `_claim_with_retry` (:1103), `_execute_claimed` (:1141), `_drain` (:1178), `initialize_session_authority` (:1370).
- **Data flow:** `submit` / `admit_native` → a durable `session_admissions` row in state.db → the per-session drain claims it (`claim_session_input`, off the owner loop but **in admission order**) → execution → off-loop settlement → the completion frame + waiter resolution + delivery.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the claim commits in a worker thread but ordered (`tracked_write(ordered=True)`) so a held SQLite writer cannot freeze every session, while the admission's own steps stay one step relative to other doors (:1072-1075). `_stamp_claim` publishes the execution stamp in the **same tracked task** as the commit, so no observer (or retirement's join) ever sees a committed claim that no execution stamp names (:1080-1093). `LiveSession.settling` masks a settled-but-not-yet-published row as still-started, so no snapshot shows a terminal row without its completion (:62-66, :285-296). `_route_turn` serializes one route's inputs by the order they entered `admit_native` — a photo's off-loop capture must not let the chat's next text take the earlier FIFO position (:475-504).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_require_admission_open(queue_only=True)` lets a restart's after-turn wait accept a **queued** row while the claim path stays refused (#139041) (:218-227). Transient storage errors (writer lock, full disk) are retried **in place** with capped backoff 0.25→5.0 s rather than releasing waiters — releasing them fails requests that would run a moment later (:1121-1139, :29-30). `unknown_execution` pauses the FIFO, but an unattended automation turn settles itself instead (GW-10) (:1111-1116, :1159-1171). A refused submission releases its captured bytes as durable retirement candidates, and that release's failure never replaces the refusal the client must see (:699-716). `cancel_queued` records a cancel obligation **before** the write, so a commit that reports failure is still paid by an exact retry (:759-775). A classic turn holding the route's slot parks the head as `classic_busy` rather than handing the row to the busy path (:1062-1066). `authorize` may **resurrect** a session: if it is not live but its row exists, it restores local/routed/api (:190-206).
- **Surprising:** a superseded lane — a historic messaging row mounted for reading after its route moved on — is **read-only** (`session_superseded`), because the lane's route now belongs to a newer conversation and a write would land there (:207-212). The execution stamp is cleared on **every** exit, so an idle mutation never carries a terminal generation and a versioned viewer fence cannot discard it as a late frame (:1280-1285). `initialize_session_authority` resolves the db path, begins the epoch and recovers inputs **off-loop before anything is published**, so no task observes a half-registered authority (:1376-1394). `move_route_wakes`/`reset_and_rehome_messages` carry queued route wakes onto the fresh session after a rotation, else they stay queued forever behind a refused drain (session_automation.py:493-535).

### `gateway/session_automation.py`
- **Purpose:** trusted producer admission — heartbeats, process completions and route wakes reuse the native route envelope and FIFO while keeping non-human turn semantics.
- **Key abstractions:** `producer_identity` (session_automation.py:19), `completion_admission` (:26), `deliver_heartbeat` (:43), `admit_process_notice` (:82), `prepare_heartbeat` (:100), `_replace_queued` (:150), `withdraw_process_ticks` (:163), `_heartbeat_lock` (:189), `snapshot_automation` (:345), `check_automation_route` (:391), `snapshot_local_automation` (:408), `check_local_automation` (:432), `restore_local_automation` (:448), `admit_automation` (:465), `move_route_wakes` (:493), `_reset_and_retarget_local` (:546), `reset_and_rehome_messages` (:572), `admit_route_wake` (:655).
- **Data flow:** producer event → identity JSON → snapshot that **borrows** the route's last committed native envelope (provenance + source) → `admit_session_input` under principal `automation:<route>` → the same FIFO the human turns use.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** a heartbeat tick **folds** the previous queued tick's output instead of sending its own, so a busy session gets **one** waiting turn holding every tick's output rather than one turn per tick; the tick's output is never sent twice (:100-132). The replaced tick's cancellation commits in the replacement's **own** transaction via a write guard, so neither lands without the other (:150-160, :478-487). A producer ACK means committed input, not successful inference or outbound delivery (:1-5).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a tick whose process's completion is already admitted is **dropped** as a stale "still running" turn (:117-121), and a retry of a tick already admitted is dropped as a lost-acknowledgement retry (:122-125). The heartbeat lock makes a tick's check-and-admit and a completion's admit-and-withdraw atomic relative to each other, so a tick checked before the completion landed cannot run as a stale turn (:189-196, :82-97). `withdraw_process_ticks` never raises — the completion is already admitted and its retry takes the lost-acknowledgement path (:163-186). `reset_and_rehome_messages` returns a `release()` closure that must be called **once**: until then the fresh session's `claim_gate` is held, so no scheduler starts a turn there against stale state (:586-652). A cancelled rotation still commits and takes the fence — the fence is lifted in the done callback or the fresh FIFO never runs (:627-636).
- **Surprising:** the persisted envelope's timestamp is pinned to epoch 0 and the producer timestamp / platform reply id are dropped, because those change on retry and neither belongs in the identity/fingerprint of the same immutable completion (:376-381). Synthetic wakes clear `message_id`, so the routing/provenance identity is compared rather than the platform reply anchor (:367-375). `notification_origin`, `original_trigger_message_id`, `gateway_proven_session_id` and `gateway_completion_ids` are **accepted but not persisted** — a sibling set in the fingerprint would make the same completion's retry a new identity (:301-311). `move_route_wakes` refuses to move anything behind an `unknown` head (the operator's to resume) and never moves completions pinned to a closed conversation (:493-527).

### `gateway/session_bot.py`
- **Purpose:** Turns relayed bot messages ("Bot Chat" ingress) into canonical FIFO admissions on the profile's single `Bot Chat` session, using a file-backed receipt as the durable delivery record rather than a local queue.
- **Key abstractions:** `deliver` (:700), `settle` (:791), `relay_operation` (:587), `recover_bot_deliveries` (:642), `peer_wait_admission` (:449), `displaceable_bot_chat` (:205). Internal: `_canonical_record_error` (:41), `_record_shape_error` (:62), `_is_pre_admission_pin` (:77), `_scan_records` (:87), `_scan_owner_receipts` (:112), `_admission_outcome` (:340), `_expired_outcome` (:367), `_retry_eligible` (:473), `_maybe_retry` (:491), `_admit` (:865), `_publish_result` (:913). Constants `_CANONICAL_RECEIPT_STATUSES` (:25), `_BOT_DM_MAX_CHARS` (:28), `_OWNER_STATE_REFUSALS` (:34), `_PRODUCER_MAX_CHARS` (:38), `_PIN_GRACE_SECONDS` (:109).
- **Data flow:** `deliver` → `_delivery_params` (:715) validates the identity `(message, author, notification_category)` and caps size → under `mailbox_lock` the receipt is read (`read_receipt`) → `_migrate` (:612) admits any legacy tickets first → `_target` (:182) resolves/creates the Bot Chat → `_admit` (:865) writes an `ambiguous` PIN to disk *before* `authority.admit_automation`, then flips the pin to `canonical` with the admission id → `_result` (:402) reads the outcome from the FIFO (never from transcript recency) → `_publish_result` (:913) writes the projection back. Replies arrive asynchronously: `_watch_reply` (:683) creates a task on `authority.waiters[admission_id]`; `_record_reply` (:553) re-reads the receipt, re-derives the result, and may admit the one retry.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **The pin-then-admit two-store window** (:881-895): `_admit` writes `status='ambiguous'` to the receipt file BEFORE the FIFO insert, so a process death in that gap leaves an explicit *unknown* record, never a silently lost message. The pin is withdrawn only when the FIFO definitely refused (no `bot:<id>` row) and only while the file is still that same pin (`withdraw_pin` refuses if the status/admission changed).
  - **Two refusal classes decide withdraw vs. terminal refuse** (:34-37, :903-912): `_OWNER_STATE_REFUSALS` (`queue_full`, `runtime_draining`, `runtime_retired`, `stale_epoch`, `stale_generation`, `storage_unavailable`, `runtime_coordination_required`, `session_busy`, `not_found`) describe the OWNER's state, so the pin is withdrawn and a same-id resend admits once it clears. Every other refusal (`permission_denied`, `invalid_params`, `admission_conflict`, `profile_mismatch`) is final for that delivery and gets `refuse_pin` — a terminal receipt.
  - **`settle` exists because `admitted=False` is a hint, not a verdict** (:801-829, :849-863): a refusal marked `admitted=False` proves absence only *while that request held the lock*; a same-id attempt may admit right after. The sender therefore calls `settle` before it says "NOT sent", and `_not_sent_record` (:831) re-checks there is no `bot:<id>` admission row and that a Bot Chat exists. `_held_by_another_writer` (:849) is the only refusal converted into a settleable one.
  - **The retry is admitted under a DERIVED identity written before the admission** (:491-551, :469): `bot:<key>:retry`, recorded on the same receipt first, so a death in that two-store window re-admits the SAME identity, which the FIFO answers with the existing row (never a second turn). `_retry_eligible` (:473) requires the original to have *settled* `failed` with a committed result classifying transient — queued/started/unknown admissions never qualify ("unknown execution is never replayed"), and the retry itself is never retried.
  - **A failed turn that left the DM as an OPEN tail is not retried** (:505-509): `open_user_tail` is refused, because the owner has no adoption seam and the retry's DM would merge into the open one.
  - **The canonical title can be stolen by a row no FIFO can own** (:205-278): `displaceable_bot_chat` moves such a row aside under a *visible* name (`Bot Chat (API <id>)`, or `Bot Chat (telegram <id>)`), because the canonical title is that row's identity and it would refuse every delivery forever. `_kept_local_history` (:233) protects CLI/TUI/Desktop local history a native open can adopt; `_moved_aside_by_this_door` (:250) exists so the first delivery after the door's own move is not refused `admission_conflict` and lost to the next beat.
  - **The creation identity is derived from durable rows only** (:320-338): `bot-chat:<profile>` for generation 0, `bot-chat:<profile>:<n>` after. A generation is spent once its session was deleted (retired) or renamed, so a retry never resurrects a retired session; two concurrent deliveries (serialized by the authority's titled-create lock, :288-290) pick the same identity, and an existing UNtitled session is treated as the same creation interrupted before its title.
  - **A deleted target and store damage are different outcomes** (:367-387): a retired session → `ambiguous` (never resent); a row gone with no deletion marker → `ambiguous` plus a warning ("store damage, or a marker an older release reclaimed"); a *live* session missing its admission → `RuntimeStoreError('storage_unavailable')`, a fault rather than a normal outcome.
  - **A successful bare silence marker is a delivery decision, not prose** (:352-359): the turn stays in the target's transcript but the marker is stripped from the reply the sender sees.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** blank/non-string message, over-length message, or a bad id → `invalid_params` (:715-739). The size cap is *dual*: a relayed bot DM (which carries an `author`) is capped at 16 200 chars; a trusted producer report (cron bot-chat, no author) gets a 1 MiB *resource* bound only, because the relay already capped it at the relay edge (:728-733). A malformed receipt on disk is warned and skipped in a bulk scan but still fails closed on an exact-id read (:87-104). `queue_full` on the retry keeps the delivery `claimed`/pending so the next wake retries (:539-545); `runtime_draining` on the retry leaves it `claimed` so startup recovery performs the same derived retry (:503-508). An admission that aged out after the chat was deleted leaves the receipt's own answer standing with `reason=UNKNOWN` (:417-423). `recover_bot_deliveries` (:642) catches per-receipt `RuntimeStoreError` and continues — one unreadable receipt must not stop the owner, and under multiplexing every profile rides on it (:661-670). `deliver`/`settle` wrap their work in `asyncio.shield(track_mutation(...))` (:700-705, :791-799) so a cancellation cannot abort an admission mid-flight.
- **Surprising:** `relay_operation` (:587) is a *synchronous* module-level function in a file of async ones, and it calls `require_serving(authority)` because the outbox/roster/replies belong to the home's NEXT owner, not the current one. `_migrate` (:612) computes a FIFO *barrier* — the lowest `_legacy_order` of an unresolved legacy pin — and no legacy ticket ordered after it may be admitted; a pin without an integer order sorts to `-inf` and therefore holds every legacy ticket until it resolves (:164-169). `peer_wait_admission` (:449) returns an `interim` flag meaning "pending, never the answer" while the owner's receipt task is still deciding whether to admit the retry. The `_PIN_GRACE_SECONDS = 300` (:109) is 300× the comment's claim that `_admit` takes "well under a second" — it is the safety margin for a crashed admission, and it is also the window inside which a legacy pin blocks the FIFO.

### `gateway/session_bot_mailbox.py` — Authority-local async serialization of the Bot mailbox: `mailbox_lock` (:9) is an asyncio.Lock plus an active-operation set that calls `persist_work_count` on exit, and every physical file read/write runs through `_mailbox_io` (:27), which `asyncio.shield`s a `to_thread` task so a cancelled caller still finishes its I/O; `write_receipt` (:79) is the compare-and-swap — `expect=` refuses a status change and a mismatch on ('profile_home','session_id','principal_id','message','author') or a changed `admission_id` raises `admission_conflict` (:60-77) — while `withdraw_pin`/`requeue_pin`/`refuse_pin` (:89/:109/:129) act only while the file is still the same admission-less `ambiguous` pin, and `_requeue_pin` strips the four `_PIN_FIELDS` (:106) so the restored legacy ticket keeps its id, message, sequence and created_at.

### `gateway/session_busy_controls.py`
- **Purpose:** Session-scoped (never profile-level) preferences and controls of the running execution: busy-input mode, tool-progress verbosity, reasoning display, service tier, yolo, plus steer/redirect.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (:9) maps `config.get`/`config.set`/`session.steer`/`session.redirect`; `authorize` (:18); `busy_config` (:30) dispatches to `verbose_config` (:75), `show_reasoning_config` (:90), `service_tier_config` (:111), `yolo_config` (:131); `adopts_owner_agent` (:163); `correct` (:175).
- **Data flow:** a `config.get`/`config.set` arrives with a `ref`; `busy_config` picks the per-key handler, which authorizes (`session:read` for a read, `session:control` for a write), reads or writes a field on the *live* session object (`live.busy_input_mode`, `live.tool_progress_mode`, `live.show_reasoning`, the runner's per-route service-tier override, the route's persisted `SessionEntry.yolo`), and returns `{'key', 'value', 'scope': 'session'}`. `correct` reads `authority.sessions[ref.session_id]`, takes `live.event_stream.lock`, checks the approval generation, and forwards the text to the latched or live agent.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **`config.set key=model` is refused BEFORE authorization and before any read** (:33-37), with `use_session_mutation_model`: the legacy picker verb would bypass the revision-fence, retry-idempotency and selection guard that `session.mutate operation=model` provides.
  - **The busy fallback is `queue` for the TUI and `interrupt` everywhere else** (:64-71), deliberately overriding the framework default: in a full-screen composer the next prompt is authored while the agent streams, and an unintended interrupt loses work. Only an explicit `display.busy_input_mode` overrides it; any other unrecognized value falls back.
  - **A busy write is not a settings write** (:58-61): it sets `live.busy_input_mode` and must not rebuild the frozen launch policy, toolsets, history or the retained AIAgent.
  - **The yolo write is persisted BEFORE the live flip** (:147-156, docstring :135-140), so an OFF here is not revived by the next turn's launch seeding; it also re-arms from the persisted copy after a restart, and it explicitly never touches `HERMES_YOLO_MODE` or `approvals.mode`. The persistence is a routing-store write and is done off the loop via `asyncio.to_thread`.
  - **`correct` answers `{'status':'rejected', 'delivered': False}` instead of raising when no agent exists** (:227-241): a claim can outlive the agent that carried it, and a redirect in the build window has no agent to queue behind. Raising made clients discard the message (#141644); `delivered: False` is the confirmation a client may re-admit on. The live-agent `'rejected'` case can still follow a successful delivery.
  - **`adopts_owner_agent` (:163-173)** distinguishes who can take a latched steer: a cron policy or a managed worker has no steer seam, so a latch there would be acknowledged and lost.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the per-key `allowed` set (:40) is checked with `set(params) - allowed`, so a stray parameter (e.g. `cwd`) makes a specialized key fall through to the generic branch and be refused rather than silently ignored. `service_tier_config` swallows a `ValueError` from `parse_exact_service_tier` and converts it to `invalid_params` (:112-119). `yolo_config` with `value=None` is a read, not a toggle. `show_reasoning_config` returns the string `'1'`/`'0'`, not a boolean (:109). `correct` requires `type(params['execution_generation']) is int` (:179) — a bool is refused. A managed-worker session is refused `unsupported_control` rather than acknowledged (:216-219). A local voice-chat stop phrase is handled *before* the generation check (:190-194), so it ends the chat whatever generation is running.
- **Surprising:** `verbose_config` supports a `'cycle'` value that advances `('off','new','all','verbose')` — but the modulo is hardcoded `% 4` (:79-81), so adding a fifth mode to the tuple would silently break the cycle. `authorize` treats a `profile` of `None` as "this owner" (:21-22), while a non-empty profile must match the authority's home. `show_reasoning_config` reuses the `_YOLO_WORDS` table (:128) to parse its boolean words, because both are `1/on/true/0/off/false`.

### `gateway/session_clarify_batch.py` — The clarify tool's whole question batch for a classic-CLI viewer, sent as ONE `clarify` server request so main's own batch panel answers it unchanged: `ask_clarify_batch` (:16) builds the wire from the four `_QUESTION_KEYS` (:13), blocks on `ask_viewer_result` with the shared clarify timeout, and returns `{'answers': {qid: raw|None}, 'outcome': 'submitted'|'timed_out'|'cancelled'}` — unknown qids and non-string answers are dropped (:43-46), and a missing frame is `cancelled` or `timed_out` depending on whether the agent was interrupted (:35-40).

### `gateway/session_clarify_lock.py` — `clarify.lock` on the session owner (#140400): validates `request_id`/`question_id` as non-empty strings, JSON-encodes a non-string answer, and gates the lock on `may_settle(authority, actor, session_id, events)` — the same rule the closing response frame goes through — so only a viewer attached to the request's session with `session:respond` may lock an answer; a lock arriving after the wait already ended returns `{'status': 'expired'}`, not an error.

### `gateway/session_cli_turn.py`
- **Purpose:** Carries the per-admission turn inputs a classic CLI sends with `prompt.submit` — voice prefix, author, one-turn model switch, clarify-batch flag — committed *with the admission row* as `cli_turn_v1` so a retry or a queued drain runs exactly what was admitted.
- **Key abstractions:** `submit_cli_turn_fields` (:20), `admit_cli_turn` (:25), `cli_turn_scope` (:66), `cli_turn` (:74), `rebind_api_text` (:83), `one_turn_runtime` (:92), `classic_cli_turn` (:146), `clarify_batch_turn` (:160), `take_cli_result_fields` (:170), `one_turn_route` (:191), `one_turn_reasoning` (:201), `run_classic_cli_turn` (:211). Constants `_SUBMIT_FIELDS` (:15), `_API_TEXT_LIMIT` (:16), `_RESULT_PASSTHROUGH` (:142).
- **Data flow:** the raw fields are forwarded from `prompt.submit` (`submit_cli_turn_fields`) → `admit_cli_turn` validates and reduces them to the committed `{'cli_turn_v1': {...}}` blob → the turn opens `cli_turn_scope(admission_id, committed)`, binding a ContextVar → during execution `cli_turn(message_id)` reads the blob back, but only if the message id matches the bound admission id → `one_turn_route`/`one_turn_reasoning` apply the one-turn override → `run_classic_cli_turn` captures the agent's raw result into `turn._cli_result_fields` for the committed receipt.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **The ContextVar binds by MESSAGE ID, not by task** (:74-81): `cli_turn(message_id)` returns `{}` for any other id, so a follow-up drained on the same task never inherits the admitted turn's fields. This is the isolation mechanism.
  - **`rebind_api_text` (:83-90) rewrites the ContextVar but not the committed row**, so the model reads the expanded text while the row keeps what the CLI sent — a retry expands it afresh rather than expanding twice.
  - **The same-provider branch of `one_turn_runtime` deliberately KEEPS the session's `base_url`/`api_key`** (:110-127): a mirror, a proxy or a pool is the session's, and only `api_mode` is adopted where the provider picks it per model (Copilot, Nous Portal, OpenCode, via `model_derived_api_mode`). A different provider, or a `model_aliases` entry that names its own endpoint, does *not* keep the session's pool, endpoint or key (:129-136) — the whole route `switch_model` resolved runs instead.
  - **`pending_steer` is POPPED from the result** (:176-178): main's CLI queues a leftover `/steer` as its own next turn ("delivering leftover steer"), so the gateway must not also run it as a follow-up inside this admission. `final_response` is kept as the agent's RAW text because main renders its own `chat_error_copy` for an empty failed turn.
  - **`classic_cli_turn` (:146-158) requires a local-viewer turn AND a policy whose `platform == 'cli'`** — a frozen policy that is not restored raises `RuntimeStoreError` and is treated as "not a CLI viewer turn", not as an error.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `api_text` over 1 000 000 chars or non-string → `invalid_params` (:40-42); an *empty* `api_text` is valid but is simply not committed (:44-45). `model_override` must be a dict with keys ⊆ `{model, provider, reasoning}`, a non-blank string `model`, and a `reasoning` value that `parse_reasoning_effort` accepts (:50-56). `clarify_batch` must be exactly `bool` — `type(batch) is not bool` refuses `0`/`1` (:58-59). `one_turn_runtime` raises `RuntimeError` carrying `switch_model`'s own sentence when the target cannot be resolved (:102-104), and the caller fails the turn with it. `one_turn_reasoning` (:201-209) applies the override only if `'reasoning'` is *in* the override dict; otherwise the session's reasoning config stands.
- **Surprising:** there are two functions over the same data with different shapes — `submit_cli_turn_fields` returns the RAW wire fields, `admit_cli_turn` returns the COMMITTED `{'cli_turn_v1': ...}` (empty dict when nothing was committed, :63). `clarify_batch_turn` (:160-168) requires the submitting viewer to have *declared* the batch with its admission; viewers that never set it (an ACP editor, a raw WebSocket client) keep one card per question.

### `gateway/session_command_sink.py` — Captures what a messaging command handler would have SENT (its ack line and the next turn it would push onto the adapter FIFO) so a local `slash.exec` can hand both back to the client as one `send` directive: `CommandSink` (:20) holds `notices`/`prompt`/`display` and `capture()` (:31) is a ContextVar scope — the client submits the turn with its own durable input id, the gateway never does.

### `gateway/session_commands.py`
- **Purpose:** Runs a bounded set of slash commands for a local gateway session, classifying each as read / control / refused and returning a `{'type': 'exec'|'send'}` envelope.
- **Key abstractions:** `command_verdict` (:133), `execute_command` (:418), `observe_command` (:368), `_resolve_alias` (:159), `_runner_command` (:182), `_run_runner_command` (:195), `_skill_directive` (:233), `_tui_read` (:256), `_pre_command` (:313), `_on_own_thread` (:281), `_accepting` (:386), `_session_workspace` (:398), `_split` (:62), `_first_token` (:71), `_first_word` (:82). Command sets: `_READ_COMMANDS` (:22), `_CONTROL_COMMANDS` (:25), `_HANDLER_CONTROLS` (:30), `_PROMPT_COMMANDS` (:34), `_SETTING_COMMANDS` (:37), `_APPROVAL_COMMANDS` (:41), `_INFO_READS` (:44), `_OWNER_COMMANDS` (:52), `_AUTOMATION_COMMANDS` (:54), `_KANBAN_READS` (:58), `_ESCAPE_HATCHES` (:276).
- **Data flow:** `execute_command` parses the name/arg (`_parse`, :148), resolves quick-command aliases off-thread under `_profile_runtime_scope` (:433-437), asks `resolve_command` for a registry definition, computes the verdict, authorizes with a capability derived from the verdict (:450-453), then dispatches: TUI-sidecar read → runner quick/plugin command → skill directive → info read → owner cron read → the messaging handler table under `capture()` + `_accepting(before)` + `_session_workspace` (:481-500). The result is packed into `{'type': 'exec', 'output'}` or, when the handler rewrote the turn, `{'type': 'send', 'message', 'display', 'notice'}` (:505-512).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **Classification is a per-command TRIAD, and the ARGUMENT decides read vs. control** (:92-101): `/usage reset` redeems a credit and `/suggestions accept` schedules cron jobs, so the same registry name is a read or a control depending on its first word. The predicates read the argument the way the handler reads it — `_first_word` (whitespace split) for `/memory` and `/skills`, `_first_token` (shlex, `--board`-aware) for `/kanban`, raw `.strip().lower()` for `/voice`.
  - **`/cron` is an OWNER command** (:48-52, :456-458): the profile's cron store belongs to the gateway whose ticker fires the jobs, so no client writes it; and a *write* runs only on a connection NOT attached to the session (the classic CLI's command socket), because a viewer's own connection answers one request at a time with a deadline while a `/cron` run lasts as long as its job.
  - **`REFUSAL_HINTS` (:122-131) rides the `unsupported_command` refusal as `data.detail`**, leaving `reason` unchanged so shipped clients keep working; Ink and Desktop print the hint under the refusal. The listed commands are the ones that act on the HOST's profiles, terminal or git checkout, which a shared-gateway session may not reach.
  - **The default verdict is `refused`** (:147): anything not in a known set is refused, not permitted.
  - **The plugin `pre_command` hook runs on a DAEMON THREAD of its own** (:281-311), not the loop's shared executor, because a callback waiting out `plugins.hook_callback_timeout` (enforced by the hook dispatcher) from another session would otherwise hold a shared worker. The thread is spawned with `spawn_context_thread` so the caller's contextvars are inherited.
  - **One session's `pre_command` deliveries fire in ARRIVAL ORDER and a slow one causes later commands to be SKIPPED, never reordered** (:313-359): each delivery awaits the previous tail (:348-350); a still-running delivery for this session drops the later ones; a per-session queue cap of 32 drops further deliveries with a warning (:277, :337-340). A pending delivery belonging to a retired event loop is discarded (:330-332).
  - **`surface` and `platform` are both `'cli'` on every local surface** (:335-337) — Ink and Desktop fire the hook through the CLI slash worker, so the hook sees one identity.
  - **`_ESCAPE_HATCHES` = `{'stop'} ∪ _APPROVAL_COMMANDS`** (:276): while a turn runs no plugin observer sits in front of `/stop` or the approval verbs, but every other command that still runs fires.
  - **`_accepting` (:386-395) is the busy dispatcher's acceptance hook** (`BUSY_ACCEPTED`): a mid-run variant that refuses its arguments (`/goal <new text>`) never reaches it, so a *refused* command fires no `pre_command` at all.
  - **`_tui_read` fires `pre_command` AFTER the authorization** (:268-271), so a read the actor may not have is refused without ever notifying a plugin.
  - **The `exec` quick command refuses to fall back to the gateway's own folder** (:209-213): if the session policy has no cwd or it is not a directory, it raises `cwd_unavailable` — a relative command would otherwise act in the daemon's directory.
  - **The exact registered plugin name wins over Telegram's `_` → `-` form** (:182-192): `foo_bar` is its own command, looked up under both spellings, first match wins.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** an unclosed quote is refused *by name* (`invalid_params`, field `arg`) before `pre_command` fires (:62-68) — an internal error would be the wrong answer. `/voice tts` is refused (:113-116) because a local session's audio plays only in its client. `/kanban create` is excluded from the read verbs because it would subscribe the LOCAL chat to messaging notifications (:57-59). An unknown command with no quick-command or plugin entry → `unsupported_command` (:455). A `profile` naming another profile → `profile_mismatch` (:425-426). Any capability other than `session:read` requires `authority._require_admission_open()` (:462-463). `_resolve_alias` refuses an alias cycle via a `seen` set (:165-168) and refuses an `exec` entry with no command (:169-170).
- **Surprising:** the docstring's opening rule — "Desktop and Ink both submit `skill`/`send` results with their own input ID. Executing those here would double-submit and bypass their retry identity" (:1-4) — is why a handler's next turn is returned as a `send` directive for the CLIENT to submit, never submitted by the gateway. `_HANDLER_CONTROLS` includes `login`, `curator` and `autoprune` (:30-32): the messaging handler runs as-is for a local session. `_PROMPT_COMMANDS` (`plan`, `learn`, `init`, `moa`, `blueprint`, `egress`, :34) are the ones whose handler REWRITES the turn — the rewritten text is returned as the client's `send` directive. `_text` (:527) unwraps `/blueprint`'s result object, which carries its text in `.text` rather than being a string.

### `gateway/session_complete_path.py` — `complete.path` for the Ink TUI composer, rooted in the session's FROZEN policy cwd and never in a cwd the client sends or the daemon's own folder; if the policy is missing or `terminal_uses_host_cwd` is false (a remote or sandboxed backend) it returns `{'items': []}` because completion is advisory and that tree is not this host's (:31-34), and the listing runs on a thread under `_profile_runtime_scope` against the sidecar's own bound helpers (:38-43).

### `gateway/session_completion_notes.py` — A completion for a local session with NO viewer attached is written into the session's history as a note instead of running a model turn: `noted_completion` (:29) recognises only the `_NOTED_PRODUCERS` kinds (async_delegation, completion, watch_match, watch_disabled) from the admission's `local_automation_v1` descriptor, and `note_if_unviewed` (:35) appends the note keyed by the admission id — "a claim is never replayed, and this keeps a write retry exact" — then settles the admission with an empty final_response; a closed row is reopened so the turn can proceed as any first turn does (:41-45).

### `gateway/session_config.py`
- **Purpose:** Owner-bound projections of native settings and the model inventory (`config.get`, `model.options`) — read-only, redacted, and session-aware.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (:11), `_authorize` (:16), `mcp_revision` (:36), `_client_config` (:46), `config_get` (:65), `session_voice_mode` (:116), `local_voice_enabled` (:123), `set_local_voice_mode` (:138), `local_voice_transition` (:156), `_voice` (:167), `_fast` (:175), `_reasoning` (:185), `_project` (:200), `model_options` (:209).
- **Data flow:** `config_get` validates the key, delegates the four session-scoped keys to `session_busy_controls.busy_config` (:74-77), otherwise authorizes and runs a threaded `read()` closure under `_profile_runtime_scope(home)` that loads the user config and dispatches through a `getters` table (:86-111) — one entry per key, each returning `{'value': ...}` or a richer dict.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **`mcp_revision` (:36-44) is a sha256[:12] over ONLY the `mcp`/`mcp_servers`/`tools` sections, read from the session's FROZEN policy when it has one**: "a live config edit is not an MCP reload request, it reaches a chat as a committed generation", so the value a client is told to load is the value the session already holds.
  - **`_client_config` (:46-63) is a strict allowlist** (`display`, `approvals`, `paste_collapse_threshold`, `paste_collapse_char_threshold`) that deliberately does NOT export provider, MCP, plugin or terminal configuration, which can contain arbitrary secrets. The `voice` section is allowlisted *after* redaction to `record_key`/`submit_mode` because the structural masker treats every `*_key` leaf as a credential — `record_key` is a keybinding, not a secret. The `display` section is merged with the LOCAL display config so a local `/reasoning show|hide` or `/footer` wins over the global one.
  - **The `full` getter reads the USER's settings, not `DEFAULT_CONFIG`** (:86-88): "clients read an absent key as their own default (the TUI streams unless `display.streaming` is explicitly false)".
  - **`mtime` and `mcp_rev` are served by the SAME key** (:91-96) — a client polls one key for both.
  - **Voice mode is read from the RUNNER, not the session** (:116-121): `_voice_mode` is keyed by `_voice_key_for_source(live.source)`, so Ink mirrors the gateway's per-session state instead of the sidecar's process-global `HERMES_VOICE`. `local_voice_enabled`/`set_local_voice_mode` (:123-155) search ALL authorities for the session and return `None` when no owner in this process holds it — the sidecar's env flag applies then.
  - **`local_voice_transition` (:156-165) ends the push-to-talk capture the mode started**, dropping its late callbacks, and touches only ITS OWN session's capture.
  - **`_fast` (:175-183) reads the session's NEXT-turn tier** (`current_tier`), not the agent's currently running tier.
  - **`_reasoning` (:185-198)** falls back to the policy's `current_reasoning_config` for the session's route before the file config, and reports effort `'none'` when the config is explicitly disabled.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `cwd` is only accepted with `key == 'project'` (:79-80); the key must be a string (:70-72); an unknown key raises `invalid_params` from *inside* the thread closure (:109-111), so the error crosses the thread boundary to the caller; `key == 'voice'` with no `ref.session_id` → `invalid_params` (:169). `_authorize` (:16-34) checks the actor's profile match first, then the capability, then the parameter types, and returns the session's frozen policy so the caller can read the config the session actually holds.
- **Surprising:** `model_options` (:209-227) resolves a `'custom'` provider to a canonical custom identity derived from the base URL, because a custom provider's real identity is its endpoint, not its name (:216-219). `config_get` for the busy/verbose/yolo/show_reasoning keys never touches the profile config at all — those are session-scoped and live in `session_busy_controls`.

### `gateway/session_connections.py` — The `manage_connections` card on the session authority (`connection.respond`, `connectors.operation.status/wake`, and the card's Try again): `ConnectorRefusal` (:13) carries the legacy handlers' own code, human message and `ConnectorErrorReason` on the authority's error path, `_request` (:37) validates the envelope with the pydantic contracts and treats a bad ANSWER as `INVALID_ANSWER` rather than a bad envelope, and `_handle` (:63) authorizes BEFORE the existence check "so a refused caller learns nothing about the id"; `_retry` (:102) refuses a retry naming another account's alias or connection id, and `_require_connectors` (:131) re-checks `manage_connections` in the session's toolset under the session's own context.

### `gateway/session_context.py`
- **Purpose:** Replaces the old `os.environ`-based `HERMES_SESSION_*` state with task-local ContextVars so concurrently handled messages cannot clobber each other's routing ids.
- **Key abstractions:** `set_session_vars` (:133), `clear_session_vars` (:165), `reset_session_vars` (:178), `get_session_env` (:191), `set_current_session_id` (:81), `scoped_current_session_id` (:96), `scoped_session_key` (:109), `bound_session_key` (:119), `source_route_metadata` (:126), `session_context_engaged` (:21), `session_is_messaging_surface` (:209), `declare_stateless_channel` (:218), `async_delivery_supported` (:228), `session_history_delivery_supported` (:241). Constants `_UNSET` (:16), `_SESSION_VARS` (:34), `_SESSION_ASYNC_DELIVERY` (:53), `_SESSION_HISTORY_DELIVERY` (:57), `_VAR_MAP` (:66), `NON_MESSAGING_SESSION_SURFACES` (:203).
- **Data flow:** a turn's routing identity is bound by `set_session_vars` (:133-163), which zips 19 positional values onto the 19 ContextVars of `_SESSION_VARS` and returns reset tokens. `get_session_env` (:191-201) reads a legacy `HERMES_SESSION_*` name: the ContextVar wins if it was EVER set here (even to `""`), else `os.environ`, else the default. The subprocess env bridge consults `session_context_engaged()` (:21) to decide whether ContextVars are authoritative.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - **`_UNSET` is a THREE-state sentinel** (:16): "never set here" (falls back to `os.environ`) vs `""` = explicitly cleared (no fallback). `clear_session_vars` sets every var to `""` and does NOT restore prior values — the returned tokens are "accepted only for API compat" (:153-155, :165-176). `reset_session_vars` (:178-189) puts them back to `_UNSET` ("never bound here").
  - **`reset_session_vars` must be called at the TOP of a fresh task before it binds** (:179-185): `create_task` snapshots the context, so B's task would otherwise inherit A's already-set vars, and a subprocess spawned before B binds would read A's identity.
  - **`set_current_session_id` writes `os.environ` only outside a delegated child context** (:81-93): delegated subagent children are built in the parent process and get ONLY the task-local write, "or they would clobber the parent's id".
  - **`bound_session_key` (:119-124) has NO `os.environ` fallback** — "a thread that inherited no context never borrows another session's key".
  - **`session_history_delivery_supported` (:241-245) FAILS CLOSED**: only the exact string `"1"` grants wake authority; an omitted declaration cannot. `clear_session_vars` returns this var to `_UNSET` for the opposite reason to async delivery — a cleared context has declared nothing, and an undeclared capability must fail closed (:172-175).
  - **`declare_stateless_channel` (:218-226) deliberately does NOT latch `_session_context_engaged`**: latching it would turn on the subprocess env bridge, and "a one-shot CLI must not do that as a side effect".
  - **`_session_context_engaged` is a process-level latch** (:20-22): once any session has bound, the subprocess env bridge treats ContextVars as authoritative and an `_UNSET` var means "no session in THIS task".
  - **`session_is_messaging_surface` is DEFAULT-DENY** (:201-215): an unrecognized identity counts as messaging, and it consults BOTH `HERMES_PLATFORM` and `HERMES_SESSION_SOURCE` because the gateway binds the platform while CLI/TUI/desktop bind the source.
  - **`async_delivery_supported` (:228-239) is False for `HERMES_KANBAN_TASK` and for registered worker processes**: a managed worker exits at its turn boundary, so detached work it scheduled would be lost (recovered `unknown`) and its completion queue has no reader.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_runtime_cwd` (:72-79) swallows every exception — it is a best-effort bridge to `agent.runtime_cwd`. `get_session_env` on an unknown name falls straight through to `os.getenv` (:194-196). `source_route_metadata` (:126-131) keeps only `scope_id`/`parent_chat_id` anchors so a durable delivery can still route after the source is gone.
- **Surprising:** `set_session_vars` returns a list of tokens whose only real use is API compatibility — `clear_session_vars` ignores them and resets every var to `""` rather than restoring prior values, deliberately breaking the usual contextmanager contract (:153-155, :165-176). `_SESSION_VARS` (:34-51) is a 19-tuple unpacked by name, and the tuple ORDER *is* the positional order of `values` in `set_session_vars` — the zip is positional, so a reordering of the tuple would silently mis-bind every variable.

### `gateway/session_context_refs.py` — `@` reference expansion for one turn, in the session's workspace, on the text the MODEL reads: `expand_references` (:18) returns `(text, refusal)` and a failed expansion leaves the text as typed; `turn_references` (:58) expands the classic CLI's committed `api_text` when the model reads that instead of the stored text and `rebind_api_text` (:83) rewrites the ContextVar so the committed row keeps what the CLI sent ("a retry expands it afresh rather than twice"); a refused local turn leaves its refusal on the event, and `refused_turn_result` (:110) makes a finite (`chat -q`) turn FAIL with that refusal while an ordinary turn returns it as the answer.

### `gateway/session_contract.py` — The public session authority contract, where "live execution state never crosses this boundary": frozen dataclasses `Principal` (:13), `SessionRef` (:21), `SessionHandle` (:27, carrying authority_epoch, revision, execution_generation and the five-state `execution_state` 'idle'|'running'|'waiting'|'unknown'|'terminal'), `SessionCreate` (:37), `Submission` (:45, intent queue|steer|redirect), `AdmissionReceipt` (:53), `PendingAdmission` (:64, whose `controls` list names the queued-row verbs this owner serves and whose `steering` flag marks a row in transfer to the running turn), `SubscriptionSnapshot` (:75), plus the `SessionAuthority` Protocol (:85) and `CANONICAL_GATEWAY_PROTOCOL = 'hermes-gateway-v1'` (:9).

### `gateway/session_control_actions.py` — Desktop's goal/loop/heartbeat/subgoal card buttons on the owner, each action being the TYPED slash command the button stands for and run through `execute_command` (:100) so it inherits the same capability, local-source, admission and busy rules; the subgoal buttons instead call the goal manager directly (:36) and translate a missing goal or a stale index into `goal_not_active`/`invalid_params`; `publish` (:75) pushes the control snapshot to every viewer as `session.control.update`, skipping a session with no automation state or an unchanged revision, and a snapshot that cannot be read is logged at debug level because "the change itself already landed".

### `gateway/session_controls.py`
- **Purpose:** The authenticated WebSocket projection of one gateway authority — a single `AuthorityConnection` that validates, routes and answers every JSON-RPC frame clients send, with no TUI execution fallback.
- **Key abstractions:**
  - `AuthorityConnection` (session_controls.py:113) — per-connection handler table + dispatcher.
  - `handlers()` (session_controls.py:170) — ~35 built-in verbs, then extended by 10 sibling modules (`session_busy_controls`, `session_queued_controls`, `session_config`, `session_tui_config`, `session_ancillary`, `session_shell`, `session_tui_ops`, `session_tui_side`, `session_llm_oneshot`, `session_connections`) plus `image.attach_bytes`, `complete.path`, `session.prune`, `session.stats`, `session.active_list`, `session.automation`, `message.react`, `clarify.lock`, `session.control`.
  - `dispatch()` (session_controls.py:235) — the one entry point.
  - `_sibling_for()` (session_controls.py:147) — cross-profile routing for host-scoped sockets.
  - `_refusal_data()` (:22), `_log_refusal()` (:49), `_log_storage_failure()` (:71) — error shaping and log-level policy.
  - `display_projection()` (:97) and `model_only()` (:106).
- **Data flow:** frame → `dispatch` (:235) → params must be a dict (:247) → drain check for operator sockets (:250) → `profile` param routed to a sibling authority (:258-271) → `SessionRef` built (:272) → canonical contract check `canonical_param_problems` (:276) → adopt-before-restore for an unknown session (:285-291) → `owner_scope(self.authority)` (:298) so config/policy load from the OWNING profile → handler invoked (:312) → `check_params_accepted` + `check_result` (:315-316) → JSON-RPC result.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - An unknown method returns JSON-RPC's own `-32601 unknown_method` (:306-310), not the house `4001 invalid_params`, because clients key compat fallbacks on -32601.
  - `_PROFILE_IMPLICIT` (:90) lists 19 strict session verbs for which the `profile` key is STRIPPED from params, so their closed key sets stay closed; every other handler validates `profile` itself (:269-271).
  - Capability laundering: `capabilities -= {'session:operator'}` (:123) strips operator from identity-supplied capabilities, only the verified `operator=True` transport flag re-adds it (:124-125), and an identity with no `user_id` or a foreign `instance_id` gets an EMPTY capability set (:127-129).
  - `owner_scope(self.authority)` wraps EVERY handler (:298) so `_load_gateway_config` / `build_policy(get_hermes_home())` never snapshot the launch profile's config into a served secondary's session.
  - Titled create is serialized by a lazily-created per-authority `asyncio.Lock` (`_titled_create_lock`, :426-429) so concurrent programmatic `session.create` callers converge.
  - The abbreviated `local-<16 hex>` id is resolved BEFORE authorization (:573-577) so a unique hint reaches the same durable route; ambiguous/missing prefixes keep the normal not_found.
  - A branch's child session is auto-attached (:786-789) so the first submit after a branch is not refused as a stranger.
  - A voice stop typed as text is honoured only on a LOCAL session with voice enabled and the submitter attached (:725-745); a RETRY of an already-consumed stop is answered by request identity, not by the phrase (:702-712), because voice is off by then.
  - `cancel` reads the receipt first (:806-812): a started admission is unaffected, and a queued one the drain already claimed is stopped via `cancel_or_claimed` + `interrupt` rather than refused.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** non-dict params → 4001 before any `.get` (:247-250); `sqlite3.Error` → wire 5001 `storage_unavailable` (:322-327); `RuntimeStoreError` → 4001 (or the connector's own `wire_code`/`wire_message`) with `reason`/`field`/`detail` in `error.data` (:318-321); a `DeferredReply` is answered off the serial dispatch after the params check (:313-315); `mutate` re-reads `runtime_revision`/`runtime_generation` and attaches them to the refusal so the client retries without a re-read (:779-784); `close()` detaches surviving subscriptions, unbinds the native transport and pops the transport from `authority.events` (:851-866); a draining owner refuses operator sockets with `runtime_draining` (:250-257) — they may answer the parked turn and queue input, never change the owner's state.
- **Surprising:** `ping` (:485) is a capability gate (no capabilities → `permission_denied`, any params → `invalid_params`); `runtime.describe` (:492) advertises a wire-protocol list (`durable-admission-v1`, `event-replay-v1`, `local-cli-create-v1`, `acp-editor-policy-v1`, `acp-session-mcp-v1`), not the actor's capabilities; `list_sessions` with `title` (:525) is a title-as-identity lookup that returns `scope: 'stored'` without ever consulting the stored feed; `model_only` rows (micro-compaction merges) stay in `messages` as undo targets but are excluded from `display_messages` (:628-630).

### `gateway/session_cron.py`
- **Purpose:** The trusted cron producer — the gateway's own ticker creates, admits, runs and settles a scheduled job's session; public callers may only NAME a persisted job, never supply policy or jobs.
- **Key abstractions:** `operation(authority, name, params, actor=None)` (:121) — one entry for `submit`/`recover`/`status`/`cancel`; `_create()` (:64) — stages the session and freezes the job + extra prompt into `policy.request_json`; `execute(authority, ref, row, policy)` (:234) — the owner-side run; `bind_owner`/`unbind_owner`/`owner_for_home` (:22-39) — a process-local registry keyed by resolved DB parent dir; `bind_execution`/`current_execution` (:45/:41) — a ContextVar holding `(authority, session_id, job_id, execution_id)`; `scheduler_execution_id()` (:213); `RUN_JOB_FLAGS` (:118).
- **Data flow:** RPC → `rpc()` (:209) → `operation` → `_actor` (:51) normalizes the principal → `require_serving` (:125) → `submit`: `owner_scope` → `_create` (stage + `commit_local_session` off-loop via `tracked_write`, :163-166) → `admit_session_input` under `authority._admission_gate()` (:168-172) → drain admits → `execute` runs `cron.scheduler.run_job` in a thread under `_profile_runtime_scope` (:257-260) → result written to `authority.pending_results[admission_id]` (:264-266).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The principal is REWRITTEN to the cron principal even when a real actor is supplied (:58-60): the fire belongs to the profile scheduler, not the transport observing it, so the ticker and the CLI dedupe together.
  - A `:` in a FRESH execution id is refused (:84-89) because ledger ids are hex — only a hand-made id carries a colon, and the already-admitted retry path above it keeps its exact id.
  - `recover`/`status`/`cancel` are deliberately NOT fenced by a drain (:126-130): the drain is waiting for that work, and refusing it makes the firer give up (`CronExecutionUnknown` pauses the job) on every restart overlapping a run. Only `submit` calls `_require_admission_open()` (:162).
  - A cron session runs only its own frozen fire; anything else typed into it settles with the localized `cron_session_read_only` surface `{'layer':'gateway','code':'cron_session_read_only','retryable':False}` (:113, :237-249).
  - Cancellation is latched on the admission row in the `started` state (:185-188) because the claim commits before `execute()` registers its event — that window cannot lose the cancellation.
  - `_run_identified` (:219) RE-BINDS the cron execution identity in the owner's loop, because the scheduler bound it in the FIRING thread whose context the owner never saw.
  - Run-side verdicts (`_model_unreachable`, `_quota_hold_seconds`) are stamped on `run_job`'s own job copy and must ride the result back out, because the firing scheduler reads them off its own dict (:262-264).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `recover` for an unknown admission returns `{'status':'missing','result':None}` (:139) rather than raising; `status` on a terminal admission with no stored result synthesizes `[False,'','',outcome or 'unknown_execution']` (:196); `execute` raises `RuntimeError(result[3])` only AFTER recording the failure (:264-268); the `finally` block pops `owner_progress`, resets the ContextVar token and removes the cancellation event (:270-273); `recover` compares the caller's `extra_prompt` against the FROZEN prompt and raises `admission_conflict` on a mismatch (:148-150) — the request is an echo of the fire, not a new one.
- **Surprising:** `cron.status` reports `progress_at` keyed by the execution id, not the admission id (:200); `owner_for_home` (:34) refuses a binding whose recorded pid differs or whose loop is closed, so a re-forked process never inherits a dead owner's cron identity.

### `gateway/session_cron_commands.py` — `/cron` on the shared gateway: the classic CLI's handler run by the OWNER, so no client ever writes the profile's cron store; `cron_read` classifies the shlex-split argument into read forms (`overview`/`list`/`list-all`) and returns None for a write, while `run_cron` executes the write forms off the owner loop in the owning profile via `owner_lines` — deliberately on the runner's unbounded turn pool, never on the default pool the job's own admission and execution need, so piled-up `/cron` waits cannot starve the job (gateway/session_cron_commands.py:11, gateway/session_cron_commands.py:21).

### `gateway/session_db_recovery.py` — A per-path SessionDB handle cache that heals a failed open in-process: opens run OUTSIDE the lock with single-flight per path (`in_flight`) and exponential backoff 1s→60s, `close_all` bumps `_generation` so a later-completing open is REJECTED instead of resurrecting the cache, and a handle the process-wide registry force-closed is dropped and reopened rather than served (gateway/session_db_recovery.py:55, gateway/session_db_recovery.py:85, gateway/session_db_recovery.py:161).

### `gateway/session_deferred_pick.py` — A model pick on a session with a running turn lands for the NEXT turn: it is recorded durably in `state_meta` (latest pick wins, survives an owner restart) and committed by the drain at the next claim boundary, carrying a stable `application_id` so an exact retry replays the mutation receipt instead of overwriting a newer pick, while a pick that no longer resolves is dropped and announced to the viewers (gateway/session_deferred_pick.py:16, gateway/session_deferred_pick.py:28, gateway/session_deferred_pick.py:63).

### `gateway/session_discovery.py` — Authenticated, profile-bound discovery of the slash-command catalog and the plugin list: the catalog resolves project skills from the SESSION's workspace rather than the gateway process cwd, and the whole discovery runs inside `_profile_runtime_scope` on a worker thread, so the classic CLI imports no plugin code at all (gateway/session_discovery.py:8, gateway/session_discovery.py:22, gateway/session_discovery.py:38).

### `gateway/session_display.py` — Per-turn client presentation (`display_kind="hidden"`, `title_preview`) committed onto the admission row as `display_v1`: only `hidden` is authorable (anything else is refused, so a typo can never turn machine text into a visible user bubble), the preview is capped at 1000 chars, and the committed fact is bound to THAT admission's event id so a later follow-up on the same task never inherits it (gateway/session_display.py:14, gateway/session_display.py:25, gateway/session_display.py:63).

### `gateway/session_envelope.py`
- **Purpose:** Owner-only durable codec for native (messaging-platform) input — turns an adapter `MessageEvent` into a persisted `native_text_v1` envelope and validates it back, never accepting a client-supplied wire format.
- **Key abstractions:** `prepare_native(runner, event)` (:172) — the async admission path (captures provenance, freezes the source, authorizes, copies media off-loop); `snapshot_native(runner, event)` (:166) — the sync path; `_validate_native(runner, event, provenance, fresh_roles)` (:125) — the clause-based validator; `_event_clause` (:95) and `_media_clause` (:80) — the closed refusal-clause sets; `restore_native(payload, runner)` (:279), `restore_native_source` (:263), `check_native_route(runner, payload, session_id, available_source, adapter)` (:303); `_WHATSAPP_METADATA`/`_WHATSAPP_NATIVE_KEYS`/`_WHATSAPP_NATIVE_MAX_BYTES` (:31-35).
- **Data flow:** adapter event → `capture_provenance` → a frozen COPY of `event.source` (:178) → `reauthorize_roles` → `_validate_native` (re-encodes the source through `SessionSource.from_dict` and re-resolves the adapter) → `_snapshot_native` builds `{'source','route','event','timestamp'}` (:213-216) → payload `{'text','native_text_v1'}` (:246) stored on the admission row; later `restore_native` rebuilds the `MessageEvent` and `check_native_route` re-validates it before a queued row is consumed.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The source is round-tripped and the adapter re-resolved (:154-163): a forged `source` that does not map back to the SAME adapter object is refused as `adapter_mismatch`, not merely a non-matching one.
  - "Delegation" (skip the principal allowlist) is derived from `fresh_roles` OR a relay provenance OR the adapter's own `authorization_is_upstream` flag (:132-141) — the last case exists because A2A peers with bearer-token auth have no relay seal to derive delegation from, and the admission re-check was denying what the inbound filter had just accepted (#139199).
  - `role_authorized` is stored as the string `reauthorize: 'roles'` (:216-217): the envelope records that a FRESH check is required, never the verdict.
  - A WhatsApp `whatsapp_native` blob over 16 KiB is DROPPED, not refused (:57-60) — oversized vCards are routine.
  - The envelope omits new default fields so an identical retry of an older text admission keeps its old fingerprint (:234-235), and an exact legacy retry is detected by comparing the payload without `provenance` against stored admissions (:248-261).
  - Epoch-0 timestamps are replaced with `datetime.now()` on restore (:282-286) so producers without provider time keep a stable retry fingerprint while the turn records local arrival time.
  - `prepare_native` SHIELDS the media copy (:194) so cancelling the waiter cannot cancel the thread's completion observer, which might publish a path after the lease context exited (:195-203).
