- **Edge cases & failure handling:** media-list length mismatches produce distinct clauses `media_types_length` / `media_text_inlined_length` (:88-93) so the refusal names which; `check_native_route` is a read-only preflight (:303-305) so a route/auth rejection never consumes a queued row, and a route mismatch raises `admission_conflict` (:316-318); webhook destinations are captured at snapshot time (`webhook_delivery` + a `route_digest`, :218-224) and re-validated on the preflight (:324-329); a COMMAND that passed admission is replayed as TEXT (:241-243) because `/Users/x is broken` is path text.
- **Surprising:** the validator treats `event.get_command()` as a REFUSAL clause (:103-104) — a message the ingress router classified as a command never reaches native admission at all, so commands take the slash path instead.

### `gateway/session_events.py` — The authority-local event publisher: one `SessionEvents` per session stamps a private key and epoch over a shared bounded replay ring, mints a NEW epoch whenever the ring evicted the session (so a sequence number is never reused), and reports `snapshot_required` whenever the client's epoch/sequence cannot be served — a peer's bounded-backlog overflow is delivered as the same `session.replay_gap` shape as the client's own synthetic gap, so one consumer handles both (gateway/session_events.py:29, gateway/session_events.py:38, gateway/session_events.py:56, gateway/session_events.py:77).

### `gateway/session_execution.py`
- **Purpose:** The agent-resolution half of a gateway turn — reuse the session's cached `AIAgent` (frozen system prompt + tool schemas = prompt-cache hits) or build a fresh one; extracted from `gateway.run` into `GatewaySessionAgentMixin`.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewaySessionAgentMixin` (:33) with `_CachedAgentLookup` (:37, carrying `agent`/`reused`/`evicted`); `_resolve_turn_agent()` (:191) — the decision point, returns `(agent, reused)`; `_lookup_cached_agent()` (:97) — the cache-hit validator; `_build_fresh_agent()` (:151) — construction with ~30 kwargs; `_cached_sid_is_dead()` (:56); `_current_message_count()` (:75); `_pop_cached_agent_for_eviction()` (:88); `_release_evicted_agent()` (:144); `_skip_context_files()` (:42).
- **Data flow:** turn route → config signature `runner._agent_config_signature(...)` (:197) → OUTSIDE the cache lock, peek the cached session id and test whether it is dead in the DB (:56-73) and read the current `message_count` (:75-86) → under the lock compare `(sig, cached_mc, cached_sid)` (:103-129) → reuse (and refresh LRU order) or evict + build fresh → the fresh agent is stored as a 4-tuple `(agent, sig, msg_count, session_id)` (:229).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - A session-key switch (same key, different conversation) is REUSED, not invalidated (:110-112): the cached `message_count` tracks a DIFFERENT DB row, so the comparison is meaningless — and busting the prompt cache on every switch would be wasteful.
  - The "dead session" verdict is peeked OUTSIDE the cache lock (:58-73) and then re-validated against the tuple read under THIS lock (:113-118): a stale "dead" verdict must never be applied to a different (possibly live) agent.
  - A cached agent that already moved onto a fallback provider cannot serve a CONFIRMED api runtime lock, so it is evicted (:213-217).
  - The fallback chain is refreshed from disk OUTSIDE the cache lock (:218-220) because disk I/O under the lock stalls the idle-sweep watcher and Discord heartbeats.
  - Eviction releases the agent on a DAEMON THREAD with a SOFT release (:144-149), because `release_clients` can block on memory-provider/socket teardown while the sweeper waits on the same lock.
  - `fallback_model=None if api_runtime_locked() else self._runner._refresh_fallback_model()` (:180) — a locked API runtime gets no fallback.
  - `skip_context_files` is policy-first: the session's frozen `policy.ignore_rules` wins over the per-platform `gateway.platforms.<plat>.skip_context_files` config (:42-54), and a non-dict `platforms` config is treated as "no overrides" rather than crashing (:50-53).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_current_message_count` swallows every exception (:80-85) — a closed store yields `None` and the count comparison is simply skipped; the dead-session DB probe is `suppress(Exception)`-wrapped (:71-72) so a probe failure means "not dead", i.e. reuse; a REUSED agent still gets `max_iterations` re-stamped (:126) so a changed budget applies without a rebuild; `cache.move_to_end` is `suppress(KeyError)`-wrapped (:121-123) so the LRU refresh cannot break a hit.
- **Surprising:** this is a MIXIN, not a module of free functions — it is consumed by `TurnRunner` and expects `self._runner`/`self._ctx`; the logger is still named `"gateway.run"` (:30) for "log-record parity with the origin module", so records stay identical to the pre-extraction origin.

### `gateway/session_finite.py` — The finite / one-shot turn contract: `admit_finite` refuses `unattended` unless the submitter is the private local bootstrap ticket AND the session's creator AND a CLI-surface session (never a Desktop, TUI, ACP or Bot Chat another viewer watches), and `execute_finite_admission` runs the turn under four nested ContextVar scopes (finite, surface, display, cli_turn) so a bound owner turn never inherits process launch flags from another viewer (gateway/session_finite.py:44, gateway/session_finite.py:59, gateway/session_finite.py:71).

### `gateway/session_group_controls.py`
- **Purpose:** The `groups.*` + `profiles.list` RPC surface — profile-owned room controls over the canonical hosted service, plus the multi-profile "Bots" roster.
- **Key abstractions:** `GROUP_METHODS` (:13) — method → required capability; `_FIELDS` (:29) — the closed param set per method; `dispatch_group_control(connection, method, params)` (:49) — the entry point; `_group(authority, actor, method, params, *, host_scope)` (:88) — the handler table; `_execution_control(service, method, params)` (:231); `_profiles(authority, actor, home, params, siblings)` (:390) — the roster; `_read_bot_sessions(db, subject, operator)` (:317) — the three session fields per row; `_named_profile_dirs()` (:491) and `_discovered_profile_meta()` (:522) — the two caches.
- **Data flow:** `dispatch_group_control` → capability check (:51) → profile match + DB-path-parent-vs-home check (:53-66) → `invoke()` runs in a thread under `_profile_runtime_scope(home)` (:71-86) → `_group` uses the `HostedRoomService` handle or falls back to the raw `gateway.hosted_rooms` store → handlers return plain dicts. `profiles.list` → `_profiles` → own row + served siblings + hosted-room profile homes + every named profile dir under the root, each enriched with `_bot_sessions` fields.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The DB path's parent must equal the profile home (:60-61): a group control may never reach a store that is not this profile's.
  - `groups.create` claims ownership INSIDE the room insert's transaction via the `admit(conn)` callback (:106-111), so a create rejected by validation or storage cannot leave an owner-only reservation behind.
  - `groups.list` pages the ACTOR-VISIBLE collection, not the room table (:138-171): it scans the underlying cursor until the page holds `limit` visible rooms and returns that underlying cursor as `next_offset`, so a page is never short or empty while visible rooms remain.
  - `groups.disband` refuses with `runtime_coordination_required` if any task is queued/running/stopping/indeterminate/deferred when there is no live driver (:203-206) — metadata control must not destroy an active execution or bypass Stop.
  - The roster caches are keyed on CONTENT, not timestamps: `_named_profile_dirs` signs on the root's stat + each entry's stat + the lstat of every identity marker + the tombstone's presence (:495-505), and `_discovered_profile_meta` keys on the raw bytes of `profile.yaml` (:533-535). The stated reason (:466-474) is that Linux stamps mtime AND ctime from the coarse clock, FAT/SMB/HFS+ tick coarser still, and a create-then-delete inside one tick leaves every stat field of the profile dir unchanged.
  - A sibling row is metadata-only unless this connection is host-scoped AND the bot has no `problem` (:299-301): discovery alone is never a licence to read another owner's state (:419-421).
  - A sibling whose store fails to answer degrades to metadata-only for THAT row (:372-388) — the whole roster never fails; the log drops from warning to debug after the first outage, tracked in a `WeakSet` so an unserved profile drops out (:282).
  - The "worker session" query deliberately has NO owner filter and reads DENY-LISTED sources (:360-370): workers are excluded from every conversation listing, so a roster row carrying only conversation fields read "idle" while a kanban/tool run was heartbeating (#72016).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `HostedRoomError` is mapped to `RuntimeStoreError` with the error's own reason (:79-80) and `ValueError`/`TypeError` become `invalid_params` (:81-82); when the hosted service exists but is not running (or is stopping) `service` is set to `None` (:95-99) and the metadata-only path applies, with execution methods raising `runtime_coordination_required` (:119-121); an unreadable `profiles/` root logs a warning and returns `[]` — the authority's own roster survives (:508-517); a `profile.yaml` that is missing, a directory, unreadable or bad YAML degrades that one row to basic metadata (:522-541).
- **Surprising:** `groups.capabilities` advertises `room_link: {'enabled': False, 'reason': 'canonical_driver_required'}` (:134) — the feature is reported as DISABLED — and `seatable_profiles` is computed from the same roster `groups.create` validates against, so a picker never offers a bot that create would refuse (:127-130).

### `gateway/session_hosted_attachments.py` — Room byte RPCs and committed task-input materialization: `submission_payload` resolves only event-bound, member-authorized bytes (never a caller path) and stages images under a content-addressed name, while `attested_submission_payload` derives the durable payload from source-attested digests ALONE — the destination path of every input is a pure function of manifest and digest, so a re-pointed source yields a different payload and is refused without transferring any bytes, and `verify_attested_documents` re-hashes the retained documents so a mutated or deleted file pauses the row (gateway/session_hosted_attachments.py:71, gateway/session_hosted_attachments.py:146, gateway/session_hosted_attachments.py:168, gateway/session_hosted_attachments.py:192).

### `gateway/session_hosted_controls.py` — Exact operator controls for canonical hosted attempts: `discard_room_task` commits the canonical receipt FIRST and only then resolves the indeterminate cancellation (so an exact RPC replay recovers the write instead of inventing another execution), `retry_room_task` refuses an indeterminate task with `unknown_execution` (never advancing the hosted generation while leaving the canonical unknown head behind it) and refuses a busy member with `session_busy`, and `status` synthesizes the pending discard/retry actions for non-peer members (gateway/session_hosted_controls.py:27, gateway/session_hosted_controls.py:48, gateway/session_hosted_controls.py:67).

### `gateway/session_hosted_members.py` — Which local profiles a hosted group on one authority may seat: served siblings need no config entry, but only a HOST-SCOPED caller seats them that way (a profile-bound caller gets `permission_denied`), one home answers to only one name, and a bad `hosted_rooms.profiles` entry is ignored and logged ONCE per value — an alias stays in the roster so an existing room keeps running its other members (gateway/session_hosted_members.py:22, gateway/session_hosted_members.py:35, gateway/session_hosted_members.py:42, gateway/session_hosted_members.py:134).

### `gateway/session_hosted_rpc.py`
- **Purpose:** The private producer a hosted room's member speaks to — one `HostedRoomAuthorityRPC` per durable room/member/profile binding, bridging the room driver's private socket to the gateway's canonical local-session FIFO.
- **Key abstractions:** `HostedRoomAuthorityRPC` (:23) with `accepts_attachments = True` (:24); `self.creation_id` (:33-36) — `'hosted:' + sha256(room_id, member_id, profile)`, so a duplicate create converges on the same session; `_call()` (:39) — the thread→loop bridge; `_dispatch` (:52) / `_dispatch_owned` (:57); `_submit` (:152), `_history` (:199), `_results` (:214), `_info` (:240), `_interrupt` (:259), `_discard` (:273), `_approve` (:291), `_terminal` (:136), `_rows` (:119); `_RESULTS_BUDGET_BYTES = 384 * 1024` (:20) and `_RESULTLESS_OUTCOMES` (:18).
- **Data flow:** driver thread → `submit(...)` → `_call` → `run_coroutine_threadsafe` on the owner's loop (:48) → `_dispatch_owned` re-checks profile/source/session/title (:58-63) → `authorize(operation, task, generation)` (:64-66) → `_submit` builds `request_id = 'hosted:' + json([task, generation])` (:154) → `capture_lease()` holds the media lease across capture→admission (:162-172) → `authority.submit(...)` → a waiter future drives the terminal callback (:174-180); `_results` polls terminal receipts under a byte budget.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:**
  - The principal, room id, member id and title are NEVER taken from the RPC payload (:57-67): the payload's `profile`/`source`/`session_id`/`title` must equal the producer's own values, or the call is refused.
  - `_call` refuses to run when called from the owner's own loop (`invalid_params`) and when the loop is not running (`runtime_draining`) (:44-46).
  - A timeout is AMBIGUOUS by design and is never converted into "not admitted" (:48-49) — cancelling an admission would be a lie, so the future is simply awaited with a timeout.
  - The `capture_lease()` spans capture → admission commit (:163-171): documents ride inside the prompt TEXT, so submit's own lease does not hold them, and an older row's release could unlink a shared content-addressed capture in between.
  - `_results` never returns the transcript — only terminal receipts, each truncated to the driver's persisted bound, newest-first under a 384 KiB budget so the response line stays under the 512 KiB private-socket limit (:214-238).
  - The `clarify` toolset is DISABLED for room sessions (:91-95): a group exposes approvals only, so a member turn that called clarify would block for `clarify_timeout` with its question unseen.
  - The room session is created HIDDEN with a `Group: <room_id>` title and a `bot_room` platform (:97-113), and its creation id is content-derived, so a concurrent duplicate converges.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** an admission row whose `request_id` payload is not a valid `TaskIdentity` raises `storage_unavailable` (:126-133) — the row is corrupt, not the request; `_terminal` raises `storage_unavailable` when a non-interrupted/cancelled outcome has no stored result (:138-141); `_submit` refuses `unknown_execution` if ANY row for this session is unknown, before submitting (:156-158) because submit itself schedules the queue on retries; `_completed` ignores a cancelled or exceptioned future (a paused FIFO stays queued/unknown) and re-checks the authorizer before firing the callback (:185-197); `_history` authorizes before AND after the await and wraps the read in `authority.unless_deleted` (:201-210) so a delete in between answers not_found, never a KeyError; `_interrupt` raises `stale_generation` for a task mismatch and `unknown_execution` for an unknown row (:259-271); `_discard` requires EXACTLY ONE matching row else `stale_generation` (:277-283); `_approve` raises `stale_generation` when the approval prompt is gone (:295-298).
- **Surprising:** `submit` accepts an optional `member_id` and refuses when it names a different member (:313-315) — a task for another member reached the wrong producer; and the PUBLIC fence is the hosted generation while the canonical CAS uses its OWN generation (:285-288).

### `gateway/session_hosted_service.py`
- **Purpose:** The authority-bound hosted-room service: it turns a hosted room's durable membership/task state into the attestations a member's own daemon needs, so the target never opens the source's database.
- **Key abstractions:** `CanonicalHostedRoomService(HostedControls, HostedRoomService)` — `session_hosted_service.py:16`; `_make_rpc` returns `self` (no legacy session server is built) `:22`; `profile_homes` `:27`, `local_profiles` `:31`, `room_profiles` `:34`, `attest` `:43`, `bindings` `:87`, `_turn_lock` `:93`, `authorize_room` `:96`, `_owner` `:121`, `_resolve_member_transport` `:128`, `check_admission` `:202`, `approve` `:231`. Module-level: `ensure_hosted_service` `:238`, `_ensure_hosted_service` `:249`, `start_ready_hosted_services` `:263`, `stop_hosted_service` `:276`. Ownership ledger key prefix `_OWNER = 'gateway.hosted.owner.v1:'` `:13`.
- **Data flow:** A member's RPC asks the source owner to attest (`attest(selector, operation, params)` `:43`); the owner resolves the durable owner subject from `state_meta` (`:106`, `:121-126`), confirms the member is seated and local (`:52-54`), confirms `params['_target_home']` matches the member's home (`:56-57`), then returns `{owner, target_home}` plus — for `submit`/`execute`/`attachment` — the durable prompt, the attachment manifest and the upload-verified digests (`:59-83`). The target then runs its own `HostedRoomAuthorityRPC` under a synthetic principal derived from that owner subject.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The `attachment` operation returns BYTES ONLY and deliberately omits the prompt/manifest, because those already attested for the submit would otherwise compete with the chunk for the bounded response line (`:74-78`). `_turn_lock` returns `nullcontext()` (`:93-94`) — hosted rooms deliberately do NOT serialize turns. `authorize_room(..., create=True)` refuses to claim a room id that exists in `hosted_rooms` ∪ `hosted_room_retired_ids` (`:109-115`), so a retired id cannot be resurrected as a fresh room.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `invalid_params` for a selector that is not exactly `{room_id, member_id, profile}` (`:46-47`); `permission_denied` for a non-seated member (`:52-54`), a home mismatch (`:56-57`), and — critically — for anything other than EXACTLY ONE matching running task (`:71-72`). `check_admission` reconstructs the private producer from durable task state instead of trusting the request row, and folds every `ValueError/TypeError/KeyError/StopIteration` into `permission_denied` (fail closed) (`:223-229`). `start_ready_hosted_services` starts only services whose transport was installed, and only behind the published `state == 'ready'` descriptor and not draining (`:265-273`).
- **Surprising:** `bindings()` filters the inherited room list down to rooms whose owner key lives in `state_meta` under `_OWNER` (`:87-91`) — that table row IS the ownership ledger. Cross-daemon members (a target home that is not this authority's own profile) get a `HostedRoomOwnerRPC` over the private socket (`:141-146`) while same-home members get an in-process `HostedRoomAuthorityRPC` whose `authorize`/`authorize_write` share the FIFO writer's snapshot so a revocation race cannot reopen (`:147-152`); `authorize_write` raises instead of returning `False` because the admission hook signals refusal by raising (`:188-193`).

### `gateway/session_hosted_transport.py`
- **Purpose:** The private-OS-authenticated-socket transport between independently owned local profile daemons: the source owner attests durable state, the target produces the turn.
- **Key abstractions:** `owner_request(home, verb, params, timeout=30)` `:36`; `_attest` `:84`; `source_attachment_chunk` `:100`; `source_attachment_digests` `:122`; `_attachment_data` `:140`; `_principal` `:169`; `install_hosted_transport(server, authority, loop, attest)` `:177` (registers private handlers `hosted-attest` / `hosted-producer` `:262`); `check_remote_hosted_admission` `:265`; `_check_remote_hosted_admission` `:276`; `HostedRoomOwnerRPC` `:308` with `_call` `:319`, `history` `:342`, `_deliver` `:353`, `_watch` `:360`. Constants `_BINDING` `:24`, `_OPERATIONS` `:25`, `_CHUNK_BYTES = 360*1024` `:31`, `_CAPS` `:32`.
- **Data flow:** Target → `HostedRoomOwnerRPC._call` → `owner_request(home, 'hosted-producer', {...})` `:326` → target daemon's private handler `target` `:215` → `produce` `:220` → `_attest` (a reverse call to the SOURCE owner) `:233` → attested dict → `_attachment_data` pulls bytes chunk-by-chunk `:140-166` → `rpc._call(operation, **params)` `:259`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The 360 KiB chunk is derived from the control socket's 512 KiB `_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` cap: 360 KiB raw → 480 KiB base64, leaving 32 KiB for the envelope (owner subject, target home, digest) (`:27-31`). Every chunk is RE-ATTESTED at the source against the task fence (`:154-157`) — bytes only ever flow through the source owner's authenticated handler, never the target's DB. `_principal` derives the subject from `sha256([source_home, owner])` (`:172-173`) so equal owner strings on different sources cannot alias a target's principal.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `owner_request` collapses any error reason outside the known set into `runtime_draining` so an internal failure never leaks a foreign reason to the peer (`:75-77`); a request over 64 KiB is `request_too_large` (`:56-57`) — which is why a hosted submit sends `prompt_sha256` instead of the prompt (`:323`). `history` degrades to bounded receipt rows when the transcript exceeds one response line (`response_too_large`) instead of failing the recovery (`:342-351`). `_attachment_data` verifies owner, chunk length and per-file sha256 per chunk and the whole-file digest at the end, refusing with `permission_denied` otherwise (`:160-164`). `_watch` polls `results(..., admission_ids=pending)` every 0.25 s and never re-reads the transcript (`:367-370`); on `OSError/ValueError/RuntimeStoreError` it returns quietly, retaining callbacks for driver history recovery, and never asserts a timed-out request was unaccepted (`:371-374`).
- **Surprising:** `produce` DISCARDS the caller's prompt and substitutes the source's attested one (`params.pop('prompt_sha256')`, then `params['prompt'] = attested['prompt']`) (`:253-256`) — only the source's durable, attested text may become the admitted input. The binding is persisted under `_BINDING + rpc.ref.session_id` and a differing re-bind raises `permission_denied` (`:244-250`). In `_check_remote_hosted_admission`, `verify_attested_documents` is called OUTSIDE the `permission_denied` fold on purpose: a corrupt retained document is a storage fault of this destination, not a revoked source binding (`:302-304`).

### `gateway/session_identity.py`
- **Purpose:** One frozen routing identity per inbound gateway event, answering "which bot received it / who may admit it / where does the turn run" in a single place.
- **Key abstractions:** `RoutingIdentity` frozen dataclass `:42-86` — fields `transport_profile`, `runtime_profile`, `authorization_home`, `runtime_home`, `multiplexed`, a weakref `transport` and `transport_inferred` (`:53-65`); properties `namespace` `:67`, `store_path` `:74`, `session_key_profile` `:78`; `adapter()` `:84`. Module-level: `IdentityUnresolved` `:36`, `identity_of` `:89`, `clear_identity` `:95`, `transport_profile_of` `:108`, `replace_source` `:115`, `canonical_identity` `:132`, `restore_identity` `:152`, `resolve_identity` `:192`, `parse_authenticated_subject` `:281`, `authenticated_subject` `:296`.
- **Data flow:** Ingress calls `resolve_identity` once; it pins `source._identity` (`:274`), `source._authorization_profile_home` (`:246`) and `source.profile` (`:262`). Every downstream helper reads the pinned identity and keeps its legacy fallback chain when it is absent (`:6-9`), so a source built outside the runner still resolves as before.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The identity is pinned as a WIRE-INVISIBLE dynamic attribute on the source, exactly like the pre-existing `_transport_adapter_ref` (`:7-9`) — the wire format and every historical `agent:main` key are unchanged (`:12-13`). `replace_source` exists because plain `dataclasses.replace` silently produces a source that can only be routed by heuristics; it re-copies `_PROVENANCE_ATTRS` (`:33`, `:115-124`). `restore_identity` returns `None` for a non-multiplexed runner (`:167-168`) and for a row written before the `transport_profile` column existed (`:164-166`), deliberately keeping the legacy `_is_shared_bot_satellite` heuristics.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `IdentityUnresolved` must never fall through to the default profile (`:36-40`); it is raised when `profile_route_rejected` is set (`:249-250`) or when the route target is not served (`:258-261`), and the rejected profile is recorded on the source for the producer's retry (`:260`). `clear_identity` deletes the pinned identity AND resets `source.profile = None` (`:101-105`) because `source.profile` is the previous event's routing RESULT — a shared guild voice source would otherwise route the new speaker as the old one (`:96-100`).
- **Surprising:** `transport_inferred` (`:65`, set at `:230`) marks an identity whose receiving bot was never named; delivery may fall back to the runtime profile's unique adapter only for those, while an identity whose transport is KNOWN (live or restored) never does (`:61-64`). `authenticated_subject` (`:296-309`) keeps a bare subject only for the private native bootstrap (provider `local` with `profile_id` + `instance_id` + `capabilities`); every other identity is namespaced as `auth:v1:[provider, issuer, user]` so a dashboard cannot impersonate native provenance.

### `gateway/session_images.py` — Staging-only image upload on the authenticated session authority: `attach_bytes` (`gateway/session_images.py:8`) validates params, requires the session to be subscribed (`:13-14`), authorizes `session:submit` against the OWNER's own home rather than the caller's or ambient profile (`:15-19`), and stages under `<owner home>/cache/images` (`:19`) with the MIME sniffed from the BYTES, not from the request (`:30-35`); `OSError` becomes `storage_unavailable` (`:36-39`).

### `gateway/session_info_reads.py` — The shared gateway answers `/toolsets` and `/platforms` from the OWNING profile inside the owner scope by calling the classic CLI's own line builders (`gateway/session_info_reads.py:13-23`), so Ink and every other local client see identical text; `run_info_read` (`:37`) renders off the owner loop through `owner_lines` (`:26`) with `policy_for_source` supplying the session's frozen toolset route.

### `gateway/session_ingress.py`
- **Purpose:** Trusted messaging admission and the existing TurnRunner invocation boundary — the place a platform event becomes a durable row and then a turn.
- **Key abstractions:** ContextVars `admission_author` `:14`, `executing_admission` `:15`, `viewer_turn` `:17`, `busy_acceptance` `:19`, `busy_commit` `:21`; `QUEUE_FULL_REPLY` `:31`; `admit_message` `:35`; `_hand_over_turn_marker` `:104`; `retain_unsettled_delivery` `:150`; `deliver_resolved` `:169`; `pause_notice` `:186`; `row_turn_author` `:214`; `execute_admission` `:226`; `_launch_body_if_launch_owner` `:338`; `_execution_scope` `:351`; `deliver_settled` `:373`; `deliver_response` `:406`; `dispatch_shared_busy` `:421`; `_hold_until_committed` `:478`.
- **Data flow:** platform event → `admit_message` → `authority.admit_native(event)` → durable admission row → a per-admission waiter future (`:64-65`) → the drain calls `execute_admission` → `authority.runner._handle_message(event)` (`:294`) → result stashed in `authority.pending_results` (`:310-312`) → `deliver_settled` sends it (`:373-403`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The turn's durable crash marker (`_gateway_active_turn_token`) is handed from the handler to the drain and released only once the reply is in the delivery ledger (`:104-119`, `:326`) — a kill between the terminal commit and the ledger write leaves a marked turn whose persisted reply the next unclean boot ledgers and sends once, WITHOUT re-running inference (`:105-110`). `busy_acceptance` and `busy_commit` are separate futures because acceptance may resolve once the FIFO position is fixed behind an earlier capturing input, while a command barrier (`/restart` closing admission) must wait for the actual commit (`:19-23`, `:43-44`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `stale_generation` with `field == 'route'` → the event is dropped silently, because a later `/stop` or `/new` fenced it (`:46-47`). `invalid_params` names the clause to the chat ONLY for clauses in `_CONTENT_CLAUSES` (`:25-28`); trust and provenance clauses stay generic (`:48-54`). `queue_full` returns a canned reply (`:55-58`, `:31-32`). A row deleted with its chat reads as status `unknown` → `pause_notice` (`:74-82`). `pause_notice` fires once per pause episode (`live.pause_notified`) and varies the advice by reason (`:186-211`): `runtime_draining` and `session_busy` say "wait", while `unknown_execution` offers `/discard` — and `/hermes discard` on Slack, which registers no native `/discard` (`:204-205`).
- **Surprising:** For an API caller a turn that produced no TurnRunner result is a FAILURE receipt, never "a completed apology" (`:296-307`); the same applies to a finite (`chat -q`/`-z`) prompt unless it was a command whose reply IS the answer. The API event's `text` is rewritten to only the text parts of the prepared content (`:288-291`) while the durable transcript keeps the committed media references. `_execution_scope` runs owner-side execution under the OWNING profile's home (agent build, config, secrets, state.db), never the launch profile's ambient scope, and a single-profile gateway keeps its ambient scope byte-for-byte (`:351-370`); `_launch_body_if_launch_owner` marks only the launch owner's turn so a mid-turn switch to multiplexing cannot fail later credential reads closed (`:338-348`).

### `gateway/session_ingress_burst.py` — Merges a same-sender PHOTO burst or queue-mode TEXT burst into ONE shared-runtime admission: `dispatch_busy_burst` (`gateway/session_ingress_burst.py:77`) holds the burst for a bounded window (`_arm` `:119`, debounce vs hard cap), commits it exactly once via `dispatch_shared_busy(..., durable=True)` (`:153`), and every absorbed caller sees the merged admission's outcome (`:106`, `_settle` `:161`); a serial reader (`serial_receive_loop`) returns WITHOUT waiting because returning triggers no acknowledgement (`:107-108`), and `burst_gate` (`:56`) makes a command wait for every earlier input that reader already returned on.

### `gateway/session_ingress_context.py` — Pins the process-local callback provenance for native ingress: `_binding` (`gateway/session_ingress_context.py:96`) resolves the adapter, transport home and runtime home and refuses anything not backed by a reservation-backed profile home (`:112-159`), with route matching by highest specificity and an `admission_conflict` on a tie (`:146-151`); the provenance dict `{transport_home, runtime_home, platform, connector}` (`:177-178`) is compared EXACTLY on restore (`:210`), so a registry/credential change between ingress and execution fails closed instead of borrowing the old grant, and `carry_callback` (`:83`) refuses to carry provenance onto a rewritten event whose source was swapped.

### `gateway/session_ingress_media.py`
- **Purpose:** Immutable, content-addressed retention of native media references for owner-only admission — bytes a turn will execute are frozen at admission so later cache cleanup cannot change them.
- **Key abstractions:** `capture_lease` `:53`, `drop_capture_lease` `:67`, `abandon_capture` `:81`, `release_abandoned_capture` `:90`, `native_inputs_store` `:101`, `native_inputs_root` `:109`, `sniff_image_mime` `:127`, `admit_attachments` `:140`, `ingress_fingerprint` `:185`, `reconcile_native_retry` `:202`, `_attachment_hashes` `:237`, `restore_attachments` `:256`, `_open_regular` `:264`, `capture_native_media` `:274`, `validate_media_batch_size` `:347`, `hosted_document_references` `:363`, `admission_media_references` `:381`, `release_admission_media` `:429`, `sweep_settled_media` `:462`, `collect_unheld_api_images` `:490`, `_history_mentions` `:516`, `release_unheld_media` `:545`, `restore_native_media` `:595`.
- **Data flow:** staging file in the image cache → `admit_attachments` hashes it (`:161`) → `capture_native_media` copies it to `<native-inputs>/<sha256>/<name>` (`:338`) → a `{path, sha256, size}` reference lands in the committed payload (`:341`, `:182`) → `release_admission_media` / `sweep_settled_media` / `collect_unheld_api_images` unlink them.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `native_inputs_store()` deliberately returns the UNRESOLVED path — `resolve()` would erase a link planted below the Hermes home (`cache/documents` → elsewhere), and an elevated grant on the resolved path would land outside the home with no home fence (`:101-106`). The store is content-addressed (`<sha256>/<name>`), which is what lets a re-capture of the same bytes reuse one file and lets `restore_native_media` re-verify size and digest (`:605-612`). The lease counter (`:35-36`) orders a capture's lease-then-publish against a release's lease-check-then-unlink per path, so an older turn's settlement cannot unlink bytes a new admission is about to hold (`:53-57`, `:294-298`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_open_regular` refuses symlinks and non-regular files via `O_NOFOLLOW` + `fstat` (`:264-271`); a hard-linked staging file (`st_nlink != 1`) is refused because staging must not provide a second name for a file outside it (`:241-243`). A missing staging file yields `None` in the hash list rather than skipping validation of the rest of the batch (`:245-247`). `admit_attachments` reconciles a lost-ACK retry whose disposable staging file is gone by matching committed names and `media_types` (`:164-171`), and treats changed bytes as `admission_conflict` (`:168-170`). `reconcile_native_retry` makes retry identity the committed BYTES rather than the disposable staging name, and falls back to the recorded `ingress_sha256` fingerprint for a delivery committed in `pre_gateway_dispatch`-rewritten form (`:202-235`).
- **Surprising:** `_held_media_paths` keeps API images alive in EVERY status because they remain history context after settlement (`:399-400`, `:433-437`), and hosted documents are held by the PROMPT TEXT of a live hosted row — projected in SQL only for non-terminal `hosted:%` rows (`:392-393`) — not by any media reference. `_history_mentions` passes all digests as ONE bound JSON array consumed by `json_each` instead of one `instr` term each, because SQLite's expression-depth limit fails at ~1000 terms (`:529-532`). `collect_unheld_api_images` refuses to run when the DB does not belong to the ACTIVE profile (`:500-503`): a sweep for another profile would see every image here as unowned and delete it.

### `gateway/session_kanban.py`
- **Purpose:** A trusted Kanban dispatcher claim enters the ordinary owner's durable admission queue — a card becomes a normal gateway session rather than a parallel execution path.
- **Key abstractions:** `build_kanban_policy(connection, params, config)` `:14`; `run_task(connection, params)` `:73`; `bind_worker_context(frame)` `:155`; `_watch_submitter` `:208`; `run_worker_turns` `:228`; `_worker_workspace` `:238`; `_run_worker_turns` `:268`; `record_start_failure` `:291`; `_record_worker_result` `:301`; `_bound_worker_matches` `:315`; `_bound_claim_is_live` `:323`; `worker_result` `:338`; `worker_exit_code` `:347`; `_run_task_turns` `:351`.
- **Data flow:** Kanban DB (SQLite, the cross-profile dispatch bus) → `run_task` verifies the claim → creates or restores a local session → `admit_claim` writes an `owner_admitted` task_event (`:127-130`) → `authority.submit(actor, Submission(request_id, ref, payload, 'queue'))` (`:150`) → the owner's FIFO runs it.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The `request_id` IS the durable attempt identity — `'kanban:' + json.dumps([board, task_id, run_id])` (`:89`) — so a retry checks the saved claim and never reinterprets an updated card (`:87-88`). The claim check and its `owner_admitted` marker share ONE write transaction, off the owner loop (`:111-132`), so a concurrent retry of the same attempt meets the marker (same session) or a stale claim — never both. The card attempt is FINITE (`payload['finite'] = True`, `:143`) but never unattended (`:134-136`): `approvals.single_query_mode` applies, unlike the one-shot CLI's `-z` auto-approval.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Every stale-claim condition raises `RuntimeStoreError('stale_kanban_claim')` — task/run not running, `current_run_id` mismatch, `claim_lock` mismatch (`:34-37`), a bound marker carrying a different `request_id` (`:42-43`), a marker naming a different session (`:121-122`), and a re-checked claim in `bind_worker_context` (`:182-185`). The assignee's resolved profile home must equal the authority's home (`:38-39`). A retry against a saved receipt refuses any param that differs from the frozen context (`:95-97`). `_record_worker_result` writes only when the bound worker still matches (`:307`) — closing a run clears its claim/PID, so the result is appended to the immutable attempt event stream instead (`:308-312`). `record_start_failure` books the one-shot CLI's credential exit codes (75 quota wall / 78 re-login / 1) when the agent never even built (`:291-298`).
- **Surprising:** The worker no longer runs the agent itself — the dispatcher's spawned worker is this attempt's HANDLE, and `_watch_submitter` calls `os._exit(1)` when that pid dies (`:208-225`), so an operator kill ends the attempt and the dead-worker sweep books the crash. `bind_worker_context` records the bound interpreter BESIDE the run's pid (`:189-192`) so a sweep can tell when the attempt is truly gone, and it inherits the dispatcher's claim TTL from the `claimed` event rather than using its own default (`:195-199`).

### `gateway/session_lifecycle.py`
- **Purpose:** `SessionStore` explicit suspension, crash-recovery markers, pruning and the shared clock/id helpers.
- **Key abstractions:** `auto_continue_freshness_window` `:49`; `SessionLifecycleMixin` `:58` — `_is_session_ended_in_db` `:61`, `_local_receipt_target` `:92`, `_route_reset_reason` `:119`, `_update_entry` `:123`, `_update_all_entries_locked` `:133`, `suspend_session` `:143`, `_set_turn_marker_locked` `:150`, `mark_turn_active` `:167`, `clear_turn_active` `:181`, `recover_interrupted_turns` `:191`, `discard_active_turn_markers` `:224`, `mark_resume_pending` `:234`, `clear_resume_pending` `:245`, `prune_old_entries` `:255`.
- **Data flow:** sessions.json entries → `_update_entry` (lock + full save) `:123-131` → `mark_turn_active` / `clear_turn_active` (compare-and-swap on an opaque token) → an unclean boot calls `recover_interrupted_turns`, which promotes stale markers into `resume_pending` `:191-222`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_set_turn_marker_locked` persists the token BEFORE publishing it in memory (`:150-165`), so a failed write can neither leak an unowned token nor drop a live one. `mark_turn_active` stamps an AWARE UTC timestamp (`:176-178`) unlike the local wall clock used elsewhere (`:21-23`), because the next process compares it with epoch transcript timestamps and may run in another timezone. `recover_interrupted_turns` uses epoch arithmetic so a pre-upgrade NAIVE marker reads as local time (`:204-206`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_is_session_ended_in_db` treats a MISSING row exactly like an ended one (`:71-73`) — reusing the route would let run_agent's INSERT OR IGNORE resurrect the deleted session with its old id (#42422) — and DB errors are non-fatal, never blocking routing on a failed lookup (`:88-89`). `_local_receipt_target` (`:92-117`) exists because dropping a stamped-ended route minted a replacement id the authority refuses (`admission_conflict`) and stranded the conversation (#140642); every receipt read is wrapped so a malformed receipt simply yields `None` (`:111-117`). `clear_turn_active` is a compare-and-swap: a stale asynchronous unwind cannot clear a newer turn's marker (`:181-189`). `recover_interrupted_turns` clears stale/invalid markers without resuming and never re-arms a suspended session (`:198-219`).
- **Surprising:** `suspend_session` (via `_route_reset_reason`) is the ONLY thing that replaces a routed conversation — time never does (`:119-121`). `prune_old_entries` drops only the key→session_id mapping (the transcript stays) and keeps suspended entries and entries with active background processes (`:255-278`). `recover_interrupted_turns` keeps an existing `resume_pending` marker (a drain-timeout marker is more specific) but backfills `last_resume_marked_at` if it is missing (`:208-212`).

### `gateway/session_listing.py` — Read-only native picker history that never adopts a transcript: `stored_picker_sessions` (`gateway/session_listing.py:26`) offers legacy local rows ONLY to the same native transport that may resume them (`:34-36`), projects messaging conversations onto their lineage root (`:54-58`), and gates local rows through `validate_legacy_row` + `authorize_history` (`:70-78`); a canonical local session its last viewer closed is surfaced by `_closed_local_session` (`:88`) after an explicit `session:read` authorization.

### `gateway/session_live_listing.py` — The live session feeds read off the loop: `live_entries` (`gateway/session_live_listing.py:88`) authorizes each session in memory and fetches the store facts for a BATCH of 256 in ONE worker-thread read (`_store_facts` `:37`) — walking the registry row-by-row had stalled the loop past 10 s and made WS writes and the Telegram adapter's `updater.stop()` miss their deadlines (#141236) — and returns `[]` outright if the authority retired mid-read (`:98-99`); `_masked_pending` (`:62`) keeps an unpublished settlement visible as its started claim.

### `gateway/session_llm_oneshot.py`
- **Purpose:** `llm.oneshot` on the shared gateway — a session-bound one-shot borrows that session's model instead of falling through to the auxiliary task backend.
- **Key abstractions:** `_FIELDS` `:27`, `_TEXT` `:29`, `_WORKERS = 4` `:32`, `_ADMITTED = 16` `:33`; `handlers(connection)` `:36`; `_Pool` `:40` (`submit` `:56`, `release` `:84`, `drain` `:90`); `_pool(authority)` `:106`; `forget(connection)` `:113`; `stop(authority)` `:122`; `DeferredReply` `:137` (`start` `:143`); `_answer` `:159`; `_validate` `:192`; `_session_runtime` `:208`; `oneshot` `:233`.
- **Data flow:** socket request → `oneshot` → `_validate` → runtime resolution (the resident agent's live route, else the session's frozen launch route) `:208-230` → `DeferredReply(work)` → `pool.submit` → a worker thread runs `run_oneshot` under `owner_scope` `:251-261` → `_answer` writes exactly one JSON-RPC frame `:189`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_Pool` is a hand-rolled FIFO rather than a `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`:42-46`): an executor keeps a CANCELLED queued call (its params and copied context) until a worker dequeues it, so with every worker parked on the model a reconnect loop would pile up payloads past the bound — here a cancelled call leaves the queue at once (`:84-88`). The per-owner pool is deliberately separate from the loop's default executor, which session resume, socket setup and disconnect cleanup share across every connection and profile (`:11-13`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** More than `_ADMITTED` in-flight calls → `RuntimeStoreError('oneshot_busy')` with a detail string (`:63`); a closed pool → `runtime_draining` (`:61`); thread exhaustion (`RuntimeError` from the spawn) decrements the thread count and cancels the call when no worker remains to drain it (`:74-81`). `_answer` races the call against `pool.stopping` (`:162-177`): if the owner stopped first, the late result is dropped, its exception consumed unlogged, and the frame is error code 4001 `runtime_draining`; otherwise a failure is error 5030 with the exception text (`:184-188`). `forget` (socket closed) cancels only the answer task — a running call cannot be interrupted, finishes, and keeps its slot until then (`:113-119`). `stop` cancels queued calls and still answers every outstanding request once, so one profile's unserve leaves the host socket open for the others (`:122-134`).
- **Surprising:** `_session_runtime` resolves with `fallback=False` (`:222-229`) — if the session's primary route cannot resolve, the one-shot fails with 5030 instead of walking the route's fallback chain, because that walk sets the RUNNER-WIDE fallback notice the NEXT turn would report. A session-less call (no `session_id`) runs on the task backend and only requires `session:read` (`:239-249`).

### `gateway/session_local.py`
- **Purpose:** Fresh local routes owned by the authenticated gateway rather than by a viewer; source and supported launch settings are frozen per route, never from process env.
- **Key abstractions:** `LocalSessionAdapter(BasePlatformAdapter)` `:21` — `authorize_source` `:30`, `register_source` `:42`, `send` `:51`, `_record_delivery_obligation` `:58`, `send_clarify` `:74`, `_unanswerable_clarify` `:87`; `authorize_local_source(runner, source)` `:117`; `_bypass_policy` `:137`; `load_profile_config` `:154`; `profile_env_scope` `:166`; `project_plugins_warning` `:183`; `create_local_session` `:202`; `staged_creation_follows` `:216`; `stage_local_session` `:238`; `publish_local_policy` `:339`; `_lazy_model` `:350`; `_reasoning_fields` `:364`; `effort_fields` `:386`; `local_session_info` `:406`; `_classic_cli_fields` `:446`.
- **Data flow:** client `session.create` → `stage_local_session` freezes the policy and stages the receipt (`:238-336`) → `commit_local_session` → `restore_local_session` → `authority.sessions`; delivery stays in the canonical event stream via `LocalSessionAdapter.send`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `LocalSessionAdapter.send` returns `SendResult(success=True, message_id=uuid4().hex)` (`:51-53`) — a no-op, because TurnRunner already publishes model/tool deltas and settlement exactly once; there is no second local transport (`:22`). `_record_delivery_obligation` returns `None` (`:58-61`): there is no platform ACK to lose, viewers replay the durable transcript, and a ledger row would only park a raw copy of the reply in state.db that no redelivery can ever use. `send_clarify` declines with `EGRESS_DECLINE_CODE` when nobody can answer (`:74-83`) because acknowledging the question would park the turn on an answer nobody can submit — an ordinary failure would fall back to a `send()` that ACKs and the turn would wait out the whole clarify timeout (`:76-80`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `authorize_local_source` returns `False` (fail closed) for a LOCAL source naming a profile this runtime does not serve, or for a stale/foreign adapter that merely carries a LOCAL adapter — legacy runners with no authority stay `None` (`:126-133`). An exact retry carrying a different frozen request → `invalid_params` field `request_id` (`:293-295`); a client-supplied `api_key` against an owner-resolved alias → `admission_conflict` (`:296-302`); a key that does not match → `admission_conflict` via `hmac.compare_digest` (`:303-306`). A caller that asked for `-s` skills but skipped rendering them → `invalid_params` field `skills` (`:309-313`), because a session must not be minted silently missing its preload. `stage_local_session` requires the actor's profile to equal the authority's (`:244-245`) and `session:create` (`:246-247`).
- **Surprising:** `_bypass_policy` (`:137-151`) never reads the profile's `config.yaml`/`.env` at all — a malformed profile must not block or shape the session — and since there is no default model to inherit, an explicit model is REQUIRED (`:144-146`). `_lazy_model` (`:350-361`) reports the free tier's `nous/welcome` rather than the configured default, because the free tier's agent build pins that model. `load_profile_config` expands `${VAR}` against the profile's CURRENT `.env` (the source tool env refreshes from), not the env the daemon started with, and a bound scope is kept (`:154-163`). `profile_env_scope` yields unchanged when a scope is already bound (`:172-174`).

### `gateway/session_local_adoption.py` — Commits a local receipt's compression catch-up OFF the owner loop: `adopt_before_restore` (`gateway/session_local_adoption.py:43`) runs the adoption in a tracked worker (`tracked_write`) or a plain thread, and `schedule_adoption` (`:68`) does the same then publishes the session on the loop — because `adopt_untracked_compression` takes the writer lock for up to `_WRITE_PATIENCE_S` and would stall every profile the owner serves (`:1-7`); a failed adoption is logged and the receipt is left for the restore that follows to refuse (`:57-58`, `:85-86`).

### `gateway/session_local_close.py` — The verdict on which live local rows read as closed: `closed_idle_unviewed(live, execution_state, target_ended_at)` (:19) is true only for an idle row with no subscribers, a superseded row counts as closed, and the stamp itself is the owner's `session.detach` in `session_acp_lifecycle`, not this module.

### `gateway/session_local_editor.py` — Editor policy lives with execution and an ACP connection is only a viewer: `validate_editor` (:9) refuses every branch with its own field name and forces `edit_approval_policy == 'ask'`, while `request_editor_edit` (:37) mints a one-use uuid `pattern_key` with `allow_session`/`allow_permanent` both False, so a diff can never become a session or permanent grant.

### `gateway/session_local_inventory.py`
- **Purpose:** Computes the banner/status-bar inventory (tools by toolset, skills by category, MCP servers, version/release date, update notice, git branch, project, provider, system prompt) for a canonical local session whose agent does not exist until its first turn.
- **Key abstractions:** `session_inventory(authority, policy, agent, cwd)` (gateway/session_local_inventory.py:133) — the synchronous builder; `snapshot_info(authority, ref)` (:192) — the async wrapper that runs it off the owner loop; `agent_inventory(agent)` (:206) — the cheap subset only a built agent knows; `publish_settled_info(turn, agent)` (:223) — the end-of-turn emission; helpers `_grouped` (:34), `_policy_tools` (:44), `_project` (:61), `_cli_startup_notices` (:76), `_unknown_toolsets` (:98), `_cli_skills` (:111).
- **Data flow:** `snapshot_info` → `local_session_info(authority, ref)` + `policy_for_source(...)` + `authority.agent(ref)` → `asyncio.to_thread(session_inventory, ...)` → merged as `{**inventory, **info}` (:203), so the local-session fields win over the inventory's. `publish_settled_info` fires at turn end for a local viewer, once the first turn has built the agent.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_cli_skills` (:111-130) pops the module-level `skills_tool._SKILLS_CACHE[("filtered", False)]` entry, scans under a scoped kanban ContextVar, and *restores* the saved entry in a `finally` — the classic CLI pins `HERMES_KANBAN_BOARD` before `HermesCLI` starts, so the owner reproduces the kanban-relevant skill set for this scan only, without poisoning the shared catalog cache for other surfaces. `_policy_tools` (:44-58) computes the tool list a *lazy* session's first agent will load, from the frozen policy's toolsets minus the configured and platform-gated disabled ones, plus `ONESHOT_HIDDEN_TOOLS` for a `oneshot` source.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** Each section degrades to main's empty value independently — `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` wraps the availability (:163-166), skills (:169-174), MCP status (:175-177), version (:178-181) and update (:182-188) blocks; a tool-snapshot failure is caught, logged and leaves `info["tools"]` empty rather than blanking the banner (:160-161); `_project` swallows any projects.db error and names no project (:69-71).
- **Surprising:** `publish_settled_info` deliberately *evicts* the agent from the cache when the turn was a one-shot `model_override` admission (`/model --once`, `/moa`) (:241-251) — that agent was built on the one-turn route and must not become what Desktop, the TUI and resume read; the published model/provider are re-read from the policy route instead (:254-266).

### `gateway/session_local_mcp.py`
- **Purpose:** Owns an editor's MCP server configuration as gateway session state: only a fingerprint and the frozen schema manifest are durable; the transport spec (argv, headers, credentials) lives in owner memory and must be resupplied after an owner restart.
- **Key abstractions:** `validate_servers` (:41), `fingerprint` (:37), `private_editor_request` (:73), `private_mutation_payload` (:84), `session_scope` (:98), `_server_names` (:103), `_frozen_names` (:111), `_frozen_toolset` (:119), `_configs` (:128), `_Held` (:145), `_borrowed` (:182), `release_editor_mcp` (:219), `rebind_editor_mcp` (:233), `bind_editor_mcp` (:263), `attach_editor_mcp` (:271), `resume_editor_mcp` (:282), `retire_editor_mcp` (:302), `install_editor_mcp` (:310), `announce_tools_changed` (:325), `prepare_editor_mcp` (:333), `_live_tools_changed` (:349), `refreeze_editor_mcp` (:397), `prepare_branch_editor` (:462), `editor_mcp_scope` (:499). Constant `PRIVATE_KEY = ('editor_mcp',)` (:28), `_RELIST_TIMEOUT = 5` (:30).
- **Data flow:** A launch request's `editor.mcp_servers` list is validated and moved out of the request by `private_editor_request` (:73-81): the request keeps only `{'fingerprint': ...}` and the full list is stashed under `PRIVATE_KEY` in owner memory. At turn time `editor_mcp_scope` (:499-511) looks the servers up by scope, borrows them inside the session's tool scope, and refuses with `acp_mcp_schema_changed` if the registry's live schemas differ from the frozen manifest.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The session scope is `sha256(f'{profile_id}:{sid}')` (:100) — never shared with another session, a fork included — and each server's ledger name embeds a per-session tag `editor_<name>_<sha8>` (:103-108) so two sessions borrowing same-named servers can never adopt one another's connection. `_frozen_names` (:111-116) reconstructs names for manifests frozen before the `names` key existed, so an old session's toolset prefix still matches.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `validate_servers` caps at 16 servers and 65536 serialized bytes (:48-53) and deliberately *never* echoes pydantic's message, because it carries `input_value=` and a server's argv may hold a credential (:62-70) — it rebuilds `loc: type` with `include_input=False`. A held connection whose fingerprint changed (credentials swapped, or the cwd moved) while `held.users > 0` raises `session_busy` (:194-198). A server that fails discovery is dropped from the frozen selection with a warning and never respawned (:250-252). `refreeze_editor_mcp` is best-effort by contract: any failure only logs, because the probe spawns arbitrary editor servers and nothing it raises may kill the drain (:405-409).
- **Surprising:** `_live_tools_changed` (:349-394) bounds the *whole* relist at 5 s — all servers at once via `asyncio.wait(tasks, timeout=...)` — not 5 s per server, and every task is cancelled in the `finally`, so an unanswered `tools/list` counts as unchanged (:369-377). `refreeze_editor_mcp` re-commits through `session.mutate`'s own writer with a synthetic `editor-mcp-refreeze:<uuid>` request id and the session's current revision/generation (:446-449): the drain mutates the session *on the owner's behalf* and "a schema re-check needs no capability" (:439-440).

### `gateway/session_local_migration.py`
- **Purpose:** Owner-only adoption of pre-authority local transcripts — an old transcript has no authenticated subject, so only a redeemed same-profile native control ticket may adopt it.
- **Key abstractions:** `bind_native_transport` (:19), `unbind_native_transport` (:30), `require_history_claim` (:34), `resolve_local_target` (:46), `legacy_launch_params` (:70), `bot_chat_row` (:114), `composer_pick_holds` (:122), `legacy_bot_chat_launch` (:132), `follow_bot_chat` (:148), `_route_is_usage_stamp` (:189), `_configured_owner` (:204), `_endpoint_owner` (:217), `adopt_legacy_session` (:232).
- **Data flow:** `adopt_legacy_session` reads the legacy row → `legacy_launch_params` reconstructs `(create params, api_mode)` from `model_config` → `legacy_bot_chat_launch` decides whether the row is a Bot Chat that follows its profile → `build_policy` + `bind_launch_key` → `commit_local_session` under the active-session liveness guard → `restore_local_session` (:308-316).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The route rebinding has three deliberate branches (:255-281): (a) the saved route *is* the profile's current route → `provider`/`base_url` are dropped so the profile's own credential precedence (inline key, `${ENV}`, `key_env`) applies; (b) the only record is a bare `custom` usage stamp whose endpoint no configured entry serves any more → both are dropped, because freezing a dead endpoint makes every turn retry it until the cycle gives up (:265-272); (c) any other route → `provider` survives but every other `ROUTE_BOUND_KEYS` entry is dropped, so the profile's credential is never paired with the legacy route (:273-281).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `resolve_local_target` refuses a *copied* `chat_id` whose lineage owner does not match with `storage_unavailable` rather than adopting canonical corruption (:59-61). `require_history_claim` (:34-43) treats a session row with any of `session_key`/`chat_id`/`origin_json` set as already-bound, and an existing import-receipt binding as proof that the committed importer identity was already authorized. `adopt_legacy_session` raises `not_found` for a non-native or capability-less actor (:235-237) — not `permission_denied`, so the existence of another principal's row is not confirmed.
- **Surprising:** `follow_bot_chat` (:148-186) is not simply "follow the profile": it records the composer pick as `selection_profile` (the profile target the pick diverged from, :182) and pins `reasoning` under `SESSION_PINS` (:184-185), and the follow is granted only while `composer_pick_holds` is true — i.e. the profile still targets what the pick diverged from (:122-129). `legacy_launch_params` returns `api_mode` *separately* from the create params because "api_mode is no create field" (:77-78), and it preserves the legacy row's own effective route rather than combining it with a later profile default (:70-77).

### `gateway/session_local_recovery.py`
- **Purpose:** Restores only private, profile-bound local policy into the existing authority, and renders the display history of a local conversation.
- **Key abstractions:** `local_adapter_map` (:15), `local_source` (:30), `_refused_check` (:42), `_ReceiptRefused` (:54), `_CatchUpPending` (:58), `_with_untracked_compression` (:62), `_checked_entry` (:82), `owned_receipt` (:101), `_stray_route_target` (:115), `restore_local_session` (:137), `transcript_target` (:201), `display_rows` (:217), `_fit_archive` (:235), `prepare_history` (:250), `_merge_live_in_stored_order` (:256), `local_history` (:282), `reopen_local_session` (:318), `reset_local_session` (:339), `local_receipt_ids` (:354), `recover_local_sessions` (:360). Constant `RESUME_HISTORY_BYTES = 3 * 1024 * 1024` (:214).
- **Data flow:** `restore_local_session` reads the receipt (`local_receipt`) → `owned_receipt`/`_checked_entry` verify route, origin and stored identity (:82-112) → `_with_untracked_compression` commits any compression step an older build published past its tip → `validate_local_lineage` → registers the adapter policy and a `LiveSession` (:161-198). `local_history` reads the live rows plus the compression lineage and merges them.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_with_untracked_compression` (:62-79) *writes*, so it never runs on the owner loop: a sync restore on the loop that still finds pending catch-up work hands it to a tracked worker, publishes the session when it commits, and refuses the current read with `_CatchUpPending` (:74-77). `_stray_route_target` (:115-134) treats a SessionStore stale-route self-heal replacement outside the receipt's lineage as provably stray *only* when there is no history (messages, admissions, worker executions, children), and then restores the receipt target — otherwise every resume of that conversation would answer `admission_conflict` forever.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_refused_check` (:42-51) walks the `__cause__` chain to build `"<reason> at <func>:<line>, caused by …"` for the log, because the client only ever sees a bounded code (:155-160). `display_rows` returns `(live, [], False)` — live rows only, no archive — when `assert_resume_safe` fails the 8 MiB frame cap (:226-229). `_merge_live_in_stored_order` (:256-279) places each archived row before the live row it preceded in *display* order while keeping the live subsequence in stored `row_id` order, because clients take the live rows as the transcript. `local_history` re-reads the lineage when a row it read as live is no longer live (:304-306) and re-fits the archive when rows were appended after the prepare (:307-310).
- **Surprising:** Archived rows carry `archived: true` so no undo, retry or rewind may target them (:286-287), and each row carries its durable `messages.id` as `row_id` because a `session.mutate` rewind addresses `target_message_id` by it (:291-293). `reopen_local_session` (:318-336) clears the `end_reason` stamp on the first admitted turn: `SessionStore` reads a stamped row as a stale route and answers with a *fresh* session, which leaves the client waiting on an id that never emits again.

### `gateway/session_local_route.py` — `resolve_launch_route` (:5) decodes a `session.create` `-m` string through the same `resolve_startup_model_route` the CLI/oneshot/TUI use: unresolved, the alias name would silently become the model sent to the profile's DEFAULT provider with the alias's own endpoint/key unused (#103933/#107191/#109440), and an alias key is a launch key — authority memory only, revoked by restart (:36-37).

### `gateway/session_local_title.py` — Title-keyed local session selection resolved read-only by the owner: exact session id first, then title (the latest `"<title> #N"` continuation wins), projected forward to the compression tip, and a `permission_denied` is rewritten to `not_found` so another principal's titles are never confirmed (:12-34).

### `gateway/session_managed_worker.py`
- **Purpose:** Admission-owned production execution: a managed worker runs the turn out of process, and the admission (not any viewer) owns the worker's lifetime.
- **Key abstractions:** `ManagedExecutionUnknown` (:32), `managed_policy` (:36), `_bootstrap` (:56), `_worker_config` (:88), `_session_yolo` (:106), `worker_turn_scope` (:117), `ManagedWorker` (:147), `interrupt_managed` (:345), `_prompt_frame` (:355), `_worker_env` (:395), `_launch_env_only_credentials` (:433), `_interrupted_before_bootstrap` (:450), `_latched_stop_cause` (:457), `_settled_session_usage` (:471), `_execution_tip` (:491), `_continuation_path` (:500), `_compression_ancestors` (:512), `_mark_spawning` (:524), `_stopped_before_spawn` (:535), `_spawn_registered_worker` (:548), `execute_managed` (:582). Constants `HELLO_SECONDS = 60`, `STOP_ACK_SECONDS = 30`, `WORKER_POPEN` (:23-29).
- **Data flow:** `execute_managed` → `managed_turn_text` (the `@` expansion, which the in-process prepare step would otherwise have handled) → `_stopped_before_spawn` → spawn `[sys.executable, '-m', 'agent.managed_worker']` with `cwd = parents[1]` of this file (:555-561) → await the hello frame (:611) → `reserve_admission_worker` off-loop under `tracked_write` (:617) → send the `_bootstrap` frame (hydrated policy + launch key) → frame loop dispatching `prompt_settled` / `approval` / `clarify` / `ready` / `delta` / tool events / `result` / `finished` → `authority.pending_results[admission_id]` (:658-661).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The child is a *first-party module of the owner package*, not an external binary (:559), and it identifies itself before anything is reserved: the owner verifies the interpreter **behind** the Popen handle (a launcher trampoline may sit between) via `psutil` + `process_birth` and only then reserves for it (:609-619, :288-300). The Stop budget is *total from the first Stop*: `stopped_at` is stamped only by `control` (:230-232, :239-243), so a child that keeps emitting valid frames is escalated on the same deadline as a silent one, instead of leaving the turn started behind it (:245-248).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** The frame loop is fenced by the claim's execution stamp, not by a ledger read per frame: `authority.executing[session_id] != row['generation']` → `stale_generation` (:628-629). A Stop latched before the child existed is consumed by `_stopped_before_spawn` (:535-545) and settles as an ordinary interrupted turn with nothing executed (:668-672). `ManagedExecutionUnknown` (:689) stops the drain *without* the ordinary Exception→failed settlement — the committed unknown row deliberately pauses every accepted follower, and the reply is painted as `outcome: 'unknown'`, never as a normal finish (:681-683).
- **Surprising:** `_worker_config` (:88-103) treats `command_allowlist` as *approval state*, not launch policy: it pops the frozen value and substitutes the live profile's, so a grant an earlier worker persisted still applies. `_worker_env` (:395-430) returns `None` (inherit the process env byte-for-byte) for a single-profile gateway, but for a multiplexed secondary it builds a scrubbed env plus that profile's own secret scope; `_launch_env_only_credentials` (:433-447) re-adds only the launch profile's own `launch_secret_scope` credentials, never Hermes-internal secrets (`AUXILIARY_*_API_KEY`, relay auth). `_settled_session_usage` (:471-488) sums the session-cumulative usage along the compression *continuation* path (200-hop cap) so a mid-turn compression still reports against the right reservation, and swallows `sqlite3.Error` because usage is display-only.

### `gateway/session_managed_worker_log.py` — Bounded, redacted per-profile sink for managed-worker stderr: the worker `os.dup2`s its own stdout into the same stream, the drain redacts each line (this worker's exact secrets first, longest-first, then `redact_sensitive_text(force=True)`) before writing 0600 with one rotation at 1 MiB, and a write failure logs once while the drain keeps reading so the child can never block on a full pipe (:36-88).

### `gateway/session_messaging_picks.py` — A viewer's `model`/`reasoning` pick on a messaging chat is the chat's own per-chat override, not a frozen policy: `apply_chat_pick` (:68) re-resolves `/model` against the *saved on-disk* override rather than config.yaml's provider so the record step cannot overwrite it, and the receipt is keyed on the live mount, so an exact retry answers its stored receipt while a retry of an older pick refuses `revision_conflict` (:70-74).

### `gateway/session_mutation_compress.py` — `session.mutate(compress)` preparation: a focus-only payload is re-parsed through the shared `parse_compress_args` so `here [N]` stays a boundary and `--preview` stays read-only, `--aggressive` is refused outright (:12-28), and the summarization runs off-loop against a `ContextCompressor` built from the session's own runtime, returning a `noop` report when the session is too short to compress anything (:86-92).

### `gateway/session_mutation_model.py`
- **Purpose:** Resolves an explicit `session.mutate model` pick through the existing CLI/TUI model-switch pipeline, then freezes the resulting selection into the session's policy.
- **Key abstractions:** `_Route` NamedTuple (:11), `_current_route` (:18), `prepare_model` (:45), `_provider_resolution_config` (:149), `_custom_provider_name` (:183), `_selected_provider_key_env` (:189), `_freeze_selected_provider` (:198), `_provider_key_slug` (:247), `_alias_credential` (:253), `_persisted_selection_context` (:285), `_selection_guard_refusal` (:293).
- **Data flow:** `prepare_model` → `restore_policy` → `_current_route` (one profile read) → `switch_model` (the shared switch pipeline) → `_selection_guard_refusal` → `apply_model_selection` (the one selection shape config.yaml gets) → the frozen config → `recover_config_secrets` → `bind_launch_key` → the new policy (:45-146).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_selection_guard_refusal` (:293-332) returns `confirmation_required` with a SHA-256 `confirm` token bound to `[new model, provider, normalized base_url, api_mode] + [current route] + [session id, revision] + sorted guard kinds` (:319-324); only that exact token in `payload['confirm']` applies the pick, and the refusal deliberately carries **no** `model` key so a client that predates the exchange cannot paint the target as applied (:327-328). A pick resolves against the profile's *current* named providers, not the frozen ones (:52-56, :149-180).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_current_route` swallows `AuthError` and switches from the configured provider alone (:35-40) — "a route that cannot authenticate here must not block leaving it". `_provider_resolution_config` merges current over frozen providers matched by *slug*, so a respelled key is the same provider (:163-170), and keeps current custom providers at their profile positions because cold recovery re-reads those indices (:175-179). `_alias_credential` (:253-282) reads the alias's own credential from *this owner's* `config.yaml` (the file a restarted owner re-reads), never from the process-global alias cache another served profile may refill; a literal key is returned as a path pointer and the receipt keeps only that pointer.
- **Surprising:** `_freeze_selected_provider` (:198-244) copies the picked entry's *private* subtree into the policy secret store and **blanks** an earlier equivalent spelling (`frozen_entries[i] = {}`, :237-238) rather than leaving it to win; a displaced unrelated entry is appended at a new index with a `moved` mapping so its own secrets follow it (:234-236). `prepare_model` re-fingerprints the launch key and drops it entirely (`credential_ref=None`) unless `bound_route_holds` still says it authenticates the route the session runs now (:140-143) — a `custom → custom` pick on another `base_url` must not carry the old key.

### `gateway/session_mutation_policy.py` — The policy-only session edits: `prepare_reasoning` (:22) pins/unpins the effort (a bypass launch's reset falls back to the built-in default, not the profile), `prepare_workspace` (:37) moves execution cwd and `TERMINAL_CWD` together after an off-loop probe of up to three `git` spawns, and `prepare_edit_policy` (:58) is ACP-only and writes nothing when the policy is unchanged — all three re-bound like a model switch (:13-19).

### `gateway/session_mutations.py`
- **Purpose:** Action-specific authority mutations, shared by the WebSocket and authenticated HTTP paths — the single place a `session.mutate` is authorized, prepared, committed and projected.
- **Key abstractions:** `_METADATA` / `_POLICY` / `_OWNER_AUTHORIZED` / `_FIELDS` (:18-23), `_authorize_import` (:26), `_authorize_control` (:40), `_owner_ref` (:70), `mutate_session` (:101), `_owned_mutation` (:110), `_mutate_session` (:142), `_runtime_owns_authority` (:181), `write_mutation` (:195), `_project_result` (:277), `_project_committed` (:321), `retire_live_sessions` (:366).
- **Data flow:** `mutate_session` → `track_mutation` + `asyncio.shield` (:106-107), so a request socket that disappears mid-`to_thread` cannot cancel the owner task (the task keeps the session gate until commit *and* publication) → `_owned_mutation` selects the lock → `_mutate_session` validates the param shape, redacts the editor MCP servers into a fingerprint, authorizes → `write_mutation` (fenced prepare → receipt commit in `asyncio.to_thread` → `_project_result`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `compress` takes `live.claim_gate` and **not** `mutation_lock` (:120-125) because its summarization is an LLM call: a submit or a metadata edit must not park behind it, and the commit is revision/generation-fenced against whatever lands meanwhile. A `model` pick on a running session that raises `session_busy` is *deferred*, not refused (:130-136) — the pick is for the next turn and the running turn keeps its route. `_owner_ref` (:70-98) resolves metadata ops and `delete` to the conversation's *lineage owner* but keeps the listed id for a transcript/policy-changing op, because that op's busy gate, generation fence and eviction are keyed by the session it writes, and authorizing it on the owner would bypass the owner's FIFO.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_runtime_owns_authority` (:181-192) exists because raw `db_path` equality only ever holds for the launch home — the routing index is pinned to the launch home for every profile — so a multiplexed secondary's delete was refused with `runtime_coordination_required` (#138826). `write_mutation` re-runs the runtime projections on an exact retry (the first attempt may have committed and then failed before publishing them) but never re-fires the one-shot event or the post-compaction hook (:261-268). `_authorize_control` (:40-67) allows a delete of a superseded (read-only) conversation without the control capability, because a delete retires only its own lineage.
- **Surprising:** `retire_live_sessions` (:366-422) fences the **shared** routing index in the launch home's DB as well whenever it differs from `authority.db.db_path` (:384-395) — without that fence, a whole-index snapshot taken before the delete and saved after it restored the route. The fence failure surfaces only *after* the rest of the cleanup, and only `require_fence=True` (canonical delete) turns it into `storage_unavailable` (:421-422). `_project_committed` publishes the edit policy **without** evicting the agent (:323-326): "the edit policy is read per edit, never by the built agent: it keeps its agent (and prompt cache)".

### `gateway/session_operator.py` — Private provenance for already-authorized operator input: `check_local_input` (:23) accepts a `local_operator_v1` payload only when its `profile_id`/`session_id`/`principal_id` triple matches the authority and the row exactly, and otherwise requires the row's principal to be the session's own source user — public submission parameters can never supply that envelope (:26-35).

### `gateway/session_pending_controls.py` — `PendingControls` (`:12`) projects the native approval/clarify waiters into generation-bound gateway prompts; `respond` (`:66`) resolves by *exact* `prompt_id`, so an attached responder can never consume the next FIFO approval, and a prompt whose generation is stale or already settled is reaped and published as `settled` (`:53-64`).

### `gateway/session_persistence.py`
- **Purpose:** `SessionStore` storage plumbing — resolving the right `SessionDB` per profile and load/save paths for the routing index (state.db `gateway_routing` primary, `sessions.json` legacy mirror).
- **Key abstractions:** `SessionPersistenceMixin` (`:40`), `_db` property/setter (`:119`, `:128`), `_routing_db` (`:132`), `_db_for_key()` (`:187`), `_db_for_session_id()` (`:241`), `_ensure_loaded_locked()` (`:344`), `_import_legacy_sessions_json()` (`:376`), `_prune_stale_sessions_locked()` (`:416`), `_stale_entry_verdict()` (`:448`), `_next_routing_generation_locked()` (`:515`), `_persist_routing_data()` (`:556`), `_save_entry()` (`:608`), `record_chat_labels()`/`chat_labels()` (`:288`, `:303`).
- **Data flow:** `_entries` is one flat in-memory dict holding *every* profile's routing keys, so persistence must not follow the ambient scope. `_routing_db` (`:132`) always resolves to `_routing_home/state.db` regardless of which profile is scoped — reading it through `_db` made a whole-index rewrite copy every other profile's routing rows into the active profile's store (issue #66887, `:137-145`). `_db_for_key` (`:187`) resolves the owning profile from the key namespace and returns `None` (fail closed, `:213-220`) rather than splitting one session identity across two physical stores. Saves flow `_snapshot_routing_locked` (`:551`) → `_persist_routing_data` (`:556`) under a single `_save_lock`; the per-turn fast path is `_save_entry` (`:608`), a single-row UPSERT with a full-rewrite fallback.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** One monotonic routing-generation counter is shared by BOTH full snapshots and single-row upserts (`:515-520`) — the stale-write protection is a total order over serialization times, so a delayed full rewrite must not regress newer fast upserts (`:563-568`, `:588-592`). `_stale_entry_verdict` (`:448`) returns three things, not two: a same-id recovery keeps the ORIGINAL entry object because the rebuilt one would drop counters, `model_override`, resume markers and metadata (`:492-499`). `chat_labels` RAISES `LookupError` when no DB handle exists (`:305-308`) — "unreadable now" is deliberately not "never labelled".
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_DB_UNPINNED` (`:23`) distinguishes "resolve from the active scope" from a deliberate `store._db = None` (the JSONL fallback) — without it, assigning `None` would be indistinguishable from unpinned. `_open_session_db_for_active_scope` (`:90`) uses a bounded backoff cache whose failures are *not* cached when the error is the test-isolation "live-system guard" (`:35-37`, `non_cacheable=`). `_db_for_key` returns `None` when a named profile has no resolvable home (`:213-220`). A failed legacy-mirror write after a successful state.db commit is logged, not raised (`:585-591`) — state.db is authoritative. `fence_retired_routes` (`:53`) raises when the routing store cannot take the fence, so a delete never reports success un-fenced. `strip_retired_mirror_routes` (`:64`) never creates the file it edits.
- **Surprising:** `_routing_scope()` (`:271`) is the resolved `sessions_dir` path used as the routing-table *namespace* — stores with different dirs never share entries, so a multi-home install is isolated by directory, not by a config flag. `close_all_db_handles` (`:248`) closes every per-path handle, not just `store._db` — closing only the pinned one strands secondary profiles' WAL locks and produces "database is locked" on restart.

### `gateway/session_policy.py`
- **Purpose:** The frozen, immutable "launch policy" for a local-client session — the creation-time snapshot of model/route/toolsets/credentials that every later turn resolves against.
- **Key abstractions:** `LocalSessionPolicy` (`:121`, frozen dataclass), `build_policy()` (`:408`), `effective()` (`:170`), `restore_policy()` (`:952`), `policy_scope()` (`:1011`), `bind_launch_key()` (`:785`), `route_launch_key()` (`:942`), `session_pinned()` (`:35`), `pin_reasoning_effort()` (`:41`), `unpin_reasoning()` (`:57`), `present_sections()` (`:398`), `entry_serves()` (`:366`), `bound_route_holds()` (`:353`), `model_route()` (`:346`), `_apply_launch_overrides()` (`:548`), `_resolve_toolsets()` (`:620`), `_extract_config_secrets()` (`:687`).
- **Data flow:** `build_policy(params, config)` validates the create request against the `CREATE_FIELDS` whitelist (`:15`, refusal at `:419-421`), strips `api_key`/`client_env` out of `request_json` because `launch_request` is echoed to every viewer (`:461-462`), extracts secret config values into `private_secrets` leaving `None` in the frozen tree (`:687-706`), and returns a `LocalSessionPolicy` whose `config_json`/`request_json`/`terminal_json` are JSON strings.
