Each turn calls `effective(authority)` (`:170`), which for a *following* session does ONE live profile read (`:182`, cached in `_TURN_PROFILE` at `:240-243`) and overlays it onto the frozen config, skipping any `FOLLOW_SECTIONS` (`:22`) the session explicitly pinned (`:203-207`). `current_toolsets`/`current_reasoning_config`/`current_service_tier` (`:245`, `:255`, `:269`) are the per-turn resolution entry points; `policy_scope` (`:1011`) installs the cwd, terminal scope, editor/auxiliary/client-env scopes and the turn's single profile read.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A frozen *whole-policy* hash is not the credential guard — a secret is bound to the *endpoint it authenticates*, so a secret only becomes invalid if the route moved (`entry_serves`, `:366`: "a pick held on the old one ends rather than carry the new key there"). A provider pick that is not the profile's own route causes every route-bound key to be DROPPED before the override folds in (`:560-568`, `ROUTE_BOUND_KEYS` in `session_route_identity.py:18`) — including `context_length`, because the replaced model's window is wrong for the new model. `provider: auto` is a sentinel meaning "no pick" (`:508-513`), and a configured entry whose bare key shadows a canonical built-in is re-spelled `custom:<key>` before resolution (`:529-546`) so the wrong provider is not frozen.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_refuse()` (`:106`) raises `RuntimeStoreError('invalid_params', field=…, detail=…)` so ~20 different create checks are distinguishable from one error class. `_extract_config_secrets` raises `launch_credentials_unavailable` when no private store was supplied (`:700`) rather than leaving a secret in the frozen tree. `bind_launch_key` compares with `hmac.compare_digest` and raises `admission_conflict` on a key mismatch (`:816-818`); the durable ref is `<instance>:<epoch>:<sid>#<fingerprint>` (`:840`) so a restart can verify a re-supplied key without ever persisting it. `restore_policy` (`:952`) re-validates everything and raises `storage_unavailable` on any malformed field (`:979-981`) rather than rebuilding from current defaults. `policy_scope` raises `workspace_unavailable` for a deleted cwd (`:1015-1016`) but keeps the chat readable. `follows_profile` is a `cached_property` (`:229`) because the config is re-parsed ~8-10× per turn otherwise; `replace()` builds a fresh instance with its own cache.
- **Surprising:** `LocalSessionPolicy` is `@dataclass(frozen=True)` yet carries a `cached_property` — `cached_property` writes to `__dict__` directly, bypassing the frozen `__setattr__`. `SURFACES` maps `tool`/`oneshot` to the `cli` *agent surface* but `_KEEP_STORED_LABEL` (`:81`) keeps their own stored label, and `created_surface()` (`:87`) treats `local`/`unknown` as non-surfaces. `bind_turn_workspace` (`:1053`) re-registers the session cwd as the terminal's per-task override only for a Docker backend that mounts the host cwd (`:1067-1072`) — for ssh/modal the session's own cwd would wrongly win.

### `gateway/session_policy_credentials.py`
- **Purpose:** Recovering a session's *frozen* config credentials (the keys it launched with) from the live profile config, without a vault.
- **Key abstractions:** `policy_identity()` (`:60`), `_legacy_identities()` (`:91`), `_binding()` (`:176`), `config_reference()` (`:195`), `_check_scope()` (`:211`), `recover_config_secrets()` (`:244`), `secrets_moved()` (`:234`), `disable_dropped_secrets()` (`:344`), `_route_paths()` (`:30`), `_route_owner()` (`:115`), `_current_custom_path()` (`:304`), `RecoveredSecrets` (`:53`), `PREFIX = 'profile-config-v1:'` (`:13`).
- **Data flow:** At creation the private values are stripped from the frozen config into a private map; `config_reference` (`:195`) writes a durable reference containing a whole-policy hash (`policy`), a per-secret **endpoint binding** (`bound`) and a per-secret fingerprint (`entries`). On each turn `recover_config_secrets` (`:244`) re-reads the live `config.yaml`, recomputes each secret's fingerprint, and compares it to the frozen one; a match returns the live value, a mismatch on a *route* path raises `launch_credentials_unavailable` (`:288-292`), and a mismatch elsewhere is recorded as *dropped* (`:295-300`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The credential guard is per-ENDPOINT, not per-policy (`:176-193`, `CFG-10`): a change to the model name, reasoning level, toolsets or an unrelated provider does NOT invalidate a key, but moving the endpoint does. `_check_scope` (`:211`) refuses a reference from another `home` or `profile` by name. `_legacy_identities` (`:91`) enumerates every *subset* of later-added policy fields at their defaults so a reference stamped by an older gateway still matches — with the commit hashes of each field recorded inline for audit (`:85-89`). `disable_dropped_secrets` (`:344`) exists because a `None` key means "resolve another credential": leaving it would silently send a *different account's* key, so a dropped fallback is removed from the chain and a dropped `auxiliary.<task>` route falls back to `provider: main` — the session's own credential, never a substitute.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A missing/empty `ref` prefix raises `launch_credentials_unavailable` (`:251-254`). An unreadable reference (any `KeyError/TypeError/ValueError/OSError`) is re-raised as `launch_credentials_unavailable` with the exception *type* in the detail, never the value (`:298-303`). `_current_custom_path` (`:304`) re-points a frozen `custom_providers[i]` index at the profile's entry of the same canonical identity wherever a reorder moved it; a vanished entry maps *past the end* so it reads as absent rather than as whatever entry now occupies index `i` (`:330-331`). A followed session skips its `FOLLOW_SECTIONS` (`:246-249`) because the live profile supplies them, so a rotation there is not a revoked launch credential.
- **Surprising:** `_route_owner` (`:115`) recognizes more route shapes than `model:` — `model.aliases.<n>`, `model_aliases.<n>`, both dictionary and list forms of `fallback_model`/`fallback_providers`, and `auxiliary.<task>` with its `fallback_chain` — and a secret nested deeper in a route (headers, extra_body) rides with that route. The `api_key_from` alias case is special-cased at `:117-121`: the alias's key is projected into `model.api_key` at runtime, so it authenticates the *session's* route, not the alias entry it is stored under.

### `gateway/session_policy_refresh.py`
- **Purpose:** On `session.resume`, re-derive a local session's config-derived settings (toolsets, MCP servers, terminal backend, `max_turns`, checkpoints, reasoning default) from the profile's current config and commit them as a new policy generation.
- **Key abstractions:** `refreshed_policy()` (`:28`), `_settings()` (`:53`), `_terminal()` (`:82`), `_mcp_servers()` (`:92`), `_prepare()` (`:137`), `_rebind()` (`:166`), `commit_refresh()` (`:205`), `refresh_settled()` (`:195`), `refresh_resumed_policy()` (`:245`), `drop_session_terminal()` (`:263`), `_publish()` (`:284`).
- **Data flow:** `commit_refresh` (`:205`) awaits any in-flight refresh for the session, then runs `_prepare` **off the owner loop** (`asyncio.to_thread`, `:221`) which loads the stored receipt, the live config, the terminal scope and the committed picks, and returns `None` when nothing moved (`:164`, `:232`). `_commit_refresh` (`:219`) then rebinds the private values (`_rebind`, `:166`) and commits via a compare-and-swap against the receipt **as stored** (JSON lists, not the restored tuple form — `:237-241`) with the publication as a shielded follow-up (`:243`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The *creation* flags in `request_json` always win over the live config (`:56-58`, `:63-64`, `:70-71`) — only fields the launch did not pin are followed. The MCP pick is treated as an **allowlist**, not a set to resolve: a server that the config has since disabled or removed *leaves the pick* (`:110-115`) rather than the session falling through to every enabled server. A picked server whose config was removed keeps a `{'enabled': False}` stub in the stored section (`:123-127`) so a later refresh still knows the name as a pick member rather than an unknown toolset. `refresh_resumed_policy` (`:245`) NEVER fails the resume — a busy session, a revoked credential or an unreadable profile keeps the stored policy, and only a *busy* outcome is logged at INFO (`:257-260`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_prepare` returns `None` for a bypass launch or a non-CLI source (`:148-150`) — cron, Kanban and room routes are not resumed CLI sessions. A stored-policy-behind-live-policy pair is refused by `restore_local_session`, which is why the commit's publication must be shielded (`:237-241`) and why `refresh_settled` (`:195`) exists: the commit lands before its terminal cleanup (a sandbox teardown can take 10-15 s) and before its publication. `_publish` (`:284`) drops the session's cached terminal *before* the drain can run a turn on the new binding with the old environment (`:286-287`), because the terminal tool reuses one by key and its recorded cwd wins over the new binding.
- **Surprising:** `refreshed_policy` (`:28`) computes `old, config = json.loads(policy.config_json), json.loads(policy.config_json)` — the same source parsed twice, because `old` must be the pre-mutation snapshot while `config` is the mutable copy. A rotated private value alone (whose *redacted* tree is unchanged) still moves the policy (`:47-50`, `secrets_moved`), so a key rotation is visible as a policy generation bump.

### `gateway/session_prompt_pin.py` — `SessionPromptPinMixin` (`:46`) stores the durable snapshot of the system-prompt inputs internal turns reuse (kanban wakes, delegation completions, startup resume) so a gateway restart does not flip the prompt; `set_prompt_pin` (`:49`) fences on `expected_session_id` so a concurrent reset/resume makes the write a no-op instead of stamping the old conversation's bytes onto the new one.

### `gateway/session_prune.py` — `prune` (`:24`) is the owner's own `session.prune` write: the CLI only ever computes a preview, because a CLI writing the store under a live gateway would be a second writer; it is refused unless the actor is the native local operator with `session:operator` (`:29-34`).

### `gateway/session_queued_controls.py`
- **Purpose:** `prompt.steer_queued` / `prompt.replace_queued` — steering or editing a turn that is still queued, consumed inside one owner write transaction.
- **Key abstractions:** `steer_queued()` (`:136`), `replace_queued()` (`:209`), `queued_controls()` (`:52`), `pending_rows()` (`:64`), `steer_transfers()` (`:82`), `_deliver()` (`:122`), `_receiving_agent()` (`:112`), `publish_transfers()` (`:192`), `_TEXT_ONLY` (`:24`), `_INTERACTIVE_SOURCES` (`:30`).
- **Data flow:** A steer is a TRANSFER, not an outcome: the queued row goes `queued -> terminal steering` bound to the running claim's generation, and the text is handed to the claim's agent. The fence is `authority.steer_transfers` (a `{session_id: (generation, {admission_id: delivered})}` map) changed **only** under the session's `event_stream.lock` (`:105-108`, `:170-172`) — the same lock the drain clears `executing` under. The durable write (`steer_session_input`) runs with **no lock held** (`:168-170`); the registration made before it is the claim's fence, so a definite refusal unregisters and re-raises while an ambiguous failure leaves the fence for the claim's settlement to decide (`:174-179`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** A steer is delivered by **ticket** (the admission id), never by its text (`:126-131`): the agent may have buffered the text without consuming it, so `withdraw_steer(admission_id)` decides delivery. If the claim ended first, the text reached nobody, so the admission is queued *again* in its own FIFO place (`revert_steer_input`, `:181-187`) and the caller gets `steer_unavailable` — the row is never silently dropped. There is no reorder verb: one FIFO per session (`:6-7`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `queued_controls` (`:52`) returns `[]` unless the payload is text-only (`:24`) — attachments, finite runs, classic-CLI turns, native envelopes and automation rows are whole turns that neither verb may truncate. Non-interactive sources (cron, kanban, a2a, bot_room — `:30`) are refused *by name* because they read a saved result for every outcome they do not name, so a transfer or replacement would strand them. `pending_rows` (`:64`) also lists rows in `steering` state as `started`, because a client must not retire an admission it can no longer see. A steer that arrives after the row left the queue answers `already_resolved` (`:147-150`); a steer with no live agent yet raises `steer_unavailable` *before* touching the row (`:163-167`).
- **Surprising:** `replace_queued` (`:209`) does not cancel-then-admit — it binds the replacement to the admission it retired via `replacement_binding`, and the binding is enforced *inside the new admission's own transaction* (`:216-221`), so an exact retry answers the same new admission while any other old admission under that `submission_id` is `admission_conflict`. The `finally` block (`:225-231`) re-reads the old row to decide whether the obligation was really owed, so a refused write discards the cancel obligation instead of settling a phantom cancellation.

### `gateway/session_reactions.py` — `react` (`:35`) writes a Desktop tapback onto the conversation's current *physical* session via `physical_target` (`:45-47`), because the sidecar handler looked the session up in its own table, which never holds an authority session, so every reaction used to roll back as "Could not react".

### `gateway/session_readiness.py` — `readiness` (`:11`) is a profile-bound setup probe that never raises: any probe exception is caught and reported as `{'ok': False, 'error': str(exc)}` (`:40-43`), and a profile that does not resolve is `profile_mismatch` rather than an exception (`:22-26`).

### `gateway/session_recovery.py`
- **Purpose:** `SessionStore` durable-row recovery — session-key generation, legacy Slack key migration, rebuilding routing entries from state.db, and the SQLite side of routing transitions.
- **Key abstractions:** `SessionRecoveryMixin` (`:33`), `resolve_session_id_for_key()` (`:191`), `_recover_session_from_db()` (`:232`), `_query_recoverable_row()` (`:261`), `_create_entry_from_recovered_row()` (`:135`), `_recovered_row_allowed_for_active_profile()` (`:68`), `_recovered_row_matches_source_scope()` (`:119`), `_legacy_slack_session_key()` (`:100`), `_claim_legacy_slack_key()` (`:107`), `_adopt_legacy_slack_entry()` (`:354`), `_promote_session_reset()` (`:291`), `_record_gateway_session_peer()` (`:321`), `_finish_route_transition()` (`:384`), `_session_create_kwargs()` (`:414`).
- **Data flow:** A missing routing entry is rebuilt from a durable row: `_query_recoverable_row` (`:261`) finds the peer row, gates it on scope and profile, and materialises a `SessionEntry`; `_recover_session_from_db` (`:232`) then *reopens* the row and, for a migrated legacy Slack key, rewrites the peer row to the scoped key. Routing transitions go through `_finish_route_transition` (`:384`), which promotes the predecessor row to an explicit reset boundary and then INSERTs the new row plus its routing peer.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The legacy Slack key fallback is **Slack-only and scope-explicit** (`:100-105`): an unscoped Slack session may be claimed by only one workspace, and a scope-less DM is claimed once by the first workspace while a scope-less channel/group is REFUSED (`:365-371`) because channel ids collide across workspaces. `_adopt_legacy_slack_entry` MOVES the entry rather than copying it (`:368`), so a second workspace with identical Slack ids cannot attach to the same transcript. `_promote_session_reset` (`:291`) *promotes* rather than plain-ends, because a row already ended with a recoverable accidental reason (`agent_close`/`ws_orphan_reap`) must be upgraded to an explicit boundary or stale-route recovery resurrects it over the reset. `_recovered_row_matches_source_scope` (`:119`) REJECTS rows with an unparseable origin — an unattributable transcript is exactly the ambiguity.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** An invalid durable timestamp must look OLD, never freshly active (`:145-147` → `datetime.fromtimestamp(0)`). `resolve_session_id_for_key` (`:191`) returns `None` (the caller must preserve the flush file) rather than minting a session, and it compares `started_at` (REAL) against `not_after` (int seconds) with `math.floor` (`:224-227`) so a row minted in the same second as the flush is still a valid origin. `_db_for_key` failing closed returns `None` even when the routing map knows the id (`:205-207`). `_peer_row` (`:175`) swallows lookup errors to `None` unless `raise_on_lookup_error` — an indeterminate lookup must keep the only routing handle rather than prune it. `_record_gateway_session_peer` (`:321`) falls back to the older `SessionDB` signature on `TypeError` (`:344-350`).
- **Surprising:** `_recovered_row_allowed_for_active_profile` (`:68`) uses the *requested key's* profile namespace under multiplexing and the *active* profile only in single-profile mode — because in a multiplexed gateway several profiles serve traffic at once, the active profile is meaningless. Rows with no key namespace stay adoptable in both modes (legacy/keyless data).

### `gateway/session_refine.py` — `refine_local_session` (`:25`) runs `/refine` for a local session whose cached agent is gone: the review runs over the *stored transcript* on a side agent built from the session's frozen policy, publishes as `review.summary` (`:56-59`), and the side agent gets `skip_memory=True` and `_end_session_on_close = False` (`:76-84`) so closing it does not tear down the session's own terminal/browser/memory providers.

### `gateway/session_results.py` — `finish_result` (`:66`) commits the execution outcome and refuses to let a delivery failure rewrite it (a terminal row is read back instead, `:74-83`); `close_discarded_turn` (`:116`) ends a discarded `unknown` turn with a Hermes-authored `failed_turn` boundary so consecutive-user repair does not re-send the discarded turn as context, and replaces its own boundary when the tail is this turn's (`:141-148`).

### `gateway/session_retry_admission.py` — `RetryRewrite` (`:30`) is a *planned* transcript rewrite that the `/retry` handler only binds; `take` (`:59`) hands it to the re-send's own admission and only to that source object on that store, and `compose` (`:70`) runs the admission gate and the rewrite inside one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` so a refusal rolls back both.

### `gateway/session_route_identity.py` — `resolved_route` (`:63`) produces the single resolved-route identity (`ollama`/`vllm` → `custom`, `deep-infra` → `deepinfra`, a configured entry → its slug) that every launch-route gate compares, and `ROUTE_BOUND_KEYS` (`:18`) is the exact list a route change drops — including `context_length` and every credential pointer.

### `gateway/session_route_recovery.py` — `restore_routed_session` (`:110`) mounts a cold messaging-platform session into the authority for a local viewer, reviving the source through `_restored_source` so delivery stays pinned to the persisted receiving bot; a row the lane moved past mounts READ-ONLY (`superseded`) and refuses writes by name, and a compression continuation is never mounted under its physical id.

### `gateway/session_runtime_workers.py` — Physical worker liveness and cooperative Stop across every served authority: `tracked_write` (`:77`) runs each synchronous ledger write in a thread (`asyncio.to_thread`) while keeping the loop free, and `join_authority_work` (`:162`) re-issues `stop_authority_work` every 50 ms until physical workers, executing sessions, mutation tasks and side agents are all gone — raising `TimeoutError('Profile workers did not stop; ownership retained')` (`:170`) rather than letting the owner exit while a thread still holds a lease.

### `gateway/session_secret_prompt.py` — The masked skill-secret prompt for a local viewer's turn: it pins that a secret NEVER reaches the model — the value is written by `save_env_value_secure` (`:48`) and the reply text says so, and the callback is bound as a ContextVar (`:51`) so parallel tool workers inherit it.

### `gateway/session_server_requests.py` — The owner's server→client requests (`sudo`, `secret`, `clarify`) and the ONE rule for who may settle them: `may_settle` (`:50`) requires the authority's own live `event_stream` identity (`owned_by`, `:44`), the `session:respond` capability, and that the actor is in `live.subscribers` — because session ids repeat across profiles while the `_open` registry is process-wide (`:41`).

### `gateway/session_setting_commands.py` — The messaging setting handlers on a LOCAL session: it pins that a `/reasoning` command arriving mid-turn is NOT lost but deferred to the turn boundary (`live.pending_reasoning = (actor.subject, effort)`, `:121`, committed by `apply_pending_reasoning`, `:124`), and that a typed `/model` commits the ALIAS the user typed rather than the bare model id (`:160`).

### `gateway/session_settlement_recovery.py`
- **Purpose:** Fence an unsettled claim in a live owner, and commit+publish a finished turn without letting a storage hiccup manufacture a terminal answer.
- **Key abstractions:** `_mark_unknown` (`:11`) — the "unknown" fence; `recover_failed_settlement` (`:41`); `completion_payload` (`:53`) — the `message.complete` body; `owner_stop_notice` (`:119`); `gateway_refusal_boundary` (`:140`); `commit_and_publish` (`:159`); `settle_with_retry` (`:197`); `publish_terminal` (`:217`).
- **Data flow:** capture (`captured`) + row → `prepare_result` → `finish_result` (the SQLite write) → `settlement['settled']`/`['response']` → `completion_payload` → `live.event_stream.publish(session_id, complete)` → terminal media release → `authority._publish_pending(ref)`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the SQLite write happens OUTSIDE `live.event_stream.lock` (`:176`, `:183`), because attach/replay/receipts take that lock on the loop and a held writer would stall every session. Ordering is preserved instead by `live.settling[admission_id] = row` (`:177`): lock holders read the claim as still *started*, so nobody ever sees the terminal row without its completion frame. A second deliberate inversion: `after(result)` in `tracked_write` runs in the CALLER's step, but here the commit is what it is — `settle_with_retry` refuses to retry once `'settled' in settlement` (`:210`), i.e. a failure *after* the commit never retries, and a failure *before* it retries exactly.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** transient storage errors retry on a bounded ladder `(0.5, 2.0)` (`:194`, `:205`); the last attempt falls back to the unknown fence, and `recover_failed_settlement` loops forever on `OSError`/`sqlite3.Error` with a 1 s sleep, retaining the owner and the captured result (`:44-50`) rather than manufacturing a reply. `_mark_unknown` re-checks epoch AND generation and refuses unless the status is exactly `'started'` (`:17-22`). Media release happens in its own transaction after publication (`:150`, `:189`). Cleanup failures are logged, never propagated (`:103`, `:156`).
- **Surprising:** `owner_stop_notice` (`:119`) reads the cause from the interrupt message RECORDED when the turn was interrupted, never from the runner's flags at publication (`:128-129`) — a user Stop whose settlement was delayed until after shutdown began is still the user's Stop. And an unattended row (cron/automation/webhook) gets `AUTOMATION_INTERRUPTED_NOTICE` instead of "send your message again" advice (`:132-136`). `completion_payload` only sets `response_reused` when the agent's reuse site set it — never inferred from equal text (`:67`).

### `gateway/session_shell.py`
- **Purpose:** Run an Ink `!cmd` / `{!cmd}` for exactly one local session, in its frozen launch cwd, behind the same refusal gates as main.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (`:22`) → `shell.exec`; `shell_exec` (`:26`); `_run` (`:57`).
- **Data flow:** JSON-RPC `shell.exec` → param allowlist (`session_id`, `profile`, `command` only) → `authorize(..., 'session:control')` → `authority._require_admission_open()` → local-source check → terminal-backend check → hardline/dangerous-command detection → `asyncio.to_thread(_run, command, policy.cwd)` → redacted `{stdout, stderr, code}`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the command is refused outright when the session's frozen terminal policy names a non-local backend (`docker`, `ssh`, …) — the owner's host shell is not that backend, and the refusal NAMES the backend so the client can say where it would have run (`:48-50`). `shell=True` is kept so the user-facing `!cmd` grammar (pipes, redirects) survives (`:61`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a deleted checkout/worktree raises `cwd_unavailable` — it never silently falls back to the owner's cwd (`:63`); timeout 30 s → `command_timeout`; `OSError` → `command_failed`; stdin is `DEVNULL` and `creationflags=windows_hide_flags()` keeps a console window off the user's screen (`:69`). The child never inherits the long-lived owner's credentials: `build_subprocess_env(strip_launch_profile=True)` (`:65`).
- **Surprising:** redaction runs BEFORE tailing (`:75-76`), not after — so a credential straddling the 4000-char slice boundary cannot survive as fragments on either side of the cut. stdout is capped at 4000 chars, stderr at 2000.

### `gateway/session_skin.py` — The owning profile's resolved skin for a canonical client's readiness: it pins that a broken skin engine returns `{}` (`:31-33`), which the client treats as the default theme exactly as main's `resolve_skin` did — a skin failure must not stop `runtime.describe`.

### `gateway/session_stall.py` — Gateway session stall notification policy: it pins that progress has ONE source (`AIAgent.get_activity_summary()`) and that an unknown idle time is NOT recovery — `should_clear_session_stall_notification` returns False while `idle_seconds is None`, so an observation gap holds the latch (`:33-34`).

### `gateway/session_state.py`
- **Purpose:** One container for per-session gateway state, replacing ~19 `session_key`-keyed dicts on the runner that bred boundary drift and wholesale-reset races.
- **Key abstractions:** `TurnState` (`:17`), `ConversationState` (`:44`), `PersistentState` (`:65`), `SessionState` (`:93`); `_FieldSpec` (`:106`), `_spec` (`:114`); `SessionFieldView` (`:141`), `TurnLeaseTokenView` (`:190`); `LEGACY_FIELD_SPECS` (`:225`) — 17 legacy dict attributes; `legacy_dict_property` (`:259`), `legacy_lease_token_property` (`:268`).
- **Data flow:** the runner's `_sessions` dict maps a session key to a `SessionState`; each lifecycle scope owns a field set; the legacy `runner._X = {...}` dict attributes are implemented as `@property` views that are LIVE `MutableMapping`s over one field (`_legacy_property`, `:247`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** scopes are defined by WHERE each dict used to be CLEARED — `turn` at turn end, `conversation` at conversation boundaries, `persistent` having its own lifecycle (`:1-4`). The scope is not a namespace, it is a reset contract. `ConversationState.clear` is `self.__dict__.update(ConversationState().__dict__)` (`:62`) so a newly added field is cleared automatically rather than silently surviving. `_spec` (`:114`) encodes presence two different ways: a *type* default means presence is truthiness, a *sentinel* default means presence is `is not` — that is how `/fast` stores "priority" or `None` (explicit normal) where key PRESENCE, not truthiness, decides (`:12-14`, `:51`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `TurnState.clear` deliberately does NOT touch `lease_tokens` (`:19-20`, `:40`) — `_release_turn_lease` owns them, and release happens exactly once. Tokens are keyed by ACQUIRING run generation so a displaced turn's unwind frees only its own lease and never a successor's (`:31-33`). `TurnLeaseTokenView._held` (`:196`) requires a 2-tuple key and raises `KeyError` for a non-tuple — a malformed legacy key fails loudly rather than silently addressing session `"ab"`.
- **Surprising:** the whole legacy-dict layer is deliberate compatibility, not vestigial: `_RunnerView.__eq__` (`:135`) exists only because tests compare `runner._running_agents` to a plain dict, and `Mapping` has no `__eq__`. The property setter RESETS the field on every session before applying the entries (`:251-254`) — assigning a dict is a full replace, not a merge. `hygiene_failure_streak` (`:90`) is deliberately PROCESS-LOCAL (no disk flush) because `PersistentState` means "survives turn and boundary resets", NOT "survives a restart"; `run.py` mirrors it to the DB keyed by `session_key`, which is what buys correctness across compaction ROTATION where the sid changes but the chat does not.

### `gateway/session_sudo_prompt.py` — The masked sudo password prompt for a local viewer's turn: it pins that the terminal tool's sudo rewrite is answered by a `sudo` server→client request to the attached viewers (`:26`) instead of main's headless refusal ("a terminal is required"), and that the password travels only in the viewer's response frame.

### `gateway/session_surface.py` — Per-turn client surface (floating HUD, live voice) carried through canonical admission: it pins that a surface is a fact about ONE turn, never about the session (`:1-6`), and that the persisted user row stays the words the user typed — the note rides the current user message via the `api_content` sidecar, never the frozen system prefix (`:105-112`).

### `gateway/session_tool_events.py` — Viewer payload limits for tool events, with the full tool input/output left in canonical history: it pins a 64 KiB budget (`MAX_TOOL_EVENT_CHARS`, `:4`) and — the non-obvious part — that a truncated RESULT is re-wrapped as valid JSON (`{'output'|'error': …, 'truncated': True}`, `:30`) because ACP reads errors from the result as well as the event flag.

### `gateway/session_transcript.py`
- **Purpose:** `SessionStore` transcript I/O — SQLite append with a per-session retry queue, compression-reroute following, FTS corruption recovery, rewrite/rewind/load — a mixin bound onto `SessionStore` via the MRO.
- **Key abstractions:** `TranscriptReadError` (`:22`); `transcript_append_kwargs` (`:62`); `SessionTranscriptMixin` (`:96`) with `_append_to_transcript_serialized` (`:273`), `_enqueue_transcript_message` (`:179`), `_divert_transcript_after_db_replaced` (`:202`), `_live_compression_child` (`:228`), `_migrate_transcript_queue_to_child` (`:241`), `_spool_stalled_backlog` (`:380`), `_drain_spooled_drops` (`:401`), `_is_fts_corruption_error` (`:453`), `_rebuild_fts_once` (`:470`), `rewrite_transcript` (`:540`), `is_published_compression_child` (`:574`), `has_input_owner` (`:608`), `load_transcript` (`:631`), `rewind_session` (`:654`).
- **Data flow:** message dict → `transcript_append_kwargs` (field-by-field, never splatted) → per-session in-memory queue → SQLite `append_message` → on failure, the message is retried, spooled to disk, or diverted to a JSONL fallback; reads go `_follow_reroutes` (in-memory map) → durable `get_compression_tip` → `get_messages_as_conversation`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `transcript_append_kwargs` LISTS its fields instead of splatting the dict (`:63-69`) because a spooled message is arbitrary JSON from disk and one unexpected key would raise and abort the whole restart replay. `_bindable_text` (`:50`) coerces dicts/lists to JSON and rejects ints outside sqlite's signed 64-bit range — an OverflowError on a TEXT column would refuse the WHOLE row. `fallback_ts` applies only when `timestamp is None`, never on falsiness, because a truthiness test would rewrite epoch 0 (`:82`). Spooled backlog is drained BEFORE the live message (`:303`), because writing the live row first would give it a permanently lower row id than the older backlog. `rewrite_transcript` replaces only the active set and deliberately avoids an existence probe: a probe would fail OPEN into the destructive replace on any DB error and can race a concurrent `archive_and_compact` (`:554-564`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** cap 200 pending messages per session (`:100`) — the OLDEST is evicted and spooled, not dropped, and the spool failure is logged separately (`:187-199`). A replaced/quarantined handle diverts the whole backlog to the on-disk spool plus a JSONL fallback and the FTS rebuild is explicitly forbidden there (`:202-206`). A compression-ended session with no unique live child DROPS the head so it cannot poison later writes (`:337-345`). A bare `SQLITE_CORRUPT` does NOT authorize an FTS rebuild — only errors naming `messages_fts` or carrying FTS provenance do, because a generic "database disk image is malformed" can mean structural B-tree damage (#97940, `:453-468`). The rebuild runs at most once per 300 s (`:104`, `:482`) and is skipped while a foreign process holds the DB (WAL split-brain guard, `:489-496`). `load_transcript` FAILS CLOSED: a failed canonical read raises `TranscriptReadError` rather than returning `[]`, because empty history is valid data and a failed read is not (`:646-652`). `has_input_owner` queries existence only, never content (`:608-613`).
- **Surprising:** `is_published_compression_child` FAILS OPEN (returns False on any DB error) while `load_transcript` fails CLOSED — deliberately opposite defaults, each commented with the failure it avoids. `hygiene`-style escalation here is a log-level ladder: 3 consecutive append failures move WARNING→ERROR and spool the stalled backlog to disk so a crash during the outage no longer loses it (#114266, `:360-365`, `:380`). `_live_compression_child` uses the PARENT's proven owner handle because the child's id is unpublished until its write succeeds — a by-id lookup would hit the ambient store (`:228-232`).

### `gateway/session_tui_config.py`
- **Purpose:** Owner-side `config.set` for the TUI's profile preferences — writes the OWNING profile's `config.yaml` under main's per-key semantics, off the owner loop.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (`:25`); `_toggles` (`:38`) / `_words` (`:57`) — the key tables; `_set_display` (`:76`); `_set_focus` (`:117`); `_write_profile_config` (`:137`); `config_set` (`:173`); `current_tier` (`:221`), `_set_fast` (`:229`); `_set_personality` (`:267`), `_set_profile_leaf` (`:284`), `_set_approval_mode` (`:300`), `_set_command_setting` (`:312`); `_broadcast_skin` (`:203`).
- **Data flow:** `config.set` frame → key classification → profile-scope + capability check → raw YAML read → `edit(cfg)` mutates the dict in place → `atomic_config_replace` only if the dict actually changed → result extras (`value`, sometimes `tool_progress`) → for `skin`, a `skin.changed` event to every attached session of the profile.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the write is a RAW ROUND-TRIP — no defaults and no overlay are persisted, only what the user actually set (`:138-139`). The session-control keys (`busy`, `verbose`, `yolo`, `show_reasoning`, `service_tier`, `model`) deliberately do NOT go to config.yaml; they live in `gateway.session_busy_controls`, and `model` is refused there (`:17`). `_WRITE_LOCK` serializes one read-modify-write of config.yaml at a time (`:22`) because a lost update would be silent. `_set_focus` stashes the configured `tool_progress` mode and pins the focus view's mode, restoring the stash when focus is turned off (`:117-134`) — `/focus` is a mode switch, not a boolean.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** an unparseable config raises `storage_unavailable` and the user's file is NEVER overwritten (`:146-147`); a failed atomic replace also maps to `storage_unavailable` (`:153-154`). Unknown keys and bad words raise `invalid_params`. `details_mode` cascades to all four sections, while `details_mode.<section>` with an empty value CLEARS the override so the client falls back to its section default (`:96`, `:104-105`). `/fast` is validated against the session's route, never config.yaml, and `ultrafast` additionally requires `ultrafast_offered` for that model/provider/base_url (`:246-261`).
- **Surprising:** `_broadcast_skin` (`:203`) also reaches into `sys.modules['tui_gateway.server']` to call `_note_skin_broadcast()`, so the config watcher does not repaint the skin a second time — a deliberate de-duplication between two independent repaint paths. The `reasoning` key is handled by the SLASH COMMAND's own handler (`commit_reasoning_command`) so the picker, the keybind and the typed command cannot disagree (`:175`, `:346-347`); `yolo` with a `scope` is main's Shift+click zap and is GLOBAL-only (`:323-325`).

### `gateway/session_tui_ops.py`
- **Purpose:** Ink's session utilities on the shared owner — `/save`, `session.export`, `/reload`, `/agents pause|resume`, `/rollback` and `/diff session`.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers` (`:24`, ten verbs); `save_session` (`:58`), `export_session` (`:87`), `session_history` (`:121`), `reload_env` (`:136`), `pause_delegation` (`:169`), `rollback` (`:205`), `_session_diff` (`:285`), `_wire_bytes` (`:300`), `_fit_frame` (`:305`), `_rewind_last_turn` (`:320`).
- **Data flow:** verb frame → `_live_ref` (param allowlist + capability) → policy/target resolution (`physical_target`, `transcript_target`) → the expensive part in `asyncio.to_thread` → a result dict; for `restore`, a successful whole-tree restore also issues a revision-fenced `rewind` mutation and returns `history_removed`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `session.export` renders the transcript FOR THE CLIENT TO WRITE — nothing is written on the gateway host, so a remote Desktop and a local Ink both save to the user's own disk (`:87-90`), whereas `session.save` writes a file on the owner's profile disk (`:76-80`). The checkpoint manager is built from the session's FROZEN policy rather than read off the resident agent (`:231-239`) because it carries no per-session state these reads need, and a restarted owner or a just-rewound session has no resident agent at all. `rollback` restore without a `file_path` is refused while the turn is not idle (`session_busy`, `:226-228`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the session diff is bounded to 4 MiB on the wire — half the 8 MiB frame every client accepts (`:199-202`) — and the budget is counted in JSON-ESCAPED bytes, because a control character is one character but six bytes (`:200-201`, `_wire_bytes` `:300`). `_fit_frame` cuts only at `\n`: `splitlines` would also cut at `\r`, `\f` and U+2028 inside a diff line (`:311-312`). The client is told the uncut line count (`diff_lines`, `:297`) so it can name the cut rather than the frame dropping the socket. `rollback.restore` passes `safe=not file_path and not params.get('all')`, so main's whole-tree restore keeps the user's hand-edits unless `--all` says otherwise (`:276-278`).
- **Surprising:** the `/agents pause` gate is PROCESS-WIDE, not per-session (`:169-176`) — main's sidecar served one TUI so its gate covered every agent in that process; on the shared owner it covers every chat the gateway runs, which is what an operator's kill switch is for, and `delegation.status` reports the same flag to every client. `reload.env` has two completely different implementations: a single-profile owner re-reads the process `os.environ` (what its turns read), while a MULTIPLEXED owner must not touch it (it is shared by every served profile) and instead re-pulls the profile's external secret-source snapshot and returns the COUNT of changed keys (`:153-166`).

### `gateway/session_tui_reads.py` — Main's TUI text for the session reads its sidecar answered itself (`/status`, `/context`): it pins that a TUI-sourced session renders main's exact wording — including the hardcoded `Provider: auto` (`:87`), kept because main read the provider off a compute-host metadata mirror a local session never filled — and that the context breakdown is CACHED on a key of (agent identity, agent context marks, conversation revision) because the probe is two indexed reads while the rebuild walks the whole transcript and the system prompt (`:146-157`).

### `gateway/session_tui_side.py`
- **Purpose:** Implements the three detached "side agent" verbs — `/bg` (`prompt.background`), `/btw` (`prompt.btw`) and the Desktop `preview.restart` — each of which answers *later* on the parent session's event stream instead of becoming a turn.
- **Key abstractions:** `handlers(connection)` (:27) registers `prompt.background`/`prompt.btw`/`preview.restart`; `_admit()` (:32) is the shared admission gate returning `(live, policy, text, task_id)`; `_side_scope()` (:56) binds owner scope + frozen policy scope + session vars + turn workspace; `_side_agent()` (:86) builds a fresh `AIAgent` (`side_agent=True`) on the session's frozen route; `_spawn()` (:123) owns the daemon thread and the `authority._side_agents` ledger; `_SideQuestionStop` (:219) is `/btw`'s cancellation holder; verbs `background` (:171), `btw` (:192), `preview_restart` (:249).
- **Data flow:** params → `_admit` (authorizes `session:submit`, requires the session be in `connection.subscriptions`, resolves `policy_for_source`) → `_spawn` returns `{task_id}` immediately → a daemon thread runs `body()` inside `_side_scope` → the text is published on the session's `event_stream` as `background.complete` / `btw.complete` / `preview.restart.complete` with `{task_id, text}`; a raised exception becomes the literal reply string `error: <exc>` (:148) rather than an error event.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The side agent is deliberately **not an admission** — never in the parent FIFO, never a transcript row, never replayable (docstring :2-8); it is published out-of-band so a `/bg` cannot be re-run by a session restore. `preview_restart` deliberately **skips `AIAgent.close()`** (:280-281) because closing would kill the background dev server the task exists to leave running. `_side_scope` binds the profile by *its own name* only when `_is_routed_home(home)` is true (:62-67), so a served secondary keys its own persistent Docker workspace container instead of the launch profile's live container for the same folder. `_spawn` registers the thread in `authority._side_agents` so the restart wait, shutdown drain, SessionDB close gate and idle exit all count it as owner work (:126-133).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_admit` refuses unknown extra params, blank text, non-string optionals, unknown session (`not_found`), missing subscription and missing policy (`permission_denied`) before any work starts (:33-53). The thread body catches `Exception` and publishes `error: …`; the `finally` always pops the ledger entry and schedules `persist_work_count` on the owner loop via `loop.call_soon_threadsafe` (:153-157), tolerating an already-closed loop. `thread.start()` failure pops the ledger and re-raises (:162-165). `_SideQuestionStop.abandoned()` (:243) is true once stopped with no fork running, which makes the leftover publish nothing — so a retiring profile's reservation is not held by the uncancellable 180 s read-only aux fallback (:191-194).
- **Surprising:** `/btw` prefers the resident agent's in-memory `_session_messages` and only falls back to the stored transcript (:199-201) — a session whose agent was evicted answers from the DB, so the two paths can return different context. `/btw` injects the *parent's* `api_key` into the aux call's runtime (:210-212). `preview_restart` builds its agent with `session_db=None` and `skip_memory=True` (:283-284), so the preview agent writes nothing into the owner's session store.

### `gateway/session_unattended_unknown.py` — Settles `unknown` admissions that no human submitted (cron `CRON_PRINCIPAL_ID`, `automation:` principals, `local_automation_v1`/`kanban_card_v1` payloads, native `automation`/`webhook_delivery` blocks — `is_unattended` :23) as terminal `interrupted` through the *same* fenced `resolve_unknown_session_input` a person's Discard uses (:41-65, :67-83), because a paused `unknown` FIFO head stalls every message behind it (a Bot Chat sat ten hours behind one) and there is no one to ask whether to resend.

### `gateway/session_usage.py` — Projects the gateway-hosted agent's counters into the classic CLI's `session.usage` / `session.stats` shape; the load-bearing decision is `usage_frame()` (:140) returning **both** views in one frame — `{"usage": tui_usage(stats), **stats}` (:165) — because the classic CLI's `SessionState.apply_event` and ACP read the top-level counters while a Desktop bundle older than `usageFromTick` reads only `payload.usage`, so publishing one alone starves half the viewers (:148-160); `tui_usage` (:110) omits absent figures rather than fabricating zeros, and `carried_compression_count` (:86) keeps the compression tally alive across the agent eviction a canonical compress causes.

### `gateway/session_viewer_automation.py` — Exposes the classic CLI's `/goal`, `/loop`, `/heartbeat` managers as one `session.automation` verb whose calls run *in the gateway* against the session's physical row, because the gateway is the single writer and must own every model call; the subtle part is that a "read" can still write (`is_waiting` / `goal_blocks_tick` clear a satisfied goal barrier), so the code snapshots the three `state_meta` rows before and after and republishes only if they changed (:164-168), and `viewer_driven` (:36) is the flag that stops the gateway's own post-turn hooks from judging a turn the viewer already judged.

### `gateway/session_viewer_close.py`
- **Purpose:** Runs end-of-conversation on the *owner* when the last viewer detaches, because the agent, its memory manager and the Relay scope now live in the gateway and the classic CLI's in-process `end_session` / `on_session_end` / `on_session_finalize` no longer have a home in the client.
- **Key abstractions:** `CLOSE_PREFIX` (:28, aliased from `VIEWER_CLOSE_PREFIX` so route retirement drops it too); `_BUSY_SQL` (:30) — a session is busy if it has a queued/started/unknown admission *or* a registered/running/unknown worker execution; `_PendingClose` (:52) carries a unique operation id plus the reason, so *identity* (not the reason) tells a settler its close is still the pending one; `close_viewer_session` (:70), `_persist_close` (:96), `forget_persisted_close` (:106), `cancel_persisted_close` (:123), `rearm_persisted_closes` (:135), `settle_viewer_close` (:155), `_resolve_abandoned_unknown` (:191), `_still_pending` (:221), `_requeue_if_busy` (:225), `_run_close_lifecycle` (:239).
- **Data flow:** `session.detach {end_reason}` → refuse + interrupt the session's own async delegations (:80-85) → write a `state_meta` record `CLOSE_PREFIX+session_id` under the owner epoch (:96-104) → settle at the FIFO's next idle point: resolve any `unknown` turn as Discard would (:191-219), re-check busy (:169), stamp the row via `end_idle_viewer_session` inside `tracked_write` (:176-180), then run the memory commit + `on_session_finalize` hooks in the owning profile's scope (:239-260).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The pending close is persisted in `state_meta` so a gateway stop/restart *before* the FIFO goes idle does not silently drop it; boot recovery re-arms it (:135-153) and the restarted owner's first idle point ends it (:129-132). Every delete is a **compare-and-delete on the exact record** (`DELETE … WHERE key=? AND value=?`, :112-114) under the owner-epoch fence, so a cancelled older close, a reattach or a stale owner can only remove the record it saw, never a newer close stored since (:33-35, :106-120). The close is *claimed* (`del authority.pending_closes[session_id]`, :176) with no await between the last check and the claim, so exactly one settler stamps the row (:174-177).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A viewer attaching mid-flight cancels the close (`cancel_persisted_close` :123-133 drops the stored record unless it is the one pending in memory). A refused stamp triggers `_requeue_if_busy` (:225-237): if input was admitted after the idle check the close goes back to the drain's next idle point; any other refusal (row already ended) leaves nothing to finish. `forget_persisted_close` swallows `RuntimeStoreError` and returns False, leaving the record in place rather than deleting it under a foreign epoch (:118-120). A session with an `unknown` turn is resolved *as the owner* with the viewer's close standing in for the person's Discard (:205-213); a refused resolution leaves the FIFO paused.
- **Surprising:** `_RESET_REASONS = {'new_session', 'tui_close'}` (:49) is the only trigger for the gateway's own `/new` reset arguments, and the reset runs with `new_session_id=None` because the viewer opens its next session only *after* the detach (:257-260). A close that produced finalize messages logs "dropped: no viewer" rather than sending them (:253-255).

### `gateway/session_worker.py`
- **Purpose:** The `worker.register` / `worker.adopt` / `worker.persist` verbs on the ordinary authenticated authority, restricted to *explicit local compute registrations* — a delegated child is never registered here, only reserved by the owner on its parent's receipt stream.
- **Key abstractions:** `_SCOPE` (:16) = `{profile_id, session_id, execution_id, generation, pid, birth, secret}`; `_claim` (:19) reuses the durable adoption digest as the producer claim; `_verify` (:48); `worker_request` (:65).
- **Data flow:** the caller's `{pid, birth, secret}` is proven live by `psutil` (same birth time, running, not a zombie, same OS username, not the gateway's own pid — :31-40), folded into an `admission_fingerprint` over the durable worker origin (:41-46), and the digest is then checked with `hmac.compare_digest` against the stored `worker_executions.adoption_digest` (:48-63).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `register` demands the `worker:adopt` capability *and* `kind == 'compute'` — an interactive client must not park a `registered` row nothing reaps, because the FIFO would pause until restart (:80-89). `require_serving(connection.authority)` runs first (:67) because adopt/persist would write with a released epoch. `execution.finish` explicitly wakes the session's FIFO (:102-105) since input parked behind the worker would otherwise never restart.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `pid == os.getpid()` is rejected outright (:30). A pid that died between registration and use is `worker_not_live` (:41). Every write goes through `asyncio.to_thread` so the SQLite write never blocks the event loop (:88, :95, :98).
- **Surprising:** the `delegation.*` and `child.reserve` operations are *routed out* of the normal path before the `set(params) != _SCOPE | extra` check (:70-76), so those two carry an extra `payload` key and a different verification contract.

### `gateway/session_worker_child.py`
- **Purpose:** Lets a *verified* worker execution ask the owner to reserve a delegated child execution, on the parent's own fenced receipt stream, without the worker ever minting a child itself.
- **Key abstractions:** `_DEFAULTS = {'max_concurrent_children': 10, 'max_spawn_depth': 1}` (:16); `delegation_limits` (:26); `reserve_child_execution` (:42); `worker_child_request` (:72).
- **Data flow:** the parent sends `worker.persist {operation: 'child.reserve', payload: {'secret'}}` → the parent's full claim and assignment are verified first (:76-81) → inside the receipt transaction the owner creates the child's session row + `worker_executions` row with the *worker's fresh secret* and an `admission_fingerprint` claim (:64-70) → the child then persists through the ordinary `worker.persist` path under its own claim.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The caps are read from the **root conversation's** frozen policy, never from the asking worker (:26-35): the root execution id is recovered by splitting on `CHILD_SEPARATOR` and the root's `session_id` looked up in `worker_executions` (:28-32). Refusals are **receipts, not errors** (`{'refused': 'delegation_depth_exceeded' | 'delegation_capacity'}`, :56-62) — an error would leave the parent's journal pending and refuse every later write of its turn (docstring :8-10). `reserve_child_execution` runs inside `apply_worker_receipt` so it shares the parent's receipt sequence (:88-94).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_limit` clamps a malformed config value to ≥1 and falls back to the default (:19-24). The live-child count is a SQL range scan over the execution-id prefix, counting only non-terminal descendants whose own id has no `/` (:49-51). The payload must be exactly `{'secret'}` with a 16–256 char string (:45-47); the child id is `prefix + secrets.token_hex(8)` (:63).
- **Surprising:** a child gets a **new session row** (`kind='subagent'`, `parent_session_id` set, `model_config={'_delegate_from': parent}`) but a *generation of 0* and inherits the parent's secret — the secret is the parent's worker's, rotated by the parent, not minted by the owner (:63-69).

### `gateway/session_worker_construct.py`
- **Purpose:** Resolves the three agent-construction inputs (`fallback_model`, `ephemeral_system_prompt`, `prefill_messages`) that a fresh managed worker cannot reconstruct for itself, and ships them to the child as a validated `construct_v1` frame.
- **Key abstractions:** `KEYS` (:11, the closed shape); `construct_inputs` (:14, owner side); `construct_kwargs` (:133, child side, raises `ValueError('invalid_managed_worker_bootstrap')` on a bad shape); `route_runtime` (:150) — the *one* resolver shared by the in-process turn and a keyless managed worker; `worker_api_key` (:192); `checkpoint_agent_kwargs` (:208); `routing_agent_kwargs` (:226); `StagedReceipt` (:70) and `_PrefillPublication` (:78) for the prefill snapshot handoff; `stage_session_prefill` (:96), `release_session_prefill` (:109).
- **Data flow:** owner → `construct_inputs()` under `owner_scope` (:15-20) → `construct_v1` rides the hydrated request beside `turn_v1` → the child parses it out of `frame['policy']['request_json']` and validates the exact key set (:135-143) → `AIAgent` kwargs.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Every rung of the route reads the session's **FROZEN** config, never live `config.yaml` — a later `model.base_url` edit must not carry this route's frozen credential to a new host (:165-168, R2-M2). The *creation request's* `base_url` is treated as immutable identity, not the current selection: once a canonical model mutation moved the route off the launch endpoint, the policy's selection wins (:157-163). A **primary AuthError walks the chain the agent will actually get** — the owner's per-turn `construct_v1` chain when it was sent, the frozen snapshot's otherwise (:192-206, #139155) — and a fallback entry's key belongs to another provider, so the primary never carries it (`worker_api_key` returns None when a fallback entry was chosen, :203). `checkpoint_agent_kwargs` re-implements the defaults because the gateway bypasses `load_config()` (:208-218).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `construct_kwargs` returns `{}` for a frame without the object (older owner) rather than failing (:135-137) and rejects any key set other than `KEYS` (:138-143). `_dict_list` accepts `None` or a list of dicts only (:146-148). `_read_prefill` resolves a relative profile home to `get_hermes_home()` (:122-124) and reads the file under `policy.client_env_scope()` so `HERMES_PREFILL_MESSAGES_FILE` is the *client's* value (:129).
- **Surprising:** The prefill snapshot is staged per *attempt*, not per session: `_PrefillPublication.publish()` stores a boxed snapshot and `withdraw()` removes it only if the box is still the one held (:83-93) — so an overlapping retry or conflicting create never replaces the winner's snapshot, and a refused create leaves nothing. `session_prefill` reads the file on first use and keeps that read for a session this owner did not create (restored after a restart, a branch) (:57-68).

### `gateway/session_worker_delegation.py`
- **Purpose:** The closed set of delegation-ledger operations that ride the *existing* worker receipt transaction — no handler opens a database or commits independently.
- **Key abstractions:** `_TASK_KEYS` (:13), `_ROUTE` (:14), `_EVENT_KEYS` (:16); `_shape`/`_text`/`_number` (:23-35) as the closed-shape validators; `_owned` (:38); `_dispatch` (:48), `_complete` (:89), `_child` (:108), `_read` (:124), `_claim` (:132), `_delivery` (:154), `_unscheduled` (:167); `_DELIVERY_UPDATES` (:146, a constant map of SQL fragments); `worker_delegation_handlers` (:176); `persist_worker_delegation` (:190); `worker_delegation_request` (:204).
- **Data flow:** `worker.persist {operation: 'delegation.<name>', payload}` → the parent's claim/assignment is verified (:207-211) → `apply_worker_receipt` dispatches to the handler *inside* the worker's own fenced transaction (:190-199) → `delegation.complete` additionally queues the event on `process_registry.completion_queue` **after** the commit (:222-229).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The caller's identity is *never* taken from the payload — the verified process identity (`worker_pid`, `worker_birth`) is bound separately and re-proved inside `_dispatch` by comparing `process_birth` (:74-81). A delegation may only be dispatched from a compute registration whose session source is one of `('cli','tui','gui','tool','oneshot')` (:55-58) — native/child origins need an owner-reserved source envelope, not worker-selected transport identities. The route fields in the payload must match the *session's own* stored `session_key` and ids (:59-65) so a worker cannot route a delegation into someone else's session. The `_delivery` SQL is assembled only from the constant `_DELIVERY_UPDATES` map (:154-163) — no caller string reaches the query.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_text` caps strings at 512 chars and `_number` rejects NaN/inf/negatives (:28-35). A duplicate `delegation_id` is `admission_conflict` after an ownership check (:66-70). A completion for a non-`running` row is `admission_conflict` (:103-104). The delivery claim uses a 300 s lease (`delivery_claimed_at < now-300`, :136-142) and `release` decrements the attempt counter, dropping the row once attempts hit `_MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS` (:146-151). `_child` rewrites `result_json` wholesale, removing the previous entry with the same `task_index` before appending (:114-120) — idempotent partial results.
- **Surprising:** `_unscheduled` **deletes** the row rather than marking it (:167-173), and refuses if `result_json` is already set (:170-171). `delegation.complete` publishes to the completion queue with `{'restored': True}` and never ACKs at publication (:225-229) — a lost ACK causes a repeat, arbitrated by the token-guarded claim.

### `gateway/session_worker_reservation.py` — Private owner-side reservation for an already-started admission: the producer keeps the `Popen` handle and the child's bootstrap pipe until `reserve_admission_worker` (:53) returns. The load-bearing rule is that `Popen.pid` may be a *launcher trampoline* (uv's Windows `python.exe`, pip's exe shim), so the worker reports its own pid/birth in a `hello` frame and the **owner** proves that pid is alive, carries that birth, and descends from the reserved handle within `MAX_LAUNCHER_HOPS = 3` (`verify_worker_identity` :18-50) — the worker never decides equality; the returned scope's secret is `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` and is never published on a client event stream (:64, :70-71).

### `gateway/session_worker_spawn.py` — `acquire_process` (:6) is a cancellation-safe handoff of a managed subprocess acquired off the owner loop: the spawn runs in a worker thread, the `asyncio.shield`ed task survives the caller's cancellation, and on `CancelledError` the state is flipped to `cancelled` so the still-running creation thread closes the process itself instead of leaking it — the done callback then *consumes* the eventual spawn exception so it never surfaces as "exception never retrieved" (:11-32).

### `gateway/shutdown_flush.py`
- **Purpose:** The on-disk safety net that keeps a session's messages alive when SQLite/FTS5 is refusing writes — pending messages, FIFO overflow tails, cap-dropped transcript rows and agent-history snapshots are all written as atomic JSON payloads under `<hermes_home>/pending_messages/` and replayed later.
- **Key abstractions:** `TRANSCRIPT_CAP_DROP_REASON` / `AGENT_HISTORY_REASON` / `QUARANTINE_SUFFIX = ".bad"` (:33-36); `_REPLAYED_UNREMOVABLE` (:41); `_get_flush_dir` (:44); `_write_payload` (:56); `flush_pending_to_file` (:91); `flush_overflow_to_file` (:104); `spool_dropped_transcript_message` (:127); `drain_transcript_spool` (:145); `is_row_rejection` (:254); `_remove_replayed` (:267); `_quarantine_spool_file` (:279); `_order_flush_files` (:294); `recover_pending_to_db` (:317) / `recover_pending_spool` (:323); `_recover_one_payload` (:382); `recover_gateway_pending` (:443); `flush_agent_history_to_file` (:477).
- **Data flow:** live drop → `spool_dropped_transcript_message` writes one `pending-<uuid>.json` per message with `{session_key, reason, ts, seq, data}` (:127-140) → at restart `recover_gateway_pending` runs `recover_pending_spool` per profile home (:443-475) → files are ordered by `_spool_sort_key` (:245-252) → each is appended via `SessionDB.append_message` and deleted on success.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Ordering is `(ts, seq, filename)` because `seq` is per-process and `ts` has one-second resolution, so files spooled by two processes in the same second can interleave; within one process the order is exact (:245-252). Ordering *matters*: `SessionDB` restores a conversation by AUTOINCREMENT id and spool files have random names, so any other order permanently scrambles the recovered transcript (:294-300). A *read* that can also write is modelled as a write: `is_row_rejection` (:254-265) separates "the database refused this row's own values" (an unbindable type, an int outside 64 bits, a malformed field) from retryable failures (lock, I/O, corruption, routing) — and *constraint* failures deliberately stay retryable because they can be session-wide. Only a sqlite **binding** message counts, because `InterfaceError("no more rows available")` is WAL contention that `SessionDB` retries (:262-265).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** A row the database can never accept is **quarantined** by renaming to `<name>.bad` (:279-292) — no replay glob matches it, so it cannot hold its session's later rows back on every write and every restart; it stays on disk for manual recovery. A spool file that was written but could not be deleted goes into `_REPLAYED_UNREMOVABLE` (:267-277) so *this* process never replays it twice — but nothing on disk marks it, so a restart writes the row again (documented at :271-273). `_sort_number` (:226-243) normalises anything non-numeric, bool, overflowing or NaN to `0.0` because a sort key mixing `str` and `int` raises `TypeError` and would abort the entire recovery pass. One unparseable payload must only skip *that* file — the file is never unlinked, so aborting the pass would re-poison every later boot (:347-364).
- **Surprising:** `AGENT_HISTORY_REASON` payloads are **skipped silently** on recovery (:352-355) — they are a manual operator artifact, not a DB insert. Per-session blocking is tracked separately from global failure (`blocked_sessions`, :345, :396-400): if an older message for a session could not be replayed, a later one is *also* held back, because writing it now would give it a lower row id than the message it follows and permanently invert the transcript. `flush_agent_history_to_file` swallows all failures (:477-503) — shutdown must never block on a best-effort backup. The flush dir is chmod 0700 and each payload 0600 (:44-53, :56-76).

### `gateway/shutdown_forensics.py`
- **Purpose:** Captures *who and what* asked the gateway to shut down, from inside a signal handler that must not block.
- **Key abstractions:** `snapshot_shutdown_context` (:76, the <10 ms non-blocking probe); `spawn_async_diagnostic` (:135, the detached `ps` walk); `format_context_for_log` (:172); `context_as_json` (:192); `check_systemd_timing_alignment` (:200); `parse_systemd_duration_to_us` (:249); `_proc_summary` (:51), `_read_marker` (:68).
- **Data flow:** SIGTERM → `snapshot_shutdown_context` reads `/proc/<pid>/status` fields, `INVOCATION_ID`/`JOURNAL_STREAM`, `os.getloadavg()`, `TracerPid` and the `.gateway-takeover.json` / `.gateway-planned-stop.json` markers (:76-115) → `format_context_for_log` renders one scannable line (:172-190) → `spawn_async_diagnostic` appends a `ps`/`pstree`/`dmesg` listing to a log file in a detached subprocess (:135-170).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `/proc` reads are **comm-only** — `cmdline`/`argv` are never read because those bytes are not safe to persist (tokens, URIs, `-e KEY=` overlays) (:51-66, :135-140). The `ps` listing is `pid,ppid,user,pcpu,pmem,stat,comm` for the same reason, and the detached script deliberately uses `sort -nrk4` instead of GNU `--sort=-pcpu` so BSD `ps` (macOS) also produces a listing (:118-133). The takeover marker that does **not** name this pid is treated as a smoking gun for "another `--replace` instance is killing us" (:105-115).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** The probe is pure stdlib and never raises — every `/proc` read is wrapped in `contextlib.suppress` (:42-48, :81-89). `spawn_async_diagnostic` returns `None` on Windows (no `ps`) and on any `OSError` (:137-141, :156-158); it uses `timeout`/`gtimeout` when available and otherwise runs unbounded rather than skipping the diagnostic, because the detached script cannot block teardown either way (:150-154). The log is opened `O_APPEND` so rapid signals do not trample each other, and `fchmod`ed 0600 to tighten logs created 0644 by earlier releases (:145-149).
- **Surprising:** `check_systemd_timing_alignment` (:200-226) is a *startup* check, not a shutdown one: a stale unit file (upgraded without re-running `hermes setup`) can have `TimeoutStopSec` below the stop budget, so systemd SIGKILLs the cgroup mid-drain and the journal shows a phantom `code=killed status=9`. It returns `None` when aligned **or** undeterminable. `parse_systemd_duration_to_us` (:249-289) uses systemd's *fixed* `time-util.h` constants for month/year rather than variable calendar lengths.

### `gateway/shutdown_watchdog.py`
- **Purpose:** Four out-of-loop backstops for a frozen asyncio loop: a shutdown watchdog that hard-exits, a heartbeat file, a lifetime loop-liveness watchdog, and a self-rescheduling floor timer.
- **Key abstractions:** `start_loop_liveness_watchdog` (:87); `arm_shutdown_watchdog` (:264); `loop_heartbeat_forever` (:345); `write_loop_heartbeat` (:210); `get_loop_tick_socket_path` (:193); `_write_watchdog_dump` (:241); `_mark_exited_quietly` (:159); `_hard_exit` (:150); `_sweep_stale_tick_sockets` (:330); `_LoopFloorTimerHandle` (:56).
- **Data flow:** the runner arms `arm_shutdown_watchdog(drain_timeout + grace)` (:234-238); a daemon thread waits in 1 s chunks so a late disarm is observed within ~1 s (:278-281) → on expiry it snapshots, writes a faulthandler dump to `logs/gateway-shutdown-watchdog.log` *and* stderr (:241-262), releases the PID file + runtime lock, drains the log queue, stamps the exit records and calls `os._exit` (:294-315). Independently, `loop_heartbeat_forever` rewrites `state/gateway.heartbeat` and serves a loop-tick socket (:345-425).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** The heartbeat file's fsync write goes to a **thread** but is *awaited* (:385-388) — an inline write stalled the loop inside its own heartbeat and the liveness watchdog then killed it (WSL2 fsync p99 31 s / max 112 s vs a ~90–120 s budget), yet a fire-and-forget task would keep the file fresh while the loop was wedged. Because file freshness no longer proves schedulability, the task also arms a **loop-scheduling witness**: a PID-suffixed AF_UNIX socket answered by the loop itself (:193-204, :316-328), so a probe must require the witness to agree before classifying WEDGED. The witness is PID-suffixed so a stale node from a dead process is never mistaken for this gateway's (:193-199), and sibling nodes from other dead PIDs are swept (:330-343). The loop-tick socket exposes only a scheduling ping — no control or auth operations (:358-360).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** The liveness watchdog counts **strikes** and only exits after `max_strikes` consecutive misses (:137-141) with the default at 3 (~90–120 s of loop block), and re-checks the stop event before *each* irreversible step (:130-148) so a late `stop()` wins. `_hard_exit` first SIGKILLs in-flight foreground commands (:150-157) — they run in their own process group and would otherwise outlive the gateway, reparented to init. `_mark_exited_quietly` stamps the lifecycle ledger, the exit-metrics marker and `gateway_state.json` in that order, and the runtime-status write goes **last** because it is the record housekeeping refreshes (:159-178). The floor timer exists so selector waits stay bounded even with no other timers pending (:56-85).
- **Surprising:** `DEFAULT_LOOP_WATCHDOG_MAX_STRIKES` was *raised* and then deliberately left tight again: the false-positive class (the watchdog's own on-loop heartbeat fsync stalling the loop it monitors) was fixed at the root by the off-loop write + two-witness probe (#90502), so the default stayed at 3 for genuine wedges (:40-43). The watchdog writes its dump to **file *and* stderr** because a console-less launcher (Windows `.vbs` login item) discards stderr and a liveness death used to leave no stack anywhere (:136-141, #120356/#91097).

### `gateway/slash_access.py`
- **Purpose:** A second authorization axis beside `allow_from` — *which* allowed users may run *which* slash commands, per DM-vs-group scope.
- **Key abstractions:** `SlashAccessPolicy` (:31) with `is_admin` (:38) / `can_run` (:44); `_ALWAYS_ALLOWED_FOR_USERS = {"help", "whoami"}` (:19, a read-only floor `user_allowed_commands` can only add to); `_SCOPE_KEYS` (:24); `_DISABLED_POLICY` (:52) and `_FAIL_CLOSED_POLICY` (:56); `policy_from_extra` (:86), `policy_for_source` (:100), `policy_for_runner_source` (:137).
- **Data flow:** a platform's `extra` dict → `policy_from_extra` (:86-97) → a frozen `SlashAccessPolicy` → `can_run(user_id, canonical_cmd)` (:44-49) at the dispatch site in `gateway/run.py`.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** Gating is **enabled only when an admin list exists** for that scope (:92) — no `allow_admin_from` means allow-everything, so existing installs are unaffected until an operator lists an admin (docstring :7-9). When gating is disabled `is_admin` returns True for everyone (:38-41) so callers can use `is_admin`/`can_run` uniformly. A **blank/`None` `chat_type` is ambiguous** (relay frames send `""`/null) and resolves to whichever scope is gated, with the historical group scope on a tie (:124-131) — the comment records that `_DM_CHAT_TYPES` lists `""` but a truthiness guard made that branch unreachable. The DM scope falls back to `group_user_allowed_commands` for the *command list only* when DM set none, and admin lists are never cross-scope (:86-96).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_coerce_list` accepts `None`, a list/tuple/set, a comma string or a bare scalar (:61-73) and strips empties; `_coerce_command_list` strips a leading `/` and lowercases to match how `resolve_command()` stores names (:80-83). Under `multiplex_profiles` the runner's `config` is the *launch* profile's alone, so authorization follows the transport (bot-owning) profile via `identity.transport_profile` (:148-153); a served profile whose config is not cached **fails closed** rather than inheriting the launch profile's open policy (:154-157, #121705).
- **Surprising:** `_FAIL_CLOSED_POLICY` (:56) is gated with an *empty* admin list, so only the `{"help", "whoami"}` floor runs — the policy is enabled with nobody an admin, which is a different state from "gating disabled".

### `gateway/slash_commands.py`
- **Purpose:** The in-session slash-command handlers for `GatewayRunner`, lifted out of `gateway/run.py` into a mixin so `self._handle_*_command` keeps resolving through the MRO; the cohesive clusters live in sibling mixins (`slash_commands_model/_session/_status/_goals/_login`) and this module keeps the shared helpers plus the one-off commands.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewaySlashCommandsMixin` (:173) mixing in the five sibling mixins; the shared helpers `_cached_agent_for` (:184), `_resident_agent_for` (:204), `_reply_metadata` (:218), `_checkpoint_manager` (:233), `_write_approval_setter` (:248), `_deliver_approval_confirmation` (:270), `_typed_command_prefix_for` (:290), `_display_config_target` (:302), `_catalog_options` (:611), `_render_diff_result` (:800), `_fenced_truncated_diff` (:819), `_track_background_task` (:833), `_blocking_approval_or_stale` (:1189); the argument tables `_ROLLBACK_SKIP_LINES` (:40), `_BUSY_MODE_BEHAVIOR` (:45), `_DIFF_MODE_BY_ARG` (:52), `_VOICE_MODE_BY_ARG` (:56), `_FOOTER_STATE_BY_ARG` (:62), `_APPROVE_CHOICE_BY_ARG` (:66); the module-level `_execute` (:106) shared slash executor and `_spawn_detached_update` (:128).
- **Data flow:** a platform `MessageEvent` → a `_handle_*_command` method → either a string reply or an `EphemeralReply` → the platform adapter. Config-mutating commands route through `_execute(command, options=…)` (:106-110) so the shared CLI executor resolves process-level fallbacks, and through `_write_raw_config_leaf` (:92-99) for a strict raw config round-trip.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** `_write_raw_config_leaf` uses a **raw** round-trip rather than the merged view, because the behavioral read is fail-open (`{}`) and expanded, so writing it back would wipe the file after a read error and persist `${VAR}` values verbatim (:92-95). `/restart` is **idempotent**: a redelivery of the same `/restart` (PTB's graceful-shutdown `get_updates` ACK failing on the way out) is detected via the persisted `.restart_last_processed.json` marker (platform + `update_id`) and ignored (:544-556, :583) — otherwise every fresh gateway re-restarts. Under a service manager or a container the restart exits 75 rather than detaching, because `systemd KillMode=mixed` kills the cgroup and detached `setsid`+`bash` fails there (:586-590). `/help` and `/commands` are **filtered by the same policy that gates execution** — `_catalog_options` (:611-622) passes `allowed_commands` so the catalog never advertises a command `_check_slash_access` would refuse. `/approvals` re-checks `is_admin` at the side-effect boundary even though the central slash gate may have allowed the command to a non-admin (:942-952), and it deliberately does **not** evict the cached agent or alter the system prompt because "the prompt-cache prefix is sacred" (:951-953). `/reload-mcp` invalidates the provider prompt cache (tool schemas live in the system prompt), so it routes through slash-confirm and "Always Approve" persists `approvals.mcp_reload_confirm: false` (:1079-1112). `/reload-skills` does **not** clear the prompt cache (skills are invoked at runtime) and queues a one-shot note prepended to the *next* user message so alternation is preserved (:1113-1170).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `/stop` escalates through three tiers — the pending sentinel, the running agent, then a *chat-scoped* scan of differently shaped session keys — and picks one reason for the whole stop, not one per key (:428-476). A `/stop` with no active agent still clears the platform's stuck typing indicator (:481-488, #32295). `_handle_kanban_command` truncates the listing at 3800 chars for message length caps (:387) and auto-subscribes only when the CLI's output contains the `Created t_<hex>` success line — with `--json` there is no such line, so a scripting user gets no subscription (:362-374). `/deny` caps the relayed reason at 280 chars and distinguishes a leading `all` from the reason string (:1220-1246). `/debug local` is refused from a chat (the report cannot stay on the machine) and, from a local session, does **no** network call at all — not even the expired-paste sweep (:1276-1290). `/update` refuses on a non-messaging platform, on a managed install, and on any install method that is not `git`/`unknown` (:1320-1341).
- **Surprising:** `_cached_agent_for` has a `lockless_fallback=True` path used by exactly one caller — the manual codex `/compress` path — because historical callers read the cache only under `_agent_cache_lock` and returned `None` for a fixture that skipped `__init__` (:184-196). `_handle_yolo_command` persists the flag on the *routing entry* so it survives a gateway restart, and materializes that entry for a first-message `/yolo` that has none (:957-970). `_handle_voice_command` keys the voice mode by the owning profile's bot, not the chat, so two multiplexed bots in one chat keep independent `/voice` state (#75198, :678-700).

### `gateway/slash_commands_branch_thread.py`
— Pure helpers pinning where a `/branch` clone lands: `BRANCH_THREAD_PLATFORMS` (Discord/Telegram/Slack/Matrix, `gateway/slash_commands_branch_thread.py:18`) and `branch_dest_source` (`:51`) rebuild the clone's `SessionSource` so a FOLLOW-UP message arrives on the key the clone is bound to — Discord keys an in-thread message on the THREAD id as `chat_id` (`:60`) while Telegram/Slack/Matrix key it on the PARENT chat id with `chat_type` promoted thread→group (`:64`); `parse_branch_args` (`:22`) treats only a LEADING `--here` as a flag, so a session is never titled `--here`.

### `gateway/slash_commands_goals.py`
- **Purpose:** Autonomy-loop slash commands (`/goal`, `/subgoal`, `/heartbeat`, `/loop`, `/refine`, `/review`) as mixin methods mixed onto `GatewayRunner`.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayGoalCommandsMixin` (`gateway/slash_commands_goals.py:30`); `_mgr_call(prefix, fn, *args, errors=…)` (`:22`) turns a manager call into `(result, None)` / `(None, "<prefix>: <exc>")`; `_quiet_bool` (`:15`); `_idle_cached_agent_or_error(event, verb)` (`:162`) returns `(session_key, cached_agent, error_text)` and refuses while a run is in flight.
- **Data flow:** `MessageEvent` → manager lookup (`_get_goal_manager_for_event` / `_get_heartbeat_manager_for_event`) → the CLI mirrors (`hermes_cli.goal_command.dispatch_goal_command` `:53`, `hermes_cli.heartbeat.parse_interval` `:111`, `hermes_cli.loops.dispatch_loop_command` `:306`) → i18n `t()` strings; a drafted prompt is re-entered as an ordinary user turn through the adapter FIFO.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) `_enqueue_goal_turn` (`:76`) routes the drafted prompt through the adapter FIFO so the post-turn judge's path applies, but when a local `slash.exec` sink is active (`gateway.session_command_sink.active_sink`, `:87`) it writes `sink.prompt` instead and lets the client submit with its own input id. (2) `authorize_gate` (`:41`) treats a LOCAL session's owner as admin (slash.exec already admitted via `authorize_local_source` + the `session:control` capability) while messaging stays explicit-admin-only (`:46-49`). (3) `/refine` (`:176`) reviews a SNAPSHOT of `agent._session_messages` — live session and prompt cache untouched — and falls back to `refine_local_session` reading the stored transcript when the cached agent is gone (`:182-188`). (4) `/review` (`:203`) binds the approval session-key contextvar explicitly (`:215`) because that contextvar is only bound during agent turns; without it the completion event carries no gateway route and never re-enters the chat.
