(5) Every manager I/O hop uses `self._run_in_executor_with_context` (`:61`, `:225`) and never a bare executor hop: `SessionDB()` resolves `get_hermes_home()` at call time from a contextvar set by `_profile_runtime_scope`, so a default-executor hop starts with an empty context and would read the DEFAULT profile's `state.db` (`:59-60`). (6) `/loop` warms the SessionDB cache off-loop FIRST (`:282`) — a cold cache drops the first `/loop` write while the reply already claims the loop was set (the same class as the /goal false-ack).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** no goal manager → `gateway.goal.unavailable` (`:39`); `/subgoal remove` with a non-int index → `gateway.subgoal.remove_not_int` (`:251-254`); `/subgoal clear` with none → `gateway.subgoal.none_to_clear` (`:263`); `_mgr_call` catches only `RuntimeError`/`ValueError` by default (`:23`) and the index op widens it to `IndexError` (`:255-257`); `_clear_goal_continuations` (`:68`) and `_enqueue_goal_turn` (`:105`) swallow failures into `logger.debug` — cleanup is best-effort and never a command failure; a `ValueError` from `start_review` surfaces as its own message (`:226-227`) rather than the generic `start_failed`.
- **Surprising:** `/goal`'s admin gate is a MESSAGING-only concept (local sessions are self-admin), and `goal_blocks_loop_tick` (`:308`) appends a note that an active goal DEFERS the loop tick — the two autonomy mechanisms are deliberately serialized against each other.

### `gateway/slash_commands_login.py`
— The off-turn paired-DM `/login` sign-in flow: `_handle_login_command` (`gateway/slash_commands_login.py:82`) refuses anything that is not a paired DM (`chat_type` in dm/private plus a `chat_id`) and not in `_LOGIN_BLOCKED_PLATFORMS` {ntfy, raft, a2a} (`:36`) — those adapters' "dm" is a broadcast topic/channel/agent peer, so a consent link or sign-in code would be PUBLISHED rather than delivered to one person; a single-worker `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`:73`) is entered through `copy_context()` (`:78-80`) so the profile-scoped credential contextvar survives; the attempt is a `_SignInAttempt` dataclass (`:27`) that a LATER command may cancel only after the live attempt's key matches (`:103-108`); and `_sweep_sessions_off_welcome` (`:197`) clears the free-tier model override BEFORE evicting the cached agent (a disk-pinned override keeps the live route and is counted as a failed clear).

### `gateway/slash_commands_model.py`
- **Purpose:** Model-route slash commands (`/model`, `/codex-runtime`, `/reasoning`, `/fast`, `/personality`) mixed onto `GatewayRunner`.
- **Key abstractions:** `_ModelSwitchContext` dataclass (`gateway/slash_commands_model.py:72`) with `read_config` (`:93`) / `apply_override` (`:118`); `GatewayModelCommandsMixin` (`:150`); `_model_switch_lock()` (`:381`) — a runner-wide `asyncio.Lock`; `_FAST_SELECTIONS` (`:25`) maps a `/fast` arg to (service tier, persisted value, i18n label); `_REASONING_DISPLAY_TOGGLES` (`:46`); `_model_switch_skew_guard` (`:49`); the commit chain `_perform_model_switch` (`:155`) → `_record_model_switch` (`:212`) → `_commit_model_switch` (`:358`) / `_commit_model_switch_locked` (`:414`).
- **Data flow:** command args → `parse_model_switch_args` (single-owner parser, `:574`) → `_ModelSwitchContext` → `hermes_cli.model_switch.switch_model` off-loop (`:163`) → the unified selection guard (`combined_selection_warning`, `:536`) → commit (cached-agent swap `:187`, SessionDB row + override map `:212-266`, config write-through `:282`) → confirmation text (`:302`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) The whole `/model` read-resolve-commit runs under ONE runner-wide lock taken BEFORE the first await (`:564-568`, `:381`) because slash commands bypass the busy guard while no agent runs; two interleaved `--global` picks otherwise left `config.yaml` on whichever thread wrote last, and a `--global` cleanup wiped a session pick issued after it (#100314). The lock is created lazily through `self.__dict__` because the runner is built without `__init__` in tests (`:387-389`). (2) A `--global` switch has ONE durable authority: `config.yaml`, written FIRST; on success the session override is DROPPED, on failure the override is KEPT so the switch truthfully survives as session-only (`:276-296`) — a redundant copy would shadow every later global change after a restart. (3) `--once` overrides are deliberately EXCLUDED from the store write-through (`:296-303`): a one-turn override must never survive a restart, and the persisted value staying at the pre-once state is exactly what the finally-restore reverts to (#29923). (4) The session override is NOT cleared when `_channel_override_for(source)` is set (`:293`), because precedence is session > `channel_overrides` > `config.yaml`. (5) `_model_switch_skew_guard` (`:49`) REFUSES a switch when the running gateway's code is older than disk — a first-time lazy import on a new code path can crash on a stale cached dependency. (6) `_switch_cached_agent_model` (`:187`) relies on the agent rolling itself back to the OLD model/client and re-raising; the commit (DB, override, eviction, config) is ABORTED so the next message does not rebuild a broken agent. (7) `/fast` validates against the TURN's own gate (`_fast_route_supports` `:36` → `resolve_fast_mode_overrides`), so a tier `/fast` accepts must be one the session's next request actually carries — proxies are refused; `ultrafast` is additionally gated by `ultrafast_offered` (`:888`). (8) `/reasoning` labels tell the truth about the route: `effort_display_label(current_effort, *_route)` (`:826`) renders a Hermes-internal step (`ultra`) the wire clamps as "ultra (sends max on this route)" rather than a distinct level (#61634). (9) `_handle_model_command_locked` forwards ALL gateway routing columns at CREATE time (`:584-600`) — a crash between the branch-row creation and `switch_session()` would leave the branch permanently unroutable, unreachable by chat lookup AND by `/resume`'s IDOR guard. (10) `_record_switch_metrics` (`:392`) binds the owning `HERMES_HOME` for a multiplexed runner because slash dispatch does not install the routed profile's scope.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** local sessions refuse `--once` (`gateway.model.err_once_local`, `:421`) and instead defer the pick to the next turn boundary via `defer_local_model_pick` (`:424`); `request.errors` returns `f"❌ {request.error_messages()[0]}"` — gateway decoration over canonical copy (`:576`); `request.force_refresh` busts the disk catalog cache inside `contextlib.suppress` (`:578-582`); `_save_gateway_config_key` (`:691`) reads `read_user_config_raw` (raw, never merged defaults) and writes with `atomic_config_write`, returning False on failure so the caller degrades to session-only; `_send_model_picker` returns False when `list_picker_providers` raises or yields nothing (`:466-471`) → text-listing fallback; the picker callback fires OUTSIDE `_handle_model_command` and therefore takes the switch lock itself (`:358-366`); the listing path uses `non_blocking_catalogs=True, probe_custom_providers=False` (`:497-502`) so one degraded provider cannot stall the reply (#74003).
- **Surprising:** `/model` with no target and no provider is a READ path that still takes the WRITE lock (`:564`) — the lock covers read-resolve-commit, not mutation. And `/personality` persists only the selection NAME, never `agent.system_prompt` (`:686-688`) — the user's prompt overlay is not owned by the command.

### `gateway/slash_commands_session.py`
- **Purpose:** Session-transcript slash commands (`/new`, `/resume`, `/sessions`, `/branch`, `/title`, `/save`, `/undo`, `/retry`, `/topic`, `/compress`, `/discard`) mixed onto `GatewayRunner`.
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewaySessionCommandsMixin` (`gateway/slash_commands_session.py:138`); `_RESET_CLEANUP_TIMEOUT_S = 30.0` (`:33`); `_DM_CHAT_TYPES` (`:35`); `_BRANCH_COPIED_FIELDS` (`:37`); the IDOR guard set `_same_origin_chat` (`:312`), `_persisted_row_proves_owner` (`:359`), `_resume_target_allowed` (`:393`), `_resume_row_visible` (`:413`), `_resume_caller_is_admin` (`:347`); `_branch_row` (`:96`); `discard_lost_turns` (`:115`).
- **Data flow:** `MessageEvent` → session store (`async_session_store.get_or_create_session` / `reset_session` / `switch_session` / `rewind_session`) → optional transcript load → command-specific DB mutation → i18n reply; `/branch` also materializes a new thread through the adapter.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) `_resume_caller_is_admin` (`:347`) is deliberately STRICTER than `SlashAccessPolicy.is_admin()`, which is True for EVERY caller when slash gating is DISABLED — the default config would make everyone cross-origin-capable (IDOR). (2) `_persisted_row_proves_owner` (`:359`) fails CLOSED on legacy NULL rows: the sessions table has no `user_id_alt` column, so an alt-keyed (Signal/Feishu) caller is never proven by `user_id` alone (CWE-639). (3) `/new` bumps the run generation FIRST and then releases the running-agent slot (`:199-203`): the in-flight run's own guarded release (old generation) returns False and would leave a zombie slot that silently drops every later message — the release is idempotent. (4) The reset path funnels ALL conversation-scoped per-session state plus security state through one `_clear_conversation_scope` call (`:213`) and clears env-passthrough/credential-file state at the boundary (`:217`). (5) `/new` moves route wakes so a parked wake survives the reset (#114456, `:224`). (6) `/retry` (`:425`) pins the active message ids BEFORE reading the transcript and commits the rewrite inside the re-send's OWN admission, fenced on those ids (`:445-450`) — a write after a later row fails the fence. (7) `/retry` runs a PRE-FLIGHT `_retry_readmission_reply` (`:568`) mirroring the gates a plain turn passes (emergency-stop pause, external drain, active-session cap) so a refused `/retry` leaves the transcript UNTOUCHED; explicitly not race-free — a drain can engage between the check and the re-send (`:571-576`). (8) `/branch` (`:1191`) creates the thread BEFORE the clone so a failed thread create never orphans a branch row (`:1213-1215`), and writes ALL routing columns at CREATE time (`:1232-1241`). (9) `_branch_row` (`:96`) copies the `api_content` sidecar so the branch's first turn replays the parent's exact wire bytes (warm provider prompt cache). (10) `/compress` is a profile-scoping wrapper (`:652`): multiplexed gateways resolve credentials through a fail-closed per-profile secret scope that slash dispatch does not install — unscoped, `/compress` would raise `UnscopedSecretError`. (11) `_run_manual_compression` (`:710`) builds a TEMPORARY agent and its `finally` ALWAYS evicts and tears it down, even on success (`:750-753`), so the live cached agent rebuilds its prompt from current files. (12) `_persist_manual_compression` (`:801`) persists BEFORE repointing the session entry, so a failed write is fatal and the old history stays reachable; an unchanged id without in-place mode preserves the original transcript instead of overwriting it (#44794). (13) `/resume` clears the persisted model override explicitly after the boundary funnel (`:1093`) — the funnel clears only IN-MEMORY state, and without this the next turn's `_rehydrate_session_model_override` resurrects the pin it just cleared (#119864). (14) `/resume` evicts the cached agent because the cached AIAgent's memory provider cached `_session_id` at `initialize()` and would keep writing to the wrong session (`:1096-1098`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** a transcript that cannot be read → the resume SUCCEEDED but the count is missing: `gateway.resume.resumed_no_count` + `history_unreadable()` (`:1110-1112`), never "empty"; `/undo` with a non-int count → `gateway.undo.invalid_count` (`:626-628`); `/compress` below `MIN_MESSAGES` → `gateway.compress.not_enough` (`:695`); `/compress --aggressive` without `--preview` returns only the "unsupported" note and does nothing (`:690-692`); `_cleanup_old_agent_for_reset` (`:143`) bounds the off-loop teardown at 30 s — `wait_for` cancels the await, not the worker thread, so a wedged teardown keeps running (or leaks) and the reset proceeds either way (#35994); `/handle_discard_command` (`:179`) publishes the peeked conversation via `authority._publish_route` rather than get-or-create, because a suspended route would rotate to a new session and strand the lost turn; `/save` sanitizes the filename with `os.path.basename` — path separators from chat input are never trusted (`:917-919`) — and renders/writes off-loop because they scale with transcript size (multi-MB); `/topic` re-checks the allowlist before mutating SQLite side tables (`:838-842`).
- **Surprising:** `/branch` on a thread-capable platform leaves the CURRENT chat bound to the ORIGINAL session and binds the clone to a NEW SIBLING thread (`:1194-1197`), so both paths stay alive — `--here` and every thread-less platform switch in place. And the child row copies the parent's `system_prompt` (`:1241`): without it the branch's first turn rebuilds (re-probing the workspace) and forfeits the warm cache the copied transcript buys.

### `gateway/slash_commands_status.py`
- **Purpose:** Read-only gateway introspection commands (`/status`, `/context`, `/usage`, `/agents`, `/insights`, `/topup`).
- **Key abstractions:** `GatewayStatusCommandsMixin` (`gateway/slash_commands_status.py:239`); `_status_model_route` (`:98`) — the route resolver; `_quiet` / `_quiet_sync` (`:63` / `:90`) fail-open wrappers; `history_unreadable()` (`:78`); `_capped_rows` (`:229`) with `_LIST_CAP = 12` (`:24`); `_context_compressor_lines` (`:143`); `_agents_delegation_lines` (`:176`); `_usage_agent_stats_lines` (`:201`).
- **Data flow:** event → session store + SessionDB → the live or cached agent (its `context_compressor` for occupancy) → `agent.context_breakdown` for the per-category estimate → i18n `t()` lines → one joined string.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) `_status_model_route` (`:98`) resolves through a 4-rung ladder (live agent → active session override → persisted recent route → SessionDB row → gateway config) and carries the WINNING source's `provider`/`base_url`/`api_key` ONLY — so a later context-window lookup queries the endpoint that serves the DISPLAYED model, never a losing route's endpoint (`:102-107`). (2) No raw `model.context_length` pin is applied: the resolver applies it only while the displayed route still matches the configured one, so a session `/model` switch must not inherit it (`:138-139`). (3) `/status` keeps `_AGENT_PENDING_SENTINEL` distinct — a starting/pending run is not a usable agent for model/context display but still occupies the session slot (`:249-251`). (4) Token totals come from the SQLite session DB, NOT `SessionEntry`: `run_agent.py` persists per-turn deltas into `sessions_db` and `SessionEntry.total_tokens` is always 0 (`:319-334`). (5) A context window the resolver only INVENTED (unknown model → `DEFAULT_FALLBACK_CONTEXT`) is hidden rather than shown as a real limit; occupancy-only is the honest rendering (`:267-273`). (6) `mark = "~"` prefixes any context figure whose source is not `provider_usage` (`:291`) — the tilde marks an ESTIMATE, not a provider-reported number. (7) Matrix session keys are REDACTED to a sha256 fingerprint (`:338-342`) for shared-room status. (8) `/usage` resolves the billing route live-agent → persisted SessionDB route → configured provider (`:93-104`), so account limits render from on-disk credentials even right after login (#15167); `/usage reset` is refused for any provider but `openai-codex` (`:105-107`). (9) `/insights` normalizes Unicode dashes (`:177-178`) because Telegram/iOS auto-convert `--` to an em/en dash, and runs through `self._run_in_executor_with_context` (`:211`) for the same profile-scope reason as everywhere else. (10) `/context` renders a 24-cell bar (`:365-371`) and labels cumulative token totals as THROUGHPUT, not context size (`:171`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `_quiet` (`:63`) swallows ANY exception and returns a default, so every DB read fails open; a transcript that cannot be loaded → `history_unreadable()` (`:386-387`, `:585-586`) and never "empty"; `_session_context_breakdown` (`:518`) deliberately RE-RAISES `TranscriptReadError` rather than swallowing it (`:522-527`) — a read failure must not pass as an empty transcript; `/context` with no agent and no history → `gateway.context.no_data` (`:389`); `_context_breakdown_lines` returns `[]` when the header would be the only row (`:75`); a missing i18n key is detected by `label.endswith(f"breakdown_cat_{cat_id}")` because `t()` echoes the key back (`:71-72`) and the raw category label is used instead; `/agents` shows "quiet for Ns" only at `seconds_since_progress >= 60` (`:185`) and prints `gateway.agents.none` when nothing is running (`:481-482`).
- **Surprising:** `/status` calls `_rehydrate_session_model_override` (`:262`) before reading the override so its precedence survives a gateway restart, while `/context` (`:344`) does not — the two surfaces read the same route through deliberately different sequences.

### `gateway/status.py`
- **Purpose:** The gateway's process-identity and liveness layer — the PID file, the byte-range file lock, the `gateway_state.json` snapshot, the machine-wide scoped locks and the takeover/stop markers under each `HERMES_HOME`.
- **Key abstractions:** `_RuntimeStatusWriter` (`gateway/status.py:58`) — one daemon thread with a single latest-wins pending slot; `write_runtime_status` (`:1168`) / `publish_runtime_status` (`:1188`) / `read_runtime_status` (`:1201`); `resolve_gateway_liveness` (`:1379`) returning the `GatewayLiveness` dataclass (`:1261`); `acquire_scoped_lock` (`:1525`) → `_acquire_scoped_lock_at` (`:1543`); `get_running_pid` (`:1974`), `get_running_pid_cached` (`:2081`), `get_running_pid_identity_strict` (`:2030`); `terminate_pid` (`:402`), `take_over_scoped_lock_holder` (`:1845`); the command-line identity matchers `_gateway_command_subcommand` (`:541`) and `gateway_spawn_intent_subcommand` (`:612`); the multiplex projections `multiplexer_liveness_for_profile` (`:1294`), `profile_platforms_from_multiplexer` (`:1359`), `shared_listener_mirror_platforms` (`:1333`); `retained_gateway_state` (`:380`), `normalize_updated_at` (`:348`), `record_start_and_check_storm` (`:205`).
- **Data flow:** the daemon writes `gateway.pid` (O_CREAT|O_EXCL, `:1066`) + `gateway.lock` (byte-range lock) + `gateway_state.json` (merged snapshot, `:1089`) under `{HERMES_HOME}`; readers walk the liveness ladder (`:1379-1460`) PID file + lock → caller-supplied HTTP health probe → runtime-status PID validated against the live process table → the live default multiplexer that records the profile in `served_profiles`; scoped locks live in a machine-wide dir named `scope-<sha256(identity)[:16]>.lock` (`:464-468`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) The Windows file lock is taken at a byte offset of 1 MiB (`:53`, `:926-941`) so the JSON payload at the start of the file stays READABLE while another process holds the lock — Windows byte-range locks are mandatory for other readers. (2) `_cleanup_invalid_pid_path` (`:911`) NEVER unlinks the sibling `gateway.lock`, held or not: another contender may already have opened that inode, and a fresh one would let two processes each hold "the" lock — its `unlink_lock` parameter is documented as moot. (3) The lock dir's scope is the OS USER, not the kernel host (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/hermes/gateway-locks`, `:321-338`): separate users have separate `$HOME`s, profile roots and credentials. A RELATIVE `$XDG_STATE_HOME` is ignored per the XDG spec, because honouring one made the lock dir CWD-relative and two serves from different directories shared no singleton (`:330-334`). (4) `_pid_exists` (`:942`) deliberately avoids `os.kill(pid, 0)` on Windows — that sends `CTRL_C_EVENT` to the whole console group (bpo-14484) — preferring psutil, then ctypes; POSIX zombies are reported DEAD because treating one as alive makes `--replace` wait forever under systemd `Restart=always` (`:950-975`). (5) The PID-reuse guard is a `(pid, start_time)` PAIR (`:472-505`): Linux reads field 22 of `/proc/<pid>/stat` (clock ticks), elsewhere psutil `process_birth` quantized to centiseconds; the two scales are never mixed on one platform, so cross-platform unit differences are irrelevant — only same-source equality matters. `START_TIME_DRIFT_TOLERANCE = 200` (`:454`) is 2 s on either scale. (6) `terminate_pid` (`:402`) requires a matching `expected_start_time` for a Windows force-kill — `taskkill` on a recycled PID has killed svchost.exe — and a mismatch refuses the kill on EVERY platform (`:406-417`). (7) `_acquire_scoped_lock_at` (`:1543`) opens an existing lock for READING first (`:1553-1554`): a lock this token may not read (left by an ELEVATED gateway) must raise, because `_read_json_file` would treat it as empty and O_EXCL would report "in use" — a conflict with no holder. (8) A stale lock is renamed to a `.stale` tombstone via `os.replace` and THEN unlinked (`:1586-1590`): with unlink()+O_EXCL two racing starters could both win; `os.replace` lets exactly one claim it. (9) The SAME live PID as this process always self-reacquires WITHOUT a start_time equality check (`:1578-1583`): requiring equality falsely rejects reconnects when the on-disk record has `start_time: null`, making the gateway report itself as the foreign squatter of its own token (#81468). (10) `_RuntimeStatusWriter` (`:58`) is a single daemon thread with ONE pending slot — newer submissions REPLACE the pending snapshot, bounding memory and keeping every status producer off asyncio; `submit()` deep-copies the payload and `wait(generation)` resolves against a monotonic generation counter, so a caller waiting on generation N is satisfied by any LATER snapshot. (11) `_merge_over_on_disk` (`:162`) lays the canonical snapshot OVER whatever is on disk right before writing, so out-of-process writers (the migration's compensator, `container_boot`) keep their fields while the gateway's win. (12) `resolve_gateway_liveness` (`:1379`) replaced two open-coded ladders that disagreed on the SAME page load (`:1394-1401`) — a cross-container gateway, a profile-scoped dashboard and a launch-service gateway with no PID file each broke one surface only. A rung that RAISES degrades to the next and sets `probe_error` (`:1409-1416`), never a 500. (13) A scoped home with no `gateway_state.json` gets an EMPTY record, never None (`:1443-1447`): None makes the probe re-read the PROCESS home's record and lend that gateway's PID to a home it does not own. (14) `profile_platforms_from_multiplexer` (`:1359`) re-keys `<profile>:<platform>` entries to bare platform names; for `profile == "default"` it ALSO merges the FLAT keys (`:1367-1372`) — a multiplex host's primary map is always `default`, and dropping them projected `{}` → "Restart needed" forever (#123088, #123869). (15) `_gateway_command_subcommand` (`:541`) refuses loose substring matches ("gateway" also matched `gateway status` / `python -m tui_gateway`): it needs a Hermes entrypoint PLUS the `gateway` subcommand, or a gateway-dedicated entrypoint, and `--profile`/`-p` selectors are stripped ANYWHERE in argv because `_apply_profile_override` removes them before argparse. (16) `gateway_spawn_intent_subcommand` (`:612`) is the deliberate OPPOSITE of the identity matcher — "if I launch this, does a gateway runtime eventually appear?" — peeling the `python -c <src> …` wrapper and trying every suffix, because the restart watcher hides a real `… gateway run` behind it (#107002). (17) `hermes_home_assignments` (`:692`) is TOKEN-bounded and quote-stripped: the substring test it replaced let `HERMES_HOME=/root/profiles/ops` claim `/root/profiles/ops2`. (18) `recorded_gateway_home_conflicts` (`:258`) fails CLOSED on a comparison failure and treats a legacy record with no `hermes_home` as proving nothing. (19) `retained_gateway_state` (`:380`) separates a retained `startup_failed` / watchdog `degraded` from a plain `stopped`: a profile the operator STOPPED is "stopped", not a current failure, and the durable stop intent lives in `desired_state`.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `normalize_updated_at` (`:348`) coerces a legacy epoch, tolerates a trailing `Z` (Python < 3.11 rejects it) and rejects epochs before 2000-01-01 or more than a day ahead as corrupt (`:365-370`); `runtime_status_is_stale` (`:1213`) uses a 120 s TTL = 2× the 60 s housekeeping interval (`:1209-1212`); `record_start_and_check_storm` (`:205`) is best-effort — a broken ledger is logged and swallowed — and ring-buffers the file to `max(max_starts*4, 40)` lines with backoff `min(cap, 5 * 2^min(n-max, 6))`; `_write_json_excl` (`:1066`) unlinks on a failed write; `_marker_is_stale` (`:1620`) treats an unparseable timestamp as stale; `_read_live_pid_marker` (`:1628`) unlinks malformed/expired markers so they cannot wedge a new instance — EXCEPT a request stamped `accepted`, which stays until the pause is settled; `consume_planned_stop_marker_for_self` (`:1944`) distinguishes a busy mutex from a real stop — on OSError it classifies without consuming and leaves a receipt beside the request so recovery keeps the drain owed; `reap_gateway_children` (`:1798`) is POSIX-only (`[]` on Windows, where `taskkill /T` tree-kills) and skips a child whose ppid still equals the parent.
- **Surprising:** `get_running_pid` (`:1974`) is the most heavily commented function in the file — while the runtime lock is HELD, a live SAME-home record the identity matcher rejects is NEVER cleanup-unlinked (`:2004-2018`): the holder is a gateway whatever its command line reads as, and unlinking a held `gateway.lock` leaves it locking a deleted inode so the next starter double-runs (#125610, #123109). `get_running_pid_identity_strict` (`:2030`) RAISES rather than returning None on ambiguous state (malformed metadata, disagreeing identities, changed creation time) — the strict reader wants "unknown" to be loud. And `_get_process_hermes_home` (`:240`) deliberately uses `get_process_hermes_home()` rather than `get_hermes_home()`, because the latter honours the per-session `_HERMES_HOME_OVERRIDE` and would MISROUTE the identity files.

### `gateway/status_inline_source.py`
— Decides whether a command line is an interpreter running INLINE SOURCE (`python -c <src> …`), because everything after `-c` is DATA for that program, not the gateway's own identity (#107002): `inline_source_flag_index` (`gateway/status_inline_source.py:17`) walks the option block VALUE-AWARE — it imports the canonical `_PYTHON_SHORT_OPTIONS_WITH_OPERANDS` / `_PYTHON_LONG_OPTIONS_WITH_OPERANDS` sets from `hermes_state_holders` instead of hand-rolling a second copy (`:44-47`), handles clustered short options so `-Xc` is `-X c` and NOT an inline `-c` (`:60-67`), and requires CASE-PRESERVING tokens because the operand-taking `-Q`/`-W`/`-X` differ from the operand-less `-q`/`-b` (`:39-42`); `_bootstrap_entry` (`:107`) then maps Hermes' OWN inline bootstraps (runpy `run_module`/`run_path`, an exec'd launcher, a base64-encoded `.cmd` launcher) back to the equivalent `python -m <module>` argv, and `inline_bootstrap_argv` (`:144`) finds the source by trying the SHORTEST token run that ends in `)` — command lines arrive space-joined, so the source is split across tokens.

### `gateway/status_lock_dir.py`
— The one place that reports an UNUSABLE scoped-lock directory: `ScopedLockDirUnusable` (`gateway/status_lock_dir.py:13`) subclasses `PermissionError` so existing callers keep their handling, and `unusable_lock_dir` (`:29`) logs the dir WARNING exactly ONCE per process (DEBUG afterwards, via the `_warned` set at `:26`) because the reconnect watcher and every adapter sharing the dir re-enter the acquire path — the message names the repair via `hermes_dir_acl.unusable_dir_remedy` and explains the Windows elevated-install cause (`:33-35`); `unusable_lock_dir_recovered` (`:39`) forgets the dir so a later failure warns again.

### `gateway/status_phrases.py`
— Resolves the chat-safe generic status phrases: `resolve_status_phrase_catalog` (`gateway/status_phrases.py:111`) merges built-ins (`gateway/assets/status_phrases.yaml`, loaded with `mode: replace` at `:95-98`) → conventional profile-relative user files (`status_phrases.yaml` / `status_phrases/`, `:25` and `:119`) → `display.status_phrases` (or the legacy alias `generic_status_phrases`, `:27`) → the per-platform section; every path is resolved under `HERMES_HOME` and absolute paths, `..` escapes and paths escaping the base are IGNORED on purpose so config stays profile-portable and cannot read arbitrary files (`:63-79`); `choose_status_phrase` (`:140`) accepts `preview`/`args` for callback compatibility but NEVER interpolates them — only configured phrase strings reach the user (`:143-144`) — and de-duplicates against the last 5 phrases (`:148-153`).

### `gateway/status_scoped_locks.py`
— Scoped-lock ownership and release, split out of `gateway.status` (acquire stays there): `_scoped_lock_record_is_stale` (`gateway/status_scoped_locks.py:15`) recovers only dead/reused/stopped owners and deliberately leaves an UNKNOWN identity reserved rather than guessing it is dead (`:16-19`) — a non-gateway owner may be a dashboard or setup worker; `release_scoped_lock` (`:37`) skips the start_time equality check on purpose, because an on-disk null vs a live fingerprint would wedge reconnects (`:38-39`); `release_all_scoped_locks` (`:45`) is the `--replace` cleanup, narrowed by `owner_pid` and optionally `owner_start_time` to survive PID reuse, and swallows `OSError` per file while still counting the removal.

### `gateway/status_served.py`
— One rule for choosing which runtime record describes a profile the host multiplexer may serve, so `/api/status` and the messaging card can never disagree: `served_profile_runtime` (`gateway/status_served.py:21`) returns None when the profile's OWN runtime proves a live gateway of that home (`:36-37`), when the multiplexer record is missing/unreadable — `(pid, {})` is no record (`:38-40`) — or when the caller's proven `gateway_pid` is not the multiplexer (`:42-43`); `local_liveness_pid` (`:15`) returns None for a `source == "health"` answer because that PID belongs to the remote container and may collide with an unrelated local one; the fold uses the profile NAME, never `home.name` (`:44-46`), since the default root's basename is `.hermes` and its flat keys never matched (#123088).

### `gateway/sticker_cache.py`
— A JSON cache of vision-generated Telegram sticker descriptions keyed by `file_unique_id`: `_resolve_cache_path` (`gateway/sticker_cache.py:21`) returns the patched `CACHE_PATH` only when a TEST changed it, else the live profile-scoped `HERMES_HOME` — the import-time constant would pin every profile to the launch home under the multiplexed gateway; `cache_sticker_description` (`:60`) does load→merge→save under a `threading.Lock` (`:52`, `:71`) so the read-modify-write triple is atomic across worker threads, and the async wrapper (`:75`) moves the whole thing to a thread because `atomic_json_write` ends in `os.replace`, whose duration is unbounded under filesystem pressure and would stall every adapter and every in-flight turn in the process.

### `gateway/stream_consumer.py`
- **Purpose:** Bridges the sync agent callback to async platform delivery — it progressively edits ONE platform message from streamed deltas.
- **Key abstractions:** `StreamConsumerConfig` (`gateway/stream_consumer.py:61`); `_Tick` (`:80`) — one drain's decisions; `GatewayStreamConsumer(StreamTransportMixin, StreamFallbackMixin, StreamThinkFilterMixin)` (`:99`); the queue sentinels `_DONE`/`_NEW_SEGMENT`/`_COMMENTARY`/`_TOOL_PROGRESS`/`_FINAL_TEXT`/`_FLUSH`/`_APPROVAL_BOUNDARY`/`_REOPEN_SEED` (`:46-53`); the turn-final delivery flags `_final_response_sent`, `_final_content_delivered`, `_delivered_final_text`, `_turn_split_delivery`, `_delivery_ambiguous` (`:209-227`); the reconcile API `delivered_final_matches` (`:330`), `has_delivered_text` (`:360`), `has_durably_delivered_text` (`:367`).
- **Data flow:** agent worker thread → `on_delta(text)` (`:527`) → a `queue.Queue` → `run()` (`:544`) drains to a `_Tick` via `_drain_queue` (`:656`) → `_push_update` (`:827`) → `_send_or_edit` → adapter; the authoritative final arrives as `(_FINAL_TEXT, text)` from `finish(final_text)` (`:536`) and is adopted by `_adopt_final_text` (`:696`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) `delivered_final_matches` (`:330`) is a TRI-STATE (True/False/None) because a SUCCESSFUL finalize edit can still carry a stale preview, so "success" alone must not confirm delivery (`:331-334`); None means nothing was recorded on a legacy/ambiguous path and the caller trusts the flags. (2) A payload-less delivery flag is judged against the FINAL CONTENT, not trusted blindly (`:346-352`): every internal flag-setting site records a payload, and a record-less consumer whose visible text does not contain the completed response demonstrably has NOT delivered it. (3) `_delivery_ambiguous` — a full-final send that timed out and MAY have landed — is the ONLY case where a payload-less flag keeps legacy trust (`:356`); re-sending there risks a duplicate rather than recovering a loss. (4) `_stream_ledger` (`:196`) mirrors `_accumulated` but is NOT truncated when an overflow split seals head chunks, so the recorded turn-final payload stays reconcilable (`:322-327`). (5) `_draft_id_counter` is a class-wide RANDOM seed of 49 bits (`:111-114`): draft_id keys the relay connector's sealed-stream tombstones, which OUTLIVE the process, so a replayed id is answered out of the OLD tombstone and dropped; 49 bits stays inside JS 2^53. (6) `finish(final_text)` (`:536`) carries the AUTHORITATIVE completed response including post-stream augmentation the accumulator never saw. (7) `_adopt_final_text` (`:696`) refuses to adopt when the consumer never streamed anything (a no-stream turn keeps the gateway's final-send ownership, `:699-700`) and, on a SPLIT delivery, appends only the SUFFIX when the final strictly prefix-extends the ledger (`:707-712`) — wholesale adoption would repeat sealed heads, refusing would make the gateway resend the ENTIRE body. (8) `_should_edit` (`:732`) refuses mid-stream edits while the buffer could still resolve to a silence marker ("NO"→"NO_REPLY") (`:748-750`); got_done always resolves the buffer. (9) `_seal_overflow_heads` (`:808`) finalizes the head with `is_turn_final=False` — a split head is not the answer, so fresh-final must not mark the turn delivered on it (`:815-816`). (10) `_split_first_send` (`:755`) strips the `(n/n)` indicator `truncate_message` suffixes to multi-chunk output, because the tail is the LIVE preview later deltas extend and a kept indicator ends up embedded mid-reply (`:786-788`). (11) `_cumulative_transport` (`:909`) marks stream-is-the-message drafts and WeCom native as ONE append-only stream per turn: clearing `_accumulated` on a segment break would make the next frame a non-prefix snapshot and the connector would re-append the whole answer (`:925-928`). (12) `_end_segment` (`:925`) preserves the `"__no_edit__"` sentinel (Signal, github_comment webhook) — without it every tool boundary posts a new message (`:930-932`). (13) `_reset_segment_state` (`:451`) retains the segment's visible text in `_delivered_segment_texts` so `has_delivered_text` still matches after the reset (`:453-455`). (14) The approval boundary NEVER keeps the stream open across a prompt (`:472-473`): the WeCom finalize ack only confirms server receipt, and after a long idle gap the client may stop tracking the stream. (15) `close_for_approval_prompt` (`:382`) returns an already-resolved Future when native streaming is off (`:391-393`), so the caller never blocks. (16) `flush_pending_sync` (`:412`) is a `(_FLUSH, threading.Event)` barrier, and `_signal_flush` (`:443`) MUST be called on every path that consumed the barrier — a missed set stalls the caller for the full timeout.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** `run()` checks `_run_still_current()` at the top of every loop (`:546-549`) — a session reset (`/new`, `/stop`) ABANDONS the stream rather than delivering stale deltas; `CancelledError` → `_on_cancelled` (`:942`) does a best-effort finalize edit with `is_turn_final=False` (the handler owns the flags) and marks delivery only on a successful edit (`:952-953`); the run loop's `finally` always calls `_wake_flush_waiters` (`:619-620`), which drains still-queued flush barriers so a consumer dying mid-flush does not cost the full timeout (`:959`); a head-send failure in `_split_first_send` keeps the FULL text for the fallback final and skips the boundary reset but STILL yields (`await asyncio.sleep(0.05)`, `:775-782`) — otherwise the buffer stays over the debounce threshold and the loop spins without ever sleeping; `_push_update` sets `tick.draft_final_fresh_send` only when the draft transport actually sent fresh (`:831-833`), since an EDIT is not a fresh send; `_finalize_edit_path` (`:878`) is a strict priority ladder — fallback-final → already-sent → visible-update → existing-message edit → retry — each branch guarding the specific duplication it would cause (`:880-900`); `_clean_for_display` (`:974`) strips `MEDIA:<path>` tags using the shared anchored regex from `gateway/platforms/base.py`, so only tags whose path ends in a deliverable extension are removed and an unknown-extension path stays visible (#34517).
- **Surprising:** the `_Tick` dataclass (`:80`) carries delivery-decision flags (`update_visible`, `draft_final_fresh_send`) set by `_push_update` and read by `_finalize_turn` — the tick is not merely a queue snapshot but the channel through which a send reports back what it actually did. And `_DEFAULT_BOUNDARY_PLACEHOLDER = "⏸ 等待审批中..."` (`:57`) is hard-coded CHINESE in an otherwise fully i18n'd gateway.

### `gateway/stream_consumer_fallback.py`
- **Purpose:** The non-streaming delivery paths used once progressive edits stop working — continuation sends, chunking, cursor cleanup, commentary and silence retraction.
- **Key abstractions:** `StreamFallbackMixin` (`gateway/stream_consumer_fallback.py:17`), a mixin mixed into `GatewayStreamConsumer`; `_send_new_chunk` (`:20`), `_visible_prefix` (`:43`), `_continuation_text` (`:50`), `_split_text_chunks` (`:73`), `_truncate_for_stream` (`:81`), `_send_fallback_final` (`:96`), `_fallback_when_nothing_unseen` (`:164`), `_fallback_len_budget` (`:211`), `_send_with_flood_retry` (`:224`), `_send_empty_fallback_final` (`:246`), `_send_failure_may_have_delivered` (`:283`), `_fallback_flood_retry_delay` (`:292`), `_is_flood_error` (`:306`), `_flush_segment_tail_on_edit_failure` (`:311`), `_try_strip_cursor` (`:335`), `_send_commentary` (`:345`), `_raw_message_limit` (`:378`), `_suppress_silence_marker` (`:400`).
- **Data flow:** fallback final → `ensure_closed_code_fences` (fences balanced BEFORE the continuation is computed, so the closing fence reaches the user even when only the tail is delivered, `:109-111`) → `_continuation_text` (strip what the user already saw) → chunk split → `adapter.send` with one bounded flood retry → the turn-final flags.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) `_continuation_text` (`:50`) backs the cut up to the last space/newline when the visible prefix ends MID-WORD (`:63-69`): edits fire on a throttle tick, not at a word boundary, so the prefix can end inside a word and the continuation re-sends the broken word's tail; a prefix with no boundary keeps the original cut, and a prefix already ending on a whole word needs no back-up (doing it re-sent that word at the seam). (2) The stale partial is deleted ONLY when the FULL final was re-sent (`:151-154`): if only the missing tail went out, the partial IS the head of the answer ("sent only the second half" symptom). (3) Threading is applied only to a FULL resend — `anchor = self._initial_reply_to_id if continuation == final_text else None` (`:128`) — because a tail continuation keeps its existing unthreaded delivery on every platform. (4) `_send_fallback_final` sets `_already_sent = sent_any_chunk` on a partial failure but deliberately NOT `_final_response_sent` (`:133-142`): the gateway must still deliver the full answer, and `_already_sent` only prevents a duplicate of the partial. (5) `_send_empty_fallback_final` returns FOUR outcomes, not a bool — "delivered", "failed" (the gateway may retry), "ambiguous" (a timeout may have landed), "preview" (flood control; the complete ACKed preview is authoritative) (`:246-249`); "ambiguous" and "preview" keep dup suppression, "failed" lets the gateway perform its normal final send (`:179-182`). (6) The "preview" branch calls `_record_turn_final_payload` (`:188`) so the gateway's reconciliation confirms delivery instead of re-sending a second bubble next to the never-deleted preview (#71047 Problem B). (7) `_send_empty_fallback_final` records `_delivered_final_text` VERBATIM rather than via `_record_turn_final_payload` (`:273-276`): the sealed previews were just deleted, so the ledger (still holding sealed heads) would claim delivery for text this path removed. (8) `_send_failure_may_have_delivered` (`:283`) treats a timeout as "may have delivered", but a result with `retryable is True` is explicitly NOT ambiguous (`:286-287`). (9) `_send_with_flood_retry` (`:224`) breaks out of the retry when `raw_response.partial_overflow` is set (`:239-241`): the split head is already on screen, so re-sending the whole content duplicates it. (10) `_fallback_flood_retry_delay` (`:292`) refuses a retry when the requested delay exceeds `_max_fallback_flood_retry_seconds` (5 s — Telegram bounds edit retries at 5 s, `gateway/stream_consumer.py:140`) and leaves final delivery to the gateway (`:300-303`). (11) `_send_commentary` (`:345`) deliberately does NOT set `_already_sent` (`:364-366`): commentary is interim, and the flag would suppress the real final after multiple tool calls; it appends to `_delivered_commentary_texts` (`:372`) so run.py can tell whether an interim send actually carried the final (#14238). (12) `reply_to` on commentary is applied ONLY for buzz/slack/mattermost/feishu (`:357-363`): Discord/Telegram use thread_id metadata, and `reply_to` on every commentary is spam. (13) `_send_commentary` sets `_interim_send=True` (`:354`) so a stream-is-the-message adapter's seal-interception does not turn it into `draft(final=true)`, which would seal the live stream with interim text and orphan the true final (`:351-353`). (14) `_truncate_for_stream` (`:81`) calls the adapter's canonical `truncate_message` only when the adapter IS a `BasePlatformAdapter`; non-base test doubles and legacy adapters keep the two-argument call shape (`:88-91`), and a malformed return falls back to `_split_text_chunks` (`:92-94`). (15) `_fallback_len_budget` (`:211`) asks the adapter for a PER-CHAT cap and length unit (a relay adapter fronts N platforms), falling back to `MAX_MESSAGE_LENGTH`/4096 (`:212-213`).
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** the egress guard suppresses the fallback continuation (`:99-108`) and the segment tail flush (`:314-315`) when `_egress_declined` — the whole point of these paths is to deliver as a NEW message, which is exactly the re-addressing the egress guard exists to stop; `_send_new_chunk` returns the existing `reply_to_id` when the text is blank or the send failed, and sets `_edit_supported = False` on failure (`:24-32`); `_try_strip_cursor` (`:335`) wraps its edit in `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` — it must never block the fallback path (`:340`); `_suppress_silence_marker` (`:400`) closes an open NATIVE bubble with an empty finalize (a native bubble is not deletable, `:404-407`), deletes the stale previews, clears the accumulated/ledger/last-sent text and ALL turn-final flags (`:409-414`), and leaves those flags False so the gateway's whole-response filter owns what goes out next (`:401-403`); `_flush_segment_tail_on_edit_failure` (`:311`) sends the unseen tail with `_interim_send=True` so it must never seal a native stream (`:326-328`).
- **Surprising:** `_send_fallback_final` sets `self._last_sent_text = chunks[-1]` (`:160`) — only the LAST chunk, even though the earlier chunks are also on screen — so `_visible_prefix` (`:43`) under-reports what the user saw after a multi-chunk fallback, which is why `_fallback_prefix` exists as a separate override.

### `gateway/stream_consumer_fences.py`
— Two code-fence helpers shared by the stream consumer and the gateway final send: `escape_code_fences_for_display` (`gateway/stream_consumer_fences.py:8`) replaces each ``` with \`\`\` so text can be wrapped in an outer ``` block (reasoning that quotes code would otherwise break the outer fence), and `ensure_closed_code_fences` (`:16`) appends a closing ``` when the count is odd — output truncated mid-code-block (`finish_reason="length"`) would otherwise render everything after the orphan as one code block, and a spurious close is far less harmful — then strips complete fenced regions (and a trailing unclosed ```) before counting lone backticks, because a fenced region's internal backticks would otherwise pollute the standalone count (`:32-36`).

### `gateway/stream_consumer_think.py` — `StreamThinkFilterMixin` (`:18`) suppresses `<think>…</think>` blocks *progressively* over streamed deltas, mirroring the CLI's state machine by reusing `agent.think_scrubber`'s tag primitives (`:13-14`); the decision a reader would not guess is that an opening tag counts **only at a line boundary** (`_at_block_boundary` `:24`) so prose that merely mentions `<think>` is not swallowed, and a partial tag at the tail is held in `_think_buffer` until enough characters arrive to decide (`_filter_and_accumulate` `:52-90`, `_flush_think_buffer` `:97`).

### `gateway/stream_consumer_transport.py`
- **Purpose:** The transport layer of `GatewayStreamConsumer` — the send/edit/frame primitives and the transport-ordered `_send_or_edit` that decides how accumulated assistant text reaches a chat.
- **Key abstractions:** `StreamTransportMixin` (`:26`) with `_send_or_edit` (`:313`, the ordered fallthrough native frame → draft frame → edit existing → first send), `_native_push` (`:373`), `_draft_push` (`:428`), `_first_send` (`:449`), `_edit_existing` (`:478`), `_on_edit_failure` (`:532`), `_enter_fallback_mode` (`:525`), `_try_fresh_final` (`:269`), `_delete_previews` (`:106`), `_resolve_draft_streaming` (`:127`), `_resolve_native_streaming` (`:151`), `_send_draft_frame` (`:166`), `_abandon_native_stream` (`:202`), `_adopt_message_id` (`:303`), `_track_preview_ids_from_result` (`:238`), `_stale_preview_ids` (`:98`), `_draft_metadata` (`:90`), `_degrade_native_to_buffered_send` (`:83`), `_close_empty_native_bubble` (`:77`), `_try_frame` (`:55`); the cap `_MAX_EDIT_BACKOFF_SECS = 30.0` (`:23`); the `@stops_reply_clock` decorator on `_send_or_edit` (`:312`).
- **Data flow:** accumulated assistant text → `_clean_for_display` → `ensure_closed_code_fences` (`:320-322`, applied to the *final* only — draft frames keep the raw pre-fence text so a stream stays prefix-stable, `:328`) → cursor stripping (`:323-325`) → transport order: native `send_stream_frame` with an empty **seed frame** as the typing indicator (`:46`) → adapter `send_draft` → `edit_message` on the live preview → plain `send` → on failure, fallback mode where only the *missing tail* is sent at `got_done` (`:525-530`).
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** (1) the transport order is a **fallback chain, not a preference** — a native or draft transport that fails disables itself and falls through so the accumulated text still reaches the user (`:365-378`); (2) an egress *authorization decline* is terminal for the whole run, not merely "drafts are unusable": `_send_draft_frame` sets `_egress_declined` when the connector's guard refuses the destination and every later send is suppressed, because the fallback path would otherwise post the answer into the chat the connector just refused (`:186-200`, `:449-457`, `:540-550`); (3) the turn-final frame is marked delivered **optimistically before the ack wait** (`:398-402`) because WeCom renders the bytes before the ack — a join-cancel mid-wait must not strand `final_content_delivered=False` and duplicate the send — and a definitive failure rolls the mark back so the fallback delivers exactly once (`:410-417`); (4) the flood backoff honours the server's own `retry_after` but caps it at 30 s (`:577-580`) because interim edits are *skipped*, not slept, so a 30 s Telegram penalty costs only a stale preview.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** every adapter call is wrapped (`_try_frame` `:55`, `_edit_message` `:31`) so a raise is logged at DEBUG and reads as "transport unavailable"; a failed first send sets `_edit_supported = False` and only the final is sent (`:464-476`); an oversized edit that split across continuations retargets edits at the *last* continuation id and sets `_turn_split_delivery`, which makes fresh-final refuse afterwards — deleting sealed heads would erase delivered text (`:271-274`, `:504-514`); a `partial_overflow` response preserves the visible prefix so `got_done` sends only the missing tail (`:553-570`); a rate-limited *cosmetic* cursor edit on a turn-final is treated as delivered (the answer is already on screen) so it is not sent twice (`:551-556`); Telegram's `delete_message` reports failure by returning `False` rather than raising, so preview deletion gets one bounded 1 s retry (`:117-124`); an orphaned draft bubble is sealed on turn death via `abandon_open_draft` (`:202-215`).
- **Surprising:** `_send_or_edit` deliberately **drops** a 1–2 token preamble at a tool boundary instead of carrying it (`:337-346`) — on non-cumulative transports the held preamble is lost and the segment resets; `_MIN_NEW_MSG_CHARS = 4` (`:27`) is the same threshold. And two features are explicit ports of `openclaw/openclaw#72038` (`_should_send_fresh_final` `:221`, `_try_fresh_final` `:269`) rather than fresh design.

### `gateway/stream_dispatch.py` — `GatewayEventDispatcher` (`:21`) routes typed stream events onto an adapter: message events go to the sink via `adapter.render_message_event` (`:62`), tool events are *formatted by the adapter* and enqueued onto the same tool-progress queue the gateway drains so the two paths never race (`:71-82`), and `dispatch` (`:51`) swallows every exception because "presentation must never break the agent loop" — an adapter returning `None` from `format_tool_event` means it *ate* the event on a platform without tool chrome, and `tool_mode="new"` dedups consecutive repeats of the same tool name (`:76-79`).

### `gateway/stream_events.py` — The agent→gateway streaming contract: frozen dataclasses `MessageChunk` (`:16`), `MessageStop` (`:22`), `Commentary` (`:29`), `ToolCallChunk` (`:35`), `ToolCallFinished` (`:44`), `LongToolHint` (`:54`), `GatewayNotice` (`:62`) and the explicit union `StreamEvent` (`:72`) — deliberately an **explicit union, not a marker base class**, so a missing `case` in an exhaustive match is a visible type error rather than a silent fall-through (`:70-71`); tool *output* never travels in these events (`:37-38`, `:45-47`) because it is history, and `ToolCallChunk.index` is the monotonic per-turn id that correlates a finish with its start (`:39`, `:48`).

### `gateway/streaming_tts_consumer.py`
- **Purpose:** Consumes LLM text deltas and produces streaming PCM audio for a platform adapter, so playback starts mid-generation instead of after the whole answer.
- **Key abstractions:** `StreamingTTSConsumer` (`:31`) with `on_delta` (`:76`), `finish` (`:84`), `start` (`:111`), `abort` (`:221`), `wait_complete` (`:234`), `_run` (`:139`), `_synthesise_and_write` (`:183`), `_open_handle` (`:125`), `_settle` (`:117`), `_enqueue_clauses` (`:65`), `_put_sentinel` (`:98`), `_streamer_format` (`:53`); the `_ABORT`/`_DONE` sentinels (`:23-24`) and `_HandleDeclined` (`:27`); the outcome properties `active`/`completed`/`partial`/`audible`/`dropped`/`suppress_whole_file`/`done` (`:51-57`).
- **Data flow:** `on_delta` (agent worker thread, never blocks) → `SentenceChunker.feed`/`flush` → a bounded thread-safe `queue.Queue(maxsize=256)` (`:40`) → the `_run` task on the gateway loop (`:139`) pops with a 100 ms timeout (`:143-145`) → `_synthesise_and_write` streams one clause through the provider (`next()` runs in a thread so a blocking provider cannot stall the loop, `:192`) → the adapter's `begin_streaming_tts` / `write_streaming_tts` / `finish_streaming_tts` → the outcome flags.
- **Non-obvious design decisions:** the **sentinels are load-bearing** — `_DONE` must never be lost, so `finish` evicts queued clauses until the sentinel fits (`:88-90`), and `_ABORT` does the same (`:221-223`); and the adapter handle is opened **lazily on the first PCM chunk**, not before the provider answered (`:197-199`, `:125-137`), because an OpenAI-compatible endpoint reports its real audio rate only in the response (#76466, `:36-38`), so the format is re-read from the streamer at open time rather than trusted from config.
- **Edge cases & failure handling:** queue saturation sets `_dropped` and the clause is lost (`:67-69`) — a dropped clause makes the outcome *partial*, never *completed* (`_settle` `:117-121`); a `_HandleDeclined` (the adapter refused the stream) returns with nothing audible so the gateway falls back to whole-file TTS (`:152-153`); a failure **after** audio was audible aborts the adapter stream and keeps `suppress_whole_file` set, so the response is never replayed from the beginning (`:119-121`, `:210-219`); `abort` is idempotent and callable from any thread, scheduling the adapter abort onto the loop via `call_soon_threadsafe` (`:221-226`); `wait_complete` shields the drain task and reports success only when `completed` (`:234-240`).
- **Surprising:** `self._audio_format = audio_format or AudioFormat() if self._streamer is None else self._streamer_format()` (`:38`) parses as `(audio_format or AudioFormat()) if self._streamer is None else self._streamer_format()` — the caller's provisional format is used *only* when there is no streaming provider at all, which is the opposite of the comment's apparent intent; the `_strip_markdown` import is deliberately lazy because `tools.tts_tool` would cycle at module load (`:181-186`).

### `gateway/stuck_loop_counts.py` — Persisted per-session restart-failure counters (`.restart_failure_counts`, #7536): `increment_restart_failure_counts` (`:28`) rewrites the file to count **only the sessions that were active at shutdown** (the others' loops are already broken), `clear_restart_failure_count` (`:39`) drops one session's counter and deletes the file once none is left, and every load/mutate/save runs under one module-level `_COUNTS_LOCK` (`:16`) — because completions clear their counter from worker threads concurrently and an unserialized pair rewrites a stale snapshot that resurrects the other session's counter (`:3-6`); a corrupt file degrades to `{}` rather than raising (`:19-25`).

### `gateway/systemd_notify.py` — Minimal, optional systemd `sd_notify` support: `notify` (`:16`) sends a **non-blocking** AF_UNIX datagram (translating systemd's `@abstract` notation into Python's leading-NUL address form, `:18-19`) and swallows every `OSError` so a missing systemd never blocks gateway startup (`:20-22`); `SystemdWatchdog` (`:39`) feeds `WATCHDOG=1` only while the event loop wakes inside its lag budget (`record_tick` `:77`, default tolerance = 25 % of the interval, `:53-58`) and flips to `unhealthy` — and stops feeding entirely — when the loop falls behind, because a watchdog that keeps pinging a stalled gateway hides the stall it exists to expose (`:86-87`, `:92-106`); `stop()` (`:108`) emits `STOPPING=1` at most once.

### `gateway/systemd_stop_mark.py` — The `ExecStop=` shim that marks a supervisor-ordered stop as *planned* before systemd's SIGTERM lands, so `systemctl restart/stop hermes-gateway` no longer ends in "Failed with result exit-code" for an intentional restart; it resolves the PID from `argv[1]` else systemd's `$MAINPID` with **no PID-file fallback** (`_resolve_target_pid` `:22`) because marking a recorded PID without proof it is the process systemd is about to signal could mislabel an unrelated shutdown as planned (`:26-28`), and `main` (`:41`) always returns 0 so the stop proceeds even when the marker write fails — best-effort by design (`:16-20`).

### `gateway/turn_context.py` — The per-turn wiring bundle between `GatewayRunner._run_agent_inner` and `TurnRunner`: a dataclass of ~90 fields (`:17`) whose one non-obvious contract is that **only `message` is rebindable**; every other mutable piece of turn state is a single-element list (`last_progress_msg`, `last_tool`, `repeat_count`, `agent_holder`, `result_holder`, `tools_holder`, `stream_consumer_holder`, …) so mutation stays visible to the outer body without a `nonlocal` (`:39-49`, `:88-93`), which is also why `result_holder`/`tools_holder`/`stream_consumer_holder` exist at all; the second non-obvious split is `event_message_id` vs `inbound_message_id` (`:70-74`) — the raw platform id is stamped on the user turn, the reply anchor is not the same thing.

### `gateway/turn_executor.py` — `_UnboundedThreadExecutor` (`:9`) runs one thread per submitted turn body with no queue and no cap, because `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=None)` is **not** unbounded (it is `min(32, cpu_count + 4)`) — the same silent queue at a larger number (`:12-14`); `submit` (`:26`) starts the thread **before** registering it, so an OS thread-limit `start()` failure cannot leave an unstarted thread in `_threads` (which would make shutdown's `join()` raise and skip the quiesce decision, `:44-49`), and one critical section covers check + start + register so a concurrent `shutdown()` either refuses the item or sees its thread, never a live uncounted one (`:41-50`); `shutdown` accepts `cancel_futures` for API parity only — there is no queue to cancel (`:55-61`).

### `gateway/turn_lease.py` — The per-SESSION turn lease that serializes the [load history → run → flush] region: `SessionTurnLeaseRegistry` (`:74`) keys leases by **resolved `session_id`, not routing key**, because `switch_session()` makes key→id many-to-one and two keys could interleave flushes on one transcript (the `user;user` wedge, `:4-8`); a waiter that exhausts its budget fails **CLOSED** (`TurnLeaseTimeoutError` `:32`, raised at `:126-133`) rather than run unsynchronized against a still-held lease, only *idle* leases are evicted (`_evict_idle` `:89-96`) so the 512-entry cap can never break serialization, and a mid-turn rotation re-aliases the **same** `_SessionLease` under the new id via `rebind` (`:139-163`) — blocked loudly (fail-open on the old id) when the target's lease is already live.

### `gateway/wake.py` — Wakes an existing agent session from a background completion event: push-capable adapters get a synthetic internal `MessageEvent` (`deliver_wake` `:108`), stateless ones are **self-POSTed** to `/v1/chat/completions` with the raw `X-Hermes-Session-Id` so the *real* session resumes instead of an invisible parallel one (`_self_post_chat_completion` `:212`), and async-delegation completions are never self-POSTed (the client owns the next turn and a new `role=user` prompt could cross a pending human-confirmation gate) — they are persisted as a durable DELIVERY row instead (`persist_delegation_delivery` `:169`); failures **raise** after bounded retries (`:22-27`, `:256-283`) so callers can rewind cursors instead of silently losing the event, HTTP 429 is retried with backoff while any 4xx/5xx fails immediately (`:263-274`), and the profile-ownership proof reads the served profile's own `state.db` and fails closed on every doubt (`session_owned_by_profile` `:59-93`).

### `gateway/warning_notifications.py` — The delivery policy for engine diagnostics (never assistant or command responses): `warning_notifications_enabled` (`:90`) reads the canonical `suppress_warning_notifications` display setting with **no surface exemption** ("direct command/API outcomes are not notifications"), `DiagnosticText` (`:6`) is a producer-owned `str` subclass that marks the legacy two-argument status callback without changing string behaviour for existing plugin renderers (and is explicitly *not* a durable carrier), and the admission rules `diagnostic_turn_muted` (`:49`) / `diagnostic_wake_muted` (`:56`) mute a turn's presentation only when the owning policy hides diagnostics — human content never mutes (`:49-52`); `render_notification` (`:65`) / `present_notification` (`:78`) return *whether the renderer ran*, not whether a transport delivered anything.

### `gateway/whatsapp_identity.py` — Canonicalises WhatsApp sender identity so authorization (`gateway.run`) and session keys (`gateway.session`) never drift apart: `normalize_whatsapp_identifier` (`:29`) strips JID/LID/device/plus syntax to bare digits, `to_whatsapp_jid` (`:35`) is the inverse for *outbound* targets — a bare phone becomes `<digits>@s.whatsapp.net` because Baileys' `jidDecode` crashes on one (`:38-40`) — `expand_whatsapp_aliases` (`:69`) walks the bridge's `lid-mapping-*.json` files transitively (read `utf-8-sig` because Windows wrote BOM'd ones, `:84-86`, and guarded by `_SAFE_IDENTIFIER_RE` against path separators, `:22`, `:78`), and `canonical_whatsapp_identifier` (`:102`) picks the **shortest** alias so one human keeps one identity across phone-JID and LID variants.

### Tier 3 - skipped with a reason

| File | Why it was not read line by line |
| --- | --- |
| `gateway/assets/telegram-botfather-threads-settings.jpg` | SKIPPED: binary asset (118213 bytes, JPEG; image content not read) |

## 2. Ideas worth stealing

Marked `CONVERGENT` where the same shape appears in ≥2 of the three reference repos (as
observed in this shard) and `UNIQUE` where only this repo does it.

1. **Resolve identity ONCE per inbound event, then freeze it on the source.**
   `gateway/session_identity.py::resolve_identity` answers "which bot received it / who may
   admit it / where does it run" exactly once and pins a frozen `RoutingIdentity` on the
   source as a *wire-invisible* attribute; every downstream reader reads the pin and keeps
   its old fallback chain only for sources nothing resolved (restored rows, hand-built
   sources) — "Never derive a second answer next to the identity; extend the object"
   (`gateway/AGENTS.md:107-112`). The adapter side and the runner side each have their own
   `_canonicalize` seam, both called *before* any key derivation
   (`gateway/platforms/base.py:2405-2427`, `gateway/run_adapters.py:1689-1708`).
   *Why it transfers:* August Trading already has this smell — `threadForProvider` still
   falls back to a provider+model pair while `Message.botId` is the real claim
   (AGENTS.md, "An AI row is claimed by identity"). The freeze-at-the-seam pattern turns
   "who owns this row" from a scattered lookup into a value you can assert on.
   `UNIQUE` to this repo's shape, but the *rule* converges with `pi`/`deepseek-harness`
   session-identity discipline.

2. **Two resolvers, never one: the bot that RECEIVED vs the bot that ANSWERS.**
   `authz_mixin.py::_intake_adapter_for` (`:259`) and `_delivery_adapter_for` (`:295`) are
   separate, and the contract is a five-row topology matrix
   (`gateway/AGENTS.md:124-130`) with the ruling "never read `self.adapters[platform]` for a
   source; never add a third resolver" (`:118-124`). A source with no live provenance
   **fails closed to `None`** — never to a default bot.
   *Why it transfers:* August has the same two questions (the surface that received the
   question vs the surface that may show the answer) answered in one place. `CONVERGENT`
   shape: this is the same "one identity, two consumers" idea as the `pi` repo's
   session-key discipline.

3. **A durable delivery-obligation ledger with per-state crash semantics.**
   `gateway/delivery_ledger.py` records every final response as *owed* to the platform
   before the send (`pending`), flips to `attempting` immediately before the await, and
   marks `delivered` only on `SendResult.success`. Crash semantics are explicit
   (`:1-11`): pending = never started (redeliver plainly); attempting = crashed mid-await
   (the platform MAY have it — redeliver **with a visible recovered marker**); failed =
   rejected once; delivered = prune.
   *Why it transfers:* an August "the desk owes the trader an answer" ledger would survive a
   crash between finalize and paint. `CONVERGENT` — `pi` and `deepseek-harness` both keep
   durable turn/response state; the specific part (an *at-least-once* marker that tells
   the user it may be a duplicate) is the steal. `UNIQUE` refinement.

4. **Three distinct visible markers for a possibly-duplicate redelivery.**
   `RECOVERED_MARKER` (crash), `RECONNECTED_MARKER` (adapter reconnect) and `FLOOD_MARKER`
   (rate limit) are three different strings, because "the rate limit may have accepted
   earlier chunks" is not the same claim as "the gateway restarted"
   (`delivery_ledger.py:38-51`). Honest at-least-once delivery is *labelled*, not hidden.
   *Why it transfers:* August's notification/agent-delivery paths should distinguish
   "re-sent because the app restarted" from "re-sent because the provider rate-limited
   you" from "re-sent because the transport reconnected". `UNIQUE`.

5. **The retry budget never spends its LAST attempt in-process.**
   `retry_not_before` returns `None` once `attempts >= MAX_ATTEMPTS - 1`
   (`delivery_ledger.py:151-168`): an unclassified outage can outlast any timer, so one
   attempt is deliberately left for the boot sweep after a restart, which is "a real
   recovery signal". A claim released *unsent* decrements the counter
   (`release_runtime_claim`, `:322`).
   *Why it transfers:* directly applicable to August's automation/alert retry policy —
   never burn the last attempt on a timer; keep one for the restart path. `UNIQUE` in
   its exact form; `CONVERGENT` in the "a timer may abandon a row for good" insight.

6. **Approval wording lives in ONE module; every surface renders it.**
   `gateway/platforms/base_exec_approval.py` exists so "one edit changes every platform"
   (`:1-14`). The button card and the plain-text `/approve` fallback must both tell the
   user the same three things: what will run, why it was flagged, and that **silence
   means the command does NOT run** once `approvals.timeout` elapses. The approval card's
   "once / session / always / deny" choice set is rendered by each adapter's own markup
   (`gateway/platforms/ADDING_A_PLATFORM.md:135`).
   *Why it transfers:* August's trade-plan proposal card and its chat row must say the
   same thing — the repo already enforces "one home for the answer"
   (`tests/referenceVoice.test.tsx`). `CONVERGENT` (same rule as August's own
   `referenceVoice` test, and as `pi`'s single-voice rule).

7. **Approval timeout is a first-class outcome, and it is announced.**
   `run_turn_runner_approval_settle.py` registers a settle hook right after the prompt is
   delivered: on `reason == "timeout"` it posts a notice to the chat and **edits the button
   card in place** (dropping the buttons) when there is one; the plain-text prompt is
   *not* rewritten, because "rewriting it would erase the record of what was asked"
   (`:26-30`). The notice is skipped when the run is no longer current, and is best-effort
   — "the approval already resolved as 'no', and nothing here may block the agent thread".
   *Why it transfers:* August's skill-draft approval and trade-plan approval should
   resolve to "no" on timeout and say so out loud. `UNIQUE` in form; `CONVERGENT` in the
   "silence is a decision" principle.

8. **The turn lease serializes by SESSION ID, not by routing key — and fails CLOSED.**
   `gateway/turn_lease.py:4-8`: busy guards are keyed by routing key but the transcript is
   owned by session id, and `switch_session()` makes key→id many-to-one, so two keys could
   interleave flushes on one transcript (the "user;user" wedge). A waiter that exhausts its
   budget raises `TurnLeaseTimeoutError` and the caller must **not** enter the turn region
   (`:32-36`). Only *idle* leases evict, so the 512-entry cap can never break serialization
   (`:89-96`); a mid-turn rotation re-aliases the *same* lease object under the new id
   (`rebind`, `:139-163`).
   *Why it transfers:* August's agent desk has the same hazard — a session reachable by two
   ids (a thread tip-walk, a CLI-continuity resume) interleaving transcript writes.
   `UNIQUE` mechanism, `CONVERGENT` bug shape.

9. **A cursor, not an unsubscribe, is the dedup mechanism.**
   `gateway/kanban_watchers.py:60-71`: "The subscription is removed only when the task is
   `archived`: `done` is reversible, so the cursor — not unsubscribing — is the dedup
   mechanism (unsub-on-terminal dropped users when the dispatcher respawned a crashed
   task)." A send failure **rewinds** the cursor (`_kanban_rewind`, `:148`).
   *Why it transfers:* August's price alerts, trade alerts and automation schedules should
   dedupe on a persisted last-seen id, never by unsubscribing on "done". `UNIQUE` phrasing,
   `CONVERGENT` principle (idempotent cursors appear in all three repos).

10. **Opposite failure postures in adjacent code, on purpose.**
    The kanban *notifier* keeps running when its config cannot be read ("continuing
    enabled", `kanban_watchers.py:66-67`) while the kanban *dispatcher* disables itself
    ("disabled", `:218-220`) — because a notification is cheap and a runaway dispatcher is
    not (`:98-100`).
    *Why it transfers:* August should decide, per feature, whether a degraded read means
    "keep firing" or "stop", and write the reason next to the branch. `UNIQUE` in this
    explicit form.

11. **Progress comes from ONE source, and a stall never kills the turn.**
    `gateway/run_watchers.py:232-248`: the stall watchdog reads progress *only* from
    `AIAgent.get_activity_summary()`; pending inbound is "a notify policy gate, not a
    progress clock", and the watchdog is **notify-only** — it never kills the turn
    (contrast `gateway_timeout` / `shutdown_watchdog`). The notice re-reads the pending
    state *immediately* before delivery and aborts, un-latching, if the session progressed
    (`:166-179`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's "still working" row should have exactly one progress
    source, and a stall should surface, never cancel. `UNIQUE`.

12. **An unclean-death sentinel + a DEFERRED, fail-closed DB integrity check.**
    `gateway/lifecycle_ledger.py`: a sentinel at `<HERMES_HOME>/state/gateway.lifecycle.json`
    closes the gap a SIGKILL leaves; a boot that finds `phase == "running"` reports an
    unclean exit with the last heartbeat's memory and an OOM suspicion (`:156-181`). The
    `PRAGMA quick_check(1)` scan runs **after** the platforms connect, not on the startup
    path, because "on a multi-GB store it gated every platform adapter for minutes"
    (`:350-370`); and only a **WAL** store is scanned, because a rollback-journal scan's
    SHARED lock would block every live commit (`:367-370`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's SQLite store should carry the same sentinel and the same
    deferred, WAL-only probe. `UNIQUE` implementation, `CONVERGENT` idea (defer
    forensic work off the startup path).

13. **Owner identity is pid + process-start fingerprint, never pid alone.**
    Across `lifecycle_ledger.py:111-140`, `delivery_ledger.py:230-262` and
    `host_rendezvous.py:298-343`: PID reuse is a first-class hazard, the sentinel carries
    the process *birth* time, and "a reused PID's exit cannot vouch for a different life".
    On Windows the code never falls back to `os.kill(pid, 0)` — that maps to
    `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent` and could Ctrl+C the gateway's own console group
    (`delivery_ledger.py:240-250`).
    *Why it transfers:* August ships an Electron shell and a Capacitor native build; any
    "is that background job still the same process" check needs the same discipline.
    `UNIQUE` detail, `CONVERGENT` principle.

14. **A record states its own limits in-band, and evidence never gates execution.**
    `gateway/hosted_room_work_records.py`: the captured evidence carries a *fixed*
    `LIMITATIONS` list (`process_local_approvals_not_captured`, `not_execution_checkpoint`,
    `absent_record_is_not_non_admission`, …) that must match exactly (`:25-26`, `:169-170`),
    so a reader is told in-band what the record cannot prove. A rejected evidence write
    rolls back **only a SAVEPOINT**; the caller's driver transition still commits
    (`:242-262`). The acknowledgement shape is explicitly "never an admission or execution
    receipt" and carries `passive: True` (`:403-410`).
    *Why it transfers:* an August "lesson"/"insight" record should carry a fixed
    limitations list ("this did not see the live order book; this is not a recommendation"),
    and a failed reflection write must not roll back the trade-log entry it describes.
    `UNIQUE` and directly stealable.

15. **Debate is bounded, opt-in, and frozen.**
    `gateway/hosted_room_discussion.py`: 2-6 members, 3 rounds, 10 member messages, 24
    delta lines, 64 KB texts (`:26-32`). Round 0 responders come from the user's mentions
    (no mention = everyone); **later rounds are opt-in** — only a peer explicitly cited by
    a bot and not heard from since gets another turn (`:700-712`). Every member's watermark
    stays intact, so a peer cited later still receives the complete bounded delta without
    consuming turns meanwhile. A member message becomes policy input only once its
    terminal `turn.settled` names it (`:630-646`) — publication writes the visible message
    *before* the terminal event, so a crash in that gap would otherwise leave an uncommitted
    message in the transcript.
    *Why it transfers:* this is the concrete answer to "where does debate add value over
    one agent" — it adds value only when turns are *invited*, bounded, and the context each
    peer sees is a bounded delta rather than the whole transcript. `CONVERGENT` (all three
    repos limit agent fan-out; the opt-in-round rule is `UNIQUE`).

16. **The turn identity is a parseable coordinate, so a restart can re-derive and VERIFY it.**
    `hosted_room_discussion.py:50, :588-616`: the `turn_id` is
    `d<seq>.r<round>.p<pos>.s<seen>.m<member_digest>`; `reconstruct_task_plan` re-derives
    the plan and requires `reconstructed.identity == identity` **and** equal payloads
    (`:795-802`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's desk tool rounds could carry such an id, so a turn
    interrupted by a reload is reconstructible and provably the same turn. `UNIQUE`.

17. **Idempotent replay must still succeed after the fence moved on.**
    `gateway/hosted_room_driver.py::_transition(lease_first=...)` (`:474-516`): recovery
    paths check the lease *before* the row load, settlement paths check it *after* the
    idempotent replay — "an identical replay still succeeds after the lease moved on"
    (`:481-489`). A crashed-then-retried settlement still reports success; a recovery
    requeue refuses to run under a dead lease.
    *Why it transfers:* a re-delivered tool call or cron fire with the same result should
    succeed even across a restart, while a *recovery* action must not run under a dead
    owner. `UNIQUE` in form, `CONVERGENT` in principle.

18. **The lease is the fence, not the task; the lease may not outlive its work.**
    The run fence binds `(gateway_id, process_generation, lease_generation)`, so a task
    started under a reclaimed lease cannot settle under the new one
    (`hosted_room_driver.py:395`); `release_lease` refuses while any task is `running`
    (`:604-606`); a takeover increments `lease_generation` monotonically in a fenced UPDATE
    (`:570-582`); `acquire_lease` extends with `max()` so an expiry can never move
    backwards (`:558-566`).
    *Why it transfers:* a fencing token is the cheapest correct way to stop a resurrected
    worker from writing. `UNIQUE` mechanism, `CONVERGENT` principle (leases appear in all
    three repos).

19. **Control records get reserved capacity so the system can still close itself out.**
    `hosted_rooms.py:542-548`: `CONTROL_EVENT_COUNT_RESERVE = 64` events and
    `CONTROL_EVENT_BYTE_RESERVE = 1 MiB` are added when `allow_control=True`, so a room
    already at its byte limit can still be claimed, disclaimed or disbanded. The logical
    budget is also kept well below the physical limit because "event accounting excludes
    SQLite indexes" (`:40-43`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's trade journal must be able to record a *close/cancel* even
    when the log is at its cap. `UNIQUE`.

20. **Enforce the invariant in the database, not in Python, when more than one writer exists.**
    `gateway/hosted_room_work_storage.py`: four trigger families guard the same rows — a
    **budget** trigger (aborts when the store would exceed its byte/row cap, "a Python
    check is racy"), an **immutability** trigger, a **delete** trigger, and a **replace**
    trigger that refuses `INSERT OR REPLACE` because "INSERT OR REPLACE performs a delete
    internally" (`:106-157`). Orphans are *moved* to an `INVALID_TABLE` that is absolutely
    immutable, never deleted (`:56-63`, `:241-243`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's SQLite journal should enforce immutability and caps at the
    SQL level, and quarantine an invalid row rather than repair it into a plausible shape.
    `UNIQUE` implementation, `CONVERGENT` principle.

21. **The error's AUDIENCE decides how much detail it gets.**
    `gateway/session_ingress.py`: `RuntimeStoreError` reasons are distinguished —
    `stale_generation` → drop silently; `invalid_params` → a refusal notice naming only
    *content* clauses (bounded by the `_CONTENT_CLAUSES` allowlist, `:25-28`) because the
    reply reaches the whole chat, while trust/provenance clauses stay generic; `queue_full`
    → a canned reply. Separately, "An API caller asked for work, so its receipt is a
    failure either way, never a completed apology" (`:300-307`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's error surfaces should be split by audience, and a
    trust/provenance failure must never print its reason into a shared channel.
    `CONVERGENT` (all three repos separate user-facing from log-only errors).

22. **"Could not read it" must never read as "fine".**
    `gateway/disk_status.py:30-41` degrades an unreadable filesystem to
    `pressure="unknown"`; `gateway/memory_status.py:50-52` keeps a stale heartbeat's
    numbers but refuses to report a live "critical" banner, and "could not read it must
    never read as fine" (`:30`, `:100-104`).
    *Why it transfers:* a failed market-data fetch or an unreadable journal in August must
    render as *unknown*, never as "no signal" or "all clear". `UNIQUE` phrasing,
    `CONVERGENT` principle.

23. **Config layering by PRESENCE, with five named bridge modes — and "authored" means one file.**
    `gateway/config_loader.py`: top-level key *presence* wins over the nested
    `gateway.<key>` form — not truthiness (`:107-128`); the bridge table is five named
    modes (`presence` / `gwdata` / `none` / `dict` / `nested`, `:41-49`); "authored" means
    `config.yaml` **only** (`:262-272`); a managed (administrator) layer wins over the
    user's `platforms.<plat>.extra` by *removing* the pinned keys and re-merging, because
    "key membership alone cannot carry a value across a cross-shape merge" (`:274-302`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's provider config + local override + managed-policy
    layering. `UNIQUE` mechanism, `CONVERGENT` principle (all three repos layer config).
