24. **The env bridge must not write to the process env, and the child must not inherit a derived grant.**
    `config_loader.py:446-466, :57-66`: the bridge writes to `os.environ` only while no
    multiplexed secondary profile's scope is active (a write there is first-writer-wins
    process-wide poisoning, #80099); and `GATEWAY_ALLOW_ALL_USERS` is **dropped from the
    child process env** when the bridge itself wrote it, "so a restarted gateway re-derives
    the grant from its config.yaml instead of inheriting a stale open posture".
    *Why it transfers:* August must not persist a derived "open" posture into a child
    process or a saved snapshot. `UNIQUE`.

25. **Content-derived idempotency keys, shared between the ticker and the CLI.**
    `delivery_ledger.py::compute_obligation_id` = `sha256(session_key|message_ref|content)`
    truncated to 24 hex (`:264-267`); `session_cron.py` uses `cron_request_id(jid, request_id)`
    so "the same execution ID from ticker and CLI must dedupe together" (`:59-60`).
    *Why it transfers:* a schedule fired by the automation and re-submitted by hand must
    collapse to one run. `CONVERGENT` (idempotency keys are in all three repos).

26. **A "no consumer" check that a notification is not actually delivered.**
    `warning_notifications.py`: `render_notification` / `present_notification` return
    **whether the renderer ran**, not whether a transport delivered anything (`:65-78`).
    Elsewhere the contract is "a handler returning `None` is not acceptance" and "refused
    admission refunds every claimed batch sibling without spending an attempt"
    (`gateway/AGENTS.md:63-65`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's "sent" badge must mean delivered, not "we called the
    function". `UNIQUE` phrasing.

27. **A stable byte-order for the prompt block, because a reorder busts the cache.**
    `gateway/config.py:666-670`: `get_connected_platforms` sorts by platform value so the
    rendered "Connected Platforms" prompt block is byte-stable — "a reorder would bust the
    prompt cache".
    *Why it transfers:* August assembles prompts from lists (models, tools, bots). Sort
    them deterministically or the provider's prompt cache misses. `UNIQUE` rationale,
    `CONVERGENT` principle (all three repos care about prompt-cache stability).

28. **A contract sheet written FOR the coding agent, with the wrong fixes named.**
    `gateway/AGENTS.md` is a set of negative rulings, each traced to an incident:
    "Do NOT 'fix' gateway-dies-with-app by re-parenting the gateway under the backend", "do
    NOT 'fix' update locks by widening the tree-kill" (`:85-89`); "never read
    `self.adapters[platform]` for a source; never add a third resolver" (`:118-124`); the
    gateway reads user YAML **raw**, "a key the CLI sees but the gateway doesn't means
    you're on the wrong loader" (`:12-14`).
    *Why it transfers:* August already does this (the `tsc` blind spot, the render-probe
    rules, the ref-during-render rule). The transferable *addition* is naming the wrong
    fixes a future agent is likely to attempt. `CONVERGENT` (all three repos carry an
    AGENTS.md).

29. **An integration checklist that ends with a grep that finds what you missed.**
    `gateway/platforms/ADDING_A_PLATFORM.md` is a 16-step checklist of every integration
    point a new adapter must touch (enum, factory, authz maps, session source, prompt
    hint, toolset, cron delivery, send-message routing, channel directory, status
    display, setup wizard, redaction, docs, tests) and ends with a grep over the repo for
    the *other* platforms' names — "Check each file in the output — if it mentions other
    platforms but not yours, you missed it" (`:433-444`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's feature work has the same shape (a new provider, a new
    tool, a new surface). A grep-based "did I miss an integration point" check is cheap
    and catches the class of bug a checklist alone misses. `UNIQUE` in form.

30. **The empty package kept as a seam.**
    `gateway/builtin_hooks/__init__.py` is 57 bytes: a one-line docstring and nothing else
    — "the documented extension point for always-registered gateway hooks, with **none
    shipped**" (`gateway/AGENTS.md:11-12`).
    *Why it transfers:* naming the seam before you need it is cheap; filling it is not.
    `UNIQUE` but `CONVERGENT` in spirit (all three repos keep deliberate extension points).

31. **Adapter acceptance is NOT proof of delivery — pre-flight before you ack.**
    `gateway/run_notifications.py:1111-1117`: the inner resolver can still fail closed
    *inside* the pipeline *after* the adapter accepted, which would falsely ack the
    durable row, so the target is verified **first**, giving a drop an honest durable
    disposition. The injection itself is tri-state: `True` = adapter accepted, `False` =
    retryable adapter failure, `None` = no gateway route **or** a notice pinned to a
    permanently-gone session (a terminal drop) (`:868`, `:1136-1152`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's "the answer was delivered" bookkeeping needs the same
    three-way answer, and it must not be derived from "the function returned". `UNIQUE`.

32. **Only PERMANENT refusals count toward a drop cap; a transient one waits it out.**
    `run_notifications.py:752`, `:75-78`: `_requeue_refused_watch_event` counts only
    refusals proven permanent (`_refusal_permanent`) toward the cap of 8; a transient
    refusal (a restart wait, a parked profile, a full FIFO) is retried on a jittered
    backoff from 4 s capped at 60 s, however long it lasts.
    *Why it transfers:* this is exactly the shape August's automation breaker needs: the
    "three runs that produced nothing" breaker must not fire on a transient error, and a
    permanent one must be countable. `UNIQUE` in form, `CONVERGENT` in principle.

33. **Whole-slot key matching, never prefix matching.**
    `run_busy.py:63`, `:82`: `_strip_slot` matches a `:`-delimited slot as a WHOLE slot and
    `_same_chat_key_slots` matches the remainder as TEXT, because chat ids may themselves
    contain `:` (Matrix `!room:example.org`) — "a naive `startswith` would let session A's
    key match session B's".
    *Why it transfers:* August's session keys are composite (surface+chat+thread). A
    prefix comparison will cross-match two threads on the same chat. `UNIQUE` detail,
    `CONVERGENT` bug shape.

34. **Interrupt is DEMOTED to queue when it would corrupt state.**
    `run_busy.py:671-680`: an interrupt is demoted to a queue when active subagents or
    in-flight context compression would be corrupted; `_session_has_compression_in_flight`
    fails **CLOSED** — an unreadable lock holder is treated as "compression active"
    (`:411-416`, `:439`). A leaked lease is **re-acquired, not fenced**: `live_session_id`
    is the writer identity, so a lease leaked by this process is reclaimed by the next
    turn, and pruning only reclaims entries whose *process* died (`:334-341`).
    *Why it transfers:* August's `/stop` and "cancel the turn" paths should demote rather
    than interrupt when a write is in flight, and should fail closed on an unreadable
    lock. `UNIQUE` mechanism, `CONVERGENT` principle.

35. **A restart-loop breaker chained on the INTER-BOOT GAP, failing open.**
    `gateway/restart_loop_guard.py:101`: `check_and_record` records a restart-interrupted
    boot in `<HERMES_HOME>/gateway/restart_loop.json` and returns True once a *chained*
    boot count reaches `max_restarts` (3), at which point auto-resume is skipped. The
    chain is built on the inter-boot gap (`DEFAULT_MAX_GAP_SECONDS = 300`) rather than a
    fixed window, "so a slow loop cannot drop its history every boot", and every I/O
    failure fails **open** (`_load_boots` returns `[]` on any error) "so a broken state
    file never wedges the gateway".
    *Why it transfers:* this is the reference answer to "what breaks a repeating
    automation". It is a *chain* counter with a gap window, not a rolling rate limit, and
    it fails open on I/O error. `UNIQUE`.

36. **"Unknown idle time" is NOT recovery.**
    `gateway/session_stall.py:33-34`: `should_clear_session_stall_notification` returns
    False while `idle_seconds is None`, so an *observation gap* holds the latch rather
    than clearing it — "an unknown idle time is NOT recovery". The stall watchdog is
    notify-only and never kills the turn (`run_watchers.py:232-248`).
    *Why it transfers:* a stall detector must distinguish "the work is progressing" from
    "I could not tell", and only the first may clear an alarm. `UNIQUE`.

37. **A dedup map that says out loud what it does NOT guarantee.**
    `run_notifications.py:1002-1008`: the completion-identity dedup map is an in-process
    `OrderedDict` capped at 2048; it "closes queue/watcher races inside one gateway,
    *not* exactly-once across a crash" — the durable replay state stays owned by
    `tools.async_delegation`.
    *Why it transfers:* August's in-memory dedup tables should carry the same sentence.
    `UNIQUE`.

38. **The CLI computes a PRUNE PREVIEW; only the owner writes.**
    `gateway/session_prune.py:24-34`: `prune` is the owner's own `session.prune` write;
    the CLI only ever computes a preview, "because a CLI writing the store under a live
    gateway would be a second writer", and the write is refused unless the actor is the
    native local operator with `session:operator`.
    *Why it transfers:* August's desktop/CLI surfaces must not write the SQLite journal
    the running app owns. `UNIQUE`.

## 3. Trading-specific findings

Answering the six questions for **this shard** (the gateway transport/runtime layer). Where a
question belongs to another shard, this report says so.

**Q1 — Long-lived monitoring & scheduled work.** This shard is the cron and watcher layer.
- A cron job is a **named, persisted job**, never an ad-hoc prompt: "Public callers name a
  persisted job, never supply agent policy or arbitrary jobs. The ordinary admission
  ledger owns execution; the scheduler owns final delivery" (`gateway/session_cron.py:1-5`).
  A public caller passes `{job_id, request_id, extra_prompt}` and nothing else
  (`:74-76`); anything else is `invalid_params`.
- **Idempotency across the ticker and the CLI**: the fire's `request_id` *is* the
  `cron/executions` row id (`scheduler_execution_id`, `:213-216`), and `_create` returns
  the **existing** session when the request id was already admitted (`:82-83`) — a retry is
  a restore, not a second run. `cron_request_id(jid, request_id)` makes the ticker and a
  manual `/cron` dedupe together (`:59-60`).
- **Recovery after a lost answer**: `operation('recover')` polls the admission ledger and
  returns "the receipt a firer whose submit answer was lost polls, exactly as if it had
  arrived" (`:151-152`). `recover`/`status`/`cancel` are deliberately **not** fenced by a
  drain — only *new* work is — "because refusing them makes the firer give up
  (CronExecutionUnknown pauses the job) on every restart that overlaps a cron run"
  (`:125-128`).
- **A scheduled job's session is read-only except for its own frozen fire**: anything else
  typed into it fails with a *specific* descriptor (`gateway.errors.cron_session_read_only`),
  never a generic failure (`:111-113`, `:238-249`).
- **The store's own health is a first-class event**: when a profile's cron store becomes
  unwritable it posts ONE "unwritable" notice to that profile's home channels and one
  "recovered" notice when it comes back, debounced by `NOTICE_REPEAT_SECONDS = 3600`, and
  the delivery **re-checks the store's real state** so "the last notice a home channel
  sees always matches the store's real state" (`gateway/cron_store_notices.py:1-7`,
  `:36-50`, `:61-70`).
- **The watcher loops are deliberately dull**: every tick body is individually
  `try/except`ed so "one tick's failure never stops the next"
  (`gateway/kanban_watchers.py:105-108`, `:303-306`); the sleep is in 1-second slices so
  `stop()` never waits a full interval (`:52-55`); a stuck detector fires only after 6
  consecutive ticks with a non-empty ready queue and zero spawns, at most once per 300 s
  (`:310-322`).
- **A corrupt board is quarantined by FINGERPRINT, not by name**: `(path, mtime_ns, size)`,
  held for 300 s only while the fingerprint is unchanged — "transient WAL/open races can
  look like 'malformed' for one tick" (`gateway/kanban_watchers_dispatcher.py:140-171`).
- **A scheduled delivery target that is proven dead is remembered** per profile so cron
  ticks stop burning flood-control budget — but only *whole-chat* deaths count; a deleted
  forum topic must not mark the whole chat dead, and a later successful send clears the
  flag (`gateway/dead_targets.py:23-45`, `:94-107`).
- **A breaker for the restart loop itself, chained on the inter-boot gap**:
  `restart_loop_guard.py:101` — after 3 *chained* restart-interrupted boots (chain built
  on a 300 s inter-boot gap, not a fixed window) auto-resume is skipped; every I/O error
  fails **open** so a broken state file never wedges the process.
- **The completion-notification delivery is a claim/preflight/ack state machine**, not a
  fire-and-forget: the durable row is claimed only after the target is *proved*
  (`run_notifications.py:1111-1117`); the injection is tri-state
  (accepted / retryable / terminal drop, `:868`); only refusals proven **permanent**
  count toward the drop cap of 8, and a transient one is retried on a jittered 4 s→60 s
  backoff however long it lasts (`:752`, `:75-78`).
- **The busy path is a first-class trading concern**: a message that arrives while a
  session's agent is running is queued, steered, or interrupts — and an interrupt is
  **demoted to a queue** when a subagent or a context compression is in flight, with the
  compression check failing **closed** (`run_busy.py:671-680`, `:411-416`). The busy ack
  is debounced 30 s, and the suppression flag is tested *before* the debounce stamp is
  written so a suppressed ack never eats the next real one (`:976`, `:982`). Session
  keys are matched by **whole slot**, never by prefix, because chat ids contain `:`
  (`:63`, `:82`).
- **The stall watchdog is notify-only and never kills the turn**; progress comes from
  ONE source (`AIAgent.get_activity_summary()`), an *unknown* idle time is not treated as
  recovery, and the notice re-reads the pending state immediately before delivery
  (`run_watchers.py:232-248`, `:166-179`; `session_stall.py:33-34`).

**Q2 — Human-in-the-loop approval.** See ideas 6, 7 and 25 above.
- One module owns the wording; the button card and the plain-text fallback must say the
  same three things (`gateway/platforms/base_exec_approval.py:1-14`).
- The choice set is exactly `once / session / always / deny` (`base_exec_approval.py:60-67`).
- Silence is a decision: "⌛ Approval timed out after {window} — the command was NOT run.
  Ask me to try again if you still want it, or raise approvals.timeout in config.yaml"
  (`:23-26`).
- The timeout notice is registered *right after the prompt was delivered* (the original
  bug: only the TUI registered the hook, so on Telegram/Slack/WhatsApp a card whose timer
  ran out kept live buttons and the user never learned the command did NOT run) —
  `run_turn_runner_approval_settle.py:1-9`.
- The approval is *scoped*: `approval_mode ∈ {manual, smart, off}`, and advertising
  `off` to a peer is refused because a process-wide YOLO mode bypasses the scoped approval
  contextvar (`gateway/hosted_room_execution_policy.py:54`, `:263-265` in
  `hosted_room_peer.py`).
- The audit trail is a **digest-signed immutable policy** — `{version, target_profile,
  enabled_toolsets, approval_mode, max_iterations, policy_digest}` — and the digest
  mismatch is a refusal (`hosted_room_execution_policy.py:39-80`).

**Q3 — Post-mortem / reflection loops.** Partly in this shard (the hosted-room evidence
stack); the learning/rule loop itself lives in another shard.
- Outcomes become a **bounded, digest-sealed, passive evidence record**, not a new rule
  (`gateway/hosted_room_work_records.py:1`). It carries a fixed `LIMITATIONS` list so a
  reader knows in-band what it cannot prove (`:25-26`, `:169-170`).
- Bad accumulation is prevented by making the evidence **immutable and quarantined**:
  SQLite triggers forbid rewriting a non-current row, forbid deleting a live room's
  evidence, and refuse `INSERT OR REPLACE` because it deletes internally
  (`gateway/hosted_room_work_storage.py:136-157`). A malformed record is moved to an
  absolutely immutable `INVALID_TABLE` and **never** replaced with a fabricated fresh
  snapshot (`hosted_room_work_records.py:267-276`).
- The record is *versioned by authority epoch*: v2 requires `lineage_sha256` plus
  `incompleteness == ["prior_authority_work_unknown"]` — the record **admits it cannot
  know the previous authority's work** (`hosted_room_work_records.py:154-157`).
- A **schema-version bump rebuilds the derived projection, never the durable log**:
  all projection tables are deleted, the cursor reset to 0, and the room log is never
  rewritten (`gateway/hosted_room_policy_checkpoint.py:262-274`).
- The acknowledgement is explicitly "never an admission or execution receipt" and carries
  `passive: True` (`hosted_room_work_records.py:403-410`).

**Q4 — Multi-agent: subagents vs debate.** This shard carries the debate implementation.
- Debate adds value **only** as bounded, invited rounds: 2-6 members, 3 rounds, 10 member
  messages, 24 delta lines, 64 KB texts (`hosted_room_discussion.py:26-32`).
- Round 0 responders are chosen by the user's **mentions** (no mention = everyone); later
  rounds are **opt-in** — a peer must be explicitly cited *and* not have spoken since
  (`:700-712`). A cited-but-silent peer keeps its watermark and receives the complete
  bounded delta later without consuming turns meanwhile.
- A peer's message becomes policy input only once its terminal `turn.settled` names it
  (`:630-646`) — publication writes the visible message *before* the terminal event, so a
  crash in that gap would leave an uncommitted message in the transcript.
- The **roster is frozen** and cross-gateway fields are rejected outright, so a member
  cannot smuggle remote routing into a local roster (`:58-60`, `:253-256`).
- The **subagent** path is a closed delegation ledger with its own ownership proof: the
  worker supplies a verified execution id + PID + **birth time**, never payload identity
  (`session_worker_delegation.py:1-6`); delivery to the parent is a claim/ack protocol with
  a token-guarded claim and a `defer` action that *refunds* the attempt
  (`:152-176`).
- Where debate is *wasted*: the host adapter's own verdict is "the room log carries only
  server-minted ids and bounded metadata" — the honest summary is that the repo spends
  its complexity on *determinism and boundedness*, not on more agents.

**Q5 — Structured logs the model can query, without a heavy database.**
- The gateway's durable store is a single **SQLite `state.db` in WAL mode** with a
  10-second busy timeout (`delivery_ledger.py:179-185`); the hosted-room stack keeps
  rooms in a **separate `shared-state.db`** because "pointing profile gateways at the
  master session store makes every profile process a long-lived writer on state.db — the
  recurring multi-writer corruption vector observed across a 6-gateway fleet"
  (`hosted_rooms.py:404-419`).
- The **admission ledger** is the one FIFO: every inbound (human, cron, automation
  heartbeat, producer wake) becomes a row with a principal, a `request_id`, a payload and a
  status; the status set includes `unknown`, which means "an interrupted started turn" —
  a *distinct* state from "failed" (`session_automation.py:199-217`,
  `session_ingress.py:69-82`).
- The hosted-room log is **append-only and byte-budgeted**: `read_events` computes a
  cumulative byte window in SQL and then **binary-searches the largest prefix whose
  serialized page fits the page limit** (2 MiB) — `hosted_rooms.py:1198-1207`. A room
  event that does not fit alone is a hard error.
- The transcript of a finished discussion is stored as **references into the room log**
  ("prompt payloads are never duplicated outside room byte limits") and joined back on
  read (`hosted_room_policy_checkpoint.py:50-52`, `:155`).
- Forensics are **JSONL**: `gateway-exit-diag.log` gets one JSON line per unclean exit
  and one per deferred integrity verdict (`lifecycle_ledger.py:98-108`, `:392-400`).
- A memory monitor emits one grep-friendly `[MEMORY] rss=… gc=… threads=… uptime=…` line
  every N seconds (`memory_monitor.py`) — the model can grep its own runtime.
- What the store does NOT claim: the completion-identity dedup map is an in-process
  `OrderedDict` capped at 2048 and says so — it closes queue/watcher races inside one
  gateway, "not exactly-once across a crash"; the durable replay state belongs to
  `tools.async_delegation` (`run_notifications.py:1002-1008`).

**Q6 — Staying simple as features accumulate.**
- A **contract sheet with negative rulings** keeps the seams from multiplying
  (`gateway/AGENTS.md`) — "never add a third resolver", "no `if canonical == ...` chains"
  (`:16-17`).
- One facade, phases as separate files, slash commands as mixins looked up by name through
  `_command_handler_table` (`gateway/AGENTS.md:6-18`).
- **Bounds are constants, not configuration**: 2-6 members, 3 rounds, 10 messages, 24
  delta lines, 64 KB texts, 512 leases, 500 ledger rows, 64 control events, 1 MiB control
  bytes, 16 MiB per-room event cap, 256 MiB replica cap, 256 rooms, 1024 authority spans.
- The `Platform` enum accepts unknown names **only** for registered plugin adapters — no
  enum pollution (`gateway/config.py:244-276`).
- `builtin_hooks/` is an empty package kept as a named seam, with nothing shipped.
- A plugin path exists that needs **zero core changes**: `plugin.yaml` + `adapter.py` +
  `ctx.register_platform()` (`ADDING_A_PLATFORM.md:5-15`).

## 4. Non-obvious decisions and gotchas

Things a reader would get wrong:

1. **The gateway does NOT read `DEFAULT_CONFIG`.** It reads the user YAML **raw**
   (`run.py` + `config.py`); "a key the CLI sees but the gateway doesn't means you're on
   the wrong loader" (`gateway/AGENTS.md:12-14`). `resolve_agent_cache_bounds` likewise
   reads the raw config so "operator chose 128" is distinguishable from "unset"
   (`agent_cache_pressure.py:121-126`).
2. **TWO message guards, and approval/control commands must bypass BOTH.** The base
   adapter queues inbound into `_pending_messages` when the session is active; the runner
   then intercepts `/stop`, `/new`, `/queue`, `/status`, `/approve`, `/deny`. Such a
   command must be dispatched **inline** — "never via `_process_message_background()`,
   which races session lifecycle" (`gateway/AGENTS.md:20-28`).
3. **The streaming contract is four invariants, each from a live duplicate-final
   incident**: draft frames must be *prefix-stable* (frame N is a string prefix of N+1);
   "the consumer declares the final; the adapter never guesses"; every non-final send
   carries `metadata["_interim_send"] = True`; and a lane delivering a final beside a
   sealed stream reconciles **by edit, never by plain send**
   (`gateway/AGENTS.md:30-53`). Seal-interception exists at BOTH egress doors — a new
   egress door needs the same two checks.
4. **`os.kill(pid, 0)` is NOT a no-op on Windows** — it maps to
   `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent(0, pid)` and can Ctrl+C the gateway's own console group
   (`delivery_ledger.py:240-250`). Use the cross-platform existence probe.
5. **A `sqlite3.Row` membership test scans VALUES, not column names** — `"x" in row` is
   wrong; the code must use `row.keys()` (`hosted_rooms.py:500-502`).
6. **`INSERT OR REPLACE` performs a delete internally** — it will destroy an immutable
   snapshot, so it is refused by a trigger before the uniqueness conflict can fire
   (`hosted_room_work_storage.py:146-147`).
7. **Profile gateways must write to the shared ROOT `shared-state.db`, never the master
   `state.db`** — otherwise every profile is a long-lived writer on one WAL/FTS database
   and the fleet corrupts it (`hosted_rooms.py:404-419`).
8. **`hosted_room_driver.TerminalStatus` is `{settled, failed}` but `TERMINAL_STATUSES` is
   `{settled, failed, cancelled}`** — `cancelled` is terminal for transition *guards* yet
   is not a legal settlement status, so a cancelled task can never be settled
   (`hosted_room_driver.py:27`, `:36`).
9. **`_cancel_generation` accepts `bool`** ("a bool is an int; do not swap for
   non_negative_int") while `hosted_rooms_common.bounded_int` rejects bools — a documented
   divergence, not an oversight (`hosted_room_driver.py:419`).
10. **A scoped secret miss must return the DEFAULT, never `os.environ`.** Under
    multiplexing `os.environ` holds the *default* profile's values, so borrowing it leaks
    one profile's allowlist into another's decision and "silently rejects every
    secondary-profile sender" (#86905) (`platforms/_shared.py:70-75`).
11. **`check_fn` must be a passive probe and `ensure_deps_fn` the active installer.**
    Wiring the installer as the check pip-installed Discord/Telegram/Slack SDKs on *every*
    `load_gateway_config()` call — including a `GET /api/status` readiness probe — and
    boot-looped the desktop app at 94% (#79812) (`config_env.py:203-218`).
12. **Relay is EXCLUSIVE**: `GATEWAY_RELAY_URL` disables every other messaging platform
    even explicitly-enabled ones, because a directly-connected adapter is a second
    unmanaged ingress (`config_env.py:300-345`).
13. **`multiplex_profiles` defaults to `None`, not `False`** — `None` means "a request the
    gateway settles at boot"; every reader tests truthiness, so an unresolved value never
    multiplexes by accident (`config.py:612-619`).
14. **YAML 1.1 booleans break transport modes**: bare `mode: off` parses as `False`, and
    `str(False).lower()` is `"false"` — which would have *enabled* streaming
    (`config.py:93-101`).
15. **The API server rebuilds the agent per request but must NOT rebuild the memory
    provider** — the `MemoryManager` is parked between requests, because the previous
    turn's queued recall never reaches the next request otherwise (#120116)
    (`platforms/api_server_memory_sessions.py:1-13`; `gateway/AGENTS.md:139-144`).
16. **The api_server adapter bypasses the `TurnRunner` and the agent cache entirely** —
    per-request callbacks, model route and ephemeral prompt — so the usual turn lifecycle
    does not apply to it.
17. **A `deliver` cron artifact is never dropped by the silence filter.** The
    silence-narration filter (`*(silent)*`, 🔇, a bare `.`) is a substrate-level anti-loop
    guard for bot-to-bot channels, but it is **skipped for cron output** — "dropping it
    while returning success is how a cron gets logged 'delivered' with nothing on the
    wire" (#77763) (`delivery.py:280-292`).
18. **`_is_shared_bot_satellite` is the legacy fallback** for rows written before the
    `transport_profile` column existed; every restored source is re-pinned with
    `RoutingIdentity(transport=None)` so the new readers never fall back
    (`gateway/AGENTS.md:139-144`; `session_identity.py:152-189`).
19. **`session_context` uses a tri-state `_UNSET` sentinel**: `""` means "explicitly
    cleared, no fallback" and is *not* the same as "never set here, fall back to
    `os.environ`" (`session_context.py:13-14`, `:191-197`).
20. **`reset_session_vars` exists because `create_task` snapshots the context** — task B
    inherits task A's already-set vars, and a subprocess spawned before B binds would read
    A's identity (`session_context.py:178-188`).
21. **A `MemoryManager` checkout is fenced by `RETRY_HORIZON_S`**: a checked-in manager is
    shut down if the housekeeping fence on that id aged out, "the id may now name a
    different session and must not inherit its recall"
    (`platforms/api_server_memory_sessions.py:66`).
22. **The watchdog stops feeding when the event loop falls behind** — "a watchdog that
    keeps pinging a stalled gateway hides the stall it exists to expose"
    (`systemd_notify.py:86-106`).

## 5. Weak spots

1. **Scale and the "facade" illusion.** The gateway subtree is 328 files / 6.6 MB, and
   `gateway/run.py` is 275 KB while `gateway/platforms/base.py` is 278 KB and
   `gateway/platforms/api_server.py` is 258 KB. The `run.py` "facade + `run_*.py` phases"
   split is real at the *file* level but `run.py` is still a monolith that owns the
   profile-scope helpers, the admission seams and the boot order. August's `App.tsx` has
   the same shape of risk: a "split the big file" rule that produces many files without
   reducing the *coupling*.
2. **The hosted-room stack is ~250 KB of fail-closed machinery for an unshipped
   feature.** `hosted_room_driver.py` states it plainly: "The schema has never shipped, so
   the whole draft is fail-closed by design" (`:291-293`), and the module validates its
   own column set and refuses to start if it differs (`:255`). Three of its four
   status/flag sets disagree with each other by design (`TerminalStatus` vs
   `TERMINAL_STATUSES`). This is careful engineering, but it is the kind of speculative
   surface that `pi` and `deepseek-harness` do not carry.
3. **Intent is only recoverable from the incident numbers.** Comments cite `#64934`,
   `#86905`, `#80099`, `#115542`, `#120116`, `#110549`… — a reader of the *current* code
   learns that a rule exists but not why it was written, unless the git history is
   available. The `AGENTS.md` sheet is the mitigation, and it is good, but it covers the
   seams, not the ~200 individual rules.
4. **Config is a five-layer tangle**: raw YAML (five named bridge modes) + a managed
   administrator layer + env overrides + per-plugin `env_overrides` + a per-profile secret
   scope, with the rule that the gateway reads the raw file and *not* `DEFAULT_CONFIG`.
   Each layer is individually documented; the composition is where a mistake will hide.
5. **Some `os.environ` writes are still process-global mutations**, scoped and restored
   by hand: the auto-decomposer sets `HERMES_KANBAN_BOARD` per board in a `try/finally`
   (`kanban_watchers_dispatcher.py:262-270`), and the YAML→env bridge writes to
   `os.environ` at all (guarded by the scope check). Each is defended in a comment, which
   is a sign the defence is load-bearing.
6. **The delivery ledger's error classification is partly heuristic** — a flood refusal is
   detected by regex over the platform's own wording
   (`_RAW_FLOOD_RE`, `delivery_ledger.py:82`) and by a `flood_control:<seconds>` prefix
   that adapters must produce. A provider that words it differently is treated as an
   ordinary failure and gets claimed immediately, spending an attempt inside the penalty
   window.
7. **The size of the enterprise machinery is not proportional to the domain.** For a
   *messaging gateway*, the shard contains a cross-gateway federation protocol
   (HMAC room grants, epoch lineage, replica promotion, work-record evidence with
   in-band limitation lists). It is well-engineered, but it is a different product from
   "deliver a message", and a smaller project copying the *shape* of these files will
   inherit the cost without the benefit.
8. **Some guarantees are local and are honestly scoped, but a reader can still
   over-read them.** The completion dedup map closes races "inside one gateway, *not*
   exactly-once across a crash" (`run_notifications.py:1002-1008`); the admission
   identity dedup map is an in-process `OrderedDict` too. The code says it, but the
   *type* of the guarantee (in-process dict) does not. A crash-consistent claim needs a
   durable store, and the repo has one (`tools.async_delegation`) — the risk is a reader
   citing the in-process map.
9. **Two readers of the same concept disagree about which is authoritative.** The
   `delivery_ledger` open path uses `get_process_hermes_home()` ("the ONE shared store")
   while the hosted-room stack deliberately writes to the shared ROOT `shared-state.db`
   and NOT the master `state.db` (`hosted_rooms.py:404-419`). Both are right in their
   own scope, and the resulting rule ("which database am I in?") is the single most
   error-prone thing in the shard for a newcomer.
